“You are not old enough to be a general servant or a cook; you have not experience enough to be a housemaid; you don’t take to sewing, and there is no chance of being accepted as a hospital nurse: you must confess there is nothing you can do. You are really a very useless girl for your age.”
===Chinese===
“当粗使女佣或厨娘，你年纪不够；当房里侍女，你历练不足；针线你又不上手，进医院当护士又没有指望：你得承认，你什么也干不来。你这个年纪的姑娘，实在是百无一用。”

===Original===
“There are heaps of things I could do.”
===Chinese===
“我能做的事多着呢。”

===Original===
“Tell me a few of them.”
===Chinese===
“说几样来听听。”

===Original===
I was silent. The professions at which I felt I had the latent power to excel, were I but given a chance, were in a sphere far above us, and to mention my feelings and ambitions to my matter-of-fact practical mother would bring upon me worse ridicule than I was already forced to endure day by day.
===Chinese===
我沉默了。我自觉有潜力干得出色的那些行当——只要给我一个机会——都远在我们够不着的天地里；而对着讲求实际、一是一二是二的母亲吐露我的心思与抱负，只会招来比我现在日日强忍的更难堪的讥诮。

===Original===
“Mention a few of the things you could do.”
===Chinese===
“说说你几样会做的事。”

===Original===
I might as well have named flying as the professions I was thinking of. Music was the least unmentionable of them, so I brought it forward.
===Chinese===
我心里盘算的那些行当，说出来还不如干脆说“我要飞”。其中数音乐最拿得出手，我便把它端了出来。

===Original===
“Music! But it would take years of training and great expense before you could earn anything at that! It is quite out of the question. The only thing for you to do is to settle down and take interest in your work, and help make a living at home, or else go out as a nurse-girl, and work your way up. If you have any ability in you it would soon show. If you think you could do such strokes, and the home work is not good enough for you, go out and show the world what a wonderful creature you are.”
===Chinese===
“音乐！可要靠它挣到钱，先得搭上多少年的苦练和一大笔开销！根本免谈。你只有一条路：安下心来，对自己的活计上点心，帮衬着在家里挣口饭吃；再不然就出去当个看孩子的保姆，一步一步往上熬。你要真有能耐，早晚自会显出来。你要是自以为能干那样的大事业，嫌家里的活计辱没了你，那就出去，让世人瞧瞧你是怎样一位了不得的人物。”

===Original===
“Mother, you are unjust and cruel!” I exclaimed. “You do not understand one at all. I never thought I could do strokes. I cannot help being constituted so that grimy manual labour is hateful to me, for it is hateful to me, and I hate it more and more every day, and you can preach and preach till you go black in the face, and still I’ll hate it more than ever. If I have to do it all my life, and if I’m cursed with a long life, I’ll hate it just as much at the end as I do now. I’m sure it’s not any wish of mine that I’m born with inclinations for better things. If I could be born again, and had the designing of myself, I’d be born the lowest and coarsest-minded person imaginable, so that I could find plenty of companionship, or I’d be born an idiot, which would be better still.”
===Chinese===
“母亲，你不公道，也残忍！”我喊道。“你根本不理解人。我从不以为自己能干什么大事业。天生长了这么一副性子，见了乌糟糟的体力活就憎厌——这由不得我，它就是叫我憎厌，而且我一天比一天恨得更深；你尽可以训呀劝呀，训到脸皮发黑，我也照恨不误，只有恨得更凶。倘若我得一辈子干它，又倘若老天爷罚我长寿，那么到末了，我恨它的分量会同今天一般无二。我敢担保，生来心性向往更好的事物，绝不是我自己乐意的事。倘若能再投一回胎，由得我自己打样，我情愿生作天底下顶低下、顶粗鄙的人，好寻得一帮同伴；要不然就生作个白痴，那还更痛快些。”

===Original===
“Sybylla!” said my mother in a shocked tone. “It is a wonder God doesn’t strike you dead; I never heard﻿—”
===Chinese===
“西比拉！”母亲用震骇的声气说。“真是奇了，上帝怎么不打个雷劈死你；我从没听说过——”

===Original===
“I don’t believe there is a God,” I said fiercely, “and if there is, He’s not the merciful being He’s always depicted, or He wouldn’t be always torturing me for His own amusement.”
===Chinese===
“我不信有什么上帝，”我凶狠地说，“就算有，他也决不像人人画的那副慈悲模样，不然他不会成天拿折磨我来给他自己取乐。”

===Original===
“Sybylla, Sybylla! That I should ever have nurtured a child to grow up like this! Do you know that﻿—”
===Chinese===
“西比拉，西比拉！我怎么竟养出这么个孩子来！你可知道——”

===Original===
“I only know that I hate this life. I hate it, I hate it, I hate it,” I said vehemently.
===Chinese===
“我只知道我恨这种生活。我恨它，我恨它，我恨它。”我激愤地说。

===Original===
“Talk about going out to earn your own living! Why, there’s not a woman living would have you in her house above a day. You are a perfect she-devil. Oh God!” And my mother began to cry. “What have I done to be cursed with such a child? There is not another woman in the district with such a burden put upon her. What have I done? I can only trust that my prayers to God for you will soften your evil heart.”
===Chinese===
“还谈什么出去自食其力！哼，天底下没有一个活着的女人肯把你留在家中超过一天。你十足是个母夜叉。哦，上帝呀！”母亲哭了起来。“我作了什么孽，要受罚摊上这么个孩子？这一带再没有第二个女人担着这样的重负。我作了什么呀？我只指望我对上帝替你作的祷告，能把你那颗狠心焐软了。”

===Original===
“If your prayers are answered, it’s more than ever mine were,” I retorted.
===Chinese===
“要是你的祷告应验了，可比我的祷告强多了。”我回敬道。

===Original===
“*Your* prayers!” said my mother, with scorn. “The horror of a child not yet sixteen being so hardened. I don’t know what to make of you, you never cry or ask forgiveness. There’s dear little Gertie now, she is often naughty, but when I correct her she frets and worries and shows herself to be a human being and not a fiend.”
===Chinese===
“*你*的祷告！”母亲满含鄙夷地说。“一个还不满十六岁的孩子，心肠竟硬到这般地步，想想都叫人骇然。我真不知该拿你怎么办，你从来不哭，也从不讨饶。就说亲爱的格蒂吧，她也常常淘气，可我一管教她，她就抹眼泪、发愁，显出她是个人，不是个恶魔。”

===Original===
So saying my mother went out of the room.
===Chinese===
母亲说着便走出了房间。

===Original===
“I’ve asked forgiveness once too often, to be sat upon for my pains,” I called out.
===Chinese===
“我讨饶讨过太多回，落下的只有白讨一场、还遭压服。”我扬声喊道。

===Original===
“I believe you’re mad. That is the only feasible excuse I can make for your conduct,” she said as a parting shot.
===Chinese===
“我看你是疯了。对你的所作所为，我能替你找的、唯一说得过去的托词，也就这一个了。”她临出门丢下这么一句。

===Original===
“Why the deuce don’t you two get to bed and not wrangle like a pair of cats in the middle of the night, disturbing a man’s rest?” came in my father’s voice from amid the bedclothes.
===Chinese===
“你们娘儿俩究竟见什么鬼，半夜三更不睡觉，跟一对猫似的斗口，搅得人不得安生？”被窝里传来父亲的声音。

===Original===
My mother is a good woman﻿—a very good woman﻿—and I am, I think, not quite all criminality, but we do not pull together. I am a piece of machinery which, not understanding, my mother winds up the wrong way, setting all the wheels of my composition going in creaking discord.
===Chinese===
我的母亲是个好女人——一个非常好的女人——而我自己，我想，也还不至于浑身是邪性；只是我们拧不成一股绳。我好比一台机器，母亲不懂它的构造，偏往反方向上发条，把我浑身的齿轮驱得吱嘎乱响、彼此倾轧。

===Original===
She wondered why I did not cry and beg forgiveness, and thereby give evidence of being human. I was too wrought up for tears. Ah, that tears might have come to relieve my overburdened heart! I took up the homemade tallow candle in its tin stick and looked at my pretty sleeping sister Gertie (she and I shared the one bed). It was as mother had said. If Gertie was scolded for any of her shortcomings, she immediately took refuge in tears, said she was sorry, obtained forgiveness, and straightaway forgot the whole matter. She came within the range of mother’s understanding, I did not; she had feelings, mother thought, I had none. Did my mother understand me, she would know that I am capable of more depths of agony and more exquisite heights of joy in one day than Gertie will experience in her whole life.
===Chinese===
她纳闷我为什么不哭、不讨饶，好显出自己毕竟是人。可我激动过了头，流不出泪来。啊，但愿眼泪肯落下来，救一救我这颗超载的心！我端起锡烛台上那支自家炼的牛油烛，端详着睡熟的漂亮妹妹格蒂（她跟我合睡一张床）。果然同母亲说的一样。格蒂若为哪桩短处挨了责备，立刻便躲进眼泪里，说声对不起，讨得饶恕，转眼就把整桩事忘个干净。她落在母亲理解的射程之内，我落在射程之外；母亲以为她有感情，我没有。母亲若真懂得我，便会知道：我一天里所能沉入的苦痛之深、所能升到的欢乐之高，胜过格蒂一辈子经历的总量。

===Original===
Was I mad as mother had said? A fear took possession of me that I might be. I certainly was utterly different to any girl I had seen or known. What was the hot wild spirit which surged within me? Ah, that I might weep! I threw myself on my bed and moaned. Why was I not like other girls? Why was I not like Gertie? Why were not a new dress, everyday work, and an occasional picnic sufficient to fill my mind? My movements awakened Gertie.
===Chinese===
我真像母亲说的疯了吗？一阵恐惧攫住了我：说不定真是。我确实同我见过、认得的任何姑娘都截然不同。在我胸中翻腾的这团火热狂野的魂灵，究竟是什么？啊，但愿我哭得出来！我扑倒在床上呻吟。我为什么不像别的姑娘？为什么不像格蒂？为什么一件新衣裳、日日的活计、偶尔一回野餐，竟填不满我的心？我的动静惊醒了格蒂。

===Original===
“What is the matter, dear Sybylla? Come to bed. Mother has been scolding you. She is always scolding someone. That doesn’t matter. You say you are sorry, and she won’t scold any more. That’s what I always do. Do get into bed. You’ll be tired in the morning.”
===Chinese===
“怎么啦，亲爱的西比拉？上床来睡吧。母亲骂你了。她老是骂这个骂那个。骂骂不打紧的。你说声对不起，她就不再骂了。我回回都是这么办。快上床吧。明早该累了。”

===Original===
“What does it matter if I will be. I wish I would be dead. What’s the good of a hateful thing like I am being alive. No one wants or cares for me.”
===Chinese===
“累不累的有什么要紧。我巴不得自己死了才好。像我这么个可憎的东西，活着有什么用。没有人要我，也没有人心疼我。”

===Original===
“I love you, Sybylla, better than all the rest. I could not do without you,” and she put her pretty face to mine and kissed me.
===Chinese===
“我爱你，西比拉，胜过爱所有人。没有你我可不行。”说着，她把她那张漂亮的脸蛋贴上我的脸，亲了我一下。

===Original===
What a balm to the tempest-tossed soul is a little love, though it may be fleeting and fickle! I was able to weep now, with wild hot tears, and with my sister’s arms around me I fell asleep without undressing further.
===Chinese===
哪怕它转瞬即逝、哪怕它易变无常，一点点爱，对暴风雨里颠簸的灵魂是多大的抚慰啊！这回我哭得出来了，热泪狂涌；妹妹的臂膀搂着我，我余下的衣裳也不脱了，就此睡去。

## VII Was E’er a Rose Without Its Thorn? / 第7章 哪有玫瑰不长刺

===Original===
I arose from bed next morning with three things in my head﻿—a pair of swollen eyes, a heavy pain, and a fixed determination to write a book. Nothing less than a book. A few hours’ work in the keen air of a late autumn morning removed the swelling from my eyes and the pain from my temples, but the idea of relieving my feelings in writing had taken firm root in my brain. It was not my first attempt in this direction. Two years previously I had purloined paper and sneaked out of bed every night at one or two o’clock to write a prodigious novel in point of length and detail, in which a full-fledged hero and heroine performed the duties of a hero and heroine in the orthodox manner. Knowing our circumstances, my grandmother was accustomed, when writing to me, to enclose a stamp to enable me to reply. These I saved, and with them sent my book to the leading Sydney publisher. After waiting many weeks I received a polite memo to the effect that the story showed great ability, but the writer’s inexperience was too much in evidence for publication. The writer was to study the best works of literature, and would one day, no doubt, take a place among Australian novelists.
===Chinese===
第二天早晨下了床，我脑袋里装着三样东西——一双哭肿的眼睛、一阵沉沉的胀痛，还有一个写一本书的铁定决心。非一本书不可。在暮秋清晨凛冽的空气里干了几个钟头的活，眼中的浮肿退了，鬓角的疼痛也散了，可借写作一吐胸臆的念头，已在我脑子里扎下了牢牢的根。这并非我头一回朝这个方向用劲。两年前我就偷过纸，每夜一两点钟溜下床去，写一部论篇幅、论细节都蔚为大观的长篇小说，里面一对羽翼已丰的男女主人公，一板一眼照着正统的路数履行男女主人公的职责。外婆知道我们的境况，来信时惯常附上一枚邮票，好让我有回信的资费。我把这些邮票攒下来，拿它们把我的书寄给了悉尼首屈一指的出版商。苦等了好多星期之后，我收到一纸客客气气的短函，大意说：故事颇见功力，只是作者的稚嫩显露得太甚，不宜出版。作者当研习文学中的上乘之作，将来无疑能在澳大利亚小说家中占得一席之地。

===Original===
This was a very promising opinion of the work of a child of thirteen, more encouraging than the great writers got at the start of their literary career; but it seemed to even my childish intelligence that the memo was a stereotyped affair that the publisher sent in answer to all the MSS. of fameless writers submitted to him, and also sent in all probability without reading as much as the name of the story. After that I wrote a few short stories and essays; but now the spirit moved me to write another book﻿—not with any hope of success, as it was impossible for me to study literature as advised. I seldom saw a book, and could only spare time in tiny scraps to read them when I did.
===Chinese===
对一个十三岁孩子的作品，这评语实在算是青眼有加，比那些大作家文学生涯起步时得到的还更鼓舞人；可是连我那点孩童的悟性也看得分明：这短函是套着模板印出来的货色，出版商拿来打发所有无名作者投上门的手稿，多半连书名都懒得瞧上一眼便发了出来。在那以后我又写过几篇短篇小说和几篇随笔；可如今那股心劲又攥住了我，要我再写一本书——倒不指望成功，因为要我照着劝告去研习文学，根本办不到。我难得见着一本书，见着了，也只能抠出针头线脑的零碎工夫来读。

===Original===
However, the few shillings I had obtained at odd times I spent on paper, and in secret robbed from much-needed rest a few hours weekly wherein to write. This made me very weary and slow in the daytime, and a sore trial to my mother. I was always forgetting things I should not have forgotten, because my thoughts were engaged in working out my story. The want of rest told upon me. I continually complained of weariness, and my work was a drag to me.
===Chinese===
不过，我在零星活计里挣下的几个先令（shilling）都拿去买了纸，还偷偷从急需的睡眠里每星期克扣出几个钟头来写作。这使得我白天委顿迟钝，也成了母亲一桩苦刑。我总是忘掉不该忘的事，因为心思正忙着编排我的故事。缺觉在我身上显了形。我成天嚷累，活计成了磨人的苦役。

===Original===
My mother knew not what to make of it. At first she thought I was lazy and bad, and punished me in various ways; but while my book occupied my mind I was not cross, gave her no impudence, and did not flare up. Then she began to fear I must be ill, and took me to a doctor, who said I was much too precocious for my years, and would be better when the weather got warmer. He gave me a tonic, which I threw out the window. I heard no more of going out as nurse-girl: father had joined a neighbour who had taken a road contract, and by this means the pot was kept, if not quite, at least pretty near, boiling.
===Chinese===
母亲摸不清这其中的路数。起初她当我又懒又坏，变着法儿罚我；可只要我的书占住了我的心，我就不闹性子，不冲她顶嘴，也不一点就着。后来她又开始疑心我是病了，带我去看医生。医生说我相对年纪太过年早熟，等天气转暖自会好起来。他给我开了一剂补药，我抬手就从窗户扔了出去。再没人提让我出去当保姆的话了：父亲搭了一个邻居的伙，那人揽下一份修路的合同；靠这个进项，家里的锅纵然烧不到滚沸，至少也一直咝咝地热着。

===Original===
Life jogged along tamely, and, as far as I could see, gave promise of going to the last slip-rails without a canter, until one day in July 1896 mother received a letter from her mother which made a pleasant change in my life, though, like all sweets, that letter had its bitter drop. It ran as follows:﻿—
===Chinese===
日子就这样温温吞吞地小跑着，照我看来，大有就这么一路走到最后一道栏门、连一遭快步也懒得撒的指望；直到 1896 年 7 月的一天，母亲收到她母亲的一封信，给我的生活带来一个可喜的转机——只是天下的甜东西都带着一滴苦汁，这封信也不例外。信是这样写的：——

===Original===
> Only a short letter this time. I am pressed for time, as four or five strangers have just come and asked to stay for the night, and as one of the girls is away, I have to get them beds. I am writing about Sybylla. I am truly grieved to hear she is such a source of grief and annoyance to you. The girl must surely be ill or she would never act as you describe. She is young yet, and may settle down better by and by. We can only entrust her to the good God who is ever near. Send her up to me as soon as you can. I will pay all expenses. The change will do her good, and if her conduct improves, I will keep her as long as you like. She is young to mention in regard to marriage, but in another year she will be as old as I was when I married, and it might be the makings of her if she married early. At any rate she will be better away from Possum Gully, now that she is growing into womanhood, or she may be in danger of forming ties beneath her. She might do something good for herself up here: not that I would ever be a matchmaker in the least degree, but Gertie will soon be coming on, and Sybylla, being so very plain, will need all the time she can get.
> Your loving mother,
> L. Bossier.
===Chinese===
> 这回只写短短几行。我正赶时间，刚来了四五个过路的生人，求在此借宿一夜，偏巧有个女仆又不在，铺盖都得我来张罗。我要说的是西比拉的事。听说她竟成了你这样一桩伤心事、一个烦恼根子，我实在难过。这孩子准是身子有病，不然决计不会像你描述的那样行事。她还小，往后兴许能安顿下来、慢慢见好。我们只能把她托付给时刻在近旁的仁慈上帝。你尽早送她上我这儿来。一切盘缠我全数承付。换个环境对她有好处；她若行事见好，你愿意留她多久，我就留她多久。论说亲，她还小；可再过一年，她就同我出嫁那年一般年纪了，若嫁得早，兴许倒成就了她。无论如何，如今她将成个大姑娘，离袋熊谷远些总是好事，不然恐怕有结下低于她身份的情缘的危险。她到这儿来，兴许能为自家谋点好处：倒不是我存一分一毫做媒的心，只是格蒂眼看着也要到年纪了，西比拉生得又这么平常，能争取的时日都得争取才是。
> 爱你的母亲，
> L. Bossier.

===Original===
My mother gave me this letter to read, and, when I had finished perusing it, asked me would I go. I replied coldly:
===Chinese===
母亲把这封信交给我读，等我读完，问我想不想去。我冷冷地答道：

===Original===
“Yes. Paupers and beggars cannot be choosers, and grandmother might as well keep me at Caddagat as at Possum Gully”﻿—for my grandmother contributed greatly to the support of our family.
===Chinese===
“想。穷光蛋和叫花子没得挑三拣四，外婆与其把我养在袋熊谷，倒不如就把我养在卡达加特”——外婆对我们一家的生计，接济得着实不少。

===Original===
As regards scenery, the one bit of beauty Possum Gully possessed was its wattles. Bowers of grown and scrubs of young ones adorned the hills and gullies in close proximity to the house, while groves of different species graced the flats. Being Sunday, on this afternoon I was at liberty for a few hours; and on receiving the intelligence contained in the letter, I walked out of the house over a low hill at the back into a gully, where I threw myself at the foot of a wattle in a favourite clump, and gave way to my thoughts.
===Chinese===
论风景，袋熊谷仅有的那么一点美，全在它的金合欢。成树搭起花亭、幼树簇成丛薮，装点着紧邻宅子的丘峦沟壑；各个品种的小树林，又给平地添了风致。这天是星期天，下午我有几个钟头可以自由支配；得了信里的消息，我便走出家门，翻过屋后一座小丘，进了一道山谷，在一丛我素来喜爱的金合欢底下就地躺倒，由着思绪翻腾。

===Original===
So mother had been telling my grandmother of my faults﻿—my grandmother whom I loved so dearly. Mother might have had enough honour and motherly protection to have kept the tale of my sins to herself. Though this intelligence angered, it did not surprise me, being accustomed to mother telling every neighbour what a great trial I was to her﻿—how discontented I was, and what little interest I took in my work. It was the last part of the letter which finished up my feelings. Oh heavens! Surely if my mother understood the wild pain, the days and hours of agony pure and complete I have suffered on account of my appearance, she would never have shown me that letter.
===Chinese===
原来母亲一直在把我那些短处讲给外婆听——讲给我那么至亲至爱的外婆听。母亲本该有那么一点体面、那么一点为母的护犊之心，把我那本罪状账捂在自己怀里。这消息虽叫我恼火，倒不叫我吃惊，我早听惯了母亲对每一个邻人数说我是她多大的一个磨难——说我多么不知足，对活计多么不上心。把我的心情彻底了结掉的，是信的末尾那几句。哦，苍天！倘若母亲懂得，我这副容貌叫我吃过怎样狂野的苦、受过怎样纯而又纯、整日整时的煎熬，她断不会把那封信拿给我看。

===Original===
I was to be given more time on account of being ugly﻿—I was not a valuable article in the marriage market, sweet thought! My grandmother is one of the good old school, who believed that a girl’s only proper sphere in life was marriage; so, knowing her sentiments, her purpose to get me married neither surprised nor annoyed me. But I was plain. Ah, bosh! Oh! Ah! I cannot express what kind of a feeling that fact gave me. It sank into my heart and cut like a cruel jagged knife﻿—not because it would be a drawback to me in the marriage line, for I had an antipathy to the very thought of marriage. Marriage to me appeared the most horribly tied-down and unfair-to-women existence going. It would be from fair to middling if there was love; but I laughed at the idea of love, and determined never, never, never to marry.
===Chinese===
原来只因我生得丑，便要多宽限我些时日——在婚姻市场上我算不得一件值钱的货色，多贴心的主意呀！外婆是老派贤人里头的一个，坚信姑娘家一辈子唯一本分的出路就是嫁人；所以既然晓得她的心思，她那要把我嫁掉的盘算既不叫我惊奇，也不叫我恼怒。可我是长得平常的呀。呸，扯淡！哦！唉！这个事实给了我一种什么样的滋味，我表达不出来。它沉进我心里，像一柄凶残的豁口钝刀来回地锯——倒不是因为它会拖我婚事的后腿，我对结婚这念头本身就怀着憎恶。婚姻在我看来，是眼前顶顶可怕的、捆住手脚专亏待女人的活法。倘若有爱情，还能算个中不溜；可我把爱情这念头当笑柄看，拿定主意永远、永远、永远不嫁。

===Original===
The other side of the letter﻿—the part which gave me joy﻿—was the prospect of going to Caddagat.
===Chinese===
信的另一头——给我欢喜的那一头——是去卡达加特的前景。

===Original===
Caddagat, the place where I was born! Caddagat, whereat, enfolded in grandmotherly love and the petting which accrued therefrom, I spent some of my few sweet childish days. Caddagat, the place my heart fondly enshrines as home. Caddagat, draped by nature in a dream of beauty. Caddagat, Caddagat! Caddagat for me, Caddagat forever! I say.
===Chinese===
卡达加特，我出生的地方！卡达加特，在那儿，裹在外婆的疼爱和由此而来的百般娇宠里，我度过了一生中为数不多的几个甜美的孩童日子。卡达加特，我心中深情供奉为家的地方。卡达加特，被大自然披挂上一袭梦一般美的衣装。卡达加特，卡达加特！我要说：卡达加特归我，卡达加特万岁！

===Original===
Too engrossed with my thoughts to feel the cold of the dull winter day, I remained in my position against the wattle-tree until Gertie came to inform me that tea was ready.
===Chinese===
我一心沉在思绪里，全然不觉那个阴沉冬日的寒冷，就那么倚着金合欢树待在原地，直到格蒂来告诉我茶点备好了。

===Original===
“You know, Sybylla, it was your turn to get the tea ready; but I set the table to save you from getting into a row. Mother was looking for you, and said she supposed you were in one of your tantrums again.”
===Chinese===
“你知道，西比拉，本来轮到你摆茶点；可我把桌子摆好了，省得你挨一顿排头。母亲刚才还在找你，说她想你准是又犯牛脾气了。”

===Original===
Pretty little peacemaker! She often did things like that for me.
===Chinese===
漂亮的小和事佬！她常替我做这样的事。

===Original===
“Very well, Gertie, thank you. I will set it two evenings running to make up for it﻿—if I’m here.”
===Chinese===
“好啦，格蒂，谢谢你。我连着两个傍晚摆桌子补上——要是我还在的话。”

===Original===
“If you are here! What do you mean?”
===Chinese===
“要是你还在！你这是什么意思？”

===Original===
“I am going away,” I replied, watching her narrowly to see if she cared, for I was very hungry for love.
===Chinese===
“我要走了。”我答道，一面紧紧盯着她，看她是否在乎，因为我渴爱渴得厉害。

===Original===
“Going to run away becauses mother is always scolding you?”
===Chinese===
“是不是因为母亲老骂你，你要逃跑啦？”

===Original===
“No, you little silly! I’m going up to Caddagat to live with grannie.”
===Chinese===
“不是，小傻瓜！我要上卡达加特去，跟外婆一起住。”

===Original===
“Always?”
===Chinese===
“一直住吗？”

===Original===
“Yes.”
===Chinese===
“嗯。”

===Original===
“Really?”
===Chinese===
“真的？”

===Original===
“Yes.”
===Chinese===
“真的。”

===Original===
“Honour bright?”
===Chinese===
“说话算话？”

===Original===
“Yes; really and truly and honour bright.”
===Chinese===
“嗯；真的，真的，说话算话。”

===Original===
“Won’t you ever come back again?”
===Chinese===
“那你再也不回来了吗？”

===Original===
“I don’t know about *never* coming back again; but I’m going up for always, as far as a person can lay out ahead of her. Do you care?”
===Chinese===
“*永远*不回来，这个我说不准；可我是去长住的，人能往前打算多远，就住多远。你在乎吗？”

===Original===
Yes she cared. The childish mouth quivered, the pretty blue-eyed face fell, the ready tears flowed fast. I noticed every detail with savage comfort. It was more than I deserved, for, though I loved her passionately, I had ever been too much wrapped in self to have been very kind and lovable to her.
===Chinese===
她在乎的。那张孩子气的嘴哆嗦起来，漂亮的蓝眼睛小脸垂了下去，说来就来的眼泪淌得飞快。我怀着凶狠的快意，把每一处细节都收在眼里。这份情分是我受之有愧的，因为尽管我热烈地爱她，我却一向太裹在自己身上，不曾对她多么温存、多么讨人喜欢。

===Original===
“Who will tell me stories now?”
===Chinese===
“往后谁给我讲故事呀？”

===Original===
It was a habit of mine to relate stories to her out of my own fertile imagination. In return for this she kept secret the fact that I sat up and wrote when I should have been in bed. I was obliged to take some means of inducing her to keep silence, as she﻿—even Gertie, who firmly believed in me﻿—on waking once or twice at unearthly hours and discovering me in pursuit of my nightly task, had been so alarmed for my sanity that I had the greatest work to prevent her from yelling to father and mother on the spot. But I bound her to secrecy, and took a strange delight in bringing to her face with my stories the laughter, the wide-eyed wonder, or the tears﻿—just as my humour dictated.
===Chinese===
凭我自己丰饶的想象给格蒂编故事讲，是我的一桩老习惯。作为回报，她替我瞒着一件事：本该上床睡觉的时辰，我总坐起来写字。我不得不想些法子哄她守口，因为她——连格蒂这样死心塌地信我的人——也在不是人起的鬼时辰里醒来过一两回，撞见我正赶我的夜间功课，为我的神志吓得魂不附体，我费了九牛二虎之力，才拦住她没当场喊爹叫娘。可我总算哄得她守住秘密，并从中得着一种古怪的快乐：借我的故事，把笑声、圆睁双眼的惊奇或是眼泪，唤到她脸上——全凭我一时的心绪指派哪一样。

===Original===
“You’ll easily get someone else to tell you stories.”
===Chinese===
“再找人给你讲，还不容易。”

===Original===
“Not like yours. And who will take my part when Horace bullies me?”
===Chinese===
“总不像你讲的。还有，霍勒斯欺负我的时候，谁来护着我呀？”

===Original===
I pressed her to me.
===Chinese===
我把她紧紧搂向我。

===Original===
“Gertie, Gertie, promise me you will love me a little always, and never, never forget me. Promise me.”
===Chinese===
“格蒂，格蒂，答应我，往后要永远爱我一点点，永远、永远不忘记我。答应我。”

===Original===
And with a weakly glint of winter sunshine turning her hair to gold, and with her head on my shoulder, Gertie promised﻿—promised with the soluble promise of a butterfly-natured child.
===Chinese===
一缕稀薄的冬日斜照把她的头发映成金色，她把头靠在我肩上，答应了——用的是蝴蝶心性的孩子那份一化就化的许诺。

### Self Analysis / 自我剖析

===Original===
*N.B.﻿—This is dull and egotistical. Better skip it. That’s my advice﻿—S. P. M.*
===Chinese===
*注——这一节枯燥乏味，又满纸自我。最好跳过去别读。这是我的忠告——S. P. M.*

===Original===
As a tiny child I was filled with dreams of the great things I was to do when grown up. My ambition was as boundless as the mighty bush in which I have always lived. As I grew it dawned upon me that I was a girl﻿—the makings of a woman! Only a girl﻿—merely this and nothing more. It came home to me as a great blow that it was only men who could take the world by its ears and conquer their fate, while women, metaphorically speaking, were forced to sit with tied hands and patiently suffer as the waves of fate tossed them hither and thither, battering and bruising without mercy. Familiarity made me used to this yoke; I recovered from the disappointment of being a girl, and was reconciled to that part of my fate. In fact, I found that being a girl was quite pleasant until a hideous truth dawned upon me﻿—I was ugly! That truth has embittered my whole existence. It gives me days and nights of agony. It is a sensitive sore that will never heal, a grim hobgoblin that nought can scare away. In conjunction with this brand of hell I developed a reputation of cleverness. Worse and worse! Girls! girls! Those of you who have hearts, and therefore a wish for happiness, homes, and husbands by and by, never develop a reputation of being clever. It will put you out of the matrimonial running as effectually as though it had been circulated that you had leprosy. So, if you feel that you are afflicted with more than ordinary intelligence, and especially if you are plain with it, hide your brains, cramp your mind, study to appear unintellectual﻿—it is your only chance. Provided a woman is beautiful allowance will be made for all her shortcomings. She can be unchaste, vapid, untruthful, flippant, heartless, and even clever; so long as she is fair to see men will stand by her, and as men, in this world, are “the dog on top,” they are the power to truckle to. A plain woman will have nothing forgiven her. Her fate is such that the parents of uncomely female infants should be compelled to put them to death at their birth.
===Chinese===
小小年纪的时候，我心里就装满了长大成人后要干那番大事业的梦。我的抱负，同我始终栖身其中的莽莽丛林一样，无边无际。随着年岁渐长，我才渐渐醒悟：我是个女孩——一副女人的坯子！只不过是个女孩——仅此而已，别无其他。有一桩事实当头一棒让我明白过来：只有男人，才能揪住世界的耳朵、征服自己的命运；而女人呢，打个比方说，只配捆着双手坐在那里，忍气吞声，任命运的波涛把自己抛来掷去，无情地捶打磕碰。日子久了，这副轭我也套惯了；我从做女孩的失望里缓过劲来，同命运的这一部分也讲和了。说实在的，我发觉做姑娘原本相当惬意，直到一个狰狞的真相在我头上炸开——我长得丑！这个真相把我的一生都泡苦了。它日日夜夜给我熬人的痛楚。它是一处永不合口的敏感溃疡，一个什么都吓不退的凶恶妖怪。与这枚地狱烙印形影相伴，我又挣来一个聪明的名声。糟上加糟！姑娘们！姑娘们！你们中间凡是有心的——因而盼着日后有幸福、有家、有丈夫的——千万别挣一个聪明的名声。它能把你的婚事逐出局，效力不亚于四下传开你染了麻风。所以，倘若你自觉受着超平常人的智力的作弄，尤其是倘若你还生得平常，那就藏起你的脑子，捆住你的心智，下功夫学做一个不露聪明的人——那是你唯一的机会。只要一个女人生得美，她一切短处都会得到宽宥。她可以不贞、乏味、不老实、轻浮、没心肝，甚至可以聪明；只要她生得好看，男人们就肯替她撑腰；而男人在这个世界上是“骑在上头的狗”，是要巴结奉承的权势。一个长得平常的女人，却什么也得不到宽恕。她们的命运既是如此，那生了丑女婴的爹娘，实在应当被勒令孩子一落地就把她结果了。

===Original===
The next unpleasant discovery I made in regard to myself was that I was woefully out of my sphere. I studied the girls of my age around me, and compared myself with them. We had been reared side by side. They had had equal advantages; some, indeed, had had greater. We all moved in the one little, dull world, but they were not only in their world, they were of it; I was not. Their daily tasks and their little pleasures provided sufficient oil for the lamp of their existence﻿—mine demanded more than Possum Gully could supply. They were totally ignorant of the outside world. Patti, Melba, Irving, Terry, Kipling, Caine, Corelli, and even the name of Gladstone, were only names to them. Whether they were islands or racehorses they knew not and cared not. With me it was different. Where I obtained my information, unless it was born in me, I do not know. We took none but the local paper regularly, I saw few books, had the pleasure of conversing with an educated person from the higher walks of life about once in a twelvemonth, yet I knew of every celebrity in literature, art, music, and drama; their world was my world, and in fancy I lived with them. My parents discouraged me in that species of foolishness. They had been fond of literature and the higher arts, but now, having no use for them, had lost interest therein.
===Chinese===
我在自己身上发现的第二桩不痛快，是我凄凄惨惨地错生了自己的圈子。我端详周围同年纪的姑娘们，拿自己同她们逐一相比。我们是一道长大的。她们享有过同等的便宜，有几个的便宜确实还更大。我们全都活动在同一个狭小无聊的世界里，可她们不但在她们的世界里，而且就属于那个世界；我不是。她们的日常活计和小小的乐子，足够给她们各自的命灯添油——我那盏灯要的油，袋熊谷供不上。她们对外面的世界全然无知。帕蒂（Patti）、梅尔巴（Melba）、欧文（Irving）、特里（Terry）、吉卜林（Kipling）、凯恩（Caine）、科雷利（Corelli），连格莱斯顿（Gladstone）这名字，在她们听来都不过是几个名字。那些是海岛还是赛马，她们不知道，也无意知道。我却不然。那些见闻我从哪儿得来的，除非是娘胎里带的，我自己也说不上。我们家定期订的只有本地报纸，我见不着几本书，同一位出身上流、受过教育的人士晤谈的乐趣，一年约莫摊得上一回；然而文学、艺术、音乐、戏剧里的每一位名人，我全都有数；他们的世界就是我的世界，我在遐想里同他们朝夕共处。父母从不鼓励我这一路的痴愚。他们原是爱好文学和高雅艺术的，如今既然用不上了，也就断了兴致。

===Original===
I was discontented and restless, and longed unendurably to be out in the stream of life. “Action! Action! Give me action!” was my cry. My mother did her best with me according to her lights. She energetically preached at me. All the old saws and homilies were brought into requisition, but without avail. It was like using common nostrums on a disease which could be treated by none but a special physician.
===Chinese===
我不知足、不安分，难忍难熬地巴望着投身到生活的激流里去。“行动！行动！给我行动！”是我的呼号。母亲照她的见识，尽了力来管教我。她起劲地对我说教。一切老掉牙的格言训诫都被征调出阵，却一概无效。这就好比拿市面上的万金油，去治一种非专科圣手治不了的病。

===Original===
I was treated to a great deal of harping on that tiresome old string, “Whatsoever your hand findeth to do, do it with all your might.” It was daily dinned into my cars that the little things of life were the noblest, and that all the great people I mooned about said the same. I usually retorted to the effect that I was well aware that it was noble, and that I could write as good an essay on it as any philosopher. It was all very well for great people to point out the greatness of the little, empty, humdrum life. Why didn’t they adopt it themselves?
===Chinese===
我还被灌了一耳朵又一耳朵陈年老调的絮叨：“凡你手所当做的事，要尽力去做。”他们天天往我耳朵里聒噪：生活里的小事才是顶高贵的，我所痴心妄想的那班伟人，说的也是同一套。我通常回嘴说：我完全清楚这很高贵，这个题目我能写出一篇同任何哲学家一样漂亮的文章。大人物们指点说，渺小、空虚、千篇一律的生活自有其伟大——说得倒轻巧。他们自己怎么不照着办呢？

===Original===
> “The toad beneath the harrow knows
 

 Exactly where each tooth-point goes.
 

 The butterfly upon the road
 

 Preaches contentment to the toad.”
===Chinese===
> “耙下的蟾蜍知道，
 

 每一枚耙齿咬向何处。
 

 大路上的蝴蝶，
 

 却向蟾蜍宣讲知足。”

===Original===
I wasn’t anxious to patronize the dull kind of tame nobility of the toad; I longed for a few of the triumphs of the butterfly, decried though they are as hollow bubbles. I desired life while young enough to live, and quoted as my motto:
===Chinese===
我无意高攀蟾蜍那种驯顺的、闷气的高贵；我渴望的是蝴蝶的那几场得意，哪怕它们被贬作空心的泡泡。趁着还年轻得能活，我要的是生活，于是引了下面的诗当作座右铭：

===Original===
> “Though the pitcher that goes to the sparkling rill
 

 Too oft gets broken at last,
 

 There are scores of others its place to fill
 

 When its earth to the earth is cast.
 

 Keep that pitcher at home, let it never roam,
 

 But lie like a useless clod;
 

 Yet sooner or later the hour will come
 

 When its chips are thrown to the sod.
> “Is it wise, then, say, in the waning day,
 

 When the vessel is crack’d and old,
 

 To cherish the battered potter’s clay
 

 As though it were virgin gold?
 

 Take care of yourself, dull, boorish elf,
 

 Though prudent and sage you seem;
 

 Your pitcher will break on the musty shelf,
 

 And mine by the dazzling stream.”
===Chinese===
> “水罐虽然去那波光潋滟的小溪，
 

 到头来常常摔个粉碎，
 

 有的是旁的水罐顶它的缺，
 

 只待它的泥身归了泥土。
 

 把那水罐守在家里，别让它出远门，
 

 只管像块无用的土坷垃躺着；
 

 可迟早总有一刻要来，
 

 它的碎片终要抛给草土。
> “那么请问，在日色向晚的时分，
 

 当罐子已经裂纹斑斑、老朽，
 

 还把砸伤的陶土当宝贝疼惜，
 

 仿佛它是足赤的黄金，可算聪明？
 

 照看好你自己吧，蠢笨粗鄙的乡佬，
 

 尽管你显得又稳重又贤明；
 

 你的水罐会碎在发霉的搁板上，
 

 我的却碎在那耀眼的溪流中。”

===Original===
I had sense sufficient to see the uselessness of attempting to be other than I was. In these days of fierce competition there was no chance for me﻿—opportunity, not talent, was the main requisite. Fate had thought fit to deny me even one advantage or opportunity, thus I was helpless. I set to work to cut my coat according to my cloth. I manfully endeavoured to squeeze my spirit into “that state of life into which it has pleased God to call me.” I crushed, compressed, and bruised, but as fast as I managed it on one side it burst out on another, and defied me to cram it into the narrow box of Possum Gully.
===Chinese===
我的神志尚且够用，看得出想活得不像我自己，纯属枉然。在这个竞争白热化的年头，我没有出头之日——要紧的资格是机会，不是才具。命运认定不宜赏我哪怕一星半点的便宜或机会，于是我束手无策。我只得看布裁衣，拿出男子汉的狠劲，竭力把我的魂灵往“上帝乐意召我进去的那一等生活地位”里塞。我压、我挤、我磕得它青一块紫一块，可这头刚塞服帖，那头又迸出来，公然抗命，说什么也不容我把它塞进袋熊谷这只窄箱子。

===Original===
> “The restless throbbings and burnings
 

 That hope unsatisfied brings,
 

 The weary longings and yearnings
 

 For the mystical better things,
 

 Are the sands on which is reflected
 

 The pitiless moving lake,
 

 Where the wanderer falls dejected,
 

 By a thirst he never can slake.”
===Chinese===
> “那无休无止的悸动与灼烧，
 

 是未得满足的希望带来的；
 

 那困人的憧憬与渴念，
 

 只为那缥缈的更美事物；
 

 正是那片沙岸，上面映着
 

 那无情游移的湖影，
 

 流浪人在那儿颓然倒下，
 

 挨着一场永远解不了的渴。”

===Original===
In a vain endeavour to slake that cruel thirst my soul groped in strange dark places. It went out in quest of a God, and finding one not, grew weary.
===Chinese===
在消解那酷渴的徒劳挣扎里，我的灵魂在古怪幽暗的角落中摸索。它出去寻访一位上帝，寻访不获，便倦怠了。

===Original===
By the unknown way that the atmosphere of the higher life penetrated to me, so came a knowledge of the sin and sorrow abroad in the world﻿—the cry of the millions oppressed, downtrodden, Godforsaken! The wheels of social mechanism needed readjusting﻿—things were awry. Oh, that I might find a cure and give it to my fellows! I dizzied my brain with the problem; I was too much for myself. A man with these notions is a curse to himself, but a woman﻿—pity help a woman of that description! She is not merely a creature out of her sphere, she is a creature without a sphere﻿—a lonely being!
===Chinese===
高尚生活的气息循着某条无名的路径渗到我这里，随之而来的是一种知晓：知晓世上遍布的罪孽与忧患——千百万受压迫、遭践踏、被上帝抛却之人的哭喊！社会机器的轮子需要重新校准——一切都拧歪了。啊，但愿我能觅得一剂良方，献给我的同类！这道难题搅得我脑仁发晕；我自己都快盛不下自己了。怀这一路念头的男人，已是自身的祸殃；而女人——上帝怜恤这样的女人吧！她不只是错出圈子的生灵，她是根本没有圈子的生灵——一个孤零零的生命！

===Original===
Recognizing this, I turned and cursed God for casting upon me a burden greater than I could bear﻿—cursed Him bitterly, and from within came a whisper that there was nothing there to curse. There was no God. I was an unbeliever. It was not that I sought after or desired atheism. I longed to be a Christian, and fought against unbelief. I asked the Christians around me for help. Unsophisticated fool! I might as well have announced that I was a harlot. My respectability vanished in one slap. Some said it was impossible to disbelieve in the existence of a God: I was only doing it for notoriety, and they washed their hands of me at once.
===Chinese===
认清了这一层，我便掉头诅咒上帝，怪他把一副我担不起的担子压到我肩上——狠狠地诅咒了他；可心底里随即传来一声耳语：那儿空空如也，没什么可诅咒的。没有上帝。我是个不信神的人。这并非我追求或乐于做无神论者。我巴望做基督徒，同不信的念头苦苦搏斗过。我向周围的基督徒们讨个帮助。没见过世面的蠢材！我还不如干脆宣布自己是个娼妇。我的体面在一记耳光之下荡然无存。有些人说，不信上帝的存在根本不可能：我这般作为无非是图个出风头——他们当即同我洗净了手，撇清了干系。

===Original===
Not believe in God! I was mad!
===Chinese===
竟然不信上帝！我疯了！

===Original===
If there really was a God, would they kindly tell me how to find Him?
===Chinese===
倘若真有一位上帝，可否劳他们的驾告诉我，怎样才能寻着他？

===Original===
Pray! pray!
===Chinese===
祷告！祷告！

===Original===
I prayed, often and ardently, but ever came that heart-stilling whisper that there was nothing to pray to.
===Chinese===
我祷告了，屡屡而热切地祷告，可回来的永远只有那声叫人心死的耳语：并没有可祷告的对象。

===Original===
Ah, the bitter, hopeless heart-hunger of godlessness none but an atheist can understand! Nothing to live for in life﻿—no hope beyond the grave. It plunged me into fits of profound melancholy.
===Chinese===
啊，无神的那种苦涩无望的心头饥饿，除了无神论者，谁也体会不得！生活里没有可为之而活的东西——坟墓那头没有指望。它把我一阵阵掀进深不见底的忧郁里。

===Original===
Had my father occupied one of the fat positions of the land, no doubt as his daughter my life would have been so full of pleasant occupation and pleasure that I would not have developed the spirit which torments me now. Or had I a friend﻿—one who knew, who had suffered and understood, one in whom I could lose myself, one on whom I could lean﻿—I might have grown a nicer character. But in all the wide world there was not a soul to hold out a hand to me, and I said bitterly, “There is no good in the world.” In softer moods I said, “Ah, the tangle of it! Those who have the heart to help have not the power, and those who have the power have not the heart.”
===Chinese===
倘若父亲占着国内的哪一桩肥缺，毫无疑问，身为他的女儿，我的日子会被称心的营生和赏心的乐事塞得满满当当，也就养不成如今这般折磨我的心性。又或者，我若有一个朋友——一个懂的、受过苦而能体谅的人，一个我能把整个自己消融进去的人，一个我能倚靠的人——我兴许会长成一副好些的品性。可是茫茫寰宇，竟没有一个人肯朝我伸出手来，于是我刻毒地说：“这世上没有一丝儿好。”心肠软和些的时候，我又说：“唉，这团乱麻呀！有心相帮的没有力量，有力量的没有心肠。”

===Original===
Bad, like a too-strong opponent in a game of chess, is ever at the elbow of good to checkmate it like a weakly managed king.
===Chinese===
恶，好比棋局里过于强大的对手，永远贴在善的肘边，把它当作一只调度无方的王，一步一步将死。

===Original===
I am sadly lacking in self-reliance. I needed someone to help me over the rough spots in life, and finding them not, at the age of sixteen I was as rank a cynic and infidel as could be found in three days’ march.
===Chinese===
我太缺乏自立的骨气。我需要有人扶我一把，好走过人生的坎坷处；这样的人求而不得，于是在十六岁上，我已是三天路程之内所能找到的、顶顶地道的愤世者兼异端。

## VIII Possum Gully Left Behind. Hurrah! Hurrah! / 第8章 告别袋熊谷，好哇！好哇！

===Original===
If a Sydney man has friends residing at Goulburn, he says they are up the country. If a Goulburn man has friends at Yass, he says they are up the country. If a Yass man has friends at Young, he says they are up the country, and so on. Caddagat is “up the country.”
===Chinese===
悉尼人若有朋友住在古尔本，他便说人家住在“内地”。古尔本人若有朋友住在雅斯（Yass），他便说人家住在“内地”。雅斯人若有朋友住在扬格（Young），他也还是说人家住在“内地”，如此类推。卡达加特便在“内地”。

===Original===
Bound thither on the second Wednesday in August 1896, I bought a ticket at the Goulburn railway station, and at some time about 1 a.m. took my seat in a second class carriage of the mail-train on its way to Melbourne. I had three or four hours to travel in this train when I would have to change to a branch line for two hours longer. I was the only one from Goulburn in that carriage; all the other passengers had been in some time and were asleep. One or two opened their eyes strugglingly, stared glumly at the intruder, and then went to sleep again. The motion of the train was a joy to me, and sleep never entered my head. I stood up, and pressing my forehead to the cold windowpane, vainly attempted, through the inky blackness of the foggy night, to discern the objects which flew by.
===Chinese===
1896 年 8 月的第二个星期三，我踏上了去那儿的路，在古尔本火车站买了票，凌晨一点来钟，在开往墨尔本的邮政列车一节二等车厢里落了座。我得在这列车上坐三四个钟头，然后换乘一条支线，再走两个钟头。这节车厢里，从古尔本上车的只有我一个；其余乘客都已上来好一阵，正睡着。有一两个勉力睁开眼，闷闷地瞪一眼这不速之客，随即又睡了过去。车轮的颠动于我是桩乐事，睡意压根儿没进过我的脑子。我站起身，把额头抵在冰凉的车窗玻璃上，想透过雾夜墨黑的一片，辨认那一掠而过的景物，只是徒然。

===Original===
I was too full of pleasant anticipation of what was ahead of me to think of those I had left behind. I did not regret leaving Possum Gully. Quite the reverse; I felt inclined to wave my arms and yell for joy at being freed from it. Home! God forbid that my experiences at Possum Gully should form the only food for my reminiscences of home. I had practically grown up there, but my heart refused absolutely to regard it as home. I hated it then, I hate it now, with its narrowing, stagnant monotony. It has and had not provided me with one solitary fond remembrance﻿—only with dreary, wing-clipping, mind-starving recollections. No, no; I was not leaving home behind, I was flying homeward now. Home, home to Caddagat, home to ferny gullies, to the sweet sad rush of many mountain waters, to the majesty of rugged Borgongs; home to dear old grannie, and uncle and aunt, to books, to music; refinement, company, pleasure, and the dear old homestead I love so well.
===Chinese===
我心里满满盛着对前路的甜美期待，哪里还顾得上身后撇下的人。离开袋熊谷，我毫无遗憾。非但如此，我直想挥舞双臂、放声欢呼，庆贺自己从中挣脱出来。家！但愿上帝保佑，别叫我在袋熊谷的经历构成我关于“家”的回忆的唯一食粮。我差不多就是在那儿长大的，可我的心断然不肯把它认作自己的家。当年我恨它，如今我照旧恨它——恨它那叫人日益狭隘、死水一潭的单调。它不曾、也从来不曾给过我一星半点温存的忆念——给的只有凄凉的、剪翅膀的、饿煞心智的回忆。不，不；我抛在身后的不是家，我此刻是正飞奔回家。回家，回卡达加特的家，回那蕨草萋萋的沟谷，回那群山众水甜中带愁的奔泻，回那嶙峋博尔贡（Borgongs）的雄浑气象；回到亲爱的老外婆、舅舅和姨妈身边，回到书籍，回到音乐；回到风雅、伴侣、欢乐，回到我深爱的那座老宅。

===Original===
All in good time I arrived at the end of my train journey, and was taken in charge by a big red-bearded man, who informed me he was the driver of the mail-coach, and had received a letter from Mrs. Bossier instructing him to take care of me. He informed me also that he was glad to do what he termed “that same,” and I would be as safe under his care as I would be in God’s pocket.
===Chinese===
一路顺遂，我到了火车旅程的尽头，一位红胡子大个儿汉子接管了照应我的差事。他自称是邮政马车的车夫，收到博西耶太太的信，托他路上照应我。他还说，他很高兴做他所谓的“那码事”，说在他照应之下，我保管安然无恙，同揣在上帝口袋里一样稳当。

===Original===
My twenty-six miles’ coach drive was neither pleasant nor eventful. I was the only passenger, and so had my choice of seats. The weather being cold and wet, I preferred being inside the box and curled myself up on the seat, to be interrupted every two or three miles by the good-natured driver inquiring if I was “all serene.”
===Chinese===
那二十六英里的马车路，既不舒服，也无波澜。乘客只有我一人，座位尽由我挑。天气又冷又潮，我宁可待在密闭的车厢里，蜷作一团坐在座位上，只是每隔两三英里，好性子的车夫就要来打一回岔，问我“一路安生么”。

===Original===
At the Halfway House, where a change of the team of five horses was affected, I had a meal and a warm, and so tuned myself up for the remainder of the way. It got colder as we went on, and at 2:30 p.m. I was not at all sorry to see the iron roofs of Gool-Gool township disclosing to my view. We first went to the post office, where the mailbags were delivered, and then returned and pulled rein in front of the Woolpack Hotel. A tall young gentleman in a mackintosh and cap, who had been standing on the veranda, stepped out on the street as the coach stopped, and lifting his cap and thrusting his head into the coach, inquired, “Which is Miss Melvyn?”
===Chinese===
在半途客栈（Halfway House）换过一套五匹马的班，我吃了顿饭，暖了暖身子，把精神重新抖擞起来，好走完剩下的路。越往前越冷，到下午2:30，古尔古尔（Gool-Gool）镇的一片铁皮屋顶展现在我眼前，我一点也不觉得遗憾。我们先去了邮局，把邮袋交付了，然后折回来，在羊毛包旅馆（Woolpack Hotel）门前勒住缰绳。一位穿胶布雨衣、戴便帽的高个子年轻绅士本站在游廊上，马车一停便踱到街心，举了举帽子，把头探进车厢，问道：“哪位是梅尔文小姐？”

===Original===
Seeing I was the only occupant, he laughed the pleasantest of laughs, disclosing two wide rows of perfect teeth, and turning to the driver, said, “Is that your only passenger? I suppose it is Miss Melvyn?”
===Chinese===
见我是车里唯一的乘客，他发出了顶愉快的一声笑，露出两排齐整的好牙，转头对车夫说：“这就是您唯一的乘客？我猜这位就是梅尔文小姐吧？”

===Original===
“As I wasn’t present at her birth, I can’t swear, but I believe her to be that same, as sure as eggs is eggs,” he replied.
===Chinese===
“她落草那会儿我又没在场，赌不得咒，不过我信她就是那一位，鸡蛋是鸡蛋——千真万确。”他答道。

===Original===
My identity being thus established, the young gentleman with the greatest of courtesy assisted me to alight, ordered the hotel groom to stow my luggage in the Caddagat buggy, and harness the horses with all expedition. He then conducted me to the private parlour, where a friendly little barmaid had some refreshments on a tray awaiting me, and while warming my feet preparatory to eating I read the letter he had given me, which was addressed in my grandmother’s handwriting. In it she told me that she and my aunt were only just recovering from bad colds, and on account of the inclemency of the weather thought it unwise to come to town to meet me; but Frank Hawden, the jackeroo, would take every care of me, settle the hotel bill, and tip the coach-driver. Caddagat was twenty-four miles distant from Gool-Gool, and the latter part of the road was very hilly. It was already past three o’clock, and, being rainy, the short winter afternoon would close in earlier; so I swallowed my tea and cake with all expedition, so as not to delay Mr. Hawden, who was waiting to assist me into the buggy, where the groom was in charge of the horses in the yard. He struck up a conversation with me immediately.
===Chinese===
我的身份既已这样坐实，这位年轻绅士便以极其周到的礼数扶我下车，吩咐旅馆马夫把我的行李装上卡达加特的小马车，火速套马。随后他把我引进专用客厅，一位和气的小酒吧女侍正端着托盘，备了些茶点候着我。趁进食前烘脚的当口，我读了那封他交给我、出自外婆手笔的信。信上说，她和我姨妈重感冒才刚好，天气又这般酷劣，觉得不宜进城来接我；不过牧场学徒弗兰克·霍登（Frank Hawden）会悉心照应我，付清旅馆账目，再赏车夫几个酒钱。卡达加特离古尔古尔二十四英里，后一段路丘陵起伏得厉害。这时已过三点钟，又落着雨，冬日短短的午后天黑得更早；我便火急把茶和饼吞下肚，免得耽搁霍登先生——他正等着扶我上马车，马夫已在院子里看住马匹。他立刻同我攀谈起来。

===Original===
“Seeing your name on yer bags, an’ knowin’ you was belonging to the Bossiers, I ask if yer might be a daughter of Dick Melvyn, of Bruggabrong, out by Timlinbilly.”
===Chinese===
“瞧见你行李上的名字，又知道你是博西耶家的人，我就寻思，你兴许是布鲁加布朗的迪克·梅尔文的闺女吧——就在蒂姆林比利外头那块儿。”

===Original===
“Yes, I am.”
===Chinese===
“是的，正是。”

===Original===
“Well, miss, please remember me most kindly to yer pa; he was a good boss was Dick Melvyn. I hope he’s doin’ well. I’m Billy Haizelip, brother to Mary and Jane. You remember Jane, I s’pose, miss?”
===Chinese===
“哦，小姐，请替我向你爹多多问好；迪克·梅尔文是位好东家。但愿他如今一切都好。我叫比利·海兹利普（Billy Haizelip），是玛丽和简的兄弟。简你总还记得吧，小姐？”

===Original===
I hadn’t time to say more than promise to send his remembrances to my father, for Mr. Hawden, saying we would be in the dark, had whipped his horses and was bowling off at a great pace, in less than two minutes covering a rise which put Gool-Gool out of sight. It was raining a little, so I held over us the big umbrella, which grannie had sent, while we discussed the weather, to the effect that rain was badly needed and was a great novelty nowadays, and it was to be hoped it would continue. There had been but little, but the soil here away was of that rich loamy description which little water turns to mud. It clogged the wheels and loaded the break-blocks; and the near side horse had a nasty way of throwing his front feet, so that he deposited soft red lumps of mud in our laps at every step. But, despite these trifling drawbacks, it was delightful to be drawn without effort by a pair of fat horses in splendid harness. It was a great contrast to our poor skinny old horse at home, crawling along in much-broken harness, clumsily and much mended with string and bits of hide.
===Chinese===
我只来得及答应替他向我父亲致意，因为霍登先生嘴上说天快黑了，已经一鞭催马，车子飞也似的滚了出去，不到两分钟便翻过一道坡，把古尔古尔甩得没了影。天落着些小雨，我便撑开外婆捎来的那把大伞罩在我们头顶，一面谈论天气——大意是这场雨下得正解渴，如今可是稀罕物事，但愿能这么下下去。雨其实下得不多，可此地的土是那种肥沃的壤土，一点点水就化作泥。泥塞住车轮，糊满刹车瓦；靠里那匹马又有个讨人嫌的毛病，前蹄乱甩，一步一步把软塌塌的红泥块甩进我们怀里。不过，撇开这些小小的不痛快不提，由着两匹膘肥体壮的马儿套着漂亮驾具毫不费力地拉着自己走，实在叫人心旷神怡。这同家里那匹可怜的瘦骨嶙峋的老马，真有天壤之别——它拖着七零八落、到处用麻绳和碎皮子笨手笨脚补了又补的挽具，一路爬行。

===Original===
Mr. Hawden was not at all averse to talking. After emptying our tongues of the weather, there was silence for some time, which he broke with, “So you are Mrs. Bossier’s granddaughter, are you?”
===Chinese===
霍登先生一点也不嫌开口。我们把天气谈了个够之后，沉默了一阵，还是他先开的口：“这么说，你就是博西耶太太的外孙女，是吧？”

===Original===
“Not remembering my birth, I can’t swear; but I believe myself to be that same, as sure as eggs is eggs,” I replied.
===Chinese===
“我出生那会儿自己不记得，赌不得咒；不过我信我就是那一位，鸡蛋是鸡蛋——千真万确。”我答道。

===Original===
He laughed. “Very good imitation of the coach-driver. But Mrs. Bossier’s granddaughter! Well, I should smile!”
===Chinese===
他笑了。“学车夫学得真像。可是，博西耶太太的外孙女！好家伙，笑死人了！”

===Original===
“What at?”
===Chinese===
“笑什么？”

===Original===
“Your being Mrs. Bossier’s granddaughter.”
===Chinese===
“笑你竟是博西耶太太的外孙女呀。”

===Original===
“I fear, Mr. Hawden, there is a suspicion reverse of complimentary in your remark.”
===Chinese===
“恕我直言，霍登先生，你这番话里恐怕带着几分不中听、倒过来贬损人的味道。”

===Original===
“Well, I should smile! Would you like to have my opinion of you?”
===Chinese===
“好家伙，笑死人了！想不想听听我对你的高见？”

===Original===
“Nothing would please me more. I would value your opinion above all things, and I’m sure﻿—I feel certain﻿—that you have formed a true estimate of me.”
===Chinese===
“那真是再乐意不过了。你的高见在我眼里比什么都金贵，而且我敢说——我深信不疑——你对我的估量必定分毫不差。”

===Original===
At any other time his conceit would have brought upon himself a fine snubbing, but today I was in high feather, and accordingly very pleasant, and resolved to amuse myself by drawing him out.
===Chinese===
换了别的任何时候，他这份自负准要给自己招来一顿好排揎，可是这一天我兴致极高，人也跟着格外和气，便打定主意逗他多说说话，自己取乐。

===Original===
“Well, you are not a bit like Mrs. Bossier or Mrs. Bell; they are both so good-looking,” he continued.
===Chinese===
“唔，你可一点儿也不像博西耶太太，也不像贝尔太太；她们俩都那么好看。”他接着说。

===Original===
“Indeed!”
===Chinese===
“是嘛！”

===Original===
“I was disappointed when I saw you had no pretensions to prettiness, as there’s not a girl up these parts worth wasting a man’s affections on, and I was building great hopes on you. But I’m a great admirer of beauty,” he twaddled.
===Chinese===
“一见你跟漂亮二字沾不上边，我可失望了——这一带的姑娘，没有一个值得男人为她浪费感情，我原先对你抱着老大指望来着。不过我这个人，天字第一号爱慕美貌。”他瞎扯道。

===Original===
“I am very sorry for you, Mr. Hawden. I’m sure it would take quite a paragon to be worthy of such affection as I’m sure yours would be,” I replied sympathetically.
===Chinese===
“我真替你难过，霍登先生。我敢说，得有十足十的绝世佳人，才配得上你的这份情意——我保准你的情意必定不同凡响。”我深表同情地答道。

===Original===
“Never mind. Don’t worry about it. You’re not a bad sort, and think a fellow could have great fun with you.”
===Chinese===
“不打紧。别为这个操心。你这人不坏，我看小伙子跟你在一块儿能有不少乐子。”

===Original===
“I’m sure, Mr. Hawden, you do me too much honour. It quite exhilarates me to think that I meet with your approval in the smallest degree,” I replied with the utmost deference. “You are so gentlemanly and nice that I was alarmed at first lest you might despise me altogether.”
===Chinese===
“那真是不敢当，霍登先生，您实在太抬举我了。一想到我哪怕在最微末的地方也蒙您首肯，我就振奋得很。”我以无比恭顺的态度答道。“您这样一位绅士、这样和蔼可亲，起初我还真担着心，唯恐您会把我整个人瞧到脚底板底下去。”

===Original===
“No fear. You needn’t be afraid of me; I’m not a bad sort of fellow,” he replied with the greatest encouragement.
===Chinese===
“不怕，不怕。你用不着怕我；我这人可不坏。”他以最大的鼓励答道。

===Original===
By his accent and innocent style I detected he was not a colonial, so I got him to relate his history. He was an Englishman by birth, but had been to America, Spain, New Zealand, Tasmania, etc.; by his own make out had ever been a man of note, and had played Old Harry everywhere.
===Chinese===
凭他的口音和那副天真做派，我听出他不是本地生长的殖民地人，便引他讲起自己的来历。他生在英国，却去过美洲、西班牙、新西兰、塔斯马尼亚等地；照他自己的说法，他从来都是个人物，走到哪儿就在哪儿搅得天翻地覆。

===Original===
I allowed him to gabble away full tilt for an hour on this subject, unconscious that I had taken the measure of him, and was grinning broadly to myself. Then I diverted him by inquiring how long since the wire fence on our right had been put up. It bore evidence of recent erection, and had replaced an old cockatoo fence which I remembered in my childhood.
===Chinese===
我由着他就这个题目全速喋喋不休了一个钟头，浑然不觉我早已掂清了他的斤两，正自咧开嘴暗笑。后来我把话岔开，问右手边这道铁丝围栏立起来多久了。看得出是新近立的，换掉了我童年记忆里那道旧日小农搭的粗糙篱笆。

===Original===
“Fine fence, is it not? Eight wires, a top rail, and very stout posts. Harry Beecham had that put up by contract this year. Twelve miles of it. It cost him a lot: couldn’t get any very low tenders, the ground being so hard on account of the drought. Those trees are Five-Bob Downs﻿—see, away over against the range. But I suppose you know the places better than I do.”
===Chinese===
“好围栏，是不是？八条铁丝，一道压顶横档，桩子粗得很。是哈里·比彻姆今年包工立起来的。足足十二英里。花了他一大笔钱：赶上天旱，地硬得厉害，怎么也拿不到多低的标价。那边那些树就是五博丘——瞧，就在山岭那头对面。不过这些地方，你恐怕比我还熟些。”

===Original===
We were now within an hour of our destination. How familiar were many landmarks to me, although I had not seen them since I was eight years old.
===Chinese===
这时离目的地只有一个来钟头的路程了。多少旧日景物于我是何等熟悉，尽管打八岁起我就再没见过它们。

===Original===
A river ran on our right, occasionally a glimmer of its noisy waters visible through the shrubbery which profusely lined its banks. The short evening was drawing to a close. The white mists brought by the rain were crawling slowly down the hills, and settling in the hollows of the ranges on our left. A V-shaped rift in them, known as Pheasant Gap, came into view. Mr. Hawden said it was well named, as it swarmed with lyrebirds. Night was falling. The skreel of a hundred curlews arose from the gullies﻿—how I love their lonely wail!﻿—and it was quite dark when we pulled up before the front gate of Caddagat.
===Chinese===
一条河在我们右手边流淌，两岸密密丛丛生满灌木，偶尔能从枝叶缝里瞥见它喧腾的水光。短短的黄昏正接近尾声。雨水带来的白雾顺着山冈缓缓往下爬，沉进左手边山岭的洼坳里。岭上一道V字形的裂口，唤作雉鸡山口（Pheasant Gap），进入了眼帘。霍登先生说这名字取得好，那里面琴鸟成群。夜幕垂落。一道道沟谷里扬起上百只杓鹬的哀鸣——我多么爱它们那孤寂的悲号啊！——等我们在卡达加特前门前勒住马，天已经全黑了。

===Original===
A score of dogs rushed yelping to meet us, the front door was thrown open, lights and voices came streaming out.
===Chinese===
二十来条狗吠叫着冲上来迎接我们，前门豁然敞开，灯光和人声一齐涌流出来。

===Original===
I alighted from the buggy feeling rather nervous. I was a pauper with a bad character. How would my grandmother receive me? Dear old soul, I had nothing to fear. She folded me in a great warmhearted hug, saying, “Dear me, child, your face is cold. I’m glad you’ve come. It has been a terrible day, but we’re glad to have the rain. You must be frozen. Get in to the fire, child, as fast as you can. Get in to the fire, get in to the fire. I hope you forgive me for not going to meet you.” And there was my mother’s only sister, my tall graceful aunt, standing beside her, giving me a kiss and cordial handclasp, and saying, “Welcome, Sybylla. We will be glad to have a young person to brighten up the old home once more. I am sorry I was too unwell to meet you. You must be frozen; come to the fire.”
===Chinese===
我下了马车，心里相当忐忑。我是个声名不佳的小穷鬼。外婆会怎样接待我呢？亲爱的老人家，我根本无须害怕。她把我搂进一个盛大而热乎乎的怀抱，说道：“哎呀，孩子，你的脸好凉。你来了我真高兴。今天这天气糟透了，不过下雨我们高兴。你准是冻透了。快进屋烤火去，孩子，越快越好。进屋烤火去，烤火去。我没去接你，望你别见怪。”母亲唯一的姐妹、我那位高挑娴雅的姨妈就立在她身旁，吻了吻我，又热诚地同我握手，说道：“欢迎你，西比拉。老宅子里再有个年轻人来添些生气，我们很高兴。抱歉我不大舒服，没能去接你。你准是冻透了；来烤烤火。”

===Original===
My aunt always spoke very little and very quietly, but there was something in her high-bred style which went right home.
===Chinese===
我姨妈说话向来极少、极轻，可她那高贵教养的风度里自有一种东西，直透到人心坎上。

===Original===
I could scarcely believe that they were addressing me. Surely they were making a mistake. This reception was meant for some grand relative honouring them with a visit, and not for the ugly, useless, bad little pauper come to live upon their bounty.
===Chinese===
我几乎不能相信她们招呼的正是我。她们准是弄错了。这场欢迎礼，该是给哪位赏光来访的显贵亲戚预备的，断不是给我这么个又丑、又没用、心眼儿又坏、来靠他们周济度日的小穷丫头。

===Original===
Their welcome did more than all the sermons I had ever heard put together towards thawing a little of the pitiless cynicism which encrusted my heart.
===Chinese===
她们这一迎，比我平生听过的所有布道拢共加起来，还更能融化一点我心上结着的那层冷硬的愤世之壳。

===Original===
“Take the child inside, Helen, as fast as you can,” said grannie, “while I see that the boy attends to the horses. The plaguey fellow can’t be trusted any further than the length of his nose. I told him to tie up these dogs, and here they are yelp-yelping fit to deafen a person.”
===Chinese===
“快把孩子带进屋里去，海伦，越快越好，”外婆说，“我去盯着那小子照应马。这个讨人嫌的东西，除了鼻子尖那么点儿远，一寸也信不得。我叫他把这几条狗拴起来，瞧它们这会儿还叫得能把人的耳朵吵聋。”

===Original===
I left my wet umbrella on the veranda, and aunt Helen led me into the dining-room, where a spruce maid was making a pleasant clatter in laying the table. Caddagat was a very old style of house, and all the front rooms opened onto the veranda without any such preliminary as a hall, therefore it was necessary to pass through the dining-room to my bedroom, which was a skillion at the back. While auntie paused for a moment to give some orders to the maid, I noticed the heavy silver serviette rings I remembered so well, and the old-fashioned dinner-plates, and the big fire roaring in the broad white fireplace; but more than all, the beautiful pictures on the walls and a table in a corner strewn with papers, magazines, and several very new-looking books. On the back of one of these I saw “Corelli,” and on another﻿—great joy!﻿—was *Trilby*. From the adjoining apartment, which was the drawing-room, came the sweet full tones of a beautiful piano. Here were three things for which I had been starving. An impulse to revel in them immediately seized me. I felt like clearing the table at a bound, seizing and beginning to read both books, and rushing in to the piano and beginning to play upon it there and then, and examine the pictures﻿—all three things at once. Fortunately for the reputation of my sanity, however, aunt Helen had by this time conducted me to a pretty little bedroom, and saying it was to be mine, helped me to doff my cape and hat.
===Chinese===
我把湿伞留在游廊上，海伦姨妈领我进了餐厅，一个齐整利落的女仆正在摆桌子，响出一片悦耳的杯盘声。卡达加特是一座老式得很的宅子，前头几间屋子都直通游廊，中间连门厅这样的过渡也没有，因此要去我的卧室——后头一间披屋——就非得穿过餐厅不可。姨妈停下来吩咐女仆几句的当口，我注意到了那几枚我记得清清楚楚的沉甸甸的银餐巾环，那些老式餐盘，宽大的白色壁炉里熊熊烧旺的大火；而比这一切更要紧的，是墙上一幅幅美丽的画，还有角落里一张堆着报纸、杂志和几本簇新书籍的桌子。其中一本的书脊上，我看见“科雷利”（Corelli），另一本——天大的喜讯！——是《特里尔比》（*Trilby*）。从紧邻的客厅里，传来一架好钢琴饱满甜润的音色。这里有三样东西，都是我久已饥渴的。一股要立刻恣意受用它们的冲动攫住了我。我真想一步蹦过去掀翻桌子，抓起那两本书就读起来，再冲进客厅当场弹起琴来，还要细看那些画——三样一起来。不过，为了我神志清明的名声着想，总算万幸：海伦姨妈这时已把我领进一间漂亮的小卧室，说这间往后归我，又帮我卸下斗篷和帽子。

===Original===
While warming my fingers at the fire my eyes were arrested by a beautiful portrait hanging above the mantelpiece. It represented a lovely girl in the prime of youth and beauty, and attired in floating white dinner draperies.
===Chinese===
我在火边烘手指的当儿，目光被壁炉架上方一幅美丽的画像勾住了。画上是个正当青春妙龄、美貌极盛的可爱姑娘，一身飘垂的白色晚宴衣裙。

===Original===
“Oh, aunt Helen! isn’t she lovely? It’s you, isn’t it?”
===Chinese===
“哦，海伦姨妈！她多美啊！画的是您，对不对？”

===Original===
“No. Do you not recognize it as your mother? It was taken just before her marriage. I must leave you now, but come out as soon as you arrange yourself﻿—your grandmother will be anxious to see you.”
===Chinese===
“不是。你认不出这是你母亲么？画成于她出嫁之前。我现在得走开了，你一收拾停当就出来——你外婆急着见你呢。”

===Original===
When aunt Helen left me I plastered my hair down in an instant without even a glance in the mirror. I took not a particle of interest in my attire, and would go about dressed anyhow. This was one symptom which inclined my mother to the belief of my possible insanity, as to most young girls dress is a great delight. I had tried once or twice to make myself look nice by dressing prettily, but, by my own judgment, considering I looked as ugly as ever, I had given it up as a bad job.
===Chinese===
海伦姨妈一走，我转眼间就把头发抿平了，连镜子都没照一眼。我对自己的衣着打扮没有一丁点儿兴趣，穿成什么样都肯到处走。这是使我母亲倾向于相信我可能精神失常的症候之一，因为对大多数年轻姑娘，穿装打扮是天大的乐事。我也试过一两回，想靠穿得漂漂亮亮把自己拾掇好看，可依我自己的裁定，我照旧丑得可以，便死了这条心，随它去了。

===Original===
The time which I should have spent in arranging my toilet passed in gazing at my mother’s portrait. It was one of the loveliest faces imaginable. The features may not have been perfect according to rule of thumb, but the expression was simply angelic﻿—sweet, winning, gentle, and happy. I turned from the contemplation of it to another photograph﻿—one of my father﻿—in a silver frame on the dressing-table. This, too, was a fine countenance, possessed of well-cut features and refined expression. This was the prince who had won Lucy Bossier from her home. I looked around my pretty bedroom﻿—it had been my mother’s in the days of her maidenhood. In an exclusive city boarding-school, and amid the pleasant surroundings of this home, her youth had been spent.
===Chinese===
本该花在梳洗打点上的工夫，都耗在凝望母亲的画像上了。那是可以想见的最可爱的脸庞之一。五官若照刻板的老规矩量，也许算不得十全，可那神情简直如天使一般——甜美、动人、温婉、欢喜。我从这凝望里转过身，去看另一张照片——父亲的一——装在梳妆台上的银相框里。这也是一张出色的脸，眉目轮廓分明，气韵清雅。这正是当年把露西·博西耶（Lucy Bossier）从娘家迎娶走的那位王子。我环顾我这间漂亮的卧室——它当年是母亲闺中岁月的居室。她的青春，就消磨在一所门禁森严的城市寄宿学校里，和这座宅子愉快的环境之间。

===Original===
I thought of a man and his wife at Possum Gully. The man was blear-eyed, disreputable in appearance, and failed to fulfil his duties as a father and a citizen. The woman was work-roughened and temper-soured by endless care and an unavailing struggle against poverty. Could that pair possibly be identical with this?
===Chinese===
我想起袋熊谷的一对夫妻。男的眼睛熬得昏花，形容委琐潦倒，做父亲、做公民的本分一概尽不到。女的被无尽的操劳磨粗了手脚，又被那场对抗贫穷的徒劳挣扎熬酸了脾气。那一对，难道同眼前这一双竟会是同两个人么？

===Original===
This was life as proved by my parents! What right had I to expect any better yield from it? I shut my eyes and shuddered at the possibilities and probabilities of my future. It was for this that my mother had yielded up her youth, freedom, strength; for this she had sacrificed the greatest possession of woman.
===Chinese===
我的父母已然这样验证了生活！我还有什么权利指望从生活里得到更好的收成？我阖上眼，为前途的种种可能与必然不寒而栗。母亲就是为了这个，交出了她的青春、自由和气力；为了这个，她牺牲了女人最宝贵的所有。

===Original===
Here I made my way to the dining-room, where grannie was waiting for me and gave me another hug.
===Chinese===
随后我移步来到餐厅，外婆正等着我，又搂了我一回。

===Original===
“Come here, child, and sit beside me near the fire; but first let me have a look at you,” and she held me at arm’s length.
===Chinese===
“到这儿来，孩子，坐在我身边火旁边；不过先让我端量端量你。”说着，她伸直臂膀把我端在眼前。

===Original===
“Dear, oh, dear, what a little thing you are, and not a bit like any of your relations! I am glad your skin is so nice and clear; all my children had beautiful complexions. Goodness me, I never saw such hair! A plait thicker than my arm and almost to your knees! It is that beautiful bright brown like your aunt’s. Your mother’s was flaxen. I must see your hair loose when you are going to bed. There is nothing I admire so much as a beautiful head of hair.”
===Chinese===
“哎呀呀，你这个小不点儿，跟你的亲戚们没有一处相像！亏得你的皮肤这样细腻光洁；我的孩子们个个都有好肤色。天哪，我从没见过这样的头发！一根辫子比我胳膊还粗，长得几乎到膝盖！正是你姨妈那样好看的亮棕色。你母亲的头发是亚麻色的。等你上床睡觉时，我得看看它披散下来的样子。再没有比一头好头发更叫我叹赏的了。”

===Original===
The maid announced that dinner was ready, grannie vigorously rang a little bell, aunt Helen, a lady, and a gentleman appeared from the drawing-room, and Mr. Hawden came in from the back. I discovered that the lady and gentleman were a neighbouring squatter and a new governess he was taking home. Grannie, seeing them pass that afternoon in the rain, had gone out and prevailed upon them to spend the night at Caddagat.
===Chinese===
女仆禀报晚饭已备好，外婆起劲地摇响一只小铃，海伦姨妈、一位女士、一位绅士从客厅里出来，霍登先生也从后头进来。我这才弄明白，那位女士和绅士，是邻家的一位大牧场主和他正带回家去的新聘女家庭教师。外婆那天下午瞧见他们冒雨路过，便赶出去劝他们在卡达加特过夜。

===Original===
Mr. Hawden took no notice of me now, but showed off to the others for my benefit. After dinner we had music and singing in the drawing-room. I was enjoying it immensely, but grannie thought I had better go to bed, as I had been travelling since about midnight last night. I was neither tired nor sleepy, but knew it useless to protest, so bade everyone good night and marched off. Mr. Hawden acknowledged my salute with great airs and stiffness, and aunt Helen whispered that she would come and see me by and by, if I was awake.
===Chinese===
霍登先生此刻不理睬我了，却在旁人跟前卖弄起来，好做给我看。晚饭后，大家在客厅里奏乐唱歌。我正听得津津有味，外婆却认为我顶好上床去睡，因为我从头天夜里约莫半夜时分起就一直在路上。我既不累也不困，可知道抗议也是无用，便向众人道了晚安，迈步走了。霍登先生回我这一礼时，架子摆得十足，僵硬无比；海伦姨妈则悄悄说，要是我还醒着，她过一会儿就来看我。

===Original===
Grannie escorted me to my room, and examined my hair. I shook it out for her inspection. It met with her approval in every way. She pronounced it beautifully fine, silky, and wavy, and the most wonderful head of hair she had seen out of a picture.
===Chinese===
外婆把我送到我的房间，验看我的头发。我把它抖散开来给她检阅。它方方面面都赢得了她的嘉许。她品评道，这头发细得好生漂亮，滑如丝，波纹宛转，是她在图画之外见过的最了不得的一头头发。

===Original===
A noise arose somewhere out in the back premises. Grannie went out to ascertain the cause of it and did not return to me, so I extinguished my lamp and sat thinking in the glow of the firelight.
===Chinese===
后院什么地方起了一阵喧哗。外婆出去查问缘故，没有再回到我这儿来，我便熄了灯，坐在炉火的红光里出神。

===Original===
For the first time my thoughts reverted to my leave-taking from home. My father had kissed me with no more warmth than if I had been leaving for a day only; my mother had kissed me very coldly, saying shortly, “It is to be hoped, Sybylla, that your behaviour to your grandmother will be an improvement upon what it has ever been to me.” Gertie was the only one who had felt any sorrow at parting with me, and I knew that she was of such a disposition that I would be forgotten in a day or two. They would never miss me, for I had no place in their affections. True, I was an undutiful child, and deserved none. I possessed no qualities that would win either their pride or love, but my heart cried out in love for them.
===Chinese===
我这才头一回回想起离家时的道别。父亲吻我，那份热络，同我不过出门一天似的；母亲吻得非常冷淡，短短说了句：“但愿，西比拉，你在外婆跟前的行止，能比向来待我的强些。”只有格蒂一人，同我分别时真觉着难过，可我也知道她那性子，过一两天就会把我忘个干净。他们永远不会想念我，因为我在他们的感情里没有位置。不错，我是个不孝的孩子，也不配有。我身上没有一点能赢得他们骄傲或疼爱的品性，可我的心却为爱他们而哭喊。

===Original===
Would Gertie miss me tonight, as I would have missed her had our positions been reversed? Not she. Would my absence from the noisy tea-table cause a blank? I feared not.
===Chinese===
今夜格蒂会想念我么——倘若我们的处境对调，我会想念她的？她才不会。热闹的茶桌旁少了我，会留出一个空缺么？我怕不会。

===Original===
I thought of poor mother left toiling at home, and my heart grew heavy; I failed to remember my father’s faults, but thought of his great patience with me in the years agone, and all my old-time love for him renewed itself. Why, oh, why, would they not love me a little in return! Certainly I had never striven to be lovable. But see the love some have lavished upon them without striving for it! Why was I ugly and nasty and miserable and useless﻿—without a place in the world?
===Chinese===
我想到可怜的母亲留在家中操劳，心便沉了下去；父亲种种不是，我竟一时想不起来，只想起往昔岁月里他待我的那份大耐心，我对他的旧日之爱便又整个儿苏醒过来。为什么，哦，为什么，他们不肯也爱我一点点作为回报呢！的确，我从不曾努力讨人喜欢。可瞧瞧有些人，不曾争取，爱却泼洒在他们身上！为什么我丑陋、可憎、悲苦、无用——在这世上没有一个位置？

## IX Aunt Helen’s Recipe / 第9章 海伦姨妈的妙方

===Original===
“Dear me, Sybylla, not in bed yet, and tears, great big tears! Tell me what is the cause of them.”

===Chinese===
“哎呀，西比拉，还没上床，还挂着泪珠，好大的泪珠！告诉我，这是为了什么。”

===Original===
It was aunt Helen’s voice; she had entered and lit the lamp.

===Chinese===
是海伦姨妈（aunt Helen）的声音；她已经进得屋来，点亮了灯。

===Original===
There was something beautifully sincere and real about aunt Helen. She never fussed over anyone or pretended to sympathize just to make out how nice she was. She was real, and you felt that no matter what wild or awful rubbish you talked to her it would never be retailed for anyone’s amusement﻿—and, better than all, she never lectured.

===Chinese===
海伦姨妈身上，有一种说不出的美好的真诚与实在。她从不对谁大惊小怪，也从不为了显得自己和善而假意同情。她是实实在在的，你感觉得到，任凭你对她说出怎样狂乱、怎样骇人的浑话，也决不会被她转述出去给人取乐——而比这一切更好的是，她从不训人。

===Original===
She sat down beside me, and I impulsively threw my arms around her neck and sobbed forth my troubles in a string. How there was no good in the world, no use for me there, no one loved me or ever could on account of my hideousness.

===Chinese===
她在我身旁坐下，我一时冲动，张开两臂搂住她的脖子，呜呜咽咽地把满腹苦水一股脑儿倒了出来：这世上毫无美好可言，我在世上毫无用处，因为我生得这么丑，没有人爱我，往后也决不会有人爱。

===Original===
She heard me to the end and then said quietly, “When you are fit to listen I will talk to you.”

===Chinese===
她听我说完，才平静地说：“等你听得进去了，我再同你讲。”

===Original===
I controlled myself instantly and waited expectantly. What would she say? Surely not that tame old yarn anent this world being merely a place of probation, wherein we were allowed time to fit ourselves for a beautiful world to come. That old tune may be all very well for old codgers tottering on the brink of the grave, but to young persons with youth and romance and good health surging through their veins, it is most boresome. Would she preach that it was flying in the face of providence to moan about my appearance? it being one of the greatest blessings I had, as it would save me from countless temptations to which pretty girls are born. That was another piece of old croaking of the Job’s comforter order, of which I was sick unto death, as I am sure there is not an ugly person in the world who thinks her lack of beauty a blessing to her. I need not have feared aunt Helen holding forth in that strain. She always said something brave and comforting which made me ashamed of myself and my selfish conceited egotism.

===Chinese===
我当即克制住自己，满怀期待地等着。她会说什么呢？总不至于还是那套不咸不淡的老生常谈——说这尘世不过是一处考验之所，容我们在世的岁月里把自己修整妥帖，好去配将来那个美满世界吧。那支老曲子，拿去安抚在坟墓边缘打晃的老朽们，或许尽自甚好，可对于血管里奔涌着青春、绮念与康健的年轻人，它就乏味透顶了。她会不会训诫说，埋怨自己的容貌是触犯天意？说容貌原是我顶大的福分之一，正好保我免去漂亮姑娘们生来便要招惹的无数诱惑。这又是约伯的安慰者那一流的老聒噪，我早腻烦到了极点——我敢担保，世上没有一个长相丑的人，会觉得自己不美是一种福。其实我尽不必担心海伦姨妈也发这样的高论。她说出来的话，从来勇敢又暖心，倒叫我为我自己、为我那份自私自负的自以为是而羞愧。

===Original===
“I understand you, Sybylla,” she said slowly and distinctly, “but you must not be a coward. There is any amount of love and good in the world, but you must search for it. Being misunderstood is one of the trials we all must bear. I think that even the most common-minded person in the land has inner thoughts and feelings which no one can share with him, and the higher one’s organization the more one must suffer in that respect. I am acquainted with a great number of young girls, some of them good and true, but you have a character containing more than any three of them put together. With this power, if properly managed, you can gain the almost universal love of your fellows. But you are wild and wayward, you must curb and strain your spirit and bring it into subjection, else you will be worse than a person with the emptiest of characters. You will find that plain looks will not prevent you from gaining the *friendship* love of your fellows﻿—the only real love there is. As for the hot fleeting passion of the man for the maid, which is wrongfully designated love, I will not tell you not to think of it, knowing that it is human nature to demand it when arriving at a certain age; but take this comfort: it as frequently passes by on the other side of those with well-chiselled features as those with faces of plainer mould.”

===Chinese===
“我懂得你，西比拉，”她说得又慢又清晰，“可你不许做懦夫。这世上有的是爱和善，只是你得自己去寻。不被人理解，是我们人人都得经受的磨难之一。我想，哪怕全国心地最平庸的人，内里也藏着无人能够分享的心思与感受；而一个人的天资越高，在这上头受的苦便越多。我认得许许多多年轻姑娘，其中也不乏善良真诚的，可你一个人的性灵，抵得上随便三个她们凑在一起。有这样的力量，若驾驭得当，你几乎能博得身边众人一致的喜爱。可你狂野任性，你必须勒住你的性子、绷紧它、叫它俯首帖耳，不然你还不及一个性灵最空虚的人。你会发现，平凡的相貌拦不住你赢得同伴们的*友谊*之爱——那是世上唯一真正的爱。至于男人对姑娘那股火热而易逝的情热——世人错把它唤作爱——我不会叫你别去想它，我知道人到了一定的年纪，天性自会去索求它；但你且拿这个宽心：它从眉目俊秀的人身边擦身而过，同从相貌平平的人身边擦身而过，一样地频繁。”

===Original===
She turned her face away, sighed, and forgetful of my presence lapsed into silence. I knew she was thinking of herself.

===Chinese===
她把脸扭了过去，叹了口气，忘了我就守在跟前，渐渐沉入沉默。我知道，她想起了她自己。

===Original===
Love, not *friendship* love, for anyone knowing her must give her love and respect, but the other sort of love had passed her by.

===Chinese===
是爱，而不是*友谊*之爱——凡是认得她的人，不能不爱她敬她——可那另一种爱，已同她失之交臂。

===Original===
Twelve years before I went to Caddagat, when Helen Bossier had been eighteen and one of the most beautiful and lovable girls in Australia, there had come to Caddagat on a visit a dashing colonel of the name of Bell, in the enjoyment of a most extended furlough for the benefit of his health. He married aunt Helen and took her to some part of America where his regiment was stationed. I have heard them say she worshipped Colonel Bell, but in less than a twelvemonth he tired of his lovely bride, and becoming enamoured of another woman, he tried to obtain a divorce. On account of his wife’s spotless character he was unable to do this; he therefore deserted her and openly lived with the other woman as his mistress. This forced aunt Helen to return to Caddagat, and her mother had induced her to sue for a judicial separation, which was easily obtained.

===Chinese===
我去卡达加特之前十二年，海伦·博西耶（Helen Bossier）正当十八岁，是澳大利亚数一数二美丽可爱的姑娘。那时有一位风流倜傥的上校，姓贝尔（Colonel Bell），为着养身调理，享着一段格外长久的休假，来到卡达加特作客。他娶了海伦姨妈，带她去了美洲他那个团驻扎的地方。我听人说过，她当年把贝尔上校崇拜得五体投地；可是不出一年，他便腻烦了这位可爱的新娘，又迷上了另一个女人，于是图谋离婚。只因妻子品行毫无瑕疵，他未能如愿；他便抛弃了她，公然与那个女人同居，把她当作情妇。这逼得海伦姨妈回到卡达加特，她母亲便劝她起诉求得司法分居，那是轻而易举就批下来的。

===Original===
When a woman is separated from her husband it is the religion of the world at large to cast the whole blame on the wife. By reason of her youth and purity Mrs. Bell had not as much to suffer in this way as some others. But, comparatively speaking, her life was wrecked. She had been humiliated and outraged in the cruellest way by the man whom she loved and trusted. He had turned her adrift, neither a wife, widow, nor maid, and here she was, one of the most estimably lovable and noble women I have ever met.

===Chinese===
女人一旦同丈夫分居，把罪责整个推在妻子头上，便是普天下世人共奉的教义。亏得年轻又纯洁，贝尔太太在这上头吃的苦头，还不像别的女人那样多。可是相对来说，她这一生是毁了。她被自己所爱所信的男人，用最残忍的方式羞辱作践了一场。他把她抛向茫茫人海，使她非妻、非寡、亦非闺女——而她如今就在这里，仍是我平生所遇最可敬爱、最高贵的女人之一。

===Original===
“Come, Sybylla,” she said, starting up brightly, “I have a plan﻿—will you agree to it? Come and take one good long look at yourself in the glass, then I will turn it to the wall, and you must promise me that for three or four weeks you will not look in a mirror. I will put as many as I can out of your way, and you must avoid the remainder. During this time I will take you in hand, and you must follow my directions implicitly. Will you agree? You will be surprised what a nice-looking little girl I will make of you.”

===Chinese===
“来吧，西比拉，”她蓦地活泼泼地直起身来，说道，“我有个法子——你可肯依？来，对着镜子把自己痛痛快快端详个够，然后我把镜面转向墙，你得答应我，三四个星期里不看一回镜子。我能挪开的镜子，都替你挪开去，剩下的你自己躲着些。这些日子里我来调教你，你必须一板一眼照我的话做。肯不肯？到时候你要吃惊，我会把你调理成多么标致的一个小姑娘。”

===Original===
Of course I agreed. I took a long and critical survey of myself in the glass. There was reflected a pair of hands, red and coarsened with rough work, a round face, shiny and swollen with crying, and a small round figure enshrouded in masses of hair falling in thick waves to within an inch or two of the knees. A very ugly spectacle, I thought. Aunt Helen turned the face of the large mirror flat against the wall, while I remarked despondently, “you can make me only middling ugly, you must be a magician.”

===Chinese===
我自然依了。我对着镜子，把自己久久地、苛刻地端量了一遍。镜中映出一双手，叫粗活磨得又红又糙；一张圆脸，哭得又亮又肿；还有一副圆滚滚的小身材，裹在重重叠叠的浓发里，那头发一波一波厚厚地垂下来，离膝盖只差一两英寸。好丑的一幅景象，我想。海伦姨妈把那面大镜子的镜面平平地扣向墙壁，我只管丧气地嘟囔：“你至多能把我弄成个中等程度的丑，那你准是个魔法师。”

===Original===
“Come now, part of my recipe is that you must not think of yourself at all. I’ll take you in hand in the morning. I hope you will like your room; I have arranged it on purpose to suit you. And now good night, and happy dreams.”

===Chinese===
“好了，我的妙方里还有一条：你压根儿不许想到你自己。明儿一早我就来着手调教你。希望你会喜欢你那间屋子；我是特意照着你的心意布置的。这就晚安了，好梦。”

===Original===
I awoke next morning in very fine spirits, and slithering out of my bed with alacrity, revelled﻿—literally wallowed﻿—in the appointments of my room. My poor old room at Possum Gully was lacking in barest necessaries. We could not afford even a wash-hand basin and jug; Gertie, the boys, and myself had to perform our morning ablutions in a leaky tin dish on a stool outside the kitchen door, which on cold frosty mornings was a pretty peppery performance: but this room contained everything dear to the heart of girlhood. A lovely bed, pretty slippers, dainty white China-matting and many soft skins on the floor, and in one corner a most artistic toilet set, and a washstand liberally supplied with a great variety of soap﻿—some of it so exquisitely perfumed that I felt tempted to taste it. There were pretty pictures on the walls, and on a commodious dressing-table a big mirror and large hand-glasses, with their faces to the wall at present. Hairpins, fancy combs, ribbons galore, and a pretty workbasket greeted my sight, and with delight I swooped down upon the most excruciatingly lovely little writing-desk. It was stuffed full with all kinds of paper of good quality﻿—fancy, all colours, sizes, and shapes, plain, foreign note, pens, ink, and a generous supply of stamps. I felt like writing a dozen letters there and then, and was on the point of giving way to my inclination, when my attention was arrested by what I considered the gem of the whole turnout. I refer to a nice little bookcase containing copies of all our Australian poets, and two or three dozen novels which I had often longed to read. I read the first chapters of four of them, and then lost myself in Gordon, and sat on my dressing-table in my nightgown, regardless of cold, until brought to my senses by the breakfast-bell. I made great pace, scrambled into my clothes helter-skelter, and appeared at table when the others had been seated and unfolded their serviettes.

===Chinese===
第二天清晨我一觉醒来，精神好极了，麻利地溜下床，便在我那间屋子的种种陈设里恣意受用起来——简直是打滚撒欢。我在袋熊谷那间可怜的老屋子，连顶起码的必需品都欠缺：我们连一只洗手盆、一把水壶都置办不起，格蒂、弟弟们和我，清晨的洗漱只好在厨房门外一张凳子上摆的漏底铁盆里将就，赶上寒冷结霜的早晨，这桩差事真够呛：而这间屋子里，少女心尖上疼爱的物事样样齐全。一张可爱的床，漂亮的拖鞋，地上铺着雅致的白色中国席，还有许多柔软的兽皮；屋角摆着一套极有艺术情味的梳妆器具，妆台上慷慨地陈着各色香皂，琳琅满目——有的熏香那般精妙，馋得我直想尝一尝味道。墙上挂着好看的画，宽敞的妆台上立着一面大镜子，另有几面大手镜，眼下都面朝墙放着。发针、花梳、成把的缎带，还有一只漂亮的针线筐，一一迎入眼帘；我欢天喜地，一头扎向那张精美得叫人心尖儿发颤的小书桌。桌里塞满了各式上好的纸——花笺，红的绿的、大大小小、方的斜的；素笺、外国信笺、钢笔、墨水，还有一大叠慷慨奉送的邮票。我当下便想一口气写上一打信，正要顺着这股劲头动笔，注意力却被一样东西攫住了——在我看来，那是全套陈设里的掌上明珠。我说的是一只小巧的书柜，里头立着咱们澳大利亚诗人人手一册的诗集，还有两三打我时常渴想一读的小说。我拣出四本读了开头几章，随后便一头沉进戈登（Gordon）的诗里，穿着睡衣坐在妆台上，也不顾天冷，直到早餐铃响才回过神来。我赶紧加快手脚，慌慌张张套上衣裳赶到饭桌前，这时旁人早已坐定，餐巾也抖开了。

===Original===
Aunt Helen’s treatment for making me presentable was the wearing of gloves and a shady hat every time I went outside; and she insisted upon me spending a proper time over my toilet, and would not allow me to encroach upon it with the contents of my bookshelf.

===Chinese===
海伦姨妈要我拿得出人样子来，她的疗法是：每逢出门必戴手套、必扣遮阳帽；她还坚持要我在梳洗上花足工夫，不许我拿书柜里的藏书来侵占这份工夫。

===Original===
“Rub off some of your gloomy pessimism and cultivate a little more healthy girlish vanity, and you will do very well,” she would say.

===Chinese===
“把你那套阴郁的悲观削掉几分，再养出一点儿健全的姑娘家的虚荣心，你就很好了。”她总这么说。

===Original===
I observed these rites most religiously for three days. Then I contracted a slight attack of influenza, and in poking around the kitchen, doing one of the things I oughtn’t at the time I shouldn’t, a servant-girl tipped a pot of boiling pot-liquor over my right foot, scalding it rather severely. Aunt Helen and grannie put me to bed, where I yelled with pain for hours like a mad Red Indian, despite their applying every alleviative possible. The combined forces of the burn and influenza made me a trifle dicky, so a decree went forth that I was to stay in bed until recovered from both complaints. This effectually prevented me from running in the way of any looking-glasses.

===Chinese===
这些仪轨，我极虔诚地奉行了三天。随后我染上轻微的流感，又在厨房里探头探脑，在不该去的当口做了不该做的事，一个女仆把一锅滚沸的肉汤泼上了我的右脚，烫得着实不轻。海伦姨妈和外婆把我安顿上床，我疼得嚎了几个钟头，活像个撒野的红印第安人，任凭她们千方百计施尽缓痛的法子也无济于事。烫伤与流感两路夹攻，把我弄得有几分虚弱，于是一道谕旨颁了下来：我须卧床静养，直到两桩病症一齐痊愈。这一来，可算彻底断了我撞上任何镜子的路数。

===Original===
I was not sufficiently ill to be miserable, and being a pampered invalid was therefore fine fun. Aunt Helen was a wonderful nurse. She dressed my foot splendidly every morning, and put it in a comfortable position many times throughout the day. Grannie brought me every dainty in the house, and sent special messengers to Gool-Gool for more. Had I been a professional glutton I would have been in paradise. Even Mr. Hawden condescended so far as to express his regret concerning the accident, and favoured me with visits throughout each day; and one Sunday his gallantry carried him to a gully where he plucked a bouquet of maidenhair fern﻿—the first of the season﻿—and put them in a bowl beside my bed. My uncle Julius, the only other member of the family besides the servants, was away “up the country” on some business or another, and was not expected home for a month or so.

===Chinese===
我病得还不到难受的份上，当个娇宠的病人因此其乐无穷。海伦姨妈是天底下顶好的看护。她每天早晨替我包脚包得漂漂亮亮，一天里还好几回把它搁到舒舒服服的位置上。外婆把家里的每一样精美吃食都捧来给我，还专程打发人去古尔古尔（Gool-Gool）添购。我若是个职业老饕，这会儿也算进了天堂。连霍登先生（Mr. Hawden）都不惜纡尊，就这桩意外表示了遗憾，还天天赏光来看望我；一个礼拜天，他胸中那股向女人献殷勤的豪兴更是驱着他跑到一条山沟里，采了一束铁线蕨——今年头一茬——插在碗里供在我床头。家里除了仆役之外仅剩的一位成员，朱利叶斯舅舅（uncle Julius），正出门“上内地”办事去了，预计个把月不得回家。

===Original===
The Bossiers and Beechams were leaders of swelldom among the squattocracy up the country, and firm and intimate friends. The Beechams resided at Five-Bob Downs, twelve miles from Caddagat, and were a family composed of two maiden ladies and their nephew, Harold. One of these ladies was aunt Helen’s particular friend, and the other had stood in the same capacity to my mother in days gone by, but of late years, on account of her poverty, mother had been too proud to keep up communication with her. As for Harold Beecham, he was nearly as much at home at Caddagat as at Five-Bob Downs. He came and went with that pleasant familiarity practised between congenial spirits among squatterdom. The Bossiers and Beechams were congenial spirits in every way﻿—they lived in the one sphere and held the one set of ideas, the only difference between them, and that an unnoticeable one, being that the Bossiers, though in comfortable circumstances, were not at all rich, while Harold Beecham was immensely wealthy. When my installation in the role of invalid took place, one Miss Beecham was away in Melbourne, and the other not well enough to come and see me, but Harold came regularly to inquire how I was progressing. He always brought me a number of beautiful apples. This kindness was because the Caddagat orchard had been too infested with codlin moth for grannie to save any last season.

===Chinese===
博西耶家与比彻姆家（the Beechams），是内地牧场主阶层里头等时髦人家的魁首，又是交谊深厚的老朋友。比彻姆家住在五博丘（Five-Bob Downs），离卡达加特十二英里，家中成员是两位老小姐和她们的侄子哈罗德。两位小姐中，一位是海伦姨妈的至交，另一位当年也曾与我母亲有过同样深的情分；只是近些年我家穷了，母亲心高气傲，不肯再同她通音信。至于哈罗德·比彻姆（Harold Beecham），他在卡达加特差不多同在五博丘一样地如归自己家。他来来去去，带着牧场主圈子中意气相投者之间那种惬意的熟不拘礼。博西耶家与比彻姆家处处气味相投——他们居于同一个天地，守着同一套见识；两家之间唯一的差别——而且微不足道——在于博西耶家虽家境宽裕，却根本算不得富，而哈罗德·比彻姆却是豪富无涯。我被安顿上病人宝座的当口，比彻姆家一位小姐去了墨尔本（Melbourne），另一位身子不爽，来不了，只有哈罗德照旧常来问询我的起色。他每回总捎给我一些顶漂亮的苹果。他这片好心，是因为卡达加特的果园去年苹果蠹蛾（codlin moth）闹得太凶，外婆一只苹果也没能留下。

===Original===
Aunt Helen used to mischievously tease me about this attention.

===Chinese===
为着他这份殷勤，海伦姨妈常常促狭地拿我打趣。

===Original===
“Here comes Harry Beecham with some more apples,” she would say. “No doubt he is far more calculating and artful than I thought he was capable of being. He is taking time by the forelock and wooing you ere he sees you, and so will take the lead. Young ladies are in the minority up this way, and everyone is snapped up as soon as she arrives.”

===Chinese===
“哈里·比彻姆又送苹果来啦，”她会说。“不用问，他可比我想的有算计、有心眼儿多了。他这是抓住时机先声夺人，人还没见着你，就先大献起殷勤，好拔个头筹。这一带年轻小姐稀罕，哪一位刚一露面，转眼就被人抢定了。”

===Original===
“You’d better tell him how ugly I am, auntie, so that he will carry apples twelve miles on his own responsibility, and when he sees me won’t be vexed that all his work has been for nothing. Perhaps, though, it would be better not to describe me, or I will get no more apples,” I would reply.

===Chinese===
“姨妈，你还是告诉他我有多丑的好，免得他自担干系把苹果驮了十二英里来，一见我人，恼恨自己白忙一场。不过话说回来，还是别把我描画给他的好，不然我连苹果也落不着了。”我总这样回她。

---

===Original===
Aunt Helen was a clever needlewoman. She made all grannie’s dresses and her own. Now she was making some for me, which, however, I was not to see until I wore them.
