Aunt Helen had this as a pleasant surprise, and went to the trouble of blindfolding me while I was being fitted. While in bed, grannie and auntie being busy, I was often left hours alone, and during that time devoured the contents of my bookshelf.

===Chinese===
海伦姨妈是位巧手的针线娘。外婆的衣裳和她自己的，都出自她一双手。如今她又在给我做几件，只是要我穿上身才许见。海伦姨妈留着这个当一件愉快的意外惊喜，试身时竟不惮麻烦，把我的眼睛蒙起来。卧床期间，外婆和姨妈各有各的忙，我常常一连几个钟头独自留着，便趁那些时候，把书柜里的存货囫囵吞枣地扫荡了个遍。

===Original===
The pleasure, so exquisite as to be almost pain, which I derived from the books, and especially the Australian poets, is beyond description. In the narrow peasant life of Possum Gully I had been deprived of companionship with people of refinement and education who would talk of the things I loved; but, at last here was congeniality, here was companionship.

===Chinese===
我从这些书、尤其是澳大利亚诗人们的集子里领受的欢愉，精妙得近乎疼痛，绝非笔墨所能形容。在袋熊谷狭窄的农家生活里，我素来无缘同有教养、有学养的人们为伴，没人肯谈谈我心爱的东西；可如今，总算有了投契，有了侣伴。

===Original===
The weird witchery of mighty bush, the breath of wide sunlit plains, the sound of camp-bells and jingle of hobble chains, floating on the soft twilight breezes, had come to these men and had written a tale on their hearts as had been written on mine. The glory of the starlit heavens, the mighty wonder of the sea, and the majesty of thunder had come home to them, and the breathless fullness of the sunset hour had whispered of something more than the humour of tomorrow’s weather. The wind and rain had a voice which spoke to Kendall, and he too had endured the misery of lack of companionship. Gordon, with his sad, sad humanism and bitter disappointment, held out his hand and took me with him. The regret of it all was I could never meet them﻿—Byron, Thackeray, Dickens, Longfellow, Gordon, Kendall, the men I loved, all were dead; but, blissful thought! Caine, Paterson, and Lawson were still living, breathing human beings﻿—two of them actually countrymen, fellow Australians!

===Chinese===
浩莽丛林的诡谲魔力，辽阔日照平原的气息，营地铃铛的声响、绊马链的叮当，随着黄昏柔软的微风飘荡而来——这一切也曾降临到这些人身上，在他们心头写下一个故事，如同在我心头写下的那个一样。星光满天的穹苍之辉煌，大海之浩瀚奇观，雷霆之庄严雄伟，都曾降临到他们心里；日落时分那令人屏息的圆满，也曾向他们低语，所诉说的还不止是明日天气的阴晴。风和雨有一种声音，向肯德尔（Kendall）诉说，他也曾尝过缺少知音的孤苦。戈登，携着他那悲哀复悲哀的人道情怀与痛切的幻灭，向我伸出手来，携我同去。唯一的遗憾是，我永远无缘同他们相见——拜伦、萨克雷、狄更斯、朗费罗、戈登、肯德尔，这些我所爱的人，都已辞世；可是，多么叫人快慰的念头啊！凯恩（Caine）、帕特森（Paterson）和劳森还活着，还是呼吸着的血肉之人——其中两位竟还是同乡，是地道的澳洲人！

===Original===
I pored with renewed zeal over the terse realism and pathos of Lawson, and enjoyed Paterson’s redolence of the rollicking side of the wholesome life beneath these sunny skies, which he depicted with grand touches of power flashing here and there. I learnt them by heart, and in that gloriously blue receptacle, by and by, where many pleasant youthful dreams are stowed, I put the hope that one day I would clasp hands with them, and feel and know the unspeakable comfort and heart-rest of congenial companionship.

===Chinese===
我以重新燃起的热情，埋头细读劳森那简洁遒劲的写实与哀婉，也陶然于帕特森笔下这片丽日晴空之下健康生活里欢腾恣纵的一面——他描绘起来，处处闪现着大家手笔的力度。我把他们熟记于心，又把一个愿望，藏进那湛蓝美妙的容器的深处——多少愉快的少年梦都收贮在那里面——盼着有朝一日，我能同他们执手相握，亲身体味、亲身知晓投契相伴那无可言说的慰藉与心安。

## X Everard Grey / 第10章 埃弗拉德·格雷

===Original===
Uncle Julius had taken a run down to Sydney before returning to Caddagat, and was to be home during the first week in September, bringing with him Everard Grey. This young gentleman always spent Christmas at Caddagat, but as he had just recovered from an illness he was coming up for a change now instead. Having heard much of him, I was curious to see him. He was grandmamma’s adopted son, and was the orphan of very aristocratic English parents who had left him to the guardianship of distant relatives. They had proved criminally unscrupulous. By finding a flaw in deeds, or something which none but lawyers understand, they had deprived him of all his property and left him to sink or swim. Grannie had discovered, reared, and educated him. Among professions he had chosen the bar, and was now one of Sydney’s most promising young barristers. His foster-mother was no end proud of him, and loved him as her own son.

===Chinese===
朱利叶斯舅舅回卡达加特之前，先南下悉尼跑了一趟，定于九月第一个礼拜到家，还要带着埃弗拉德·格雷（Everard Grey）一道回来。这位年轻先生向来是在卡达加特过圣诞的，只因大病初愈，这次改在眼下上来换换环境。我久闻其名，急着要见见他。他是外婆的养子，本是英国极有贵族气派的双亲留下的孤儿，父母把他托给远亲照管。那些亲戚却黑了心肠、无法无天。他们在地契里寻出一处破绽——反正那名堂只有律师们才弄得懂——就此剥夺了他的全部财产，任他自生自灭。是外婆把他发掘出来，抚养成人，供他受了教育。在各行各业里，他选中了律师业，如今已是悉尼最有前途的年轻出庭律师之一。他这位养母得意得不得了，把他当亲生儿子一样疼爱。

===Original===
In due time a telegram arrived from uncle Julius, containing instructions for the buggy to be sent to Gool-Gool to meet him and Everard Grey.

===Chinese===
到了日子，朱利叶斯舅舅来了电报，吩咐把轻便马车赶到古尔古尔（Gool-Gool）去接他和埃弗拉德·格雷。

===Original===
By this time I had quite recovered from influenza and my accident, and as they would not arrive till near nightfall, for their edification I was to be dressed in full-blown dinner costume, also I was to be favoured with a look at my reflection in a mirror for the first time since my arrival.

===Chinese===
这时我的流感和烫伤都已痊愈；他们要到暮色将合才到，为着让他们开开眼界，我要穿上全套盛大的晚装，还蒙受恩典，可以照一照镜中的自己——自打来到卡达加特，这还是头一回。

===Original===
During the afternoon I was dispatched by grannie on a message some miles away, and meeting Mr. Hawden some distance from the house, he took it upon himself to accompany me. Everywhere I went he followed after, much to my annoyance, because grannie gave me many and serious talkings-to about the crime of encouraging young men.

===Chinese===
下午外婆打发我出门送个口信，去的地方离宅子好几英里，走了一程便撞见霍登先生，他竟自作主张陪我同行。我走到哪儿他跟到哪儿，烦得我要命，因为外婆为着“勾引年轻男人”这桩罪过，已经郑重其事地训过我好些回。

===Original===
Frank Hawden had changed his tune, and told me now that it mattered not that I was not pretty, as pretty or not I was the greatest brick of a girl he had met. His idea for this opinion was that I was able to talk theatres with him, and was the only girl there, and because he had arrived at that overflowing age when young men have to be partial to some female whether she be ugly or pretty, fat or lean, old or young. That I should be the object of these puerile emotions in a fellow like Frank Hawden, filled me with loathing and disgust.

===Chinese===
弗兰克·霍登（Frank Hawden）换了论调，如今对我说，我不漂亮也无关紧要，因为不论漂亮不漂亮，我都是他遇到过的顶顶了不起的姑娘。他这番见地的来由是：我能同他谈谈戏，又是此地独一无二的姑娘，还因为他已到了那个满得外溢的年纪——年轻男人一到这年纪，不管对方是丑是俊、是胖是瘦、是老是少，总得偏疼某一个女性不可。像弗兰克·霍登这样的人，竟然叫我成了他这份幼稚情愫的对象，只令我满怀憎嫌与恶心。

===Original===
It was late in the afternoon when Hawden and I returned, and the buggy was to be seen a long way down the road, approaching at the going-for-the-doctor pace at which uncle Julius always drove.

===Chinese===
霍登和我回到家时已近日暮，远远便望见大路尽头那辆轻便马车正一路赶来，那赶着去请医生似的疾驰劲儿，正是朱利叶斯舅舅向来驾车的路数。

===Original===
Aunt Helen hustled me off to dress, but I was only half-rigged when they arrived, and so was unable to go out and meet them. Uncle Julius inquired for that youngster of Lucy’s, and aunt Helen replied that she would be forthcoming when they were dressed for dinner. The two gentlemen took a nip, to put a little heart in them uncle Julius said, and auntie Helen came to finish my toilet while they were making theirs.

===Chinese===
海伦姨妈赶紧催我去梳妆，可我才穿戴了一半他们便到了，也就没能出去迎接。朱利叶斯舅舅问起露西（Lucy）家的那个小丫头，海伦姨妈答道，等大家换好晚餐衣裳，她自会露面。两位先生先抿了一口酒——用朱利叶斯舅舅的话说，好给心上添点底气——趁他们拾掇的工夫，海伦姨妈来替我完成梳妆。

===Original===
“There now, you have nothing to complain of in the way of looks,” she remarked at the completion of the ceremony. “Come and have a good look at yourself.”

===Chinese===
“瞧，论模样，你可没什么好抱怨的啦。”仪式完成时她说道。“来，把自己好好端详端详。”

===Original===
I was decked in my first evening dress, as it was a great occasion. It was only on the rarest occasion that we donned full war-paint at Caddagat. I think that evening dress is one of the prettiest and most idiotic customs extant. What can be more foolish than to endanger one’s health by exposing at night the chest and arms﻿—two of the most vital spots of the body﻿—which have been covered all day? On the other hand, what can be more beautiful than a soft white bosom rising and falling amid a dainty nest of silk and lace? Every woman looks more soft and feminine in a *decollete* gown. And is there any of the animal lines known pleasanter to the eye than the contour of shapely arms? Some there are who cry down evening dress as being immodest and indecent. These will be found among those whose chest and arms will not admit of being displayed, or among those who, not having been reared to the custom, dislike it with many other things from want of use.

===Chinese===
因为是个大场合，我头一回穿上了晚礼服。在卡达加特，非得逢着顶难得的场合，我们才披挂起全套“战妆”。依我看，晚礼服是现今顶漂亮也顶愚蠢的风俗之一。还有什么比这更荒唐——夜里偏把捂了一整天的胸口和手臂袒露出来受凉，那可是全身顶要害的两处？可话说回来，又有什么比一幅柔白的胸脯，在丝绸与花边织就的精巧窠臼里一起一伏，更教人赏心悦目？女人穿了袒胸的礼服（*decollete*），个个都显得更温柔、更有女性味。再者，人所熟知的种种肉身线条里，可有一条比一双匀称手臂的轮廓更中看？也有人痛贬晚礼服，说它不端庄、不体面。这类人不外乎两种：一种人的胸口和手臂见不得人；一种人自小没养成这习惯，出于不惯，连同许许多多别的东西一并对它嫌弃。

===Original===
Aunt Helen took me into the wide old drawing-room, now brilliantly lighted. A heavy lamp was on each of the four brackets in the corners, and another swung from the centre of the ceiling, and candelabra threw many lights from the piano. Never before had I seen this room in such a blaze of light. During the last week or two aunt Helen and I had occupied it every night, but we never lighted more than a single candle on the piano. This had been ample light for our purpose. Aunt Helen would sing in her sweet sad voice all the beautiful old songs I loved, while I curled myself on a mat at her side and read books﻿—the music often compelling me to forget the reading, and the reading occasionally rendering me deaf to the music; but through both ever came the solemn rush of the stream outside in its weird melancholy, like a wind ceaselessly endeavouring to outstrip a wild vain regret which relentlessly pursued.

===Chinese===
海伦姨妈领我进了那间宽敞的老客厅，此刻灯烛辉煌。四角的托架上各立着一盏沉甸甸的灯，天花板正中又垂下一盏，钢琴上的枝形烛台也洒下一片光焰。我还从未见过这间屋子这样灯火通明。前两个来礼拜，海伦姨妈和我夜夜都消磨在这里，可我们点的灯从没多过钢琴上的一支蜡烛。那点光亮，尽够我们用的了。海伦姨妈用她那甜美而忧伤的嗓子，唱着我心爱的那些美丽老歌，我则蜷在她身旁的一张垫子上看书——音乐常常使我忘了读书，读书偶尔又叫我对音乐充耳不闻；可在两者之间，始终透进来屋外那条溪流庄严的奔涌声，带着它诡谲的忧郁，像一阵风，竭力想赶过一段狂乱而虚妄的怅恨，那怅恨却在后头无情地追赶。

===Original===
“Your uncle Julius always has the drawing-room lighted like this; he does not believe in shadowy half light﻿—calls it sentimental bosh,” said aunt Helen in explanation.

===Chinese===
“你朱利叶斯舅舅向来要把客厅照得这么亮堂；他不信半明半暗的幽光那一套——管它叫多愁善感的胡话。”海伦姨妈解释道。

===Original===
“Is uncle like that?” I remarked, but my question remained unanswered. Leaving a hand-mirror with me, aunt Helen had slipped away.

===Chinese===
“舅舅是这么个人吗？”我说，可我的问话没了下文。海伦姨妈留给我一面手镜，人已悄悄溜开了。

===Original===
One wall of the drawing-room was monopolized by a door, a big bookcase, and a heavy bevelled-edged old-fashioned mirror﻿—the two last-mentioned articles reaching from floor to ceiling. Since my arrival the face of the mirror had been covered, but this evening the blue silken curtains were looped up, and it was before this that I stood.

===Chinese===
客厅的一面墙，叫一扇门、一座大书柜和一面厚重的斜边老式镜子独占了——后两样东西都是从地板直抵天花板。自打我来了，那镜面一直蒙着，今晚蓝绸帘子却挽了起来，我正是站在这面镜子跟前。

===Original===
I looked, and looked again in pleased surprise. I beheld a young girl with eyes and skin of the clearest and brightest, and lips of brilliant scarlet, and a chest and pair of arms which would pass muster with the best. If Nature had been in bad humour when moulding my face, she had used her tools craftily in forming my figure. Aunt Helen had proved a clever maid and dressmaker. My pale blue cashmere dress fitted my fully developed yet girlish figure to perfection. Some of my hair fell in cunning little curls on my forehead; the remainder, tied simply with a piece of ribbon, hung in thick waves nearly to my knees. My toilet had altered me almost beyond recognition. It made me look my age﻿—sixteen years and ten months﻿—whereas before, when dressed carelessly and with my hair plastered in a tight coil, people not knowing me would not believe that I was under twenty. Joy and merriment lit up my face, which glowed with youth, health, and happiness, which rippled my lips in smiles, which displayed a splendid set of teeth, and I really believe that on that night I did not look out of the way ugly.

===Chinese===
我望了又望，又惊又喜。我看见一个年轻姑娘，眼睛和皮肤最是清亮，嘴唇红得鲜明，一副胸口、一双臂膀，同最出色的摆在一处也过得去。倘使造化捏塑我的脸时心境不佳，那么塑造我的身材时，她手中的家伙可使得精妙极了。海伦姨妈证明了自己是个巧手的女仆兼裁缝。我那件浅蓝的开司米衣裙，把我那已然发育、却仍是少女身段的身姿，衬贴得十全十美。我的一部分头发卷成俏皮的小圈儿垂在额前，其余的只用一根缎带随便束着，厚厚的波纹几乎垂到膝盖。这一番梳妆把我改换得几乎认不得了。它叫我显出了自己的年纪——十六岁零十个月——而从前，穿得随随便便、头发紧盘成一个髻的时候，不认得我的人还不肯信我不到二十岁呢。欢喜和快活照亮了我的脸，脸上焕发着青春、健康和幸福，幸福使我的嘴唇漾出笑意，露出一口漂亮的牙齿——我真信，那天夜里我并不至于丑得格外出奇。

===Original===
I was still admiring my reflection when aunt Helen returned to say that Everard and uncle Julius were smoking on the veranda and asking for me.

===Chinese===
我还对着镜中的自己顾影自赏，海伦姨妈回来了，说埃弗拉德和朱利叶斯舅舅正在游廊上抽烟，问我要去呢。

===Original===
“What do you think of yourself, Sybylla?”

===Chinese===
“你觉得自己怎么样啊，西比拉？”

===Original===
“Oh, aunt Helen, tell me that there is something about me not completely hideous!”

===Chinese===
“噢，海伦姨妈，快告诉我，我身上总算还有点什么，不至于丑到十足吧！”

===Original===
She took my face between her hands, saying:

===Chinese===
她双手捧起我的脸，说道：

===Original===
“Silly child, there are some faces with faultless features, which would receive nothing more than an indifferent glance while beside other faces which might have few if any pretensions to beauty. Yours is one of those last mentioned.”

===Chinese===
“傻孩子，世上有那么一些面孔，五官生得毫无瑕疵，可一旦挨着另一些几乎谈不上半点姿色的面孔，也只配得一个淡淡的眼色。你的脸，就属于这后一种。”

===Original===
“But that does not say I am not ugly.”

===Chinese===
“可这并不等于说我不丑啊。”

===Original===
“No one would dream of calling you plain, let alone ugly; brilliant is the word which best describes you.”

===Chinese===
“谁做梦也不会说你平常，更别提丑了；‘神采照人’才是顶贴切地描摹你的字眼。”

===Original===
Uncle Julius had the upper part of his ponderous figure arrayed in a frock-coat. He did not take kindly to what he termed “those skittish sparrow-tailed affairs.” Frock-coats suited him, but I am not partial to them on everyone. They look well enough on a podgy, fat, or broad man, but on a skinny one they hang with such a forlorn, dying-duck expression, that they invariably make me laugh.

===Chinese===
朱利叶斯舅舅把他那魁伟身躯的上半截，装裹在一件长礼服（frock-coat）里。他素来看不惯被他唤作“那些轻佻的雀尾玩意儿”的衣裳。长礼服穿着他正相称，不过换了旁人穿我可未必喜欢。矮墩墩、肥实实或是宽阔的男人穿上它还够体面，可套在瘦子身上，它便吊出那么一副凄凄惶惶、垂死鸭子似的神情，回回都逗得我发笑。

===Original===
Julius John Bossier, better known as J. J. Bossier, and better still as Jay-Jay﻿—big, fat, burly, broad, a jovial bachelor of forty, too fond of all the opposite sex ever to have settled his affections on one in particular﻿—was well known, respected, and liked from Wagga Wagga to Albury, Forbes to Dandaloo, Bourke to Hay, from Tumut to Monaro, and back again to Peak Hill, as a generous man, a straight goer in business matters, and a jolly good fellow all round.

===Chinese===
朱利叶斯·约翰·博西耶（Julius John Bossier），人们更常唤他 J. J. 博西耶，叫得最顺口的还是“杰杰”（Jay-Jay）——块头大、身量肥、粗壮、宽阔，四十岁的快活单身汉，因为太喜欢普天下的异性，从没把情分定在哪一个身上——从瓦加瓦加（Wagga Wagga）到奥尔伯里（Albury），从福布斯（Forbes）到丹达卢（Dandaloo），从伯克（Bourke）到海伊（Hay），从图穆特（Tumut）到莫纳罗（Monaro），再兜回皮克山（Peak Hill），他人人知晓、人人敬重、人人喜爱：是个慷慨的人，生意场上堂堂正正，里里外外都是个好得没话说的家伙。

===Original===
I was very proud to call him uncle.

===Chinese===
我以唤他一声舅舅为荣。

===Original===
“So this is yourself, is it!” he exclaimed, giving me a tremendous hug.

===Chinese===
“这么说，这就是你本人啦，是不是！”他嚷道，给了我一个结结实实的大拥抱。

===Original===
“Oh, uncle,” I expostulated, “wipe your old kisses off! Your breath smells horribly of whisky and tobacco.”

===Chinese===
“哎呀，舅舅，”我抗辩道，“把你那些老亲吻擦掉吧！你满嘴威士忌和烟草的气味，冲得很。”

===Original===
“Gammon, that’s what makes my kisses so nice!” he answered; and, after holding me at arm’s-length for inspection, “By George, you’re a wonderful-looking girl! You’re surely not done growing yet, though! You are such a little nipper. I could put you in my pocket with ease. You aren’t a scrap like your mother. I’ll give the next shearer who passes a shilling to cut that hair off. It would kill a dog in the hot weather.”

===Chinese===
“胡说，正是它才叫我的亲吻这么香呢！”他答道；随后把我伸直了胳膊上下端详：“天哪，你真是个好看得了不得的姑娘！不过你准还没长成个儿吧！你这么个小不点儿，我轻轻松松就能把你揣进兜里。你跟你妈没有一丝儿相像。回头打这儿过的第一个剪羊毛的，我赏他一个先令，叫他把那头铰了。大热天里，它能把一条狗热死。”

===Original===
“Everard, this is my niece, Sybylla” (aunt Helen was introducing us). “You will have to arrange yourselves﻿—what relation you are, and how to address each other.”

===Chinese===
“埃弗拉德，这是我外甥女西比拉”（海伦姨妈正给我们引见）。“你们俩得自己商量——论的是哪门子亲，彼此怎么称呼。”

===Original===
The admiration expressed in his clear sharp eyes gave me a sensation different to any I had ever experienced previously.

===Chinese===
他那双清亮锐利的眼睛里流露出的赞赏，给了我一种从未体验过的异样感觉。

===Original===
“I suppose I’m a kind of uncle and brother in one, and as either relationship entitles me to a kiss, I’m going to take one,” he said in a very gallant manner.

===Chinese===
“我想我算是一半舅舅、一半哥哥；而论哪一门亲眷，我都够资格讨一个吻，所以我这就讨一个。”他说得殷勤潇洒。

===Original===
“You may take one if you can,” I said with mischievous defiance, springing off the veranda into the flower-garden. He accepted my challenge, and, being lithe as a cat, a tremendous scamper ensued. Round and round the flowerbeds we ran. Uncle Jay-Jay’s beard opened in a broad smile, which ended in a loud laugh. Everard Grey’s coattails flew in the breeze he made, and his collar was too high for athletic purposes. I laughed too, and was lost, and we returned to the veranda﻿—Everard in triumph, and I feeling very red and uncomfortable.

===Chinese===
“你有本事就拿去。”我淘气地挑衅道，一个箭步跳下游廊，蹿进了花园。他接受了挑战，加上身子又轻捷得像只猫，一场天翻地覆的追逐随即开场。我们绕着花坛跑了一圈又一圈。杰杰舅舅的胡子咧开一个大大的笑，末了爆成一串响亮的大笑。埃弗拉德·格雷的燕尾服后摆在他自己带起的风里飞扬，他的领子又扣得太高，不利奔跑。我也笑了，笑着便败下阵来；我们回到游廊上——埃弗拉德得胜还朝，我只觉得满脸通红，浑身不自在。

===Original===
Grannie had arrived upon the scene, looking the essence of brisk respectability in a black silk gown and a white lace cap. She cast on me a glance of severe disapproval, and denounced my conduct as shameful; but uncle Jay-Jay’s eyes twinkled as he dexterously turned the subject.

===Chinese===
外婆这时驾临现场，一身黑绸衣裙，一顶白色蕾丝便帽，一身干净利落、端庄体面的本色。她朝我投来严词苛责的一瞥，痛斥我的行径不成体统；杰杰舅舅却眼里闪着光，麻利地岔开了话头。

===Original===
“Gammon, mother! I bet you were often kissed when that youngster’s age. I bet my boots now that you can’t count the times you did the same thing yourself. Now, confess.”

===Chinese===
“胡说，妈！我敢打赌，你在那小丫头那么大的时候，亲吻也照样少不了。我这会儿敢拿靴子打赌，你自己干过同样的事，回数多到数不过来。快招了吧。”

===Original===
Grannie’s face melted in a smile as she commenced a little anecdote, with that pathetic beginning, “When I was young.”

===Chinese===
外婆的脸在一个微笑里化开了，讲起一段小掌故，开头便是那句凄婉动人的“想当年我年轻的时候”。

===Original===
Aunt Helen sent me inside lest I should catch cold, and I stationed myself immediately inside the window so that I should not miss the conversation. “I should think your niece is very excitable,” Mr. Grey was saying to aunt Helen.

===Chinese===
海伦姨妈怕我着凉，打发我进屋去，我便在窗子内侧站定，好歹不漏掉那场谈话。“我看你这位外甥女，性子极易激动。”格雷先生正对海伦姨妈说。

===Original===
“Oh, very.”

===Chinese===
“哦，极了。”

===Original===
“Yes; I have never seen any but very highly strung temperaments have that transparent brilliance of expression.”

===Chinese===
“是的；依我看，唯有天性绷得极紧的人，才会带出那样一种晶莹焕发、藏不住的神情。”

===Original===
“She is very variable﻿—one moment all joy, and the next the reverse.”

===Chinese===
“她喜怒无常——这一刻满心是欢喜，下一刻又恰恰相反。”

===Original===
“She has a very striking face. I don’t know what it is that makes it so.”

===Chinese===
“她那张脸出众极了。我说不清是什么叫它这样。”

===Original===
“It may be her complexion,” said aunt Helen; “her skin is whiter than the fairest blonde, and her eyebrows and lashes very dark. Be very careful you do not say anything that would let her know you think her not nice looking. She broods over her appearance in such a morbid manner. It is a weak point with her, so be careful not to sting her sensitiveness in that respect.”

===Chinese===
“多半是她的肤色，”海伦姨妈说，“她的皮肤比最白皙的金发女郎还要白，眉毛和睫毛却又极深。千万留神，别说出什么话来，让她知道你觉得她不好看。她对自己的相貌愁闷得太病态了。那是她的一个痛处，所以小心别在那一层上刺着她的敏感。”

===Original===
“Plain-looking! Why, I think she has one of the most fascinating faces I’ve seen for some time, and her eyes are simply magnificent. What colour are they?”

===Chinese===
“不好看！嗐，我看她这张脸，是我近来所见最动人的面孔之一，她那双眼睛简直华美极了。是什么颜色的？”

===Original===
“The grass is not bad about Sydney. I think I will send a truck of fat wethers away next week,” said uncle Jay-Jay to grannie.

===Chinese===
“悉尼一带的草不算坏。我想下礼拜就发一车皮肥阉羊出去。”杰杰舅舅对外婆说。

===Original===
“It is getting quite dark. Let’s get in to dinner at once,” said grannie.

===Chinese===
“天黑透了。咱们这就进去用晚餐吧。”外婆说。

===Original===
During the meal I took an opportunity of studying the appearance of Everard Grey. He had a typically aristocratic English face, even to the cold rather heartless expression, which is as established a point of an English blue blood as an arched neck is of a thoroughbred horse.

===Chinese===
席间我寻了个机会，端详埃弗拉德·格雷的相貌。他有一张典型的英国贵族脸，连那种冷淡而近乎无情的表情也一应俱全——那表情之于英国的蓝血贵族，正如弓起的颈项之于纯种马，是同样定型的一点品相。

===Original===
A ringer, whose wife had been unexpectedly confined, came for grannie when dinner was over, and the rest of us had a delightful musical evening. Uncle Jay-Jay bawled “The Vicar of Bray” and “Drink, Puppy, Drink” in a stentorian bass voice, holding me on his knee, pinching, tickling, pulling my hair, and shaking me up and down between whiles. Mr. Hawden favoured us by rendering “The Holy City.” Everard Grey sang several new songs, which was a great treat, as he had a well-trained and musical baritone voice. He was a veritable carpet knight, and though not a fop, was exquisitely dressed in full evening costume, and showed his long pedigreed blood in every line of his clean-shaven face and tall slight figure. He was quite a champion on the piano, and played aunt Helen’s accompaniments while he made her sing song after song. When she was weary uncle Jay-Jay said to me, “Now it’s your turn, me fine lady. We’ve all done something to keep things rolling but you. Can you sing?”

===Chinese===
晚饭刚过，一个剪羊毛快手（ringer）的妻子忽而临盆，来请外婆，其余的人便消磨了一个愉快的音乐之夜。杰杰舅舅用洪亮的男低音吼着《布雷的牧师》（The Vicar of Bray）和《喝吧，小狗，喝吧》（Drink, Puppy, Drink），把我搁在膝上，一会儿掐掐，一会儿挠挠，一会儿揪揪我的头发，一会儿又把我上下颠个不停。霍登先生赏光，为我们演唱了《圣城》（The Holy City）。埃弗拉德·格雷唱了几支新歌，真是莫大的享受，他有一条训练有素、富于乐感的男中音嗓子。他是个不折不扣的沙龙骑士，虽算不上纨绔子弟，那身全套晚礼服却穿得精美考究，修得干干净净的脸上、颀长清瘦的身段上，每一根线条都透出名门世家的悠久血统。他还是钢琴上的一把好手，一面给海伦姨妈弹伴奏，一面引得她一支接一支唱下去。她唱乏了，杰杰舅舅便对我说：“这回轮到你啦，我的大小姐。大伙儿都出了力，就数你没动静。你会唱歌吗？”

===Original===
“No.”

===Chinese===
“不会。”

===Original===
“Can this youngster sing, Helen?”

===Chinese===
“海伦，这小丫头会唱吗？”

===Original===
“She sings very nicely to herself sometimes, but I do not know how she would manage before company. Will you try something, Sybylla?”

===Chinese===
“她有时自己哼着唱唱，蛮好听的，可当着人怎么样，我就不知道了。你肯试着来一支吗，西比拉？”

===Original===
Uncle Jay-Jay waited to hear no more, but carrying me to the music-stool, and depositing me thereon, warned me not to attempt to leave it before singing something.

===Chinese===
杰杰舅舅再不等下文，径直把我拎到琴凳上，往那儿一放，警告我不唱出一支歌来，休想打那儿离开。

===Original===
To get away to myself, where I was sure no one could bear me, and sing and sing till I made the echoes ring, was one of the chief joys of my existence, but I had never made a success in singing to company. Besides losing all nerve, I had a very queer voice, which everyone remarked. However, tonight I made an effort in my old favourite, “Three Fishers Went Sailing.” The beauty of the full-toned Ronisch piano, and Everard’s clever and sympathetic accompanying, caused me to forget my audience, and sing as though to myself alone, forgetting that my voice was odd.

===Chinese===
独自躲开去，笃定没有一个人听得见我，唱啊唱啊，唱得回声嗡嗡作响——这是我平生最大的乐事之一；可当众唱歌，我从来没成功过。除了怯场，我还有一条非常古怪的嗓子，人人都要议论。不过今晚，我在我心爱的老歌《三个渔夫出海去》（Three Fishers Went Sailing）上试了一试。那架音色饱满的罗尼施（Ronisch）钢琴，加上埃弗拉德聪明而贴切的伴奏，使我忘掉了听众，只当独自个儿在唱，连嗓子的古怪也忘了。

===Original===
When the song ceased Mr. Grey wheeled abruptly on the stool and said, “Do you know that you have one of the most wonderful natural voices I have heard. Why, there is a fortune in such a voice if it were, trained! Such chest-notes, such feeling, such rarity of tone!”

===Chinese===
歌声一停，格雷先生霍地在琴凳上转过身来说道：“你可知道，你这条嗓子，是我平生听过的最美妙的天赋嗓子之一。嗐，这样的嗓子，只要加以训练，里头就藏着一份家财！这样的胸腔音，这样的感情，这样罕有的音色！”

===Original===
“Don’t be sarcastic, Mr. Grey,” I said shortly.

===Chinese===
“别挖苦人了，格雷先生。”我简短地说。

===Original===
“Upon my word as a man, I mean every word I say,” he returned enthusiastically.

===Chinese===
“我以一个男子汉的言语担保，我说的每一个字都是当真的。”他热切地答道。

===Original===
Everard Grey’s opinion on artistic matters was considered worth having. He dabbled in all the arts﻿—writing, music, acting, and sketching, and went to every good concert and play in Sydney. Though he was clever at law, it was whispered by some that he would wind up on the stage, as he had a great leaning that way.

===Chinese===
埃弗拉德·格雷在艺术上的见地，向来被人认为颇有一听的价值。他样样艺术都要沾一手——写作、音乐、演戏、素描，悉尼每一场像样的音乐会和戏剧，他都到场。他法律上固然精明，私下却有人嘀咕，说他早晚要落脚到舞台上去，因为他那颗心大大地偏向那一边。

===Original===
I walked away from the piano treading on air. Would I really make a singer? I with the voice which had often been ridiculed; I who had often blasphemously said that I would sell my soul to be able to sing just passably. Everard Grey’s opinion gave me an intoxicated sensation of joy.

===Chinese===
我脚踩着云似的从钢琴边走开。我当真能成为一名歌唱家？我——顶着一条件常被人讥笑的嗓子；我——常常大不敬地说，只要能把歌唱得还过得去，我肯把灵魂卖掉。埃弗拉德·格雷这句评语，给了我一种醺醺然的欢天喜地。

===Original===
“Can you recite?” he inquired.

===Chinese===
“你会朗诵吗？”他问。

===Original===
“Yes,” I answered firmly.

===Chinese===
“会。”我斩钉截铁地答。

===Original===
“Give us something,” said uncle Jay-Jay.

===Chinese===
“给我们来一段。”杰杰舅舅说。

===Original===
I recited Longfellow’s *The Slave’s Dream*. Everard Grey was quite as enthusiastic over this as he had been about my singing.

===Chinese===
我朗诵了朗费罗的《奴隶的梦》（*The Slave's Dream*）。埃弗拉德·格雷听了这一段，同方才听我唱歌时一样起劲。

===Original===
“Such a voice! Such depth and width! Why, she could fill the Centennial Hall without an effort. All she requires is training.”

===Chinese===
“这样一条嗓子！这样的深和阔！嗐，她不费吹灰之力，就能把百年纪念堂（Centennial Hall）灌得满满的。她缺的只是训练。”

===Original===
“By George, she’s a regular dab! But I wish she would give us something not quite so glum,” said uncle Jay-Jay.

===Chinese===
“天哪，她真是个把式！不过我希望她给我们来点不那么阴惨惨的。”杰杰舅舅说。

===Original===
I let myself go. Carried away by I don’t know what sort of a spirit, I exclaimed, “Very well, I will, if you will wait till I make up, and will help me.”

===Chinese===
我放开了手脚。叫一股不知什么劲儿裹挟着，我嚷道：“好——只要你们等我把脸子化好，再帮我一把。”

===Original===
I disappeared for a few minutes, and returned made up as a fat old Irish woman, with a smudge of dirt on my face. There was a general laugh.

===Chinese===
我消失了片刻，回来时已扮成一个胖老爱尔兰婆子，脸上还抹了一道泥。满座一阵大笑。

===Original===
Would Mr. Hawden assist me? Of course he was only too delighted, and flattered that I had called upon him in preference to the others. What would he do?

===Chinese===
霍登先生可愿给我搭把手？他当然求之不得，还因为我不挑别人单挑了他，暗暗得意。他要做什么呢？

===Original===
I sat him on a footstool, so that I might with facility put my hand on his sandy hair, and turning to uncle, commenced:

===Chinese===
我让他坐在一只脚凳上，好教我顺手就能把手搭上他那头沙色头发，然后转向舅舅，开了腔：

===Original===
“Shure, sir, seeing it was a good bhoy yez were afther to run errants, it’s meself that has brought this youngsther for yer inspection. It’s a jool ye’ll have in him. Shure I rared him meself, and he says his prayers every morning. Kape sthill, honey! Faith, ye’re not afraid of yer poor old mammy pullin’ yer beautiful cur-r-rls?”

===Chinese===
“可不，老爷，您不是正要找个跑腿儿的好娃儿么，是俺亲自把这位小哥儿带来给老爷过目咧。他呀，可是颗小宝珠哟。俺一手把他拉扯大，他天天早上都念祷告。乖乖，坐稳喽！天老爷，你该不是怕你可怜的老娘揪坏你那漂漂亮亮的卷卷毛吧？”

===Original===
Uncle Jay-Jay was laughing like fun; even aunt Helen deigned to smile; and Everard was looking on with critical interest.

===Chinese===
杰杰舅舅笑得直不起腰；连海伦姨妈也纡尊赏了一个微笑；埃弗拉德则带着品评的兴味在旁观瞧。

===Original===
“Go on,” said uncle. But Mr. Hawden got huffy at the ridicule which he suspected I was calling down upon him, and jumped up looking fit to eat me.

===Chinese===
“接着来。”舅舅说。可霍登先生疑心我在招大家笑话他，恼了，跳将起来，那副模样像要把我生吞了。

===Original===
I acted several more impromptu scenes with the other occupants of the drawing-room. Mr. Hawden emitted “Humph!” from the corner where he grumpily sat, but Mr. Grey was full of praise.

===Chinese===
我又同客厅里其余的人即兴演了几幕。霍登先生在他闷坐的角落里哼出一声“哼！”，格雷先生却赞不绝口。

===Original===
“Splendid! splendid!” he exclaimed. “You say you have not had an hour’s training, and never saw a play. Such versatility. Your fortune would be made on the stage. It is a sin to have such exceptional talent wasting in the bush. I must take her to Sydney and put her under a good master.”

===Chinese===
“妙极了！妙极了！”他嚷道。“你说你没受过一个钟头的科班训练，连一出戏都没看过。这样的多才多艺。上了舞台，你这辈子的富贵就稳稳到手了。这样出类拔萃的天才糟蹋在丛林里，简直是罪过。我一定得带她去悉尼，把她交给一位好师傅。”

===Original===
“Indeed, you’ll do no such thing,” said uncle. “I’ll keep her here to liven up the old barracks. You’ve got enough puppets on the stage without a niece of mine ever being there.”

===Chinese===
“嗐，这事儿你想都别想。”舅舅说。“我要把她留在这儿，给这座老营房添点儿生气。舞台上的傀儡你们已经够多了，犯不上再搭进去我一个外甥女。”

===Original===
I went to bed that night greatly elated. Flattery is sweet to youth. I felt pleased with myself, and imagined, as I peeped in the looking-glass, that I was not half bad-looking after all.

===Chinese===
那天夜里我上床时，心花怒放。奉承话对青春总是甜的。我心满意足，一面朝镜子里偷觑一眼，一面想：毕竟我长得也不算太寒碜嘛。

## XI Yah! / 第11章 呸

===Original===
“Bah, you hideous animal! Ha ha! Your peerless conceit does you credit. So you actually imagined that by one or two out of every hundred you might be considered passable. You are the most uninteresting person in the world. You are small and nasty and bad, and every other thing that’s abominable. That’s what you are.”

===Chinese===
“呸，你这丑八怪！哈哈！你那份举世无双的自负，真给你长脸。你竟然痴心妄想，每一百个人里头，总有一两个会把你看成勉强过得去。你是天底下顶顶没意思的人。你又矮小、又讨嫌、又坏，凡是叫人恶心的名目，你一样不落。你就是这么一号东西。”

===Original===
This address I delivered to my reflection in the glass next morning. My elation of the previous night was as flat as a pancake. Dear, oh dear, what a fool I had been to softly swallow the flattery of Mr. Grey without a single snub in return! To make up for my laxity, if he continued to amuse himself by plastering my vanity with the ointment of flattery, I determined to serve up my replies to him red-hot and well seasoned with pepper.

===Chinese===
第二天一早，我对着镜子里自己的影像，发表了这篇训词。头天夜里的那阵得意，如今瘪得像张煎饼。哎呀呀，我多蠢，竟软绵绵地咽下了格雷先生（Mr. Grey）的奉承，连半句抢白也没回敬！为了补赎这份懈怠，我打定主意：他若还拿奉承当膏药，往我的虚荣心上敷着寻开心，我就把回话烧得滚烫、撒足了胡椒再端给他。

===Original===
I finished my toilet, and in a very what’s-the-good-o’-anything mood took a last glance in the glass to say, “You’re ugly, you’re ugly and useless; so don’t forget that and make a fool of yourself again.”

===Chinese===
我梳洗完毕，怀着一种“干什么都没意思”的心情，最后朝镜子里望了一眼，说道：“你丑，你又丑又没用；把这个记牢了，别再叫自己出丑犯傻。”

===Original===
I was in the habit of doing this; it had long ago taken the place of a morning prayer. I said this, that by familiarity it might lose a little of its sting when I heard it from other lips, but somehow it failed in efficacy.

===Chinese===
这样做我早已成了习惯；很久以前，它就顶替了晨祷的位置。我念叨这些话，原指望说得熟了，往后从旁人嘴里再听到时，那根刺能钝上几分；可是不知怎么，总不见效。

===Original===
I was late for breakfast that morning. All the others were half through the meal when I sat down.

===Chinese===
那天早晨我吃早饭迟了。等我坐下时，旁人的饭都已用了一半。

===Original===
Grannie had not come home till after twelve, but was looking as brisk as usual.

===Chinese===
外婆过了十二点才到家，人却还同平素一样爽利。

===Original===
“Come, Sybylla, I suppose this comes of sitting up too late, as I was not here to hunt you to bed. You are always very lively at night, but it’s a different tune in the morning,” she said, when giving me the usual morning hug.

===Chinese===
“来，西比拉，我料想这是坐得太晚闹出来的，怨我不在家，没人撵你上床。你夜里总是生龙活虎，一到早上就换了调子。”她一边说，一边照例给了我早晨的拥抱。

===Original===
“When I was a nipper of your age, if I didn’t turn out like greased lightning every morning, I was assisted by a little strap oil,” remarked uncle Jay-Jay.

===Chinese===
“我做你这么大个小崽子的时候，哪天早上要是不像抹了油的闪电般蹿起来，就有人拿根小皮带替我上点油。”杰杰舅舅（uncle Jay-Jay）说道。

===Original===
“Sybylla should be excused this morning,” interposed Mr. Grey. “She entertained us for hours last night. Little wonder if she feels languid this morning.”

===Chinese===
“今早西比拉情有可原，”格雷先生插话道，“昨晚她招待了我们好几个钟头。今早身上发懒，不足为怪。”

===Original===
“Entertained you! What did she do?” queried grannie.

===Chinese===
“招待了你们！她做了些什么呀？”外婆问道。

===Original===
“Many things. Do you know, gran, that you are robbing the world of an artist by keeping Sybylla hidden away in the bush? I must persuade you to let me take her to Sydney and have her put under the best masters in Sydney.”

===Chinese===
“多了。外婆，你可知道，你把西比拉藏在丛林里，是在抢世人的一位艺术家啊。我一定要劝得你答应，让我带她去悉尼（Sydney），送到悉尼顶好的名师门下受教。”

===Original===
“Under masters for what?”

===Chinese===
“拜名师学什么？”

===Original===
“Elocution and singing.”

===Chinese===
“演说术和唱歌。”

===Original===
“I couldn’t afford it.”

===Chinese===
“我可供不起。”

===Original===
“But I’d bear the expense myself. It would only be returning a trifle of all you have done for me.”

===Chinese===
“可这笔开销我情愿一力承担。比起你待我的种种恩义，这不过是报答一丝一毫罢了。”

===Original===
“What nonsense! What would you have her do when she was taught?”

===Chinese===
“胡说八道！她学成了，你又想叫她做什么？”

===Original===
“Go on the stage, of course. With her talent and hair she would cause quite a sensation.”

===Chinese===
“自然是登台演戏。凭她的天资和那头头发，她准能轰动一时。”

===Original===
Now grannie’s notions re the stage were very tightly laced. All actors and actresses, from the lowest circus man up to the most glorious cantatrice, were people defiled in the sight of God, and utterly outside the pale of all respectability, when measured with her code of morals.

===Chinese===
说起外婆对戏台的成见，那可像束身衣一般勒得死紧。一切男女伶人，从最下三滥的马戏班里的人，直到最光彩夺目的歌剧名伶，拿她的道德法典来量，都是在上帝眼中玷污了的人，同一切体面半点沾不上边。

===Original===
She turned energetically in her chair, and her keen eyes flashed with scorn and anger as she spoke.

===Chinese===
她在椅子里猛地转过身来，说话时，一双锐利的眼睛迸出鄙夷与怒火。

===Original===
“Go on the stage! A granddaughter of mine! Lucy’s eldest child! An actress﻿—a vile, low, brazen hussy! Use the gifts God has given her with which to do good in showing off to a crowd of vile bad men! I would rather see her struck dead at my feet this instant! I would rather see her shear off her hair and enter a convent this very hour. Child, promise you will never be a bold bad actress.”

===Chinese===
“登台演戏！我的亲外孙女！露西的长女！做个女戏子——一个下流、卑贱、寡廉鲜耻的贱货！拿上帝赏给她行善的禀赋，去在一群下流坯男人面前卖弄！我宁可这会儿眼看她死在我脚边！我宁可看她这个钟点就绞了头发进修道院。孩子，你答应我，永远不做又泼又坏的女戏子。”

===Original===
“I will never be a *bold bad* actress, grannie,” I said, putting great stress on the adjectives, and bringing out the actress very faintly.

===Chinese===
“我决不会做*又泼又坏*的女戏子的，外婆，”我说，那两个形容词咬得极重，“女戏子”三个字却吐得轻得几乎听不见。

===Original===
“Yes,” she continued, calming down, “I’m sure you have not enough bad in you. You may he boisterous, and not behave with sufficient propriety sometimes, but I don’t think you are wicked enough to ever make an actress.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，”她接着说，火气平了下来，“我确信你肚子里没有那么些坏水。你也许闹闹腾腾，有时行事不够端方，可我看你还没坏到当得了戏子的地步。”

===Original===
Everard attempted to defend his case.

===Chinese===
埃弗拉德（Everard）还想为他那一套辩白。

===Original===
“Look here, gran, that’s a very exploded old notion about the stage being a low profession. It might have been once, but it is quite the reverse nowadays. There are, of course, low people on the stage, as there are in all walks of life. I grant you that; but if people are good they can be good on the stage as well as anywhere else. On account of a little prejudice it would be a sin to rob Sybylla of the brilliant career she might have.”

===Chinese===
“您听我说，外婆，讲戏台是下等行业，那套老观念早驳倒啦。从前或许如此，如今恰恰翻了过来。戏台上自然有下等人，各行各业里都有，这个我认；可人若是好的，在戏台上同在别处一样能好。为着一点成见，就断送西比拉或许能挣得的光辉前程，那是罪过。”

===Original===
“Career!” exclaimed his foster-mother, catching at the word. “Career! That is all girls think of now, instead of being good wives and mothers and attending to their homes and doing what God intended. All they think of is gadding about and being fast, and ruining themselves body and soul. And the men are as bad to encourage them,” looking severely at Everard.

===Chinese===
“前程！”他的养母一把抓住这个词嚷了起来。“前程！如今的姑娘们满脑子想的全是这个，倒不想正经做贤妻良母、操持家宅、尽上帝安排的本分了。她们一心只想东游西荡、举止轻佻，把自己身心一齐糟蹋掉。男人们还一味怂恿她们，也一样不是东西。”她说着，严厉地瞪了埃弗拉德一眼。

===Original===
“There is a great deal of truth in what you say, gran, I admit. You can apply it to many of our girls, I am sorry to confess, but Sybylla could not be brought under that classification. You must look at her in a different way. If﻿—”

===Chinese===
“您这话里很有几分道理，外婆，我认。把这话安到我们许多姑娘头上，我也痛心地承认使得；可西比拉归不进那一类。您得换个眼光看她。要是——”

===Original===
“I look at her as the child of respectable people, and will not have the stage mentioned in connection with her.” Here Grannie thumped her fist down on the table and there was silence, complete, profound. Few dared argue with Mrs. Bossier.

===Chinese===
“我看她，是体面人家的孩子，戏台两个字，不许同她扯到一处。”说到这里，外婆一拳捶在桌上，四下便是一片沉默，完完全全、深深沉沉的沉默。敢同博西耶太太（Mrs. Bossier）抬杠的人，没有几个。

===Original===
Dear old lady, she was never angry long, and in a minute or two she proceeded with her breakfast, saying quite pleasantly:

===Chinese===
亲爱的老太太，她的火气从来长不了，过了一两分钟便继续用她的早饭，还和颜悦色地说：

===Original===
“Never mention such a subject to me again; but I’ll tell you what you can do. Next autumn, some time in March or April, when the fruit-preserving and jam-making are done with, Helen can take the child to Sydney for a month or so, and you can show them round. It will be a great treat for Sybylla as she has never been in Sydney.”

===Chinese===
“这种话休再对我提；不过我可以告诉你一桩你使得上劲的事。明年秋天，就是三四月里，腌果子、熬果酱的活计都收了尾，可以让海伦带这孩子去悉尼住个把月，你在那儿领她们各处逛逛。西比拉还没到过悉尼，这对她可是件天大的乐事。”

===Original===
“That’s right, let’s strike a bargain on that, gran,” said Everard.

===Chinese===
“好极了，外婆，那咱们就这么讲定了。”埃弗拉德说。

===Original===
“Yes; it’s a bargain, if I hear no more about the stage. God intends His creatures for a better life than that.”

===Chinese===
“好；就这么讲定，只要我不再听见戏台两个字。上帝造他的众生，是要他们过比那更好的日子。”

===Original===
After breakfast I was left to entertain Everard for some while. We had a fine time. He was a perfect gentleman and a clever conversationalist.

===Chinese===
早饭后，陪埃弗拉德消遣一阵的差事落到了我头上。我们过得十分快活。他是十足的绅士，又是位伶俐的谈客。

===Original===
I was always desirous of enjoying the company of society people who were well bred and lived according to etiquette, and possessed of leisure and culture sufficient to fill their minds with something more than the price of farm produce and a hard struggle for existence. Hitherto I had only read of such or seen them in pictures, but here was a real live one, and I seized my opportunity with vim. At my questioning and evident interest in his talk he told me of all the latest plays, actors, and actresses with whom he was acquainted, and described the fashionable balls, dinners, and garden-parties he attended. Having exhausted this subject, we fell to discussing books, and I recited snatches of poems dear to me. Everard placed his hands upon my shoulders and said:

===Chinese===
我一向渴望同上流社会的人们做伴——他们教养良好、依礼度日，闲暇与学养又足以叫脑子里装些别的，不止是农产品的行情和一场求活的苦斗。此前这类人我只在书里读过、画里见过，如今眼前却站着个活生生的，我便攒足劲头抓住了机会。见我问个不住、又明显听得入神，他便讲起他相识的一切最新的戏剧、男演员、女演员，又描画他赴过的那些时兴的舞会、宴会和游园会。这个话题谈尽了，我们又谈起书来，我吟诵了几段心爱的诗。埃弗拉德把双手搭上我的肩头，说道：

===Original===
“Sybylla, do you know you are a most wonderful girl? Your figure is perfect, your style refreshing, and you have a most interesting face. It is as ever-changing as a kaleidoscope﻿—sometimes merry, then stern, often sympathetic, and always sad when at rest. One would think you had had some sorrow in your life.”

===Chinese===
“西比拉，你可知道自己是个多么奇妙的姑娘？你的身段完美无缺，风致清新宜人，你还有一张顶顶有意思的脸。它像万花筒一般变幻不定——时而欢快，忽而严冷，又常含温情，静下来的时候，总带着一缕哀愁。人家还当你的生活里有过什么伤心事呢。”

===Original===
Lifting my skirt at either side, I bowed several times very low in what I called my stage bow, and called into requisition my stage smile, which displayed two rows of teeth as white and perfect as any twenty-guinea set turned out on a gold plate by a fashionable dentist.

===Chinese===
我提起两侧的裙裾，用我自己称作“舞台式”的那一屈膝礼，深深行了几回，又征调出我的“舞台式”微笑，亮出两排牙齿，又白又齐整，赛得过时兴牙医装在金盘子里呈上的二十几尼一副的假牙。

===Original===
“The handsome gentleman is very kind to amuse himself at the expense of a little country bumpkin, but he would do well to ascertain if his flattery would go down before administering it next time,” I said sarcastically, and I heard him calling to me as I abruptly went off to shut myself in my room.

===Chinese===
“这位俊俏的先生拿一个小乡下土包子消遣自己，真是好心肠；只是往后施舍奉承之前，先探问探问那套奉承吃得开吃不开了，才算乖觉。”我讥诮地说完，猛地转身走开，随他还在身后叫唤，径自回房把自己关了起来。

===Original===
“How dare anyone ridicule me by paying idle brainless compliments! I knew I was ugly, and did not want anyone to perjure his soul pretending they thought differently. What right had I to be small? Why wasn’t I possessed of a big aquiline nose and a tall commanding figure?” Thus I sat in burning discontent and ill-humour until soothed by the scent of roses and the gleam of soft spring sunshine which streamed in through my open window. Some of the flowerbeds in the garden were completely carpeted with pansy blossoms, all colours, and violets-blue and white, single and double. The scent of mignonette, jonquils, and narcissi filled the air. I revelled in rich perfumes, and these tempted me forth. My ruffled feelings gave way before the delights of the old garden. I collected a number of vases, and, filling them with water, set them on a table in the veranda near one of the drawing-room windows. I gathered lapfuls of the lovely blossoms, and commenced arranging them in the vases.

===Chinese===
“人家拿些没头没脑的闲话来恭维我，这不是存心作弄人吗！我明知自己丑，也不要谁来假意另作他想，糟蹋自己的灵魂发假誓。我有什么权利生得矮小？我怎么就没长一只鹰钩大鼻子、一副高高的堂堂身材？”我就这样坐着，满腔火烧般的不平与恶气，直到玫瑰的芬芳和温软的春光从敞开的窗口流泻进来，才把我抚慰下来。园子里有几畦花床，叫三色堇的花朵铺成了整片地毯，各色都有，还有紫罗兰——蓝的白的，单瓣的重瓣的。木樨草、黄水仙和水仙的香气弥漫空中。我沉醉在浓香里，被它们引诱出了门。满腹被搅乱的意绪，在老园子的种种赏心乐事面前败下阵来。我收拢了一批花瓶，灌满清水，把它们摆在外廊上紧挨客厅一扇窗的桌上，又采了满满一怀可爱的花朵，动手往瓶里插了起来。

===Original===
Part of the old Caddagat house was built of slabs, and one of the wooden walls ran along the veranda side of the drawing-room, so the songs aunt Helen and Everard Grey were trying to the piano came as a sweet accompaniment to my congenial task.

===Chinese===
卡达加特的老宅子有一部分是木板条砌的，其中一面木墙正沿着客厅外廊的一侧，因此海伦姨妈同埃弗拉德·格雷对着钢琴试唱的歌声，便成了我这件投契活计的甜美伴奏。

===Original===
Presently they left off singing and commenced talking. Under the same circumstances a heroine of a story would have slipped away; or, if that were impossible without discovery, she would have put her fingers in her ears, and would have been in a terrible state of agitation lest she should hear something not intended for her. I did not come there with a view to eavesdropping. It is a degradation to which I never stoop. I thought they were aware of my presence on the veranda; but it appears they were not, as they began to discuss me (wonderfully interesting subject to myself), and I stayed there, without one word of disapproval from my conscience, to listen to their conversation.

===Chinese===
不多时，他们停了唱，谈起话来。换作小说里的女主角，处在同样的境地，早就悄悄溜开了；若是溜不开、一溜便要暴露，她也该把手指塞进耳朵，惴惴然生怕听见一句不是说给她听的话。我到那里去，原不存偷听的念头。那是一种堕落的行径，我从不屈就。我原以为他们晓得我在外廊上；可看样子他们并不晓得，因为他们竟议论起我来（对本人说来是顶顶有趣的题目），我便待在原处，良心上没有半句责难，把这场谈话听了下去。

===Original===
“My word, didn’t gran make a to-do this morning when I proposed to train Sybylla for the stage! Do you know that girl is simply reeking with talent; I must have her trained. I will keep bringing the idea before gran until she gets used to it. I’ll work the we-should-use-the-gifts-God-has-given-us racket for all it is worth, and you might use your influence too, Helen.”

===Chinese===
“哎呀，今早我提出来要把西比拉调教成个演戏的，外婆可闹出好一场乱子！你可知道，那姑娘浑身上下都在往外冒才气；我一定得让她受些科班的训练。我要把这个念头一遍遍送到外婆跟前，直到她听惯了为止。我还要把“上帝赏的禀赋咱们就该用足”那一套鼓吹个淋漓尽致，海伦，你也替我敲敲边鼓。”

===Original===
“No, Everard; there are very few who succeed on the stage. I would not use my influence, as it is a life of which I do not approve.”

===Chinese===
“不成，埃弗拉德；戏台上成得了器的没有几个。我不肯出这个力，因为那样的营生，我不赞成。”

===Original===
“But Sybylla *would* succeed. I am a personal friend of the leading managers, and my influence would help her greatly.”

===Chinese===
“可西比拉*一定*成得了。几位当家的经理都是我的私交，我的情面于她大有裨益。”

===Original===
“Yes; but what would you do with her? A young gentleman couldn’t take charge of a girl and bring her out without ruining her reputation. There would be no end of scandal, as the sister theory would only be nonsense.”

===Chinese===
“是；可你拿她怎么办呢？一位年轻绅士领着个姑娘、带她出头露面，不把她的名声毁了才怪。闲话会没完没了，冒充兄妹那一套又全然站不住脚。”

===Original===
“There is another way; I could easily stop scandal.”

===Chinese===
“另有一条路；堵住闲话，于我易如反掌。”

===Original===
“Everard, what do you mean!”

===Chinese===
“埃弗拉德，你这是什么意思！”

===Original===
“I mean marriage,” he replied deliberately.

===Chinese===
“我是说结婚。”他一字一板地答道。

===Original===
“Surely, boy, you must be dreaming! You have only seen her for an hour or two. I don’t believe in these sudden attachments.”

===Chinese===
“孩子，你准是在说梦话！你统共才见了她一两个钟头。这种突如其来的痴恋，我是不信的。”

===Original===
Perhaps she here thought of one (her own) as sudden, which had not ended happily.

===Chinese===
或许此刻，她想起了另一段同样突然的痴恋——她自己那一段，而那一段不曾有好收场。

===Original===
“Everard, don’t do anything rashly. You know you are very fickle and considered a lady-killer﻿—be merciful to my poor little Sybylla, I pray. It is just one of your passing fancies. Don’t wile her passionate young heart away and then leave her to pine and die.”

===Chinese===
“埃弗拉德，行事千万别莽撞。你知道自己用情极是三心二意，人家又把你算作专勾女人心的人——求你发发慈悲，可怜可怜我那可怜的小西比拉吧。这不过是你一阵过眼的心血来潮。别把她那颗火热年少的心诱了去，回头又撇下她，由她憔悴至死。”

===Original===
“I don’t think she is that sort,” he replied laughingly.

===Chinese===
“我看她不是那种人。”他笑着答道。

===Original===
“No, she would not die, but would grow into a cynic and sceptic, which is the worst of fates. Let her alone. Flirt as much as you will with society belles who understand the game, but leave my country maiden alone. I hope to mould her into a splendid character yet.”

===Chinese===
“不错，她死是死不成的，她会长成一个愤世嫉俗、什么都不信的人——那才是最坏的命。由她去吧。你要调情，尽管去同那些深谙此道的交际名花们调个够，可我这位乡下闺女，你不要去招惹。我还指望把她琢磨成一块顶美的材料呢。”

===Original===
“But, Helen, supposing I am in earnest at last, you don’t think I’d make her a bad old hubby, do you?”

===Chinese===
“可是，海伦，倘若我这回总算动了真心，你总不至于觉得，我会给她当个糟糕的老丈夫吧？”

===Original===
“She is not the girl for you. You are not the man who could ever control her. What I say may not be complimentary but it is true. Besides, she is not seventeen yet, and I do not approve of romantic young girls throwing themselves into matrimony. Let them develop their womanhood first.”

===Chinese===
“她不是配你的姑娘。你也决不是驾驭得了她的男人。我这话说得不中听，却是实话。况且她还不到十七岁，浪漫的小姑娘们一股脑往婚姻里栽，这种事我不赞成。先让她们把女性的品性长成个样子再说。”

===Original===
“Then I expect I had better hide my attractions under a bushel during the remainder of my stay at Caddagat?”

===Chinese===
“那么，在我留住卡达加特的余下日子里，我顶好还是把我这一身魅力深藏不露喽？”

===Original===
“Yes. Be as nice to the child as you like, but mind, none of those little ladies’-man attentions with which it is so easy to steal﻿—”

===Chinese===
“正是。你只管对这孩子好，只是记住，那套讨女人欢心的小殷勤，一样也不许来——靠那一套，偷起人家的——”

===Original===
I waited to hear no more, but, brimming over with a mixture of emotions, tore through the garden and into the old orchard. Bees were busy, and countless bright-coloured butterflies flitted hither and thither, sipping from hundreds of trees, white or pink with bloom﻿—their beauty was lost upon me. I stood ankle-deep in violets, where they had run wild under a gnarled old apple-tree, and gave way to my wounded vanity.

===Chinese===
后头的话我再不要听，满腔翻腾着一团混杂的情绪，一路冲过园子，直奔进老果园里去。蜂儿正忙，数不清的彩蝶翩翩地飞来飞去，在几百棵开满白花或粉花的树间啜饮——这份美丽，我却全然无心领受。我站在齐踝深的紫罗兰里，那些花在一棵虬曲的老苹果树下野漫自长，我便由着我那受了伤的虚荣心，尽情发作了一场。

===Original===
“Little country maiden, indeed! There’s no need for him to bag his attractions up. If he exerted himself to the utmost of his ability, he could not make me love him. I’m not a child. I saw through him in the first hour. There’s not enough in him to win my love. I’ll show him I think no more of him than of the caterpillars on the old tree there. I’m not a booby that will fall in love with every gussie I see. Bah, there’s no fear of that! I hate and detest men!”

===Chinese===
“好一个‘乡下小姑娘’！他才犯不着把他那身魅力装进口袋。他就是把浑身的本事都使出来，也休想叫我爱上他。我不是小孩子。头一个钟头我就把他看穿了。他肚子里那点货色，还不够赢得我的爱。我要叫他知道，我看他，还不如看那棵老树上的毛毛虫。我又不是个见着一个俊俏哥儿就发昏的傻瓜蛋。呸，才没那个怕头！我恨男人，厌恶男人！”

===Original===
“I suppose you are rehearsing some more airs to show off with tonight,” sneered a voice behind me.

===Chinese===
“我猜你又在排练什么曲子，好今儿晚上卖弄吧。”我背后一个声音冷笑道。

===Original===
“No, I’m realisticing; and how *dare* you thrust your obnoxious presence before me when I wish to be alone! Haven’t I often shown﻿—”

===Chinese===
“不，我这回排练的是写实；我只想一个人待着，你怎么*敢*把你这副讨厌的嘴脸杵到我眼前！难道我没有常常给你颜色——”

===Original===
“While a girl is disengaged, any man who is her equal has the right to pay his addresses to her if he is in earnest,” interrupted Mr. Hawden. It was he who stood before me.

===Chinese===
“姑娘家还没许人的时候，凡是同她般配的男人，只要出于真心，就有权向她求爱。”霍登先生（Mr. Hawden）打断了话头。站在我面前的正是他。

===Original===
“I am well aware of that,” I replied. “But it is a woman’s privilege to repel those attentions if distasteful to her. You seem disinclined to accord me that privilege.”

===Chinese===
“这个道理我明白得很，”我答道，“可那些殷勤若是惹她厌，回拒掉它们便是女人的特权。你倒像是打定主意，不肯给我这项特权似的。”

===Original===
Having delivered this retort, I returned to the house, leaving him standing there looking the fool he was.

===Chinese===
回敬完这一句，我便回屋里去，撇下他站在那里，摆着那副本来就有的蠢相。

===Original===
I do not believe in spurning the love of a blackfellow if he behaves in a manly way; but Frank Hawden was such a drivelling mawkish style of sweetheart that I had no patience with him.

===Chinese===
一个土著黑人，只要行事有男子汉的气概，他的爱我也不赞成糟践；可弗兰克·霍登做起情人来，是那么一副淌着口水、痴痴黏黏的样子，叫我怎么也耐不住性子。

===Original===
Aunt Helen and Everard had vacated the drawing-room, so I plumped down on the piano-stool and dashed into Kowalski’s galop, from that into “Gaite de Coeur” until I made the piano dance and tremble like a thing possessed. My annoyance faded, and I slowly played that saddest of waltzes, “Weber’s Last.” I became aware of a presence in the room, and, facing about, confronted Everard Grey.

===Chinese===
海伦姨妈和埃弗拉德已经腾空了客厅，我便一屁股坐上琴凳，一头扎进科瓦尔斯基（Kowalski）的加洛普舞曲，又从它扎进《心之欢畅》（Gaite de Coeur），直弹得那架钢琴像中了邪似的又跳又抖。我的恼气渐渐消了，便慢慢弹起那支顶顶哀伤的圆舞曲《韦伯的绝笔》（Weber's Last）。我觉察出屋里多了一个人，回身一看，正撞上埃弗拉德·格雷。

===Original===
“How long have you been here?” I demanded sharply.

===Chinese===
“你在这儿待了多久了？”我厉声质问。

===Original===
“Since you began to play. Where on earth did you learn to play? Your execution is splendid. Do sing ‘Three Fishers,’ please.”

===Chinese===
“打你一开始弹琴起。你到底是在哪儿学的琴？你的指法漂亮极了。请唱一支《三个渔夫》（'Three Fishers'）吧。”

===Original===
“Excuse me; I haven’t time now. Besides I am not competent to sing to you,” I said brusquely, and made my exit.

===Chinese===
“对不起；这会儿我没工夫。再说，我也没资格唱给你听。”我粗声粗气地说完，便退了场。

===Original===
“Mr. Hawden wants you, Sybylla,” called aunt Helen. “See what he wants and let him get away to his work, or your grannie will be vexed to see him loitering about all the morning.”

===Chinese===
“西比拉，霍登先生找你呢。”海伦姨妈喊道。“去瞧瞧他有什么事，打发他干活去，不然叫他一早上闲荡来闲荡去，你外婆看了要恼的。”

===Original===
“Miss Sybylla,” he began, when we were left alone, “I want to apologize to you. I had no right to plague you, but it all comes of the way I love you. A fellow gets jealous at the least little thing, you know.”

===Chinese===
“西比拉小姐，”等只剩下我们两人，他开口道，“我想向你赔个不是。我不该纠缠你，可这全是我爱你的那个爱法闹出来的。男人家鸡毛蒜皮的小事也要吃醋，你是知道的。”

===Original===
“Bore me with no more such trash,” I said, turning away in disgust.

===Chinese===
“这类废话，少来烦我。”我说着，厌恶地扭过脸去。

===Original===
“But, Miss Sybylla, what am I to do with it?”

===Chinese===
“可是，西比拉小姐，我拿它怎么办呢？”

===Original===
“Do with what?”

===Chinese===
“拿什么怎么办？”

===Original===
“My love.”

===Chinese===
“我的爱情。”

===Original===
“Love!” I retorted scornfully. “There is no such thing.”

===Chinese===
“爱情！”我轻蔑地回敬，“天底下压根儿没这个东西。”

===Original===
“But there is, and I have found it.”

===Chinese===
“可它有，我就寻着了。”

===Original===
“Well, you stick to it﻿—that’s my advice to you. It will be a treasure. If you send it to my father he will get it bottled up and put it in the Goulburn museum. He has sent several things there already.”

===Chinese===
“那你就守牢它吧——这是我对你的忠告。它会是个宝贝。你把它寄给我父亲，他会叫人装进瓶里，摆进古尔本（Goulburn）的博物馆去。他已经往那儿送过好几样东西了。”

===Original===
“Don’t make such a game of a poor devil. You know I can’t do that.”

===Chinese===
“别这么作弄一个可怜虫。你知道我办不到。”

===Original===
“Bag it up, then; put a big stone to make it sink, and pitch it in the river.”

===Chinese===
“那就把它装进口袋，拴块大石头叫它沉底，扔进河里去吧。”

===Original===
“You’ll rue this,” he said savagely.

===Chinese===
“你会后悔的。”他恶狠狠地说。

===Original===
“I may or may not,” I sang over my shoulder as I departed.

===Chinese===
“也许会，也许不会。”我一面走开，一面像唱歌似的从肩头抛回这么一句。

## XII One Grand Passion / 第12章 一段刻骨铭心的情

===Original===
I had not the opportunity of any more private interviews with Everard Grey till one morning near his departure, when we happened to be alone on the veranda.

===Chinese===
此后我一直无缘再同埃弗拉德·格雷（Everard Grey）单独交谈，直到他临行前的一个早晨，我们恰好在游廊上单独相对。

===Original===
“Well, Miss Sybylla,” he began, “when I arrived I thought you and I would have been great friends; but we have not progressed at all. How do you account for that?”

===Chinese===
“这么说吧，西比拉小姐，”他开口道，“我刚到的时候，还以为你我会结成要好的朋友呢；可咱们一点儿也没有进展。你说说，这该怎么解释？”

===Original===
As he spoke he laid his slender shapely hand kindly upon my head. He was very handsome and winning, and moved in literary, musical, and artistic society﻿—a man from my world, a world away.

===Chinese===
他说话间，把一只修长漂亮的手亲切地按在我头顶。他生得十分俊美动人，出入于文学、音乐与艺术的社交圈子——一个来自我的世界的人，却又隔着一整个世界。

===Original===
Oh, what pleasure I might have derived from companionship with him! I bit my lip to keep back the tears. Why did not social arrangements allow a man and a maid to be chums﻿—chums as two men or two maids may be to each other, enjoying each other without thought beyond pure platonic friendship? But no; it could not be. I understood the conceit of men. Should I be very affable, I feared Everard Grey would imagine he had made a conquest of me. On the other hand, were I glum he would think the same, and that I was trying to hide my feelings behind a mask of brusquerie. I therefore steered in a beeline between the two manners, and remarked with the greatest of indifference:

===Chinese===
唉，同他相伴，我本可以领受多少欢愉啊！我咬住嘴唇，才把眼泪逼了回去。为什么世道的安排，就不容一男一女做知己——像两个男人或两个女人之间那样的知己，彼此受用，除了纯粹的柏拉图式友谊再无半点旁的念头？可是不行；这是办不到的。男人的那份自以为是，我深有领教。我若十分亲热，恐怕埃弗拉德·格雷便要以为自己把我俘获了；我若闷闷不乐，他又会作同样的想法，只当我是在一副粗率的面具后头遮掩自己的心事。于是我在这两种态度之间笔直地取了个中道，以十二万分的冷淡说道：

===Original===
“I was not aware that you expected us to be such cronies﻿—in fact, I have never given the matter a thought.”

===Chinese===
“我可不晓得，你竟指望我们好成这样的密友——说实话，这档子事我从来想都没想过。”

===Original===
He turned away in a piqued style. Such a beau of beaux, no doubt he was annoyed that an insignificant little country bumpkin should not be flattered by his patronage, or probably he thought me rude or ill-humoured.

===Chinese===
他赌着气把脸扭开了。这样一位花花公子中的花花公子，眼见一个不起眼的乡下小土包子竟不受宠若惊于他的垂青，无疑是要恼的；再不然，就是觉得我粗鲁，或是脾气坏。

===Original===
Two mornings later uncle Jay-Jay took him to Gool-Gool *en route* for Sydney. When departing he bade me a kindly goodbye, made me promise to write to him, and announced his intention of obtaining the opinion of some good masters re my dramatic talent and voice, when I came to Sydney as promised by my grandmother. I stood on the garden fence waving my handkerchief until the buggy passed out of sight among the messmate-trees about half a mile from the house.

===Chinese===
两天后的早晨，杰杰舅舅（uncle Jay-Jay）送他去古尔古尔（Gool-Gool），再*取道*那里动身前往悉尼。临别时他向我殷殷道别，要我答应给他写信，还宣布说，待我依外婆许下的诺言去悉尼时，他要去请几位好老师，就我的戏剧才华和嗓音品评一番。我站在花园的栅栏上挥动手绢，直到那辆轻便马车在半英里外屋子附近那片斜叶桉（messmate）树林里走得没了影儿。

===Original===
“Well I hope, as that dandified ape has gone﻿—and good riddance to him﻿—that you will pay more heed to my attentions now,” said Mr. Hawden’s voice, as I was in the act of descending from the fence.

===Chinese===
“好哇，如今那只衣冠楚楚的猿猴走了——走了才干净——我希望往后你能多把我这份殷勤当回事了。”我正从栅栏上往下跳的当口，霍登先生（Mr. Hawden）的声音开了腔。

===Original===
“What do you mean by your attentions?” I demanded.

===Chinese===
“你说你的殷勤，是什么意思？”我诘问道。

===Original===
“What do I mean! That is something like coming to business. I’ll soon explain. You know what my intentions are very well. When I am twenty-four, I will come into my property in England. It is considerable, and at the end of that time I want to marry you and take you home. By Jove! I would just like to take you home. You’d surprise some English girls I know.”

===Chinese===
“什么意思！这才像谈正事的架势。我不久就讲明白。我的心思你是再清楚不过的。等我满了二十四岁，就能继承我在英国的财产。数目很可观，到那时我要娶你，带你回英国老家去。老天作证！我就是想把你带回老家去。我认得几个英国姑娘，见了你保管要大吃一惊。”

===Original===
“There would be more than one person surprised if I married you,” I thought to myself, and laughed till I ached with the motion.

===Chinese===
“要是我嫁给了你，吃惊的恐怕就不止一个人了。”我心里暗想，笑得浑身都颠疼了。

===Original===
“You infernal little vixen! What are you laughing at? You’ve got no more sense than a bat if such a solemn thing only provokes your mirth.”

===Chinese===
“你这个可恶的小泼妇！你笑什么？这么正经的大事只逗得你发笑，你的见识还赶不上一只蝙蝠。”

===Original===
“Solemn﻿—why, it’s a screaming farce!” I laughed more and more.

===Chinese===
“正经？——嘿，这分明是出笑死人的闹剧！”我越笑越厉害。

===Original===
“What’s a farce?” he demanded fiercely.

===Chinese===
“什么叫闹剧？”他气势汹汹地质问。

===Original===
“The bare idea of you proposing to me.”

===Chinese===
“你居然想向我求婚——光这么一个念头，就是闹剧。”

===Original===
“Why? Have I not as much right to propose as any other man?”

===Chinese===
“为什么？难道我没有权利求婚，别的男人倒有？”

===Original===
“Man!” I laughed. “That’s where the absurdity arises. My child, if you were a man, certainly you could propose, but do you think I’d look at a boy, a child! If ever I perpetrate matrimony the participant in my degradation will be a fully developed man﻿—not a hobbledehoy who falls in love, as he terms it, on an average about twice a week. Love! Ho!”

===Chinese===
“男人！”我笑出声来。“荒唐就荒唐在这里。我的孩子，你要是个男人，自然尽可以求婚；可你以为我会看上一个男孩儿、一个娃娃吗！我倘若当真犯下一桩婚事，陪我一同堕落的那一位，也必得是个十足长成的男人——而不是那种照他自己的说法、平均每个礼拜都要‘坠入爱河’约莫两回的半大小子。爱！哼！”

===Original===
I moved in the direction of the house. He barred my path.

===Chinese===
我朝屋子走去。他拦住了我的去路。

===Original===
“You are not going to escape me like that, my fine lady. I will make you listen to me this time or you will hear more about it,” and he seized me angrily by the wrist.

===Chinese===
“你别想就这么甩开我，我的好小姐。这一回我非叫你听我说完不可，不然还有你好看的。”说着，他怒气冲冲一把攥住了我的手腕。

===Original===
I cannot bear the touch of anyone﻿—it is one of my idiosyncrasies. With my disengaged hand I struck him a vigorous blow on the nose, and wrenching myself free sprang away, saying, “How dare you lay a finger on me! If you attempt such a thing again I’ll make short work of you. Mark my words, or you’ll get something more than a bleeding nose next time, I promise you.”

===Chinese===
我天生受不了任何人碰我——这是我的一大怪癖。我便用那只空着的手，照他的鼻子结结实实挥了一拳，趁势挣脱开，跳到一旁，说：“你怎么敢碰我一指头！你再敢来这么一回，我叫你吃不了兜着走。记住我的话，不然下回就不止流点鼻血了，我这话可是说到做到。”

===Original===
“You’ll hear more of this! You’ll hear more of this! You fierce, wild, touch-me-not thing,” he roared.

===Chinese===
“这事没完！这事没完！你这个凶巴巴、野乎乎、碰不得的玩意儿。”他吼道。

===Original===
“Yes; my motto with men is touch-me-not, and it is your own fault if I’m fierce. If children attempt to act the role of a man with adult tools, they are sure to cut themselves. Hold hard a bit, honey, till your whiskers grow,” I retorted as I departed, taking flying leaps over the blossom-burdened flowerbeds.

===Chinese===
“没错；我对男人的座右铭就是‘碰不得’，我凶，也是你自己招的。小孩子拿着大人的家伙去充汉子，保准要割破自己的手。且稍稍稳一稳吧，小甜心，等你的络腮胡子长出来再谈。”我一边离去一边回敬，飞身跃过一畦畦开得正满的花坛。

===Original===
At tea that night, after gazing interestedly at Mr. Hawden’s nose for some time, uncle Julius inquired, “in the name of all that’s mysterious, what the devil have you been doing to your nose? You look as though you had been on the spree.”

===Chinese===
那天晚上用茶点的时候，朱利叶斯舅舅（uncle Julius）饶有兴味地把霍登先生的鼻子端详了半晌，问道：“看在一切神秘事物的份上，你的鼻子到底出了什么鬼？瞧你这副模样，倒像刚纵酒胡闹了一场。”

===Original===
I was quaking lest he would get me into a fine scrape, but he only muttered, “By Jove!” with great energy, and glowered menacingly across the table at me.

===Chinese===
我正战战兢兢，生怕他会把我拖进一场大麻烦，谁知他只狠狠地咕哝出一声“老天作证”，便隔着桌子威吓地朝我瞪眼。

===Original===
After tea he requested an interview with grannie, which aroused my curiosity greatly. I was destined to hear all about it next morning. When breakfast was over grannie called me into her room and interviewed me about Mr. Hawden’s interview. She began without any preliminaries:

===Chinese===
茶点之后，他请求同外婆作一次会谈，这大大勾起了我的好奇心。命里注定，第二天早晨我就要听到事情的原委。早餐一过，外婆便把我叫进她的房里，就霍登先生的会谈对我进行了一场会谈。她开门见山地讲起来：

===Original===
“Mr. Hawden has complained of your conduct. It grieves me that any young man should have to speak to me of the behaviour of my own granddaughter. He says you have been flirting with him. Sybylla, I scarcely thought you would be so immodest and unwomanly.”

===Chinese===
“霍登先生告了你的状。一个青年男子，竟然不得不对我说起我自己外孙女的行径，这叫我心里难受。他说你一直在同他调情。西比拉，我简直想不到，你会这样不检点、这样没有姑娘家的体统。”

===Original===
On hearing this my thoughts of Frank Hawden were the reverse of flattering. He had persecuted me beyond measure, yet I had not deigned to complain of him to either uncle, grannie, or auntie, as I might reasonably have done, and have obtained immediate redress. He had been the one to blame in the case, yet for the rebuffs he had brought upon himself, went tattling to my grandmother.

===Chinese===
听了这话，我对弗兰克·霍登（Frank Hawden）的种种想法，绝无半句好话。他把我纠缠得没了边际，我却从不屑于向舅舅、外婆或姨妈告他一状——这本是我满可以做的，还能立时讨个公道。这桩事里错处分明在他，可他挨了自己招来的碰壁，倒跑去我外婆跟前搬弄口舌。

===Original===
“Is that all you have to say, grannie?”

===Chinese===
“外婆，你要说的就这些吗？”

===Original===
“No. He wants to marry you, and has asked my consent. I told him it all rested with yourself and parents. What do you say?”

===Chinese===
“不止。他想娶你，已经求过我的应允。我告诉他，这事全在你自己和你父母。你怎么说？”

===Original===
“Say,” I exclaimed, “grannie, you are only joking, are you not?”

===Chinese===
“怎么说！”我叫嚷起来，“外婆，你这是在讲笑话吧，是不是？”

===Original===
“No, my child, this is not a matter to joke about.”

===Chinese===
“不是，孩子，这可不是拿来开玩笑的事。”

===Original===
“Marry that creature! A boy!” I uttered in consternation.

===Chinese===
“嫁给那个东西！一个毛孩子！”我惊惶得失声嚷了出来。

===Original===
“He is no boy. He has attained his majority some months. He is as old as your grandfather was when we married. In three years you will be almost twenty, and by that time he will be in possession of his property which is very good﻿—in fact, he will be quite rich. If you care for him there is nothing against him as I can see. He is healthy, has a good character, and comes of a high family. Being a bit wild won’t matter. Very often, after they sow their wild oats, some of those scampy young fellows settle down and marry a nice young girl and turn out very good husbands.”

===Chinese===
“他才不是毛孩子。他成年已经好几个月了。他的年纪，同你外祖父当年娶我时一般大。再过三年你就快二十岁了，到那时他也继承到手他的财产，那份财产很不坏——说实在的，他届时相当有钱。你若中意他，依我看他没有什么可挑剔的。他身子结实，品行端正，又出身好门第。稍微野一点不打紧。那些轻狂的小伙子，往往撒过一阵野之后便安顿下来，娶上个好姑娘，结果还成了顶好的丈夫。”

===Original===
“It is disgusting, and you ought to be downright ashamed of yourself, grannie! A man can live a life of bestiality and then be considered a fit husband for the youngest and purest girl! It is shameful! Frank Hawden is not wild, he hasn’t got enough in him to be so. I hate him. No, he hasn’t enough in him to hate. I loathe and despise him. I would not marry him or anyone like him though he were King of England. The idea of marriage even with the best man in the world seems to me a lowering thing,” I raged; “but with him it would be pollution﻿—the lowest degradation that could be heaped upon me! I will never come down to marry anyone﻿—” here I fell a victim to a flood of excited tears.

===Chinese===
“这真叫人恶心，外婆，你实在该为自己害臊！一个男人尽可以过上一辈子禽兽般的生活，到头来倒被看作配娶最年轻、最纯洁的姑娘！真是无耻！弗兰克·霍登谈不上野不野，他肚子里那点货色连野都不够格。我恨他。——不，他那点货色连叫人恨都不够格。我是又嫌恶、又鄙夷他。莫说嫁他，凡是像他这样的人，哪怕他是英国国王，我也不嫁。就连嫁给世上顶好的男人，依我看，结婚这个念头本身就是一桩自贬的事，”我大发雷霆道，“而嫁给他，那更是玷污——是能堆到我头上来最下贱的糟践！我永远也不会降格去嫁任何人——”说到这里，我败给了一阵激动的泪水。

===Original===
I felt there was no good in the world, especially in men﻿—the hateful creatures!﻿—and never would be while it was not expected of them, even by rigidly pure, true Christians such as my grandmother. Grannie, dear old grannie, thought I should marry any man who, from a financial point of view, was a good match for me. That is where the sting came in. No, I would never marry. I would procure some occupation in which I could tread my life out, independent of the degradation of marriage.

===Chinese===
我觉得这世上没有善良，尤其在男人身上没有——这帮可恨的东西！——而且只要连我外婆这般严守贞洁、真诚虔诚的基督徒也不指望他们为善，善良就永远不会有。外婆，亲爱的老外婆，竟以为凡是从经济眼光看同我般配的男人，我就都该嫁。扎人的痛处正在这里。不，我永远不结婚。我要谋一份职业，把我的生命在其中一步一步走完，不沾婚姻那场糟践的边。

===Original===
“Dear me, child,” said grannie, concernedly, “there is no need to distress yourself so. I remember you were always fearfully passionate. When I had you with me as a tiny toddler, you would fret a whole day about a thing an ordinary child would forget inside an hour. I will tell Hawden to go about his business. I would not want you to consider marriage for an instant with anyone distasteful to you. But tell me truly, have you ever flirted with him? I will take your word, for I thank God you have never yet told me a falsehood!”

===Chinese===
“哎呀，孩子，”外婆关切地说，“犯不着这样折磨自己。我记得你从小就是一副火爆得吓人的性子。你小小娃娃跟着我那阵子，寻常孩子一个钟头就会忘掉的事，你能为它烦上一整天。我会打发霍登去忙他自己的事。凡是你讨厌的人，我绝不愿你有半瞬想到同他结婚。可你老老实实告诉我：你同他调过情吗？我信你的话，因为感谢上帝，你还从没有对我说过一句谎！”

===Original===
“Grannie,” I exclaimed emphatically, “I have discouraged him all I could. I would scorn to flirt with any man.”

===Chinese===
“外婆，”我斩钉截铁地说，“我一向是能多冷落他就多冷落他。要我同任何男人调情，我是不屑的。”

===Original===
“Well, well, that is all I want to hear about it. Wash your eyes, and we will get our horses and go over to see Mrs. Hickey and her baby, and take her something good to eat.”

===Chinese===
“好了，好了，这事我要听的就是这些。去洗洗你的眼睛，咱们牵上马，去看看希基太太（Mrs. Hickey）和她的娃娃，再给她带点好吃的去。”

===Original===
I did not encounter Frank Hawden again till the afternoon, when he leered at me in a very triumphant manner. I stiffened myself and drew out of his way as though he had been some vile animal. At this treatment he whined, so I agreed to talk the matter over with him and have done with it once and for all.

===Chinese===
直到下午我才又撞见弗兰克·霍登，他一副得胜的架势，斜着眼瞟我。我挺直了身子，侧身避开他，仿佛他是什么下贱的畜生。受了这样的对待，他哼哼唧唧地抱怨起来，我便答应同他把这事谈开，一劳永逸地做个了断。

===Original===
He was on his way to water some dogs, so I accompanied him out to the stables near the kennels, to be out of hearing of the household.

===Chinese===
他正要去给狗添水，我便陪他走到狗舍近旁的马厩那里，免得被家里人听见。

===Original===
I opened fire without any beating about the bush.

===Chinese===
我不兜一点圈子，开门见山就开了火。

===Original===
“I ask you, Mr. Hawden, if you have any sense of manliness, from this hour to cease persecuting me with your idiotic professions of love. I have two sentiments regarding it, and in either you disgust me. Sometimes I don’t believe there is such a thing as love at all﻿—that is, love between men and women. While in this frame of mind I would not listen to professions of love from an angel. Other times I believe in love, and look upon it as a sacred and solemn thing. When in that humour, it seems to me a desecration to hear you twaddling about the holy theme, for you are only a boy, and don’t know how to feel. I would not have spoken thus harshly to you, but by your unmanly conduct you have brought it upon yourself. I have told you straight all that I will ever deign to tell you on the subject, and take much pleasure in wishing you good afternoon.”

===Chinese===
“我问你，霍登先生，你若还有半分男子气的自觉，就请从此时此刻起，别再用你那些愚蠢的爱情表白来纠缠我。关于爱情，我怀有两种心情，无论哪一种底下，你都叫我恶心。有时候，我压根儿不信世上有爱这么一回事——我是说，男女之间的爱。处在这种心境里时，哪怕是天使向我表白爱情，我也不听。另一些时候，我相信爱，把它看作神圣而庄严的东西。每逢那种心绪，听你絮絮叨叨地嚼这个神圣的题目，在我看来便是亵渎——因为你不过是个男孩儿，还不懂得怎样去感受。我本不打算对你讲得这么狠，是你那副没有男子气的行径自己招来的。关于这个题目，我甘愿对你说的，都已直统统说尽了；并且我怀着莫大的愉快，祝你午安。”

===Original===
I walked away quickly, heedless of his expostulations.

===Chinese===
我快步走开，全不理会他的分辩。

===Original===
My appeal to his manliness had no effect. Did I go for a ride, or a walk in the afternoon to enjoy the glory of the sunset, or a stroll to drink in the pleasures of the old garden, there would I find Frank Hawden by my side, yah, yah, yahing about the way I treated him, until I wished him at the bottom of the Red Sea.

===Chinese===
我对他男子气概的呼吁毫无效用。不论我是骑马出去遛一圈，还是午后散步去看落日的辉煌，或是到老花园里信步走走，饱吸那里的种种妙趣，弗兰克·霍登总会出现在我身旁，呸呀、呸呀、呸呀地数落我待他的样子，直数落得我恨不得他沉到红海底下去。

===Original===
However, in those glorious spring days the sense of life was too pleasant to be much clouded by the trifling annoyance Frank Hawden occasioned me. The graceful wild clematis festooned the shrubbery along the creeks with great wreaths of magnificent white bloom, which loaded every breeze with perfume; the pretty bright green senna shrubs along the riverbanks were decked in blossoms which rivalled the deep blue of the sky in brilliance; the magpies built their nests in the tall gum-trees, and savagely attacked unwary travellers who ventured too near their domain; the horses were rolling fat, and invited one to get on their satin backs and have a gallop; the cry of the leather-heads was heard in the orchard as the cherry season approached. Oh, it was good to be alive!

===Chinese===
不过，在那光明灿烂的春日里，生命的意趣实在太美满，弗兰克·霍登给我招惹的这点小小烦恼，遮不成多大的阴翳。体态轻盈的野铁线莲，沿着溪畔的灌木丛，垂挂下一挂挂白花织成的华丽花环，把每一缕微风都熏满了芬芳；河岸上那些鲜绿可爱的决明灌木缀满了花朵，明艳直可与深蓝的天空争辉；喜鹊在高高的桉树上筑巢，凶悍地袭击那些不当心闯近它们地界的行人；马匹滚圆膘肥，招引着人跨上它们缎子般光溜的脊背去驰骋一番；樱桃时节将近，果园里响起了皮头鸟（leather-heads）的啼声。啊，活着真好！

===Original===
At Caddagat I was as much out of the full flood of life for which I craved as at Possum Gully, but here there were sufficient pleasant little ripples on the stream of existence to act as a stopgap for the present.

===Chinese===
在卡达加特（Caddagat），我同在袋熊谷（Possum Gully）一样，都置身于我所渴求的那股奔涌的生活洪流之外；只是在这里，生存之流上泛起的小小涟漪，愉快的已足够多，眼下足可权作填补。

## XIII He / 第13章 他

===Original===
Here goes for a full account of my first, my last, my only *real* sweetheart, for I considered the professions of that pestiferous jackeroo as merely a grotesque caricature on the genuine article.

===Chinese===
这就来原原本本讲一讲我的头一位、末一位、也是唯一*真正*的心上人——至于那个惹人嫌的牧场学徒的种种表白，我只当是给真货画的一幅荒诞漫画罢了。

===Original===
On making my first appearance before my lover, I looked quite the reverse of a heroine. My lovely hair was not conveniently escaping from the comb at the right moment to catch him hard in the eye, neither was my thrillingly low sweet voice floating out on the scented air in a manner which went straight to his heart, like the girls I had read of. On the contrary, I much resembled a female clown. It was on a day towards the end of September, and I had been up the creek making a collection of ferns. I had on a pair of men’s boots with which to walk in the water, and was garbed in a most dilapidated old dress, which I had borrowed from one of the servants for the purpose. A pair of gloves made of basil, and a big hat, much torn in struggling through the undergrowth, completed my makeup. My hair was most unbecomingly screwed up, the short ends sticking out like a hurrah’s nest.

===Chinese===
头一回在我的情人跟前露面时，我的模样同小说里的女主人公恰恰相反。我那可爱的头发，并没有在恰好的当口从梳齿间松散垂落，正正迷住他的眼；我那低回甜润、动人心魄的嗓音，也没有袅袅飘散在芬芳的空气里、径直往他心坎里去，像我读到过的那些姑娘们一样。恰恰相反，我活脱脱是个女丑角。那是临近九月末的一天，我刚上溪上游采了一趟蕨草。脚上蹬着一双男人的靴子，好在水里蹚；身上套一条破旧得不成样子的裙子，是特意向一个女仆借来充数的；一双羊皮手套，再添一顶在灌木丛里挤打得稀烂的大帽子，便凑齐了我这副行头。我的头发拧得再难看不过，短发楂子四下里支棱着，活像个乱蓬蓬的鸟窝。

===Original===
It was late in the day when, returning from my ramble, I was met on the doorstep by aunt Helen.

===Chinese===
那天我闲逛归来，天色已晚，在门口台阶上迎面撞见海伦姨妈。

===Original===
“While you are in that trim, I wish you would pluck some lemons for me. I’m sure there is no danger of you ruining your turnout. A sketch of you would make a good item for the *Bulletin*,” she said.

===Chinese===
“趁你这身打扮，倒要请你替我摘些柠檬来。保管不愁你把衣装糟蹋了。给你画张速写，管保是《公报》（*Bulletin*）上一条好材料，”她说。

===Original===
I went readily to do her bidding, and fetching a ladder with rungs about two feet six apart, placed it against a lemon-tree at the back of the house, and climbed up.

===Chinese===
我痛痛快快地去照办，搬来一架梯级间隔约莫两英尺六的梯子，搭在屋后一棵柠檬树上，爬了上去。

===Original===
Holding a number of lemons in my skirt, I was making a most ungraceful descent, when I heard an unknown footstep approaching towards my back.

===Chinese===
我裙兜里兜着好些柠檬，正极不体面地往下爬，忽然听见一个陌生的脚步声朝我背后走来。

===Original===
People came to Caddagat at all hours of the day, so I was not in the least disconcerted. Only a tramp, an agent, or a hawker, I bet, I thought, as I reached my big boot down for another rung of the ladder without turning my head to see whom it might be.

===Chinese===
卡达加特一天里什么钟点都有人来，所以我一点儿也不着慌。八成不过是个流浪汉、跑买卖的经纪人，或是挑担的货郎，我打赌，心里这样估摸着，一面把穿大靴子的那只脚往下探又一格梯级，头也不回，看都不看来的是谁。

===Original===
A pair of strong brown hands encircled my waist, I was tossed up a foot or so and then deposited lightly on the ground, a masculine voice saying, “You’re a mighty well-shaped young filly﻿—‘a waist rather small, but a quarter superb.’ ”

===Chinese===
两条结实的棕色胳膊一把搂住我的腰，我被向上颠起一英尺来高，又稳稳轻轻落在地上，一个男人的嗓音说道：“你真是匹身段顶呱呱的小母马——‘腰身略嫌细窄，后躯可是没得挑。’”

===Original===
“How dare anyone speak to me like that,” I thought, as I faced about to see who was parodying Gordon. There stood a man I had never before set eyes on, smiling mischievously at me. He was a young man﻿—a very young man, a bushman tremendously tall and big and sunburnt, with an open pleasant face and chestnut moustache﻿—not at all an awe-inspiring fellow, in spite of his unusual, though well-proportioned and carried, height. I knew it must be Harold Beecham, of Five-Bob Downs, as I had heard he stood six feet three and a half in his socks.

===Chinese===
“怎么竟有人敢这样同我说话，”我心里想着，一面转过身去，看是谁在拿戈登打趣。眼前站着一个我从未见过面的男人，正朝我调皮地笑。他是个年轻人——一个非常年轻的人，一个丛林汉，高大魁梧，晒得黢黑，眉眼开朗讨喜，蓄一部栗色小胡子——虽说身量高得出奇，却生得匀称、挺拔，全无慑人的派头。我断定这准是五博丘的哈罗德·比彻姆，因为听说他穿着袜子量身量，也有六英尺三英寸半。

===Original===
I hurriedly let down my dress, the lemons rolling in a dozen directions, and turned to flee, but that well-formed figure bounded before me with the agility of a cat and barred my way.

===Chinese===
我慌忙放下裙摆，柠檬滚得四面八方都是，我转身要溜，可那副匀称的身架已像猫儿一般敏捷地蹿到我前头，拦住了我的去路。

===Original===
“Now, not a step do you go, my fine young blood, until you pick up every jolly lemon and put them away tidily, or I’ll tell the missus on you as sure as eggs.”

===Chinese===
“听好，一步也别想走，我精神的小家伙，先把你这些宝贝柠檬一只只都捡起来，规规矩矩收放好，不然我板上钉钉要上太太跟前告你的状。”

===Original===
It dawned on me that he had mistaken me for one of the servant-girls. That wasn’t bad fun. I determined not to undeceive but to have a lark with him. I summed him up as conceited, but not with the disgusting conceit with which some are afflicted, or perhaps blessed. It was rather an air of I-have-always-got-what-I-desire-and-believe,-if-people-fail-it-is-all-their-own-fault, which surrounded him.

===Chinese===
我这才恍然醒悟，他是把我错当成女仆里的一个了。这倒不坏，有得乐了。我打定主意不点破，偏要同他耍一场。我把他掂量为一个自负的人，但不是有些人害着——或者说享着——的那种叫人作呕的自负；他周身笼着的，毋宁是一股“凡我要的从来必得，旁人若不成，那全是他们自己的不是”的气派。

===Original===
“If you please, sir,” I said humbly, “I’ve gathered them all up, will you let me go now.”

===Chinese===
“求求您，先生，”我低眉顺眼地说，“我全捡好了，这会儿您总肯放我走了吧。”

===Original===
“Yes, when you’ve given me a kiss.”

===Chinese===
“肯，等你亲了我一口。”

===Original===
“Oh, sir, I couldn’t do that!”

===Chinese===
“哎呀先生，那个使不得！”

===Original===
“Go on, I won’t poison you. Come now, I’ll make you.”

===Chinese===
“去，我又不会毒死你。来吧，我可要动手了。”

===Original===
“Oh, the missus might catch me.”

===Chinese===
“哎呀，要是让太太撞见了呢。”

===Original===
“No jolly fear; I’ll take all the blame if she does.”

===Chinese===
“怕什么；真撞见了，罪责我全担。”

===Original===
“Oh don’t, sir; let me go, please,” I said in such unfeigned distress, for I feared he was going to execute his threat, that he laughed and said:

===Chinese===
“别这样，先生；放我走吧，求您了，”我说得那样真切的惶急，生怕他当真要兑现那番恐吓，倒逗得他笑起来，说道：

===Original===
“Don’t be frightened, sissy, I never kiss girls, and I’m not going to start at this time of day, and against their will to boot. You haven’t been long here, have you? I haven’t seen you before. Stand out there till I see if you’ve got any grit in you, and then I am done with you.”

===Chinese===
“别怕，小丫头，我从不亲姑娘，这把年纪断不至于开这个头，更不会强人所难。你来了没多久吧？我从前没见过你。站到那边去，让我瞧瞧你有没有点胆量，瞧完我就放你完事。”

===Original===
I stood in the middle of the yard, the spot he indicated, while he uncurled his long heavy stock-whip with its big lash and scented myall handle. He cracked it round and round my head and arms, but I did not feel the least afraid, as I saw at a glance that he was exceedingly dexterous in the bushman’s art of handling a stock-whip, and knew, if I kept perfectly still, I was quite safe. It was thanks to uncle Jay-Jay that I was able to bear the operation with unruffled equanimity, as he was in the habit of testing my nerves in this way.

===Chinese===
我站到院子中央他指的那处，他则展开那杆又长又沉的牧鞭——鞭梢粗大，柄是散发幽香的相思木（myall）柄。他把鞭子绕着我的头和胳膊噼噼啪啪地抽响，我却没有半分害怕，因为我一眼便看出，牧人驾驭牧鞭这门功夫，他使得精熟之至；我也明白，只要我纹丝不动，就保管无虞。多亏杰杰舅舅，我才受得住这场操练而面不改色——他就惯用这一手来考验我的神经。

===Original===
“Well, I never! Not so much as blinked an eyelash! Thoroughbred!” He said after a minute or so, “Where’s the boss?”

===Chinese===
“哎哟，真是头一回见！连眼睫毛都没眨一根！好一匹纯种马！”约莫过了一分钟，他说道，“老板在哪儿？”

===Original===
“In Gool-Gool. He won’t be home till late.”

===Chinese===
“在古尔古尔。他要到很晚才回来。”

===Original===
“Is Mrs. Bossier in?”

===Chinese===
“博西耶太太在家吗？”

===Original===
“No, she’s not, but Mrs. Bell is somewhere around in front.”

===Chinese===
“不在，不过贝尔太太大概在屋子前头什么地方。”

===Original===
“Thanks.”

===Chinese===
“多谢。”

===Original===
I watched him as he walked away with an easy swinging stride, which spoke of many long, long days in the saddle. I felt certain as I watched him that he had quite forgotten the incident of the little girl with the lemons.

===Chinese===
我望着他迈着从容摇摆的大步走远，那步态诉说着许许多多漫长漫长的鞍上时日。我一面望着他，一面笃定地想，柠檬小丫头那段过节，他早忘得干干净净了。

---

===Original===
“Sybylla, hurry up and get dressed. Put on your best bib and tucker, and I will leave Harry Beecham in your charge, as I want to superintend the making of some of the dishes myself this evening.”

===Chinese===
“西比拉，快去打扮起来。穿上你最体面的行头，我要把哈里·比彻姆交给你照应，今晚有几样菜，我要亲自督着做呢。”

===Original===
“It’s too early to put on my evening dress, isn’t it, auntie?”

===Chinese===
“这会儿就换晚礼服，未免太早了点吧，姨妈？”

===Original===
“It is rather early; but you can’t spare time to change twice. Dress yourself completely; you don’t know what minute your uncle and his worship will arrive.”

===Chinese===
“是早了些；可你匀不出工夫换两回衣裳。索性一水儿穿戴齐全；说不定哪一分钟你舅舅和法官大人就到了。”

===Original===
I had taken a dip in the creek, so had not to bathe, and it took me but a short time to don full war-paint﻿—blue evening dress, satin slippers, and all. I wore my hair flowing, simply tied with a ribbon. I slipped out into the passage and called aunt Helen. She came.

===Chinese===
我先在小溪里泡过了一趟，便用不着再洗浴，不多时就把这副全套“战妆”上了身——蓝色晚礼服、缎子便鞋，一应俱全。我披散着头发，只拿一根缎带束住，悄悄溜到过道里，喊海伦姨妈。她来了。

===Original===
“I’m ready, auntie. Where is he?”

===Chinese===
“我预备好了，姨妈。他在哪儿？”

===Original===
“In the dining-room.”

===Chinese===
“在饭厅里。”

===Original===
“Come into the drawing-room and call him. I will take charge of him till you are at leisure. But, auntie, it will be a long time till dinner﻿—how on earth will I manage him?”

===Chinese===
“你到客厅去把他请出来吧。等你得空之前，先归我照管。可是姨妈，离吃饭还早着一大截呢——我到底该怎么打发他呀？”

===Original===
“Manage him!” she laughed; “he is not at all an obstreperous character.”

===Chinese===
“打发他！”她笑道；“他可不是个闹腾的角色。”

===Original===
We had reached the drawing-room by this, and I looked at myself in the looking-glass while aunt Helen went to summon Harold Augustus Beecham, bachelor, owner of Five-Bob Downs, Wyambeet, Wallerawang West, Quat-Quatta, and a couple more stations in New South Wales, besides an extensive one in Queensland.

===Chinese===
这时我们已走进客厅，我对着穿衣镜照了照自己，海伦姨妈则去请哈罗德·奥古斯都·比彻姆——单身汉，名下有新南威尔士州的五博丘、怀安比特、西瓦勒朗（Wallerawang West）、夸特夸塔（Quat-Quatta）等好几处牧场，此外在昆士兰（Queensland）还有一大片。

===Original===
I noticed as he entered the door that since I had seen him he had washed, combed his stiff black hair, and divested himself of his hat, spurs, and whip﻿—his leggings had perforce to remain, as his nether garment was a pair of closely fitting grey cloth riding-breeches, which clearly defined the shapely contour of his lower limbs.

===Chinese===
他进门时我留意到，自方才一别，他已洗过脸，把硬茬茬的黑头发梳拢了，还卸去了帽子、马刺和鞭子——绑腿却是脱不得的，因为他下身那条裹得紧绷绷的灰呢马裤，把两条腿的漂亮轮廓勾画得一清二楚。

===Original===
“Harry, this is Sybylla. I’m sure you need no further introduction. Excuse me, I have something on the fire which is likely to burn.” And aunt Helen hurried off leaving us facing each other.

===Chinese===
“哈里，这是西比拉。你们用不着我再多介绍了吧。失陪，炉子上还坐着东西，眼看要烧过头了。”海伦姨妈说着急忙走开，撇下我们俩面对面站着。

===Original===
He stared down at me with undisguised surprise. I looked up at him and laughed merrily. The fun was all on my side. He was a great big man﻿—rich and important. I was a chit﻿—an insignificant nonentity﻿—yet, despite his sex, size, and importance, I was complete master of that situation, and knew it: thus I laughed.

===Chinese===
他毫不掩饰他的讶异，低头打量我。我仰头望着他，痛痛快快地笑了。趣儿全在我这一边。他是个堂堂大汉——又有钱又有身份；我是个黄毛丫头——一个无足轻重的小东西——然而任凭他占着性别、身量、身份的便宜，我却是这场面里十足的主宰，而且心知肚明：所以我笑。

===Original===
I saw that he recognized me again by the dusky red he flushed beneath his sun-darkened skin. No doubt he regretted having called me a filly above all things. He bowed stiffly, but I held out my hand, saying:

===Chinese===
我看见他认出了我——他那晒黑的皮肤底下泛起一层暗红。不用问，把人唤作小母马那桩事，他悔得比什么都深。他生硬地欠身致意，我却伸出手去，说：

===Original===
“Do shake hands. When introduced I always shake hands with anyone I think I’ll like. Besides, I seem to know you well. Just think of all the apples you brought me!”

===Chinese===
“一定要握手。人家引见的时候，凡是我料想会喜欢的人，我总要握一握。再说，我同你好像早就熟识得很了。想想你给我捎来的那些苹果吧！”

===Original===
