He acceded to my request, holding my hand a deal longer than necessary, and looking at me helplessly. It amused me greatly, for I saw that it was he who did not know how to manage me, and not I that couldn’t manage him.

===Chinese===
他依了我的请求，握着我的手，握得比必要的久了许多，一脸没奈何地望着我。这可大大逗乐了我，因为我看出来，不知道拿我怎么办的是他，倒不是我拿他没办法。

===Original===
“ ’Pon my honour, Miss Melvyn, I had no idea it was you, when I said﻿—” Here he boggled completely, which had the effect of reviving my laughter.

===Chinese===
“凭我的名誉起誓，梅尔文小姐，我说那话的时候，真想不到是你——”说到这里他整个儿卡了壳，逗得我又笑了起来。

===Original===
“You had no right to be dressed like that﻿—deceiving a fellow. It wasn’t fair.”

===Chinese===
“你怎么能穿成那副模样——存心蒙人。这可不公道。”

===Original===
“That’s the best of it. It shows what a larrikin Don Juan sort of character you are. You can’t deceive me now if you pretend to be a virtuous well-behaved member of society.”

===Chinese===
“这就妙了。正好见出你原是个浪荡恶少唐璜一流的角色。往后你哪怕装成规行矩步的社会良民，也休想再骗过我了。”

===Original===
“That is the first time I’ve ever meddled with any of the kitchen fry, and, by Jove, it will be the last!” he said energetically. “I’ve got myself into a pretty mess.”

===Chinese===
“我这辈子还是头一回招惹厨房里的小丫头们，老天作证，也是末一回了！”他起劲地说，“这下我把自己搅进一场大麻烦里了。”

===Original===
“What nonsense you talk,” I replied. “If you say another word about it, I’ll write a full account of it and paste it in my scrapbook. But if you don’t worry about it, neither will I. You said nothing very uncomplimentary; in fact, I was quite flattered.”

===Chinese===
“瞧你说的什么傻话，”我答道，“你再多提一个字，我就把这事原原本本写下来，贴进我的剪贴簿。不过你若不放在心上，我也不放在心上。你说的实在没有半句不中听的话；说真的，我还挺受用呢。”

===Original===
I was perched on the high end of a couch, and he was leaning with big careless ease on the piano. Had grannie seen me, I would have been lectured about unladylike behaviour.

===Chinese===
我高踞在长沙发的高头，他大大咧咧、浑身松泛地倚在钢琴上。这要叫外婆瞧见了，少不得训我一顿有失小姐体统。

===Original===
“What is your uncle at today?” he inquired.

===Chinese===
“你舅舅今天忙些什么？”他问。

===Original===
“He’s not at anything. He went to Gool-Gool yesterday on the jury. Court finishes up today, and he is going to bring the judge home tonight. That’s why I am dressed so carefully,” I answered.

===Chinese===
“他什么也不忙。他昨天上古尔古尔当陪审员去了。法庭今天收场，今晚他要把法官接回家来。这就是我打扮得这么郑重的缘故，”我答道。

===Original===
“Good gracious! I never thought of court this time as I wasn’t called on the jury, and for a wonder hadn’t so much as a case against a Chinaman. I was going to stay tonight, but can’t if his worship is going to dine here.”

===Chinese===
“哎呀糟糕！我这回压根儿没想到开庭这档子事，一来没传我当陪审员，二来难得连一桩同中国人打的官司都没有。我本打算今晚住下的，可法官大人要来用饭，我就住不成了。”

===Original===
“Why? You’re surely not afraid of Judge Fossilt? He’s a very simple old customer.”

===Chinese===
“为什么？你总不至于怕福西尔特（Fossilt）法官吧？他是个再随和不过的老家伙。”

===Original===
“Imagine dining with a judge in this toggery!” and he glanced down his great figure at his riding gear.

===Chinese===
“想想看，穿着这么一身行头同法官吃饭！”说着，他顺着自己那高大的身架一路望下去，看着那身骑装。

===Original===
“That doesn’t matter; he’s nearsighted. I’ll get you put at the far end of the table under my wing. Men don’t notice dress. If you weren’t so big uncle or Frank Hawden could oblige you.”

===Chinese===
“那不要紧；他眼睛近视。我会安排你坐到桌子那一头，归我照应。男人不注意穿着。你要不是生得这么高大，你舅舅或者弗兰克·霍登倒可以借你一身呢。”

===Original===
“Do you think I could pass muster?”

===Chinese===
“你看我这副样子过得关吗？”

===Original===
“Yes; after I brush you down you’ll look as spruce as a brass penny.”

===Chinese===
“过得关；等我给你刷打一遍，保管你光鲜利落得像枚新铜板。”

===Original===
“I did brush myself,” he answered.

===Chinese===
“我自己刷过了，”他答道。

===Original===
“You brush yourself!” I retorted. “There’s a big splash of mud on your shoulder. You couldn’t expect to do anything decently, for you’re only a man, and men are the uselessest, good-for-nothingest, clumsiest animals in the world. All they’re good for is to smoke and swear.”

===Chinese===
“你自己刷的！”我回敬道。“你肩膀上还沾着一大块泥呢。别指望你能把什么事做得像样点，你不过是个男人，男人是世上最最没用、最最不长进、最最笨手笨脚的动物。他们唯一的用处，就是抽烟和骂骂咧咧。”

===Original===
I fetched a clothes brush.

===Chinese===
我去取来一把衣刷。

===Original===
“You’ll have to stand on the table to reach me,” he said, looking down with amused indulgence.

===Chinese===
“你得站到桌子上，才够得着我呢，”他说着，带着好笑的纵容低头看我。

===Original===
“As you are so impertinent you can go dusty,” and I tossed the brush away.

===Chinese===
“你既然这么放肆，就顶着一身灰去吧，”说着我把刷子一甩手扔开了。

===Original===
The evening was balmy, so I invited him into the garden. He threw his handkerchief over my chest, saying I might catch cold, but I scouted the idea.

===Chinese===
暮色温煦宜人，我便请他到花园里去。他把手绢往我胸前一搭，说我怕是会着凉，我把这话嗤之以鼻。

===Original===
We wandered into an arbour covered with wistaria, banksia, and Marechal Niel roses, and I made him a buttonhole.

===Chinese===
我们漫步走进一座爬满紫藤、山龙眼（banksia）和马雷夏尔·尼尔（Marechal Niel）玫瑰的凉亭，我给他做了朵胸花。

===Original===
A traveller pulled rein in the roadway, and, dismounting, threw his bridle over a paling of the garden fence while he went inside to try and buy a loaf of bread.

===Chinese===
大路上一个赶路人勒住了马，翻身下来，把缰绳往花园栅栏的一根板条上一搭，自进屋去想买一条面包。

===Original===
I jumped up, frightening the horse so that it broke away, pulling off the paling in the bridle-rein. I ran to bring a hammer to repair the damage. Mr. Beecham caught the horse while I attempted to drive the nail into the fence. It was a futile attempt. I bruised my fingers. He took the hammer from me, and fixing the paling in its place with a couple of well-aimed blows, said laughingly:

===Chinese===
我霍地跳起来，惊得那马挣脱跑开，把缰绳上拴着的那根板条也带了下来。我跑去取来一把锤子修这损失。比彻姆先生捉住马，由我来试着往栅栏上钉钉子。这一试徒劳无功，倒把手指磕青了。他从我手里拿过锤子，瞄得准准的两下便把板条钉回原处，笑着说道：

===Original===
“You drive a nail! You couldn’t expect to do anything. You’re only a girl. Girls are the helplessest, uselessest, troublesomest little creatures in the world. All they’re good for is to torment and pester a fellow.”

===Chinese===
“你钉钉子！别指望你能做成什么事。你不过是个姑娘。姑娘是世上最最没依没靠、最最没用、最最惹祸的小东西。她们唯一的本事，就是折磨男人、缠磨男人。”

===Original===
I had to laugh.

===Chinese===
我禁不住笑了。

===Original===
At this juncture we heard uncle Jay-Jay’s voice, so Mr. Beecham went towards the back, whence it proceeded, after he left me at the front door.

===Chinese===
正在这当口，我们听见了杰杰舅舅的嗓音，比彻姆先生便把我在前门口安顿下，自己朝声音传来的屋后去了。

===Original===
“Oh, auntie, we got on splendidly! He’s not a bit of trouble. We’re as chummy as though we had been reared together,” I exclaimed.

===Chinese===
“哎呀姨妈，我们处得再好没有了！他一点儿不费事。我们亲热得就像从小一块儿长大似的，”我叫道。

===Original===
“Did you get him to talk?”

===Chinese===
“你逗他说话了么？”

===Original===
“Oh yes.”

===Chinese===
“说了呀。”

===Original===
“Did you really?” in surprise.

===Chinese===
“当真？”她惊讶地问。

===Original===
When I came to review the matter I was forced to confess that I had done all the talking, and young Beecham the listening; moreover I described him as the quietest man I had ever seen or heard of.

===Chinese===
待我回过头来细想这事，我不得不承认，说话的全是我，听话的全是小比彻姆；不但如此，我还把他形容成我这辈子见过或听说过的最安静的男人。

===Original===
The judge did not come home with uncle Jay-Jay as expected so it was not necessary for me to shelter Harold Beecham under my wing. Grannie greeted him cordially as “Harold, my boy,” he was a great favourite with her. She and uncle Julius monopolized him for the evening. There was great talk of trucking sheep, the bad outlook as regarded the season, the state of the grass in the triangle, the Leigh Spring, the Bimbalong, and several other paddocks, and of the condition of the London wool market. It did not interest me, so I dived into a book, only occasionally emerging therefrom to smile at Mr. Beecham.

===Chinese===
法官没有像大家料想的那样随杰杰舅舅一道回家来，我也就用不着把哈罗德·比彻姆收在我的羽翼底下了。外婆亲亲热热地招呼他一声“哈罗德，我的孩子”，他在她跟前可是个大红人。这一晚，她和朱利叶斯舅舅把他独占了。席间大谈铁路运羊，谈年景前景的不妙，谈三角地、利泉（Leigh Spring）、宾巴隆（Bimbalong）等好几处围场的牧草长势，还谈伦敦羊毛市场的行情。这些一概提不起我的兴致，我便一头扎进一本书里，只是偶尔从书里冒出头来，朝比彻姆先生笑一笑。

===Original===
He had come to Caddagat for a pair of bullocks which had been fattening in grannie’s home paddock. Uncle gave him a start with them next morning. When they came out on the road I was standing in a bed of violets in a tangled corner of the garden, where roses climbed to kiss the lilacs, and spiraea stooped to rest upon the wallflowers, and where two tall kurrajongs stood like sentries over all. Harold Beecham dismounted, and, leaning over the fence, lingered with me, leaving the bullocks to uncle Jay-Jay. Uncle raved vigorously. Women, he asserted, were the bane of society and the ruination of all men; but he had always considered Harold as too sensible to neglect his business to stand grinning at a pesky youngster in short skirts and a pigtail. Which was the greatest idiot of the two he didn’t know.

===Chinese===
他上卡达加特来，是为着两头在自家宅旁围场里育肥的阉牛。第二天一早，舅舅帮他把牛起程上路。他们上到大路时，我正站在花园里一个荒蔓纠结的角落中一片紫罗兰花畦里；在那角落，玫瑰攀上枝去亲吻丁香，绣线菊俯身偎向桂竹香，两棵高大的瓶木（kurrajong）像哨兵似的守望着这一切。哈罗德·比彻姆下了马，倚在栅栏上同我流连，把牛丢给了杰杰舅舅。舅舅破口大骂了一通。女人，他断言，是社会的祸根、一切男人毁灭的根由；可他一向把哈罗德看作明白人，不至于撂下正事，站着朝一个穿短裙、拖辫子的讨厌小丫头咧嘴傻笑。这两人里头到底哪个是大一号的傻子，他可说不上来。

===Original===
His grumbling did not affect Harold in the least.

===Chinese===
他那番咕哝，对哈罗德没有一星半点影响。

===Original===
“Complimentary to both of us,” he remarked as he leisurely threw himself across his great horse, and smiled his pleasant quiet smile, disclosing two rows of magnificent teeth, untainted by contamination with beer or tobacco. Raising his panama hat with the green fly-veil around it, he cantered off. I wondered as I watched him if anything ever disturbed his serenity, and desired to try. He looked too big and quiet to be ruffled by such emotions as rage, worry, jealousy, or even love. Returning to the house, I put aunt Helen through an exhaustive catechism concerning him.

===Chinese===
“这话把咱们俩都夸上了，”他不慌不忙地翻身跨上他那匹高头大马，议论了一句，脸上浮起他那安详可亲的微笑，露出一口漂亮齐整的好牙，没让啤酒或烟草熏染分毫。他把那顶缀着绿纱防蝇罩的巴拿马帽往上一抬，催马小跑着去了。我望着他的背影，心里琢磨着，究竟可有什么事情搅得乱他的安详，并且很想去试一试。他看上去那么高大、那么沉静，不像会被暴怒、忧愁、嫉妒、乃至爱情这类情绪搅皱半分。回到屋里，我把海伦姨妈捉住，就他的事，结结实实地盘问了一番。

===Original===
**Question.** Auntie, what age is Harold Beecham?

===Chinese===
**问。**姨妈，哈罗德·比彻姆多大年纪了？

===Original===
**Answer.** Twenty-five last December.

===Chinese===
**答。**去年十二月满的二十五。

===Original===
**Q.** Did he ever have any brothers or sisters?

===Chinese===
**问。**他有没有过兄弟姐妹？

===Original===
**A.** No. His birth caused his mother’s death.

===Chinese===
**答。**没有。他的出生送了他母亲的命。

===Original===
**Q.** How long has his father been dead?

===Chinese===
**问。**他父亲去世多久了？

===Original===
**A.** Since Harold could crawl.

===Chinese===
**答。**哈罗德刚会爬的时候就没了。

===Original===
**Q.** Who reared him?

===Chinese===
**问。**是谁把他带大的？

===Original===
**A.** His aunts.

===Chinese===
**答。**他的两位姑母。

===Original===
**Q.** Does he ever talk any more than that?

===Chinese===
**问。**他说话就只有这么几句么？

===Original===
**A.** Often a great deal less.

===Chinese===
**答。**常常还少得多。

===Original===
**Q.** Is he really very rich?

===Chinese===
**问。**他真的非常有钱吗？

===Original===
**A.** If he manages to pull through these seasons he will be second to none but Tyson in point of wealth.

===Chinese===
**答。**这些年头他若熬得过去，论家财除了泰森（Tyson）便无人能及。

===Original===
**Q.** Is Five-Bob a very pretty place?

===Chinese===
**问。**五博丘是个很漂亮的地方吗？

===Original===
**A.** Yes; one of the show places of the district.

===Chinese===
**答。**是的；是本区数得着的好地方。

===Original===
**Q.** Does he often come to Caddagat?

===Chinese===
**问。**他常来卡达加特吗？

===Original===
**A.** Yes, he often drops in.

===Chinese===
**答。**常来，他常常顺脚进来坐坐。

===Original===
**Q.** What makes his hair so black and his moustache that light colour?

===Chinese===
**问。**他的头发怎么那样黑，小胡子怎么又是那种浅颜色？

===Original===
**A.** You’ll have to study science to find that out. I’m sure I can’t tell you.

===Chinese===
**答。**这个你得去钻研科学才弄得明白。反正我是答不上来。

===Original===
**Q.** Does he﻿—?

===Chinese===
**问。**他究竟——？

===Original===
“Now, Sybylla,” said auntie, laughing, “you are taking a suspicious interest in my sunburnt young giant. Did I not tell you he was taking time by the forelock when he brought the apples?”

===Chinese===
“行了，西比拉，”姨妈笑着说，“你对我这个晒得黑黑的年轻大个子，可动了可疑的心思啦。他捎苹果来的时候，我不是同你说过，他这是抓住时机先声夺人么？”

===Original===
“Oh, auntie, I am only asking questions because﻿—”

===Chinese===
“哎呀姨妈，我不过问问，因为——”

===Original===
“Yes, because, because, I understand perfectly. Because you are a girl, and all the girls fall a victim to Harry’s charms at once. If you don’t want to succumb meekly to your fate, ‘Heed the spark or you may dread the fire.’ That is the only advice I can tender you.”

===Chinese===
“是呀，因为，因为，我全明白。因为你是个姑娘，而姑娘们个个一碰上哈里的魅力就缴了械。你若不想乖乖听凭命运摆布，‘提防那点火星，免得怕那场大火。’我能给你的忠告，就只有这一句。”

===Original===
This was a Thursday, and on the following Sunday Harold Beecham reappeared at Caddagat and remained from three in the afternoon until nine at night. Uncle Julius and Frank Hawden were absent. The weather had taken a sudden backward lurch into winter again, so we had a fire. Harold sat beside it all the time, and interposed yes and no at the proper intervals in grannie’s brisk business conversation, but he never addressed one word to me beyond “Good afternoon, Miss Melvyn,” on his arrival, and “Good night, Miss Melvyn,” when leaving.

===Chinese===
这是星期四。接下来的星期天，哈罗德·比彻姆又在卡达加特露了面，从下午三点一直待到晚上九点。朱利叶斯舅舅和弗兰克·霍登都不在家。天气猛地一个趔趄，又倒退回冬天去了，所以我们生起了火。哈罗德一直坐在炉边，在外婆干脆利落、满是正事的谈话里，于恰当的空当插上一句“是”与“不是”，可除了到时一声“午安，梅尔文小姐”、走时一声“晚安，梅尔文小姐”之外，他再没有对我说过一个字。

===Original===
I studied him attentively all the while. What were his ideas and sentiments it were hard to tell: he never expressed any. He was fearfully and wonderfully quiet. Yet his was an intelligent silence, not of that wooden brainless description which casts a damper on company, neither was it of the morose or dreaming order.

===Chinese===
我自始至终细细地打量他。他心里有什么想法、什么情怀，很难说清：他从不表露。他那份安静，直是奇妙得可畏。然而他这是一种有灵性的安静，不是那种木头木脑、教满座扫兴的死气，也不属于阴郁郁的、做白日梦的一类。

## XIV Principally Letters / 第14章 多是书信

===Original===
> I have started to write no less than seven letters to you, but something always interrupted me and I did not finish them. However, I’ll finish this one in the teeth of Father Peter himself. I will parenthesize all the interruptions. (A traveller just asked me for a rose. I had to get up and give him one.) Living here is lovely. (Another man inquired the way to Somingley Gap, and I’ve just finished directing him.) Grannie is terribly nice. You could not believe. She is always giving me something, and takes me wherever she goes. Auntie is an angel. I wish you could hear the piano. It is a beauty. There are dozens of papers and books to read. Uncle is a dear old fellow. You should hear him rave and swear sometimes when he gets in a rage. It is great fun. He brings me lollies, gloves, ribbons, or something every time he comes from town. (Two Indian hawkers have arrived, and I am going out to see their goods. There were nineteen hawkers here last week. I am sitting on a squatter’s chair and writing on a table in the veranda, and the road goes right by the flower-garden. That is how I see everyone.) Have you had rain down there this week? They have great squawking about the drought up here. I wish they could see Goulburn, and then they’d know what drought means. I don’t know what sort of a bobberie they would kick up. It’s pretty dry out on the run, but everyone calls the paddocks about the house an oasis. You see there are such splendid facilities for irrigation here. Uncle has put on a lot of men. They have cut races between the two creeks between which the house is situated. Every now and again they let the water from these over the orchard gardens and about a hundred acres of paddock land around the house. The grass therein is up to the horses’ fetlocks. There is any amount of rhubarb and early vegetables in the garden. Grannie says there is a splendid promise of fruit in the orchard, and the flower-garden is a perfect dream. This is the dearest old place in the world. Dozens of people plague grannie to be let put their horses in the grass﻿—especially shearers, there are droves of them going home now﻿—but she won’t let them; wants all the grass for her own stock. Uncle has had to put another man on to mind it, or at night all the wires are cut and the horses put in. (An agent, I think by the cut of him, is asking for grannie. I’ll have to run and find her.) It is very lively here. Never a night but we have the house full of agents or travellers of one sort or another, and there are often a dozen swaggies in the one day.
> Harold Beecham is my favourite of all the men hereaway. He is delightfully big and quiet. He isn’t good-looking, but I like his face. (Been attending to the demands of a couple of impudent swaggies. Being off the road at Possum Gully, you escape them.) For the love of life, next time you write, fire into the news at once and don’t half-fill your letter telling me about the pen and your bad writing. I am scribbling at the rate of 365 miles an hour, and don’t care a jot whether it is good writing or not.
> Auntie, uncle, Frank Hawden and I, are going to ride to Yabtree church next Sunday. It is four miles beyond Five-Bob Downs, so that is sixteen miles. It is the nearest church. I expect it will be rare fun. There will be such a crowd coming home, and that always makes the horses delightfully frisky. (A man wants to put his horses in the paddock for the night, so I will have to find uncle.) I never saw such a place for men. It is all men, men, men. You cannot go anywhere outside the house but you see men coming and going in all directions. It wouldn’t do to undress without bothering to drop the window-blind like we used at Possum Gully. Grannie and uncle say it is a curse to be living beside the road, as it costs them a tremendous lot a year. There are seven lemon-trees here, loaded (another hawker). I hope you think of me sometimes. I am just as ugly as ever. (A traveller wants to buy a loaf of bread.)
> With stacks of love to all at home, and a whole dray-load for yourself, from your loving sister,
> Sybylla.
> Remember me to Goulburn, drowsing lazily in its dreamy graceful hollow in the blue distance.

===Chinese===
> 我给你起头的信，少说也有七封了，可总有什么事来打断我，一封也没能写完。不过这一封，哪怕彼得神父（Father Peter）本人挡道，我也要把它写完。种种打岔，我都拿括号记下来。（刚有个过路人问我要一朵玫瑰，我只得起身给了他一朵。）这儿的日子可爱极了。（又有一个人来打听去索明利山口（Somingley Gap）的路，我刚给他指点明白。）外婆好得不得了，你简直不能相信。她老是给我这样那样，走到哪儿都把我带在身边。姨妈是个天使。但愿你能听听那架钢琴，真是件宝贝。有几十种报刊书籍可读。舅舅是个亲爱的老伙计。他动起肝火来咆哮咒骂的那副样子，你真该听听，有趣极了。他每回从城里回来，总给我捎糖果呀、手套呀、缎带呀，总要带点什么。（来了两个印度货郎，我这就出去看他们的货。上礼拜到这儿来过的货郎有十九个。我正坐在游廊里一把宽大的牧场主座椅（squatter’s chair）上，就着一张桌子写信，大路恰好打花圃旁边经过，人人我都看得见，就是这么个来由。）你们那底下这个礼拜下雨了没有？这上头的人正为旱灾哇哇地大嚷大叫。但愿他们能去看看古尔本，那时他们才晓得旱字是怎么写的。天晓得他们会闹腾出一场多大的乱子。外头放牧地上确实干得厉害，可人人都把屋子四周的围场唤作一片绿洲。你瞧，这里灌溉的条件好极了。舅舅添雇了好些人工，宅子坐落在两条小溪之间，他们便在这溪间挖出了一道道水渠。隔不多久，他们就把渠里的水放出来，漫灌果园菜圃，连同屋子周围约莫一百英亩的围场地。那里的草深得齐到马的球节。园子里大黄和早熟蔬菜多得数不清。外婆说，果园里的果子丰收在望，花圃更是一场十足的梦。这是世上顶顶可爱的一方老地方。成打的人纠缠外婆，求她放马进来吃草——尤其是剪毛工，眼下正一伙一伙地往家赶——她偏不肯；要留着全部草料喂自家的牲口。舅舅不得不又专添一个人看管，不然一到夜里，围栏铁丝全叫人铰断，马就给放进去了。（来了个经纪人，瞧那身做派，是来求见外婆的。我得跑去把她找来。）这儿热闹得很。没有哪一晚屋里不满坐着这样那样的经纪人或过往旅客，一天里来上十来个流浪工也是常事。
> 这一带的男人里，我最喜欢哈罗德·比彻姆（Harold Beecham）。他块头大得叫人舒坦，性子又安静。他长得不算好看，可我喜欢他那张脸。（刚应付完两个没规矩的流浪工的要求。袋熊谷（Possum Gully）不在大路边上，你们倒躲开了这号人。）看在性命份上，下回写信，一上来就赶紧报消息，别拿半封信的篇幅同我絮叨你的笔和你那笔烂字。我这会儿正以每小时 365 英里的速度涂鸦，字好不好，我一丁点儿也不在乎。
> 下个礼拜天，姨妈、舅舅、弗兰克·霍登（Frank Hawden）和我，要骑马去亚布特里（Yabtree）的教堂。那地方过了五博丘（Five-Bob Downs）还有四英里，算起来一共十六英里。那是最近的一座教堂。我料想必定有趣极了。回家的一路上人山人海，马儿们总要撒欢撒得叫人痛快。（有个人要把马放进围场过夜，我得去把舅舅寻来。）我从没见过男人这么多的地方。遍地是男人，男人，男人。你只要一出屋子，无论朝哪儿去，四面八方都有男人来来往往。再不能像在袋熊谷那样，连窗帘都懒得放就宽衣就寝了。外婆和舅舅都说，住在大路边真是个祸害，一年下来要多花好大一笔钱。这儿有七棵柠檬树，全都挂满了果（又来了个货郎）。但愿你们有时还惦记着我。我还是同往常一样丑。（有个过路人要买一只面包。）
> 给家里各位送上堆叠如山的爱，给你自己的，再整整装上一大车——你亲爱的妹妹，
> 西比拉。
> 替我问候古尔本，它正卧在远处蓝天下一道如梦般秀美的洼地里，懒懒地打着盹呢。

===Original===
> Thank you very much for the magazines and *An Australian Bush Track*. I suppose you have quite forgotten us and Caddagat by this time. The sun has sunk behind the gum-trees, and the blue evening mists are hanging lazily in the hollows of the hills. I expect you are donning your “swallowtail” preparatory to leading some be-satined “faire ladye” in to a gorgeous dinner, thence to the play, then to a dance probably. No doubt all around you is bustle, glare of lights, noise, and fun. It is such a different scene here. From down the road comes the tinkle of camp-bells and jingle of hobble-chains. From down in that sheltered angle where the creek meets the river comes the gleam of campfires through the gathering twilight, and I can see several tents rigged for the night, looking like white specks in the distance.
> I long for the time to come when I shall get to Sydney. I’m going to lead you and aunt Helen a pretty dance. You’ll have to keep going night and day. It will be great. I must get up and dance a jig on the veranda when I think of it. You’ll have to show me everything﻿—slums and all. I want to find out the truth of heaps of things for myself.
> Save for the weird rush of the stream and the kookaburras’ good night, all is still, with a mighty far-reaching stillness which can be felt. Now the curlews are beginning their wild moaning cry. From the rifts in the dark lone ranges, far down the river, it comes like a hunted spirit until it makes me feel﻿—

===Chinese===
> 多谢你寄来的杂志和《一条澳洲丛林小径》（*An Australian Bush Track*）。我料想这会儿你早把我们和卡达加特（Caddagat）忘了个干净。太阳已经落到桉树背后去了，蓝色的暮霭懒懒地挂在群山的洼坳里。我料想你正在穿你的“燕尾服”，预备搀着哪位遍体绸缎的“绝色佳人”去赴一场盛大的晚宴，随后去看戏，再随后多半还有一场舞。无疑，你四下里全是喧腾、灯火的辉光、声响和热闹。这里却全然是另一番景象。大路那头传来宿营马铃的叮当和绊马链的叮啷。在小溪与大河交汇的那个避风角落里，营火的光透过渐浓的暮色闪亮，我还望得见几顶为过夜支起的帐篷，远远看去像一个个白点。
> 我盼着去悉尼（Sydney）的日子快点到来。我要叫你和海伦姨妈（aunt Helen）跟着我团团转，你们得日夜不停地跑。那才带劲呢。一想到这个，我就恨不得站起身来，到游廊上去跳一支吉格舞。到时你样样都得领我去看——贫民窟也不例外。我要亲自去探明一大堆事情的真相。
> 除了溪流奇异的奔涌和笑翠鸟（kookaburra）的道晚安，万籁俱寂，一片浩大绵邈、静得能叫人感知的寂静。这会儿杓鹬开始啼起它们狂野哀号的鸣声了。从大河下游黑暗孤寂的山岭裂罅间，那声音传来，像被追捕的幽魂，直叫我觉着——

===Original===
At this point I said, “Bah! I’m mad to write to Everard Grey like this. He would laugh and call me a poor little fool.” I tore the half-finished letter to shreds, and consigned it to the kitchen fire. I substituted a prim formal note, merely thanking him for the books and magazine he had sent me. To this I never received an answer. I heard through his letters to grannie that he was much occupied. Had been to Brisbane and Melbourne on important cases, so very likely had not time to be bothered with me; or, he might have been like the majority of his fellows who make a great parade of friendship while with one, then go away and forget one’s existence in an hour.

===Chinese===
写到这儿，我说了声：“呸！我是疯了，才这样给埃弗拉德·格雷（Everard Grey）写信。他准会笑话我，管我叫一个可怜的小傻瓜。”我把那封写了一半的信撕成碎片，把它发付给了厨房的炉火。我另写了一封拘谨刻板的正式短笺取而代之，只谢了他寄来的书和杂志。这一封，我从未收到回音。我从他写给外婆的信里获悉，他忙得很，去布里斯班（Brisbane）和墨尔本办要紧的案子了，所以多半没有工夫来为我烦心；再不然，他或许同他那类人中的大多数一个样——当着人的面，把友谊铺陈得轰轰烈烈，一走开，一个钟头便把你的存在忘了个干净。

===Original===
While at Caddagat there were a few duties allotted to me. One of these was to attend to the drawing-room; another was to find uncle Jay-Jay’s hat when he mislaid it﻿—often ten times per day. I assisted my grandmother to make up her accounts and write business letters, and I attended to tramps. A man was never refused a bit to eat at Caddagat. This necessitated the purchase of an extra ton of flour per year, also nearly a ton of sugar, to say nothing of tea, potatoes, beef, and all broken meats which went thus. This was not reckoning the consumption of victuals by the other class of travellers with which the house was generally full year in and year out. Had there been any charge for their board and lodging, the Bossiers would surely have made a fortune. I interviewed on an average fifty tramps a week, and seldom saw the same man twice. What a great army they were! Hopeless, homeless, aimless, shameless souls, tramping on from north to south, and east to west, never relinquishing their heart-sickening, futile quest for work﻿—some of them so long on the tramp that the ambitions of manhood had been ground out of them, and they wished for nothing more than this.

===Chinese===
在卡达加特，也分派给我几样差事。其一是照管客厅；另一桩是替杰杰舅舅（uncle Jay-Jay）寻他随手乱搁的帽子——常常一天要寻十回。我帮外祖母理账、写事务信件，还负责打发流浪汉。在卡达加特，从来没有一个人来讨口饭吃遭到回绝。为此每年要多买一吨面粉，还有将近一吨糖，茶叶、土豆、牛肉和所有这样散出去的残羹剩饭尚且不算。这还没算另一类旅客的饮食消耗——年复一年，屋里通常给这类人塞得满满的。倘若他们的食宿当真收钱，博西耶家（the Bossiers）肯定早发大财了。我平均每礼拜接见五十个流浪汉，难得同一张面孔见着两回。他们是何等庞大的一支大军啊！绝望的、无家的、无目标的、无羞耻的灵魂，从北到南、从东到西一路跋涉，永不放弃那令人心酸而徒劳的找工之求——有些人流浪得年深月久，连男子汉的抱负都被磨了个干净，所求的再没有比这更多的了。

===Original===
There were all shapes, sizes, ages, kinds, and conditions of men﻿—the shamefaced boy in the bud of his youth, showing by the way he begged that the humiliation of the situation had not yet worn off, and poor old creatures tottering on the brink of the grave, with nothing left in life but the enjoyment of beer and tobacco. There were strong men in their prime who really desired work when they asked for it, and skulking cowards who hoped they would not get it. There were the diseased, the educated, the ignorant, the deformed, the blind, the evil, the honest, the mad, and the sane. Some in real professional beggars’ style called down blessings on me; others were morose and glum, while some were impudent and thankless, and said to supply them with food was just what I should do, for the swagmen kept the squatters﻿—as, had the squatters not monopolized the land, the swagmen would have had plenty. A moiety of the last-mentioned﻿—dirty, besotted, ragged creatures﻿—had a glare in their eyes which made one shudder to look at them, and, while spasmodically twirling their billies or clenching their fists, talked wildly of making one to “bust up the damn banks,” or to drive all the present squatters out of the country and put the people on the land﻿—clearly showing that, because they had failed for one reason or another, it had maddened them to see others succeed.

===Chinese===
男人里头，什么形状、什么块头、什么年纪、什么品类、什么境况的都有——有含羞带臊、青春方绽的少年，那讨要的样子分明显出处境的羞辱还没有磨钝；有在坟墓边缘摇摇晃晃的可怜老东西，人生里剩下的，唯有啤酒和烟草的受用。有正当盛年、开口讨工时真心实意想干活儿的壮汉，也有巴望讨不着活儿的躲闪懦夫。有患病的，有受过教育的，有愚昧无知的，有畸形残缺的，有瞎眼的，有邪恶的，有诚实的，有疯癫的，也有神志清明的。有些人摆出十足职业乞丐的派头，为我祈降福佑；另一些人阴沉乖僻、闷声不响；还有些人无礼而不知感恩，说供他们饭食本就是我该当做的，因为养活大牧场主的正是这些背囊流浪汉——倘若大牧场主们不曾垄断了土地，流浪汉们本可以丰衣足食。上述最后一类里的一半——那些肮脏、酗得麻木、破衣烂衫的家伙——眼里闪着一股凶光，看一眼就叫人打颤；他们一阵一阵旋着手里的铁皮罐，或是攥紧拳头，狂言要纠合起一伙人去“砸烂那些该死的银行”，或是把如今这些大牧场主统统赶出国境，让人民登上土地——这分明显出：只因他们自己因这样那样的缘故败下阵来，眼见旁人成功，便气得发了狂。

===Original===
In a wide young country of boundless resources, why is this thing? This question worried me. Our legislators are unable or unwilling to cope with it. They trouble not to be patriots and statesmen. Australia can bring forth writers, orators, financiers, singers, musicians, actors, and athletes which are second to none of any nation under the sun. Why can she not bear sons, men of soul, mind, truth, godliness, and patriotism sufficient to rise and cast off the grim shackles which widen round us day by day?

===Chinese===
在一个幅员辽阔、资源无穷的年轻国度里，为什么会有这样的事？这个问题折磨着我。我们的立法者们无力应付，或者压根不愿应付。他们费不起那个神去做爱国者与政治家。澳大利亚养育得出的作家、演说家、金融家、歌唱家、音乐家、演员和运动健将，比起普天之下的任何国家都毫不逊色。为什么她就生养不出足够的儿子——有灵魂、有头脑、有真诚、有虔敬、有爱国心的男子汉——来奋起甩脱那副日甚一日朝我们围拢来的阴森镣铐呢？

===Original===
I was the only one at Caddagat who held these silly ideas. Harold Beecham, uncle Julius, grannie, and Frank Hawden did not worry about the cause of tramps. They simply termed them a lazy lot of sneaking creatures, fed them, and thought no more of the matter.

===Chinese===
在卡达加特，怀揣这些傻念头的只有我一个。哈罗德·比彻姆、朱利叶斯舅舅（uncle Julius）、外婆和弗兰克·霍登都不为流浪汉的成因操心。他们只管把这些人唤作一伙鬼鬼祟祟的懒东西，喂饱他们，便不再把这事放在心上。

===Original===
I broached the subject to uncle Jay-Jay once, simply to discover his ideas thereon.

===Chinese===
有一回，我朝杰杰舅舅提起这个话头，单只为探一探他对此的见解。

===Original===
I was sitting on a chair in the veranda sewing; he, with his head on a cushion, was comfortably stretched on a rug on the floor.

===Chinese===
我坐在游廊里一张椅子上做针线；他呢，头枕一只靠垫，舒舒服服地摊在地板的一块地毯上。

===Original===
“Uncle Boss, why can’t something be done for tramps?”

===Chinese===
“博西舅舅，怎么就不能为流浪汉们想点办法呢？”

===Original===
“How done for ’em?”

===Chinese===
“怎么个想法？”

===Original===
“Couldn’t some means of employing them be arrived at?”

===Chinese===
“就寻不出个什么法子，雇他们做工吗？”

===Original===
“Work!” he ejaculated. “That’s the very thing the crawling divils are terrified they might get.”

===Chinese===
“做工！”他失声喊道，“那恰恰是那帮爬来爬去的魔鬼生怕捞着的东西。”

===Original===
“Yes; but couldn’t some law be made to help them?”

===Chinese===
“是呀；可就立不出个什么法律，帮衬他们一下吗？”

===Original===
“A law to make me cut up Caddagat and give ten of ’em each a piece, and go on the wallaby myself, I suppose?”

===Chinese===
“立个法律，叫我切碎了卡达加特，分给他们十个每人一块，然后我自己背起行囊去流浪，是不是？”

===Original===
“No, uncle; but there was a poor young fellow here this morning who, I feel sure, was in earnest when he asked for work.”

===Chinese===
“不是，舅舅；只是今天早上这儿来了个可怜的年轻人，他讨工的时候，我敢说，是真心实意的。”

===Original===
“Helen!” bawled uncle Jay-Jay.

===Chinese===
“海伦！”杰杰舅舅扯着嗓子喊。

===Original===
“Well, what is it?” she inquired, appearing in the doorway.

===Chinese===
“嗳，什么事？”她应道，人已出现在门口。

===Original===
“Next time Sybylla is giving a tramp some tucker, you keep a sharp eye on her or she will be sloping one of these days. There was a young fellow here today with a scarlet moustache and green eyes, and she’s dean gone on him, and has been bullying me to give him half Caddagat.”

===Chinese===
“下回西比拉再拿吃食喂流浪汉，你给我把她盯紧点儿，不然迟早哪天她就要跟人开溜了。今儿来了个年轻小伙子，红胡子、绿眼睛，她可叫他迷得死去活来，一直缠磨着我，要把半个卡达加特给他。”

===Original===
“What a disgusting thing to say! Uncle, you ought to be ashamed of yourself,” I exclaimed.

===Chinese===
“这话多难听！舅舅，你真该为自己害臊。”我嚷道。

===Original===
“Very well, I’ll be careful,” said aunt Helen, departing.

===Chinese===
“好吧，我会当心的。”海伦姨妈说着走开了。

===Original===
“What with the damned flies, and the tramps, and a pesky thing called Sybylla, a man’s life ain’t worth a penny to him,” said uncle.

===Chinese===
“又是这些该死的苍蝇，又是流浪汉，又有个名叫西比拉的讨嫌东西，一个男人这条命真是不值一个铜板了。”舅舅说。

===Original===
We fell into silence, which was broken presently by a dirty red-bearded face appearing over the garden gate, and a man’s voice:

===Chinese===
我们陷入了沉默。不多时，一张脏兮兮的红胡子脸从花园门上探出来，一个男人的嗓音打破了寂静：

===Original===
“Good day, boss! Give us a chew of tobaccer?”

===Chinese===
“日安，老板！赏口烟叶嚼嚼吧？”

===Original===
“I’m not the boss,” said uncle with assumed fierceness.

===Chinese===
“我不是老板。”舅舅装出一副凶相说。

===Original===
“Then who is?” inquired the man.

===Chinese===
“那谁是？”那人问道。

===Original===
Uncle pointed his thumb at me, and, rolling out on the floor again as though very sleepy, began to snore. The tramp grinned, and made his request of me. I took him round to the back, served him with flour, beef, and an inch or two of rank tobacco out of a keg which had been bought for the purpose. Refusing a drink of milk which I offered, he resumed his endless tramp with a “So long, little missy. God bless your pleasant face.”

===Chinese===
舅舅把大拇指朝我一指，随即又滚倒在地板上，仿佛困得不行，打起呼噜来。流浪汉咧嘴一笑，便向我开口求告。我领他绕到后头，发给他面粉、牛肉，还有从一只专为这事买来的桶里截下的一两英寸气味呛人的劣等烟草。我请他喝杯牛奶，他谢绝了，重又踏上他那没完没了的流浪路，丢下一句：“再会啦，小姑娘。上帝保佑你这张可亲的脸。”

===Original===
I watched him out of sight. One of my brothers﻿—one of God’s children under the Southern Cross. Did these old fellows really believe in the God whose name they mentioned so glibly? I wondered. But I am thankful that while at Caddagat it was only rarely that my old top-heavy thoughts troubled me. Life was so pleasant that I was content merely to be young﻿—a chit in the first flush of teens, health, hope, happiness, youth﻿—a heedless creature recking not for the morrow.

===Chinese===
我望着他走得没了踪影。我的一位兄弟——南十字星下上帝的一个孩子。这些老家伙当真信奉他们那样顺口挂在嘴上的那位上帝吗？我心下疑惑。不过我庆幸，在卡达加特，我那些头重脚轻的老想头难得来搅扰我。生活如此惬意，我只消做个少年人就心满意足——一个刚刚绽出十几岁年华、健康、希望、快乐、青春的小丫头——一个浑然不为明日挂心的小东西。

## XV When the Heart Is Young / 第15章 心怀年少时

===Original===
About a week or so after I first met Harold Beecham, aunt Helen allowed me to read a letter she had received from the elder of the two Misses Beecham. It ran as follows:

===Chinese===
我初见哈罗德·比彻姆（Harold Beecham）后约莫一个礼拜，海伦姨妈（aunt Helen）便允许我看一封她收到的信，写信人是比彻姆家两位姑奶奶中年长的那位（the elder of the two Misses Beecham）。信上写道：

===Original===
> “This is a begging letter, and I am writing another to your mother at the same time. I am asking her to allow her granddaughter to spend a few weeks with me, and I want you to use your influence in the matter. Sarah has not been well lately, and is going to Melbourne for a change, and as I will be lonely while she is away Harold insists upon me having someone to keep me company﻿—you know how considerate the dear boy is. I hardly like to ask you to spare your little girl to me. It must be a great comfort to have her. I could have got Miss Benson to stay with me, but Harold will not hear of her. He says she is too slow, and would give us both the mopes. But he says your little niece will keep us all alive. Julius was telling me the other day that he could not part with her, as she makes ‘the old barracks,’ as he always calls Caddagat, echo with fun and noise. I am so looking forward to seeing her, as she is dear Lucy’s child. Give her my love,” etc., etc., and as a postscript the letter had﻿—“Harold will go up for Sybylla on Wednesday afternoon. I do hope you will be able to spare her to me for a while.”

===Chinese===
> “这是一封讨人情的信，同一时候我还给你母亲另写着一张。我求她允许她的外孙女来我这里住上几个礼拜，也想请你在这件事上帮着递句话。莎拉（Sarah）近来身子不大爽利，要去墨尔本换换空气；她不在家的日子我要孤单的，哈罗德便执意要我找个人作伴——你晓得那个好孩子多么体贴人心。要我开口求你把你的小姑娘让给我，实在难以启齿；有她在跟前，想必是极大的安慰。我本可以请本森小姐（Miss Benson）来陪我，可哈罗德连听都不要听。他说她太慢吞吞，会叫我们俩都闷出病来。他又说，你那位小外甥女来了，管保叫我们全都活泛起来。前几天朱利叶斯（Julius）还对我说，他舍不得放她走呢，因为她闹得‘老营房’——他向来这样称呼卡达加特（Caddagat）——回声里全是欢笑与喧闹。我是这么盼着见她，因为她是亲爱的露西（Lucy）的孩子。请代我疼她。”云云；信末又缀着一段附言——“星期三下午哈罗德会去接西比拉。我实在盼望你能把她让给我一阵子。”

===Original===
“Oh, auntie, how lovely!” I exclaimed. “What are you laughing at?”

===Chinese===
“哎呀，姨妈，这多好哇！”我叫道。“你笑什么呢？”

===Original===
“For whom do you think Harry wants the companion? It is nice to have an old auntie, as a blind, is it not? Well, all is fair in love and war. You have permission to use me in any way you like.”

===Chinese===
“你道哈里（Harry）要人作伴，是为了谁？拿一位老姨妈当幌子，岂不妙哉？唉，情场如战场，什么手段都不为过。我准你随意把我派上用场。”

===Original===
I pretended to miss her meaning.

===Chinese===
我装作没领会她的意思。

===Original===
Grannie consented to Miss Beecham’s proposal, and ere the day arrived I had a trunk packed with some lovely new dresses, and was looking forward with great glee to my visit to Five-Bob Downs.

===Chinese===
外婆应允了比彻姆小姐的提议；日子还没到，我已把一只大衣箱装好了几件漂亮的新衣裳，满心欢喜地盼望着去五博丘（Five-Bob Downs）做客这一遭。

===Original===
One o’clock on Wednesday afternoon arrived; two o’clock struck, and I was beginning to fear no one was coming for me, when, turning to look out the window for the eighteenth time, I saw the straight blunt nose of Harold Beecham passing. Grannie was serving afternoon tea on the veranda. I did not want any, so got ready while my escort was having his.

===Chinese===
星期三下午一点钟到了；两点钟又敲响，我开始担心不会有人来接我了，不想第十八回转身朝窗外张望时，正瞧见哈罗德·比彻姆那只挺直的钝鼻子一晃而过。外婆正在游廊上张罗午后茶点。我一点儿茶也不想用，便趁着我那位护送人用茶的当口收拾停当。

===Original===
It was rather late when we bowled away at a tremendous pace in a red sulky, my portmanteau strapped on at the back, and a thoroughbred American trotter, which had taken prizes at Sydney shows, harnessed to the front. We just whizzed! It was splendid! The stones and dust rose in a thick cloud from the whirling wheels and flying hoofs, and the posts of the wire fence on our left passed like magic as we went. Mr. Beecham allowed me to drive after a time while he sat ready to take the reins should an emergency arise.

===Chinese===
等到我们坐上一辆红色双轮轻便马车（sulky），以雷霆般的速度疾驰而去时，时候已经不早；我的旅行皮箱拴在车尾，车前套着一匹在悉尼赛会上得过奖的纯种美国快步马。我们简直是在飞！痛快极了！石子与尘土从飞旋的车轮和翻腾的四蹄下扬起浓浓一团；左手边铁丝围栏的界桩，在我们经过时像变戏法似的直往后掠。过了一阵，比彻姆先生让我执缰驾车，他自己坐在旁边，一有意外便随时准备接回缰绳。

===Original===
It was sunset﻿—most majestic hour of the twenty-four﻿—when we drove up to the great white gates which opened into the avenue leading to the main homestead of Five-Bob Downs station﻿—beautiful far-reaching Five-Bob Downs! Dreamy blue hills rose behind, and wide rich flats stretched before, through which the Yarrangung river, glazed with sunset, could be seen like a silver snake winding between shrubberied banks. The odour from the six-acred flower-garden was overpowering and delightful. A breeze gently swayed the crowd of trees amid the houses, and swept over the great orchard which sloped down from the south side of the houses. In the fading sunlight thirty iron roofs gleamed and glared, and seemed like a little town; and the yelp of many dogs went up at the sound of our wheels. Ah! beautiful, beautiful Five-Bob Downs!

===Chinese===
我们驶到那两扇高大的白大门前，正是日落——一天二十四小时中最庄严的时辰。大门敞向林荫道，直通往五博丘牧场的正屋——美丽而绵延辽阔的五博丘！身后耸起梦一般的蓝色丘陵，眼前铺展着宽绰肥沃的平川，亚兰贡河（Yarrangung）罩着一层落日的釉光，像一条银蛇蜿蜒在灌木丛生的两岸之间。六英亩花圃里涌出的香气浓烈逼人，又叫人心醉。微风轻轻摇着屋舍之间簇聚的树木，又拂过从屋子南面斜铺下去的大果园。三十座铁皮屋顶在渐渐暗下去的斜阳里灼灼闪光，恍如一座小镇；我们的车轮声一响，一片犬吠便迎面而起。啊！美丽的、美丽的五博丘！

===Original===
It seemed as though a hundred dogs leapt forth to greet us when that gate flew open, but I subsequently discovered there were but twenty-three.

===Chinese===
大门倏地敞开时，仿佛有一百条狗蹿出来迎我们；后来我才查明，统共不过二十三条。

===Original===
Two female figures came out to meet us﻿—one nearly six feet high, the other, a tiny creature, seemed about eighteen inches, though, of course, was more than that.

===Chinese===
两个女人的身影迎了出来——一个身高将近六英尺；另一个是个小不点儿，瞧上去约莫十八英寸高，当然喽，实际还不止。

===Original===
“I’ve brought her, aunt Gussie,” said Harold, jumping out of the sulky, though not relinquishing the reins, while he kissed the taller figure, and the small one attached itself to his leg saying, “Dimme wide.”

===Chinese===
“我把她给带来啦，格西姑妈（aunt Gussie）。”哈罗德说着跳下马车，手里的缰绳却不撒开，一边吻了吻那个高个儿的身影；那个小不点儿则一把攀住他的腿，说：“要坐车车。”

===Original===
“Hullo! Possum, why wasn’t old Spanker let go? I see he’s not among the dogs,” and my host picked the tiny individual up in his arms and got into the sulky to give her the desired ride, while after being embraced by Miss Beecham and lifted to the ground by her nephew, I went with the former over an asphalted tennis-court, through the wide garden, then across a broad veranda into the great, spreading, one-storeyed house from which gleamed many lights.

===Chinese===
“喂！小负鼠（Possum），怎么没把老斯潘克（old Spanker）放出来？我瞧它不在狗群里。”我的主人说着，把那个小小的人儿一把抱进怀里，坐进马车，让她称心如意地兜了一遭；这边比彻姆小姐拥抱过我，她外甥又把我抱下车来，我便跟着她走过一片铺了沥青的网球场，穿过宽绰的花园，再越过一道宽敞的游廊，走进那座铺展开去、只有一层高的宅子，宅子里已透出点点灯光。

===Original===
“I am so glad you have come, my dear. I must have a good look at you when we get into the light. I hope you are like your mother.”

===Chinese===
“你来了我真高兴，亲爱的。等到了亮处，我定要好好瞧瞧你。我盼着你生得像你母亲。”

===Original===
This prospect discomfited me. I knew she would find a very ugly girl with not the least resemblance to her pretty mother, and I cursed my appearance under my breath.

===Chinese===
这前景叫我很不自在。我知道她将看到的是一个丑得够呛的姑娘，同她那位漂亮母亲没有一丝一毫的相像，我便在心里暗暗咒骂自己的这副长相。

===Original===
“Your name is Sybylla,” Miss Beecham continued, “Sybylla Penelope. Your mother used to be very dear to me, but I don’t know why she doesn’t write to me now. I have never seen her since her marriage. It seems strange to think of her as the mother of eight﻿—five boys and three girls, is it not?”

===Chinese===
“你的名字叫西比拉，”比彻姆小姐接着说，“西比拉·佩内洛普（Sybylla Penelope）。你母亲从前同我要好得很，可我不明白她如今怎么不给我写信了。打她出嫁那年起，我就没再见过她。想想她竟已是八个孩子的母亲——五个男孩、三个女孩——岂不叫人觉得奇异？”

===Original===
Miss Beecham had piloted me through a wide hall and along an extended passage out of which a row of bedrooms opened, into one of which we went.

===Chinese===
比彻姆小姐领我穿过一道宽敞的门厅，又沿一条长走廊前行，走廊一侧开着一排卧室的房门，我们走进了其中一间。

===Original===
“I hope you will be comfortable here, child. You need not dress for dinner while you are here; we never do, only on very special occasions.”

===Chinese===
“愿你在这里住得舒坦，孩子。你在这儿的日子，晚饭不必更衣盛服；我们从不讲究这个，除非逢着顶特别的场合。”

===Original===
“Neither do we at Caddagat,” I replied.

===Chinese===
“我们在卡达加特也不讲究。”我答道。

===Original===
“Now, child, let me have a good look at you without your hat.”

===Chinese===
“来吧，孩子，摘了帽子，让我好好端详端详你。”

===Original===
“Oh, please don’t!” I exclaimed, covering my face with my hands. “I am so dreadfully ugly that I cannot bear to have anyone look at me.”

===Chinese===
“哎呀，求你别看！”我叫起来，双手捂住脸。“我丑得吓死人，实在受不住叫人瞧我。”

===Original===
“What a silly little girl! You are not like your mother, but you are not at all plain-looking. Harold says you are the best style of girl he has seen yet, and sing beautifully. He got a tuner up from Sydney last week, so we will expect you to entertain us every night.”

===Chinese===
“好一个傻丫头！你是生得不像你母亲，可你一点儿也不难看。哈罗德说，你是他至今见过的姑娘里顶上等的一个，还说你歌唱得美妙极了。上礼拜他专门从悉尼请上来一位调音师，所以我们可指望着你夜夜给我们弹唱取乐呢。”

===Original===
I learnt that what Harold pronounced good no one dared gainsay at Five-Bob Downs.

===Chinese===
我这才明白，在五博丘，凡哈罗德品定为好的，没有人敢道半个不字。

===Original===
We proceeded direct to the dining-room, and had not been there long when Mr. Beecham entered with the little girl on his shoulder. Miss Beecham had told me she was Minnie Benson, daughter of Harold’s married overseer on Wyambeet, his adjoining station. Miss Beecham considered it would have been more seemly for her nephew to have selected a little boy as a plaything, but his sentiments regarding boys were that they were machines invented for the torment of adults.

===Chinese===
我们径直进了饭厅，落座没多久，比彻姆先生便肩上扛着那小姑娘走了进来。比彻姆小姐已经告诉过我，她是明妮·本森（Minnie Benson），哈罗德那座毗邻牧场怀安比特（Wyambeet）上他那位成了家的监工的女儿。比彻姆小姐认为，她外甥若挑个小男孩当玩意儿，倒还更得体些；怎奈他对男孩一族的见解是：那本是专为折磨大人而发明出来的机器。

===Original===
“Well, O’Doolan, what sort of a day has it been?” Harold inquired, setting his human toy upon the floor.

===Chinese===
“喂，奥杜兰（O’Doolan），今儿个这一天怎么样啊？”哈罗德问道，一面把他这件活人玩具安放到地板上。

===Original===
“Fine wezzer for yim duts,” she promptly replied.

===Chinese===
“好天七，鸭鸭玩水水。”她脆生生地应声答道。

===Original===
“Harold, it is shameful to teach a little innocent child such abominable slang; and you might give her a decent nickname,” said Miss Beecham.

===Chinese===
“哈罗德，教这么个天真无邪的小娃娃说这样下流的俚语，真不害臊；你也满可以给她起个体面点儿的绰号。”比彻姆小姐说。

===Original===
“O’Doolan, this is Miss Melvyn, and you have to do the same to her as you do to me.”

===Chinese===
“奥杜兰，这位是梅尔文小姐，你待她要像待我一样。”

===Original===
The little thing held out her arms to me. I took her up, and she hugged and kissed me, saying:

===Chinese===
小家伙朝我伸出两条小胳膊。我把她抱起来，她搂着我又亲了亲，说道：

===Original===
“I luz oo, I luz oo,” and turning to Mr. Beecham, “zat anuff?”

===Chinese===
“我爱油，我爱油。”说着又转向比彻姆先生，“够了吗呀？”

===Original===
“Yes, that will do,” he said; and she struggled to be put down.

===Chinese===
“嗯，够了。”他说；她便挣着要下地来。

===Original===
Three jackeroos, an overseer, and two other young men came in, were introduced to me, and then we began dinner.

===Chinese===
三个牧场学徒（jackeroo）、一名监工，另有两位青年男子走了进来，同我一一引见过，我们便开始用晚饭。

===Original===
O’Doolan sat on a high chair beside Mr. Beecham, and he attended to all her wants. She did everything he did, even taking mustard, and was very brave at quelling the tears that rose to the doll-like blue eyes. When Mr. Beecham wiped his moustache, it was amusing to see her also wipe an imaginary one.

===Chinese===
奥杜兰坐在比彻姆先生手边一张高高的椅子上，他把她种种需求照料得妥妥帖帖。他做什么，她也跟着做什么，连芥末也照尝了一点，一面很勇敢地把涌上那双洋娃娃似的蓝眼睛的泪花强压下去。比彻姆先生抹胡子的时候，看她跟着也抹一抹自己那并不存在的胡子，真叫人忍俊不禁。

===Original===
After dinner the jackeroos and the three other men repaired to a sitting-room in the backyard, which was specially set apart for them, and where they amused themselves as they liked. My host and hostess, myself, and the child, spent the evening in a tiny sitting-room adjoining the dining-room. Miss Beecham entertained me with conversation and the family albums, and Harold amused himself entirely with the child.

===Chinese===
晚饭后，牧场学徒们和那另外三个人转移到后院一间专为他们辟出的起居室里，随意取乐去了。我的男女主人、我，还有那孩子，则在紧邻饭厅的一间小小的起居室里消磨这个夜晚。比彻姆小姐拿谈天和家庭相簿来给我解闷，哈罗德则整个儿的心思都在逗那孩子上头。

===Original===
Once when they were absent for a few minutes, Miss Beecham told me it was ridiculous the way he fussed with the child, and that he had her with him more than half his time. She also asked me what I thought of her nephew. I evaded the question by querying if he was always so quiet and good-tempered.

===Chinese===
有一回他们俩走开了几分钟，比彻姆小姐便告诉我，他这么围着一个孩子团团转，实在可笑，他还有一大半的工夫都把她带在身边。她又问我觉得她这位外甥如何。我避而不答，只反问他是不是一向都这么沉静和气。

===Original===
“Oh dear, no. He is considered a particularly bad-tempered man. Not one of the snarling nasty tempers, but﻿—”

===Chinese===
“哎呀，才不呢。人家都说他是个脾气格外坏的人。倒不是那种龇牙咬人、恶声恶气的坏，只是——”

===Original===
Here the reentry of the owner of the temper put a stop to this conversation.

===Chinese===
恰在此时，这位脾气的主人重新进场，这场谈话便打住了。

===Original===
Harold gave O’Doolan rides on his back, going on all-fours. She shouted in childish glee, and wound up by curling her small proportions on his broad chest, and going to sleep there.

===Chinese===
哈罗德趴下四肢当马，驮着奥杜兰满地爬。她乐得尖声直叫，末了把她那一小团身子蜷伏在他宽阔的胸膛上，就在那儿睡着了。

===Original===
Mrs. Benson had sent for little O’Doolan, and Harold took her home next day. He invited me to accompany him, so we set out in the sulky with O’Doolan on my lap. It was a pleasant drive of twelve miles to and from Wyambeet. O’Doolan was much distressed at parting from Mr. Beecham, but he promised to come for her again shortly.

===Chinese===
本森太太（Mrs. Benson）派人来接小奥杜兰，第二天哈罗德便送她回家。他邀我同行，于是我们坐上那辆双轮轻便马车出发，奥杜兰坐在我的膝头上。去怀安比特来回十二英里，一路驱车甚是惬意。同比彻姆先生分别的时候，奥杜兰难过得不得了，他答应过不久再去接她。

===Original===
“One little girl at a time is enough for me to care for properly,” he said to me in the winning manner with which, and his wealth, unintentionally and unconsciously made slaughter among the hearts of the fair sex.

===Chinese===
“一次照看一个小姑娘，尽够我照看得周周全全了。”他对我说这话时那副招人欢喜的风度——正是这风度，连同他的家财，在既非有意又无所知觉之间，于女儿家们的芳心里大开杀戒。

## XVI When Fortune Smiles / 第16章 时来运转

===Original===
“Now, Harold, you have compelled Sybylla to come here, you must not let the time drag with her,” said Miss Beecham.

===Chinese===
“听我说，哈罗德，是你非把西比拉弄到这儿来不可，那你就不许让她的日子在这儿过得无聊乏味。”比彻姆小姐说。

===Original===
It was the second day after my arrival at Five-Bob. Lunch was over, and we had adjourned to the veranda. Miss Beecham was busy at her worktable; I was ensconced on a mat on the floor reading a book; Harold was stretched in a squatter’s chair some distance away. His big brown hands were clasped behind his head, his chin rested on his broad chest, his eyes were closed, he occasionally thrust his lower lip forward and sent a puff of breath upwards to scatter the flies from his face; he looked a big monument of comfort, and answered his aunt’s remarks lazily:

===Chinese===
这是我来到五博丘的第二天。午饭已毕，我们移坐到游廊上。比彻姆小姐在针线桌前忙着她的活计；我窝在地板的一张席子上看书；哈罗德舒展着四肢，躺在不远处一把牧场主宽椅里。他那双棕色大手交扣在脑后，下巴抵着宽阔的胸膛，双眼阖着，时不时撮起下唇往上送一口气，把脸上的苍蝇轰散；他瞧上去俨然一尊安逸的大纪念碑，懒洋洋地应着他姑妈的话：

===Original===
“Yes, aunt, I’ll do my best;” and to me, “Miss Melvyn, while here, please bear in mind that it will be no end of pleasure to me to do anything for your enjoyment. Don’t fail to command me in any way.”

===Chinese===
“哎，姑妈，我一定尽力。”又对我说：“梅尔文小姐，您在此小住期间，请务必记着，但凡能助您尽兴的事，替您效劳都是我无穷的乐事。请尽管随时差遣，万勿客气。”

===Original===
“Thank you, Mr. Beecham. I will not fail to avail myself of your offer.”

===Chinese===
“多谢您，比彻姆先生。您这份情我一定不客气，有事必当仰仗。”

===Original===
“The absurdity of you two children addressing each other so formally,” said Miss Beecham. “Why, you are a sort of cousins almost, by right of old friendship between the families. You must call me aunt.”

===Chinese===
“你们两个孩子，彼此称呼得这么一本正经，实在可笑。”比彻姆小姐说。“嗐，论两家多年的老交情，你们差不多算得上表亲了。你得叫我姑妈。”

===Original===
After this Mr. Beecham and I called each other nothing when in Miss Beecham’s hearing, but adhered to formality on other occasions.

===Chinese===
打这以后，当着比彻姆小姐的面，比彻姆先生和我干脆彼此什么称呼也不用，别的场合却依旧恪守那套客套。

===Original===
Harold looked so comfortable and lazy that I longed to test how far he meant the offer he had made me.

===Chinese===
哈罗德那副安逸惫懒的模样，惹得我很想试探试探，他方才对我许下的那番话，究竟有几分当真。

===Original===
“I’m just dying for a row on the river. Would you oblige me?” I said.

===Chinese===
“我正馋着要上河里划一回船呢。你可肯赏我这个脸？”我说。

===Original===
“Just look at the thermometer!” exclaimed Miss Augusta. “Wait till it gets cooler, child.”

===Chinese===
“你瞧瞧温度计！”奥古斯塔小姐（Miss Augusta）叫道。“等天凉快些再去，孩子。”

===Original===
“Oh, I love the heat!” I replied. “And I am sure it won’t hurt his lordship. He’s used to the sun, to judge from all appearances.”

===Chinese===
“嗐，我就爱这份热！”我答道。“再说，我拿得准它奈何不了这位老爷。瞧他那副模样，分明是晒惯了太阳的。”

===Original===
“Yes, I don’t think it can destroy my complexion,” he said good-humouredly, rubbing his finger and thumb along his stubble-covered chin. The bushmen upcountry shaved regularly every Sunday morning, but never during the week for anything less than a ball. They did this to obviate the blue﻿—what they termed “scraped pig”﻿—appearance of the faces of city men in the habit of using the razor daily, and to which they preferred the stubble of a seven-days’ beard. “I’ll take you to the river in half an hour,” he said, rising from his seat. “First I must stick on one of Warrigal’s shoes that he’s flung. I want him tomorrow, and must do it at once, as he always goes lame if ridden immediately after shoeing.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，我想这点热毁不掉我这张脸皮。”他好脾气地说着，拇指和食指顺着满是胡茬的下巴搓了搓。内地的丛林汉每逢礼拜天早晨必定把脸刮得精光，可平日里若不是为了赴舞会，说什么也不肯动一动剃刀。他们这样，为的是免得脸上生出那种蓝幽幽的颜色——他们管那叫“刮过的猪”——惯于天天使剃刀的城里人，脸上便是那副光景；两相比较，他们宁可留一副七天的胡茬。“过半个钟头我就带你下河去。”他说着站起身来。“先得给瓦里加尔（Warrigal）钉上一片它甩脱的蹄铁。我明天要用它，这活儿得马上做，因为刚钉完蹄就骑，它总要闹腿瘸。”

===Original===
“Shall I blow the bellows?” I volunteered.

===Chinese===
“要不要我来拉风箱？”我自告奋勇。

===Original===
“Oh no, thanks. I can manage myself. It would be better though if I had someone. But I can get one of the girls.”

===Chinese===
“嗐，不用，谢谢。我一个人也应付得来。不过，能有个人搭把手自然更好。我可以唤个打杂的姑娘来。”

===Original===
“Can’t you get one of the boys?” said his aunt.

===Chinese===
“你难道不能唤个小伙子来？”他姑妈说。

===Original===
“There’s not one in. I sent everyone off to the Triangle paddock today to do some drafting. They all took their quart pots and a snack in their saddlebags, and won’t be home till dark.”

===Chinese===
“一个小伙子也不在。今儿个我把人手全打发到三角围场（Triangle paddock）分栏拣羊去了。他们把锡茶壶和干粮都揣进了鞍袋，天黑以前回不来。”

===Original===
“Let me go,” I persisted; “I often blow the bellows for uncle Jay-Jay, and think it great fun.”

===Chinese===
“让我去吧。”我执意道。“我常给杰杰舅舅拉风箱，觉得好玩得很。”

===Original===
The offer of my services being accepted, we set out.

===Chinese===
我这番报效得了应允，我们便动身了。

===Original===
Harold took his favourite horse, Warrigal, from the stable, and led him to the blacksmith’s forge under an open, stringybark-roofed shed, nearly covered with creepers. He lit a fire and put a shoe in it. Doffing his coat and hat, rolling up his shirtsleeves, and donning a leather apron, he began preparing the horse’s hoof.

===Chinese===
哈罗德从马厩里牵出他心爱的坐骑瓦里加尔，把它领到一座敞棚下的打铁炉前——棚顶以条皮桉树皮搭盖，棚身几乎爬满了藤蔓。他生起火，把一片蹄铁埋进火里。他脱去外衣和帽子，卷起衬衫袖子，系上一条皮围裙，便动手修整起马蹄来。

===Original===
When an emergency arose that necessitated uncle Jay-Jay shoeing his horses himself. I always manipulated the bellows, and did so with great decorum, as he was very exacting and I feared his displeasure. In this case it was different. I worked the pole with such energy that it almost blew the whole fire out of the pan, and sent the ashes and sparks in a whirlwind around Harold. The horse﻿—a touchy beast﻿—snorted and dragged his foot from his master’s grasp.

===Chinese===
每逢出岔子、杰杰舅舅不得不亲自给马上蹄铁的当口，掌风箱的总是我，而且我拉得规规矩矩、一丝不苟，因为他顶顶讲究，我怕惹他不痛快。这一回却不然。我鼓风箱那股猛劲儿，险些把一炉火整个儿扇出炉膛，灰烬和火星子打着旋儿，扬了哈罗德一身。那马——一个毛躁性子的畜生——打着响鼻，把蹄子从主人手里猛地挣脱出去。

===Original===
“That the way to blow?” I inquired demurely.

===Chinese===
“就这么个吹法么？”我端着架子问道。

===Original===
“Take things a little easier,” he replied.

===Chinese===
“劲儿悠着点儿。”他答道。

===Original===
I took them so very easily that the fire was on the last gasp and the shoe nearly cold when it was required.

===Chinese===
我悠得又太过了，等到要用蹄铁的时候，炉火只剩奄奄一息，铁也差不多凉透了。

===Original===
“This won’t do,” said Beecham.

===Chinese===
“这可不行。”比彻姆说。

===Original===
I recommenced blowing with such force that he had to retreat.

===Chinese===
我重新猛鼓起来，那风头逼得他连连倒退。

===Original===
“Steady! steady!” he shouted.

===Chinese===
“稳住！稳住！”他喊道。

===Original===
“Sure O’i can’t plaze yez anyhows,” I replied.

===Chinese===
“唉，瞧俺横竖是讨不好您哪。”我答道。

===Original===
“If you don’t try to plaze me directly I’ll punish you in a way you won’t relish,” he said laughingly. But I knew he was thinking of a punishment which I would have secretly enjoyed.

===Chinese===
“你要是再不赶紧着讨我欢心，我可要罚你了，那罚法保管你不受用。”他笑着说。可我心里明白，他正想着的那一罚，我私底下正求之不得呢。

===Original===
“If you don’t let me finish this work I’ll make one of the men do it tonight by candlelight when they come home tired. I know you wouldn’t like them to do that,” he continued.

===Chinese===
“你要是不让我把活儿干完，今晚伙计们累乏了一天回到家，我就点着蜡烛叫他们接着干。我知道你不忍心叫他们受这个。”他又说。

===Original===
“Arrah, go on, ye’re only tazin’!” I retorted. “Don’t you remember telling me that Warrigal was such a nasty-tempered brute that he allowed no one but yourself to touch him?”

===Chinese===
“哎呀，去你的，你不过是在逗俺耍子罢了！”我顶了回去。“你忘了你自己跟我说过，瓦里加尔那畜生脾气坏透了，除了你，谁也不许碰它一根毛？”

===Original===
“Oh well, then, I’m floored, and will have to put up with the consequences,” he good-humouredly made answer.

===Chinese===
“嗬，这么说，我算认栽了，只好领受这个后果喽。”他好脾气地应道。

===Original===
Seeing that my efforts to annoy him failed, I gave in, and we were soon done, and then started for the river﻿—Mr. Beecham clad in a khaki suit and I in a dainty white wrapper and flyaway sort of hat. In one hand my host held a big white umbrella, with which he shaded me from the hot rays of the October sun, and in the other was a small basket containing cake and lollies for our delectation.

===Chinese===
眼见惹恼他的一切努力都落了空，我便服了软；活计不久就完，随后我们动身下河——比彻姆先生穿一身卡其布衣裳，我则是一件精巧的白色罩衫，头上顶一顶轻飘飘的帽子。我的男主人一手擎一把白色大阳伞，替我挡着十月骄阳的毒热，另一只手挽一只小提篮，里头装着糕饼和糖果，供我们一路受用。

===Original===
Having traversed the half-mile between the house and river, we pushed off from the bank in a tiny boat just big enough for two. In the teeth of Harold’s remonstrance I persisted in dangling over the boat-side to dabble in the clear, deep, running water. In a few minutes we were in it. Being unable to swim, but for my companion it would have been all up with me. When I rose to the surface he promptly seized me, and without much effort, clothes and all, swam with me to the bank, where we landed﻿—a pair of sorry figures. Harold had mud all over his nose, and in general looked very ludicrous. As soon as I could stand I laughed.

===Chinese===
走完宅子到河边那半英里地，我们从岸上撑出一条小船，船身才刚容得下我们两个。我不理哈罗德的连声劝阻，执意探身舷外，去撩那又清又深又活的河水。没几分钟，我们俩都在水里了。我不会泅水，若不是身边有这位同伴，我这条命就算交待了。等我冒出水面，他一把揪住我，没费多大力气，连衣带人拖着我游到岸边，我们爬上岸——好一对狼狈相。哈罗德满鼻子是泥，通身上下瞧着滑稽透顶。我刚能站稳脚跟，便放声大笑起来。

===Original===
“Oh, for a snapshot of you!” I said.

===Chinese===
“唉，真该给你拍张快照才好！”我说。

===Original===
“We might have both been drowned,” he said sternly.

===Chinese===
“我们俩说不定都淹死在水里了。”他正色道。

===Original===
“Mights don’t fly,” I returned. “And it was worth the dip to see you looking such a comical article.” We were both minus our hats.

===Chinese===
“‘万一’又不会飞。”我回敬道。“再说，为着瞧你这副滑稽家伙的模样，湿这么一趟也值了。”我们俩的帽子都丢了。

===Original===
His expression relaxed.

===Chinese===
他的神色缓和了下来。

===Original===
“I believe you would laugh at your own funeral. If I look queer, you look forty times worse. Run for your life and get a hot bath and a drop of spirits or you’ll catch your death of cold. Aunt Augusta will take a fit and tie you up for the rest of the time in case something more will happen to you.”

===Chinese===
“我看，你连自己的葬礼都笑得出来。我若算模样古怪，你就比我古怪四十倍。快逃命去，泡个热水澡，抿一口烈酒，不然你要冻出大病来的。奥古斯塔姑妈要吓出个好歹来，往后就把你看管起来，生怕你再出什么乱子。”

===Original===
“Catch a death of cold!” I ejaculated. “It is only good, pretty little girls, who are a blessing to everyone, who die for such trifles; girls like I am always live till nearly ninety, to plague themselves and everybody else. I’ll sneak home so that your aunt won’t see me, and no one need be a bit the wiser.”

===Chinese===
“冻出大病来！”我叫道。“只有那种人人沾光的漂亮小乖乖，才会为这点子小事送命；像我这样的姑娘，偏生都能活到快九十岁，磨折自己，也磨折旁人。我溜回去，不叫你姑妈撞见，谁也不必多知道半分。”

===Original===
“You’ll be sun-struck!” he said in dismay.

===Chinese===
“你要中暑的！”他惶惶地说。

===Original===
“Take care you don’t get daughter-struck,” I said perkily, turning to flee, for it had suddenly dawned upon me that my thin wet clothing was outlining my figure rather too clearly for propriety.

===Chinese===
“那你当心，别中了小闺女的‘暑’。”我脆生生地回了一句，转身便逃，因为我猛然醒悟，一身湿衣裳薄薄地贴在身上，把身形勾得未免太分明，有失体统。

===Original===
By a circuitous way I managed to reach my bedroom unseen. It did not take me long to change my clothes, hang them to dry, and appear on the main veranda where Miss Augusta was still sewing. I picked up the book I had left on the mat, and, taking up a position in a hammock near her, I commenced to read.

===Chinese===
我拣着一条绕弯的僻径，神不知鬼不觉地回到了自己的卧室。换衣裳、晾衣裳、再出现在正游廊上，统共没费我多少工夫；奥古斯塔小姐还在那儿做针线。我拾起撂在席子上的那本书，在她近旁的一张吊床里安顿下来，读开了。

===Original===
“You did not stay long at the river,” she remarked. “Have you been washing your head? I never saw the like of it. Such a mass of it. It will take all day to dry.”

===Chinese===
“你们在河边没待多大工夫啊。”她说。“你是不是洗头来着？我从来没见过那样的头发，好大的一片，怕要晾一整天才干。”

===Original===
Half an hour later Harold appeared dressed in a warm suit of tweed. He was looking pale and languid, as though he had caught a chill, and shivered as he threw himself on a lounge. I was feeling none the worse for my immersion.

===Chinese===
半个钟头后，哈罗德换了一身厚实的粗花呢衣裳露面。他脸色发白，蔫蔫的没精神，像是着了凉，往躺椅上一倒就打起哆嗦。我这一场水泡下来，倒一点儿不见吃亏。

===Original===
“Why did you change your clothes, Harold? You surely weren’t cold on a day like this. Sybylla has changed hers too, when I come to notice it, and her hair is wet. Have you had an accident?” said Miss Augusta, rising from her chair in a startled manner.

===Chinese===
“哈罗德，你怎么换衣裳了？这样的天气，你总不至于冷着了吧。我这才留意到，西比拉也换了衣裳，头发还湿着呢。你们莫不是出了什么岔子？”奥古斯塔小姐说着，慌忙从椅子上站起身。

===Original===
“Rubbish!” ejaculated Harold in a tone which forbade further questioning, and the matter dropped.

===Chinese===
“胡说！”哈罗德喝了一声，那口气不容再问下去，这事便算揭过了。

===Original===
She presently left the veranda, and I took the opportunity to say, “It is yourself that requires the hot bath and a drop of spirits, Mr. Beecham.”

===Chinese===
她随后离了游廊，我逮着机会说道：“要说洗热水澡、抿一口烈酒，正该是您自个儿呢，比彻姆先生。”

===Original===
“Yes; I think I’ll take a good stiff nobbler. I feel a trifle squeamish. It gave me a bit of a turn when I rose to the top and could not see you. I was afraid the boat might have stunned you in capsizing, and you would be drowned before I could find you.”

===Chinese===
“是啊；我想我得好好灌上一盅顶冲的烈酒。我觉着有点儿发虚。方才我浮上水面却望不见你，心里猛地一沉。我怕船翻的时候把你砸昏了过去，等我寻着你，人早就淹死了。”

===Original===
“Yes; I would have been such a loss to the world in general if I had been drowned,” I said satirically.

===Chinese===
“可不是；我若是淹死了，这大千世界可要蒙受不得了的大损失呢。”我讥诮地说。

===Original===
Several jackeroos, a neighbouring squatter, and a couple of bicycle tourists turned up at Five-Bob that evening, and we had a jovial night. The great, richly furnished drawing-room was brilliantly lighted, and the magnificent Erard grand piano sang and rang again with music, now martial and loud, now soft and solemn, now gay and sparkling. I made the very pleasant discovery that Harold Beecham was an excellent pianist, a gifted player on the violin, and sang with a strong, clear, well-trained tenor, which penetrated far into the night. How many, many times I have lived those nights over again! The great room with its rich appointments, the superb piano, the lights, the merriment, the breeze from the east, rich with the heavy intoxicating perfume of countless flowers; the tall perfect figure, holding the violin with a master hand, making it speak the same language as I read in the dark eyes of the musician, while above and around was the soft warmth of an Australian summer night.

===Chinese===
那天傍晚，几个牧场学徒、一位邻场的大牧场主，还有两个骑自行车游历的客人来到了五博丘，我们痛痛快快乐了一夜。那间陈设华贵的大客厅灯火通明，气派堂皇的埃拉德（Erard）大钢琴乐声铮铮，回响不绝——时而雄壮喧亮，时而轻柔庄严，时而欢快明媚。我十分惬意地发现，哈罗德·比彻姆竟是位出色的钢琴家，小提琴拉得天赋横溢，又有一副清亮结实、训练有素的男高音歌喉，歌声直透进沉沉夜色。这样的夜晚，后来我在心里一遍又一遍重历过多少回啊！那间陈设富丽的大厅，那架华贵的钢琴，那些灯火，那些欢笑，那阵东风，饱含着无数花朵浓得醉人的芬芳；那颀长完美的身影，以大师之手执着提琴，让琴吐出的，恰是我从奏乐人那双黑眼睛里读出的同一番言语——而头顶四周，笼着澳洲夏夜一片柔软的温热。

===Original===
Ah, health and wealth, happiness and youth, joy and light, life and love! What a warmhearted place is the world, how full of pleasure, good, and beauty, when fortune smiles! *When fortune smiles!*

===Chinese===
啊，健康与财富，幸福与青春，欢乐与光明，生命与爱情！时来运转之际，这人世间是多么暖心的一方天地，多么充满欢愉、良善与美！*时来运转！*

===Original===
Fortune did smile, and broadly, in those days. We played tricks on one another, and had a deal of innocent fun and frolic. I was a little startled one night on retiring to find a huge goanna near the head of my bed. I called Harold to dislodge the creature, when it came to light that it was roped to the bedpost. Great was the laughter at my expense. Who tethered the goanna I never discovered, but I suspected Harold. In return for this joke, I collected all the portable clocks in the house﻿—about twenty﻿—and arrayed them on his bedroom table. The majority of them were Waterburys for common use, so I set each alarm for a different hour. Inscribing a placard “Hospital for Insane,” I erected it above his door. Next morning I was awakened at three o’clock by fifteen alarms in concert outside my door. When an hour or two later I emerged I found a notice on my door, “This way to the Zoo.”

===Chinese===
那些日子里，命运之神果真笑了，而且笑得开怀。我们彼此恶作剧，享足了天真烂漫的嬉闹。一天夜里我就寝时，发现床头边伏着一条大巨蜥（goanna），吓了一跳。我唤哈罗德来撵那东西，结果真相大白——它是拿绳子拴在床柱上的。众人拿我笑了个体无完肤。是谁拴的巨蜥，我始终没查出来，可我心里疑心是哈罗德。为了回敬这个玩笑，我把宅子里搬得动的钟全收集拢来——约莫二十来座——齐齐摆在他卧室的桌上。其中大半是家常用的沃特伯里（Waterbury）钟，我便把每座的闹铃各拨定一个不同的钟点。又写了一块牌子“疯人院”，钉在他门楣上方。第二天清早三点，我门外十五座闹铃齐声大作，把我闹醒了。过了一个多钟头我出得房来，只见我门上贴着一张告示：“动物园由此前往。”

===Original===
It was a very busy time for the men at Five-Bob. Wagons were arriving with shearing supplies, for it was drawing nigh unto the great event of the year. In another week’s time the bleat of thousands of sheep, and the incense of much tar and wool, would be ascending to the heavens from the vicinity of Five-Bob Downs. I was looking forward to the shearing. There never was any at Caddagat. Uncle did not keep many sheep, and always sold them long-woolled and rebought after shearing.

===Chinese===
这时节，五博丘的男人们忙得不可开交。大车一车车地运到，装载着剪羊毛的物料，因为一年一度的头等盛事已近在眼前。再过一礼拜，千万只羊的咩咩声，和着沥青与羊毛升腾的烟气，就要从五博丘一带袅袅直上云天了。我一心盼着剪羊毛。卡达加特从来没有剪毛这回事。舅舅不养多少羊，向来连毛卖掉，等剪过之后再买回来。

===Original===
I had not much opportunity of persecuting Harold during the daytime. He and all his subordinates were away all day, busy drafting, sorting, and otherwise pottering with sheep. But I always, and Miss Augusta sometimes, went to meet them coming home in the evening. It was great fun. The dogs yelped and jumped about. The men were dirty with much dust, and smelt powerfully of sheep, and had worked hard all day in the blazing sun, but they were never too tired for fun, or at night to dance, after they had bathed and dressed. We all had splendid horses. They reared and pranced; we galloped and jumped every log which came in our path. Jokes, repartee, and nonsense rattled off our tongues. We did not worry about thousands of our fellows﻿—starving and reeking with disease in city slums. We were selfish. We were heedless. We were happy. We were young.

===Chinese===
白天里我难得有机会去搅扰哈罗德。他和他手下的伙计们成天在外，忙着分栏、拣羊，围着羊群打转。可傍晚他们回家，我回回都去迎，奥古斯塔小姐有时也去。那才叫有趣。狗儿们又叫又跳。伙计们满身尘土，一股浓重的羊臊气，在毒日头底下苦干了一整天，可他们从不曾累得没了兴致，夜里洗过澡换过衣裳，照旧跳舞。我们人人都有顶好的马。它们人立腾跃；我们放蹄飞驰，逢着当路的每一根倒木都要跳过去。玩笑、斗嘴、浑话从我们舌尖上滚滚而出。我们不惦念成千上万的同类——他们正在城市的贫民窟里挨着饿、泡在疾病里发着臭。我们自私。我们没心没肺。我们快乐。我们年轻。

===Original===
Harold Beecham was a splendid host. Anyone possessed of the least talent for enjoyment had a pleasant time as his guest. He was hospitable in a quiet unostentatious manner. His overseer, jackeroos, and other employees were all allowed the freedom of home, and could invite whom they pleased to Five-Bob Downs. It is all very well to talk of good hosts. Bah, I could be a good hostess myself if I had Harold Beecham’s superior implements of the art! With an immense station, plenty of houseroom, tennis courts, musical instruments; a river wherein to fish, swim, and boat; any number of horses, vehicles, orchards, gardens, guns, and ammunition no object, it is easy to be a good host.

===Chinese===
哈罗德·比彻姆是位了不得的东道。但凡稍有些许享乐天分的人，做他的客人都过得快活。他好客，好得安静而不事张扬。他的监工、牧场学徒和其余雇工，人人都享着宾至如归的自由，爱请谁来五博丘做客就请谁来。说什么好客的主人，呸，我自己也能做个好客的女主人，只要我有哈罗德·比彻姆那套办这门艺术的家什！坐拥偌大一片牧场，有的是房舍、网球场、乐器；有一条能钓鱼、游泳、泛舟的河；马匹车辆要多少有多少，果园花园、枪支弹药一概不计成本——这样子，做个好客的主人有何难。

===Original===
I had been just a week at Five-Bob when uncle Julius came to take me home, so I missed the shearing. Caddagat had been a dull hole without me, he averred, and I must return with him that very day. Mr. and Miss Beecham remonstrated. Could I not be spared at least a fortnight longer? It would be lonely without me. Thereupon uncle Jay-Jay volunteered to procure Miss Benson from Wyambeet as a substitute. Harold declined the offer with thanks.

===Chinese===
我在五博丘刚住满一个礼拜，朱利叶斯舅舅便来接我回家了，于是我错过了剪羊毛。他一口咬定，卡达加特没了我成了个闷死人的窟窿，我必须当天就随他动身。比彻姆先生和比彻姆小姐再三挽留：难道连再宽限半个月也不成吗？没了我，家里要冷清的。杰杰舅舅当即自告奋勇，说要去怀安比特把本森小姐接来顶替。哈罗德道了谢，把这番好意辞了。

===Original===
“The schemes of youngsters are very transparent,” said uncle Jay-Jay and Miss Augusta, smiling significantly at us. I feigned to be dense, but Harold smiled as though the insinuation was not only known, but also agreeable to him.

===Chinese===
“年轻人心里那点小算盘，可是瞒不了人哪。”杰杰舅舅和奥古斯塔小姐说着，意味深长地朝我们微笑。我装傻充愣，哈罗德却笑吟吟的，仿佛这弦外之音不但听明白了，还正中下怀。

===Original===
Uncle was inexorable, so home I had to go. It was sweet to me to hear from the lips of my grandmother and aunt that my absence had been felt.

===Chinese===
舅舅毫不含糊，我只得回家。听外婆和姨妈亲口说，我不在家的这些日子她们真觉得缺了什么，这话在我心里甜丝丝的。

===Original===
As a confidante aunt Helen was the pink of perfection﻿—tactful and sympathetic. My featherbrained chatter must often have bored her, but she apparently was ever interested in it.

===Chinese===
说到做知己人，海伦姨妈真是十全十美——既有分寸，又解人意。我这一脑子轻飘飘的闲话，想必要常烦了她，可她瞧上去偏偏回回听得津津有味。

===Original===
I told her long yarns of how I had spent my time at the Beechams; of the deafening duets Harold and I had played on the piano; and how he would persist in dancing with me, and he being so tall and broad, and I so small, it was like being stretched on a hayrack, and very fatiguing. I gave a graphic account of the arguments﻿—tough ones they were too﻿—that Miss Augusta had with the overseer on religion, and many other subjects; of one jackeroo who gabbed never-endingly about his great relations at home; another who incessantly clattered about spurs, whips, horses, and sport; and the third one﻿—Joe Archer﻿—who talked literature and trash with me.

===Chinese===
我给她讲了长长的闲篇儿，讲我在比彻姆家是怎么消磨时日的；讲哈罗德和我在钢琴上弹的那些震耳欲聋的二重奏；讲他偏偏非要同我跳舞不可，他又高又宽，我又这么小巧，简直像被人抻在干草架上，累煞人也。我还活灵活现地讲了奥古斯塔小姐同监工的那些争辩——场场都是硬仗——从宗教辩到许许多多别的题目；讲了一个牧场学徒没完没了地吹嘘他家里那些了不起的亲戚；另一个满口不离马刺、鞭子、马匹和玩耍；第三个——乔·阿彻（Joe Archer）——同我谈文学，也扯些没要紧的闲话。

===Original===
“What was Harry doing all this time?” asked auntie. “What did he say?”

===Chinese===
“这半天哈里都在做什么呀？”姨妈问。“他说了些什么？”

===Original===
Harold had been present all the while, yet I could not call to mind one thing he had said. I cannot remember him ever holding forth on a subject or cause, as most people do at one time or another.

===Chinese===
哈罗德自始至终都在场，我却想不起他说过的哪一句话。我不记得他几时对什么题目或主张大发过议论——大多数人早晚总有那样的场合，他却从来没有。

## XVII Idylls of Youth / 第17章 青春牧歌

===Original===
In pursuance of his duty a government mail-contractor passed Caddagat every Monday, dropping the Bossier mail as he went. On Thursday we also got the post, but had to depend partly on our own exertions.

===Chinese===
一个承办公务的邮政承包人，为着职守所在，每逢星期一必经卡达加特，路过时把博西耶家的邮件卸下。星期四我们也有一班邮，只是有一部分得靠自家出力。

===Original===
A selector at Dogtrap, on the Wyambeet run, at a point of the compass ten miles down the road from Caddagat, kept a hooded van. Every Thursday he ran this to and from Gool-Gool for the purpose of taking to market vegetables and other farm produce. He also took parcels and passengers, both ways, if called upon to do so. Caddagat and Five-Bob gave him a great deal of carrying, and he brought the mail for these and two or three other places. It was one of my duties, or rather privileges, to ride thither on Thursday afternoon for the post, a leather bag slung round my shoulders for the purpose. I always had a splendid mount, and the weather being beautifully hot, it was a jaunt which I never failed to enjoy. Frank Hawden went with me once or twice﻿—not because grannie or I thought his escort necessary. The idea was his own; but I gave him such a time that he was forced to relinquish accompanying me as a bad job.

===Chinese===
狗陷（Dogtrap）在怀安比特（Wyambeet）牧区之内，打卡达加特沿大路朝那头走十英里便是；那儿有个选地农（selector），备着一辆带篷货车。每逢星期四，他赶着这辆车往返古尔古尔（Gool-Gool），好把蔬菜和别的农产品运去赶集；有人招呼，他也两头捎带包裹、载客。卡达加特和五博丘给他不少运输的活计，他还替这两处连同旁的两三个地方捎邮件。每星期四下午骑马去那儿取邮，是我的一桩差事——毋宁说是一桩殊荣——为这个，我肩上挎着一只专用皮包。我的坐骑回回都极出色，天气又热得可爱，这一趟出游，我从没有不尽兴的。弗兰克·霍登（Frank Hawden）陪我走过一两回——倒不是外婆或我觉得需要他护送；主意是他自个儿出的。可我把他整治得够呛，他只得死了心，撂开这桩砸了锅的差事。

===Original===
Harold Beecham kept a snivelling little Queensland black boy as a sort of black-your-boots, odd-jobs slavey or factotum, and he came to Dogtrap for the mail, but after I started to ride for it Harold came regularly for his mail himself. Our homeward way lay together for two miles, but he always came with me till nearly in sight of home. Some days we raced till our horses were white with lather; and once or twice mine was in such a state that we dismounted, and Harold unsaddled him and wiped the sweat off with his towel saddlecloth, to remove the evidence of hard riding, so that I would not get into a scrape with uncle Jay-Jay. Other times we dawdled, so that when we parted the last rays of sunset would be laughing at us between the white trunks of the tall gum-trees, the kookaburras would be making the echoes ring with their mocking good night, and scores of wild duck would be flying quickly roostward. As I passed through the angle formed by the creek and the river, about half a mile from home, there came to my ears the cheery clink-clink of hobble-chains, the jangle of horse-bells, and the gleam of a dozen campfires. The shearing was done out in Riverina now, and the men were all going home. Day after day dozens of them passed along the long white road, bound for Monaro and the cool country beyond the blue peaks to the southeast, where the shearing was about to begin. When I had come to Caddagat the last of them had gone “down” with horses poor; now they were travelling “up” with their horses﻿—some of them thoroughbreds﻿—rolling fat, and a cheque for their weeks of back-bending labour in their pockets. But whether coming or going they always made to Caddagat to camp. That camping-ground was renowned as the best from Monaro to Riverina. It was a well-watered and sheltered nook, and the ground was so rich that there was always a mouthful of grass to be had there. It was a rare thing to see it without a fire; and the empty jam-tins, bottles, bits of bag, paper, tent-pegs, and fish-tins to be found there would have loaded a dozen wagons.

===Chinese===
哈罗德·比彻姆（Harold Beecham）使唤着一个爱淌鼻涕的昆士兰土著黑人小男孩，充作擦靴打杂的小听差——或者说万能仆役。从前是这孩子到狗陷取邮件；自我接下这趟差，哈罗德便回回亲自来取他的信了。我们回家的路有两英里同路，他却总陪我走到几乎望得见家门的地方。有些日子我们纵马竞逐，直赛得马身汗沫泛白；有一两回我的马落到那副模样，我们便下了马，哈罗德卸下鞍子，用毛巾布的鞍垫替它把汗擦净，好抹去狂奔的罪证，免得我在杰杰舅舅（uncle Jay-Jay）跟前吃排头。另一些日子我们便磨磨蹭蹭，磨到分手时，落日最后的光线正在高大的桉树（gum-tree）白干之间冲我们笑，笑翠鸟（kookaburra）正用嘲弄的晚安叫回声震荡，成群的野鸭匆匆飞向宿处。行到家半英里外、小溪与大河夹成的那个角上，一片欢快的声响便送进我耳朵——拴马链的脆响叮当，马铃的喧闹，还望得见十几堆营火的微光。这时里弗赖纳（Riverina）那边的剪毛活计已经收场，人们正纷纷回家。日复一日，成打的人沿着长长的白路经过，往莫纳罗（Monaro）去，往东南方蓝色峰峦外那片凉爽地方去——那边的剪毛就要开场。我刚到卡达加特那阵子，他们末一批正赶着瘦马“下”去；如今他们又“上”来了，马匹——有几匹还是纯种马——滚瓜流油，兜里揣着几个礼拜弯腰苦干换来的一张支票。但无论来去，他们总要到卡达加特来扎营。那片营地是出了名的，从莫纳罗到里弗赖纳数它第一：水源充足，藏风避寒，地皮又肥，总有一口草可寻。难得见它不生火的时候；在那儿捡得出的空果酱罐、瓶子、破麻袋片、纸头、帐篷桩、鱼罐头，装满一打大车也绰绰有余。

===Original===
Thursday evening was always spent in going to Dogtrap, and all the other days had their pleasant tasks and were full of wholesome enjoyment. The blue senna flowers along the river gave place to the white bloom of the tea-tree. Grannie, uncle, and aunt Helen filled the house with girl visitors for my pleasure. In the late afternoon, as the weather got hot, we went for bogeys in a part of the river two miles distant. Some of the girls from neighbouring runs brought their saddles, others from town had to be provided therewith, which produced a dearth in sidesaddles, and it was necessary for me to take a man’s. With a rollicking gallop and a bogey ahead, that did not trouble me. Aunt Helen always accompanied us on our bathing expeditions to keep us in check. She was the only one who bothered with a bathing-dress. The rest of us reefed off our clothing, in our hurry sending buttons in all directions, and plunged into the pleasant water. Then﻿—such water-fights, frolic, laughter, shouting and roaring fun as a dozen strong healthy girls can make when enjoying themselves. Aunt Helen generally called time before we were half inclined to leave. We would linger too long, then there would be a great scramble for clothes, next for horses, and with wet hair streaming on our towels, we would go home full belt, twelve sets of galloping hoofs making a royal clatter on the hard dusty road. Grannie made a rule that when we arrived late we had to unsaddle our horses ourselves, and not disturb the working men from their meal for our pleasure. We mostly were late, and so there would be a tight race to see who would arrive at table first. A dozen heated horses were turned out unceremoniously, a dozen saddles and bridles dumped down anywhere anyhow, and their occupants, with wet dishevelled hair and clothing in glorious disarray, would appear at table averring that they were starving.

===Chinese===
星期四傍晚总是消磨在去狗陷的路上，其余的日子也各有各的乐子差事，满载着健康的欢愉。河畔决明灌木（senna）的蓝花谢了场，换上茶树（tea-tree）的白花。外婆、舅舅和海伦姨妈（aunt Helen）为叫我开心，把屋子里填满了一拨拨来做客的姑娘。午后向晚、天气热起来时，我们便到两英里外的一段河里去泡澡（bogey）。邻牧区来的姑娘们自带着马鞍，镇上来的则要主人家备鞍，于是侧鞍闹了饥荒，我只得用男人的鞍子。前头有撒欢的疾驰，又有泡澡候着，这算不得什么。海伦姨妈每回都陪着我们的戏水队伍，好约束我们。她是唯一肯费事穿浴衣的人。我们其余的人把衣裳扒个精光，慌里慌张崩得扣子四下乱飞，便一头扎进那快活的水里。于是一场水仗、一番打闹，十来个健壮姑娘尽情取乐时才有的那种笑嚷欢腾。海伦姨妈总在我们还没玩够一半时便喊停。我们总要恋过了头，于是一阵大乱：先抢衣裳，再抢马，湿头发披散在毛巾上，一路全速飞驰回家，十二副疾奔的马蹄把干硬的土路踏得一片堂皇的响。外婆立下规矩：回家晚了，须得自己卸鞍，不许为我们的乐子搅了干活的男人们用饭。我们多半要晚，于是一场紧绷绷的赛跑，看谁先挨着饭桌。十来匹跑热了的马被人草草撂了出去，十来副鞍子辔头随处乱丢，它们的主人则披着湿淋淋的乱发、衣衫散乱得光鲜热闹，出现在饭桌边，一口咬定自己快饿死了。

===Original===
The Caddagat folk were enthusiastic anglers. Fishing was a favourite and often enjoyed amusement of the household. In the afternoon a tinful of worms would be dug out of one of the water-races, tackle collected, horses saddled, and grannie, uncle, aunt, Frank Hawden, myself, and anyone else who had happened to drop in, would repair to the fish-holes three miles distant. I hate fishing. Ugh! The hideous barbarity of shoving a hook through a living worm, and the cruelty of taking the fish off the hook! Uncle allowed no idlers at the river﻿—all had to manipulate a rod and line. Indulging in pleasant air-castles, I generally forgot my cork till the rod would be jerked in my hand, when I would pull﻿—too late! the fish would be gone. Uncle would lecture me for being a jackdaw, so next time I would glare at the cork unwinkingly, and pull at the first signs of it bobbing﻿—too soon! the fish would escape again, and I would again be in disgrace. After a little experience I found it was a good plan to be civil to Frank Hawden when the prospect of fishing hung around, and then he would attend to my line as well as his own, while I read a book which I smuggled with me. The fish-hole was such a shrub-hidden nook that, though the main road passed within two hundred yards, neither we nor our horses could be seen by the travellers thereon. I lay on the soft moss and leaves and drank deeply of the beauties of nature. The soft rush of the river, the scent of the shrubs, the golden sunset, occasionally the musical clatter of hoofs on the road, the gentle noises of the fishers fishing, the plop, plop of a platypus disporting itself mid stream, came to me as sweetest elixir in my ideal, dream-of-a-poet nook among the pink-based, grey-topped, moss-carpeted rocks.

===Chinese===
卡达加特一家子都是热心的钓客。钓鱼是这户人家喜爱而常享的消遣。午后，从一条灌渠里挖出一罐蚯蚓，凑齐钓具，备好马鞍，外婆、舅舅、姨妈、弗兰克·霍登、我，再搭上碰巧上门的随便什么人，便开拔到三英里外的钓潭去。我恨钓鱼。呸！把钩子扎过一条活蚯蚓，何其野蛮；把鱼打钩上摘下来，又何其残忍！舅舅不许谁在河边闲站——人人都得操纵一根钓竿。我耽于构筑空中楼阁，总把浮子忘个干净，直待钓竿在手里猛地一抖才拉——晚啦！鱼早跑了。舅舅训我是个三心二意的寒鸦（jackdaw）；下一回我便一眨不眨地死盯浮子，一见它点头就拉——早啦！鱼又跑了，我又讨了场没趣。吃过几回亏，我发觉有个好办法：眼看钓鱼的行市又要起来，便对弗兰克·霍登客气些，他自会把我的钓线连同他自己的一并照应，我就读我偷带来的书。那钓潭藏得那样隐蔽，大路虽打两百码外经过，路上的行人同我们的马谁也瞧不见谁。我躺在软软的苔藓和落叶上，痛饮自然之美。河水柔缓的奔流，灌木的芬芳，金色的落日，路上偶尔一阵马蹄的音乐，垂钓人轻轻的声息，一只鸭嘴兽在河心戏水，扑通、扑通——这一切，在我那诗人梦境般的隐蔽角落里，在那底子粉红、顶盖灰白、苔藓为毯的岩石间，滴滴都成了最甜的琼浆。

===Original===
I was a creature of joy in those days. Life is made up of little things. It was a small thing to have a little pocket-money to spend on anything that took my fancy﻿—a very small thing, and yet how much pleasure it gave me. Though eating is not one of the great aims of my life, yet it was nice to have enough of any delicacy one fancied. Not that we ever went hungry at home, but when one has nothing to eat in the hot weather but bread and beef it gives them tendency to dream of fruit and cool dainties. When one thinks of the countless army of one’s fellows who are daily selling their very souls for the barest necessaries of life, I suppose we﻿—irresponsible beings﻿—should be thankful to God for allowing us, by scratching and scraping all our lives, to keep a crust in our mouth and a rag on our back. I am not thankful, I have been guilty of what Pat would term a “digresshion”﻿—I started about going for the mail at Dogtrap. Harold Beecham never once missed taking me home on Thursdays, even when his shearing was in full swing and he must have been very busy. He never once uttered a word of love to me﻿—not so much as one of the soft nothings in which young people of opposite sexes often deal without any particular significance. Whether he went to all the bother and waste of time accruing from escorting me home out of gentlemanliness alone, was a mystery to me. I desired to find out, and resolved to drive instead of ride to Dogtrap one day to see what he would say.

===Chinese===
那些日子里，我是个欢乐的生灵。生活原是由小事缀成的。有点儿零花钱，见着合意的东西便可买下——这是桩小事，很小很小的事，却给了我多少欢喜。吃虽算不得我人生的大目标，可想要的哪样精美吃食都管够，也委实惬意。倒不是家里挨过饿，只是大热天里除了面包牛肉再没别的入口，人便要朝思暮想水果和凉丝丝的甜点了。想到自己的同类之中有千军万马，天天为最起码的糊口之需出卖灵魂，我想，我们这些不负责任的东西，倒该感谢上帝，容我们一辈子抓抓挠挠，嘴里保得住一块干面包皮，背上保得住一片遮身布。我可不去感谢。我犯了帕特（Pat）所谓“跑题儿”（digresshion）的毛病——我原本要讲的是每星期四去狗陷取邮的事。哈罗德·比彻姆从没误过一回送我回家，连他剪羊毛正忙、想必分身乏术的时候也不例外。他从没对我吐过一个情字——连青年男女之间不当真抛来抛去的软绵绵空话，也不曾有过一句。他这般不嫌麻烦、不惜工夫地护送我回家，究竟单单是出于绅士风度，对我说来是个谜。我想探个明白，便打定主意：哪天改骑马为驾车去狗陷，看他怎么说。

===Original===
Grannie assented to the project. Of course I could drive for once if I didn’t feel able to ride, but the horses had been spelling for a long time and were very frisky. I must take Frank with me or I might get my neck broken.

===Chinese===
外婆准了这个打算。若是一时骑不得马，驾一回车当然无妨；只是那两匹马歇了很久，正撒着欢儿地躁动。我必须带上弗兰克，不然怕要摔断脖子的。

===Original===
I flatly opposed the idea of Frank Hawden going with me. He would make a mull of the whole thing. It was no use arguing with grannie and impressing upon her the fact that I was not the least nervous concerning the horses. I could take Frank with me in the buggy, ride, or stay at home. I preferred driving. Accordingly the fat horses were harnessed to the buggy, and with many injunctions to be careful and not forget the parcels, we set out. Frank Hawden’s presence spoilt it all, but I determined to soon make short work of him.

===Chinese===
我斩钉截铁地反对弗兰克·霍登同行这桩主意。有他在，整件事都得砸锅。同外婆争辩、向她申明我对这两匹马没有半点儿发怵，全是白费唇舌。我要么带弗兰克同车，要么骑马，要么待在家里。我拣了驾车。于是两匹肥壮的马套上了那辆四轮轻便马车（buggy），千叮万嘱要我小心、别忘了包裹，我们便出发了。弗兰克·霍登一在场，兴致全毁，可我打定主意，少顷便要把他打发个干净。

===Original===
There was one gate to go through, about four miles from the house. Frank Hawden got out to open it. I drove through, and while he was pushing it to, laid the whip on the horses and went off full tilt. He ran after me shouting all manner of things that I could not hear on account of the rattle of the buggy. One horse began kicking up, so, to give him no time for further pranks, I drove at a good round gallop, which quickly left the lovable jackeroo a speck in the distance. The dust rose in thick clouds, the stones rattled from the whirling wheels, the chirr! chirr! of a myriad cicadas filled the air, and the white road glistened in the dazzling sunlight. I was enjoying myself tip-top, and chuckled to think of the way I had euchred Frank Hawden. It was such a good joke that I considered it worth two of the blowings-up I was sure of getting from grannie for my conduct.

===Chinese===
离家约四英里处有一道门要过。弗兰克·霍登下去开门。我驱车过去，趁他推门掩合的当口，一鞭子抽在马身上，撒开蹄子全速窜了。他撒腿追来，嚷些什么我全听不见——车声哐当得太响。有一匹马尥起了蹶子，我索性放开一轮匀溜溜的好奔驰，不给它再撒野的工夫，这一下很快便把那位可爱的牧场学徒（jackeroo）甩成了天边一粒黑点儿。尘土腾起浓云，石子打飞旋的车轮下噼啪迸溅，万千蝉儿的知了！知了！灌满天空，白花花的路在晃眼的日头底下熠熠发亮。我快活到了顶点，想起我摆布弗兰克·霍登的这一手，直在心里咯咯地笑。这玩笑开得实在妙，我认定哪怕外婆准要为我的行径痛骂我两顿，这也值回了票价。

===Original===
It was not long before I fetched up at Dogtrap homestead, where, tethered to the “six-foot” paling fence which surrounded the flower-garden, was Harold Beecham’s favourite, great, black, saddle-horse Warrigal. The vicious brute turned his beautiful head, displaying a white star on the forehead, and snorted as I approached. His master appeared on the veranda raising his soft panama hat, and remarking, “Well I never! You’re not by yourself, are you?”

===Chinese===
没多久我便到了狗陷的庄舍。花圃四周围着“六英尺”高的板条栅栏，栏上拴着哈罗德·比彻姆最心爱的大黑鞍马瓦里加尔（Warrigal）。那匹凶悍的牲口听我走近，转过头来——好一颗俊美的头颅——亮出额上一枚白星，打了个响鼻。它的主人出现在游廊上，举了举那顶软软的巴拿马草帽，说道：“哎哟，真是稀奇！你总不是一个人来的吧？”

===Original===
