“I am. Would you please tell Mrs. Butler to bring out grannie’s parcels and post at once. I’m afraid to dawdle, it’s getting late.”

===Chinese===
“就是一个人。劳驾请巴特勒太太（Mrs. Butler）把外婆的包裹和邮件这就拿出来。我不敢磨蹭，天要晚啦。”

===Original===
He disappeared to execute my request and reappeared in less than a minute.

===Chinese===
他进去办我这桩差遣，不到一分钟便回来了。

===Original===
“Mr. Beecham, please would you examine Barney’s harness. Something must be hurting him. He has been kicking up all the way.”

===Chinese===
“比彻姆先生，麻烦你查一查巴尼（Barney）的套具。准是什么东西硌着它了。它一路上尥个不停。”

===Original===
Examining the harness and noticing the sweat that was dripping from the animals, panting from their run, he said:

===Chinese===
他检查着套具，又瞧见两匹牲口跑得直喘、汗水直滴，便说：

===Original===
“It looks as though you’ve been making the pace a cracker. There is nothing that is irritating Barney in the least. If he’s putting on any airs it is because he is frisky and not safe for you to drive. How did Julius happen to let you away by yourself?”

===Chinese===
“瞧这光景，你把车赶得跟放炮仗似的。巴尼身上没有半点儿硌它的东西。它若是拿乔，那是因为它躁得欢，你驾着不稳当。朱利叶斯（Julius）怎么会放你一个人出来？”

===Original===
“I’m not frightened,” I replied.

===Chinese===
“我不怕。”我答道。

===Original===
“I see you’re not. You’d be game to tackle a pair of wild elephants, I know, but you must remember you’re not much bigger than a sparrow sitting up there, and I won’t let you go back by yourself.”

===Chinese===
“看得出你不怕。我知道，就是一对野象你也敢招架；可你得记着，你坐在那上头，也就比一只麻雀大不了多少，我不能让你一个人回去。”

===Original===
“You cannot stop me.”

===Chinese===
“你拦不住我。”

===Original===
“I can.”

===Chinese===
“拦得住。”

===Original===
“You can’t.”

===Chinese===
“你拦不住。”

===Original===
“I can.”

===Chinese===
“拦得住。”

===Original===
“You can’t.”

===Chinese===
“你拦不住。”

===Original===
“I can.”

===Chinese===
“拦得住。”

===Original===
“How?”

===Chinese===
“怎么拦？”

===Original===
“I’m going with you,” he said.

===Chinese===
“我同你一道走。”他说。

===Original===
“You’re not.”

===Chinese===
“你才不。”

===Original===
“I am.”

===Chinese===
“我就去。”

===Original===
“You’re not.”

===Chinese===
“你才不。”

===Original===
“I am.”

===Chinese===
“我就去。”

===Original===
“You ar-r-re not.”

===Chinese===
“你就不——不——不去。”

===Original===
“I am.”

===Chinese===
“就去。”

===Original===
“You are, ar-r-re not.”

===Chinese===
“你呀——呀——呀，才不呢。”

===Original===
“We’ll see whether I will or not in a minute or two,” he said with amusement.

===Chinese===
“过一两分钟，看我到底去不去。”他觉得有趣地说。

===Original===
“But, Mr. Beecham, I object to your company. I am quite capable of taking care of myself; besides, if you come home with me I will not be allowed out alone again﻿—it will be altogether unpleasant for me.”

===Chinese===
“可是，比彻姆先生，我不领教你的作陪。我完全照管得了自己；再者，你若送我回家，往后人家再不许我一个人出门了——这于我大大的不痛快。”

===Original===
Mrs. Butler now appeared with the mail and some parcels, and Harold stowed them in the buggy.

===Chinese===
这时巴特勒太太捧着邮件和几个包裹出来了，哈罗德把它们收进了车厢。

===Original===
“You’d better come in an’ ’ave a drop of tay-warter, miss, the kittle’s bilin’; and I have the table laid out for both of yez.”

===Chinese===
“小姐，还是进来喝口茶——茶水儿吧，壶正滚开着哩；俺把桌子都给你们俩摆好啦。”

===Original===
“No, thank you, Mrs. Butler. I can’t possibly stay today, it’s getting late. I must hurry off. Goodbye! Good afternoon, Mr. Beecham.”

===Chinese===
“不啦，谢谢你，巴特勒太太。我今天实在不能耽搁，天晚了，得赶紧走。再见！再会，比彻姆先生。”

===Original===
I turned my buggy and pair smartly round and was swooping off. Without a word Harold was at their heads and seized the reins. He seized his horse’s bridle, where it was over the paling, and in a moment had him tied on the offside of Barney, then stepping quietly into the buggy he put me away from the driver’s seat as though I were a baby, quietly took the reins and whip, raised his hat to Mrs. Butler, who was smiling knowingly, and drove off.

===Chinese===
我利落地调转那对马和车，正要疾驰而去。哈罗德一言不发，已抢到马头前攥住了缰绳。他从栅栏上解过自己那匹马的笼头，转眼便把它拴到巴尼外侧的套位上，随即悄然跨进车厢，把我从驭座上挪开——像挪一个婴孩似的——不声不响接过缰绳和鞭子，朝含笑会意的巴特勒太太举了举帽，驱车而去。

===Original===
I was highly delighted with his action, as I would have despised him as a booby had he given in to me, but I did not let my satisfaction appear. I sat as far away from him as possible, and pretended to be in a great huff. For a while he was too fully occupied in making Barney “sit up” to notice me, but after a few minutes he looked round, smiling a most annoying and pleasant smile.

===Chinese===
他这一手令我心里大乐——他若依了我，我倒要鄙他是个窝囊废——只是我不肯把得意挂在脸上。我坐得离他远远的，装出一副气鼓鼓的模样。有一阵子他忙着叫巴尼“打起精神走正步”，无暇理会我；过了几分钟，他回过头来，绽出一个又可恼又可意的笑。

===Original===
“I’d advise you to straighten out your chin. It is too round and soft to look well screwed up that way,” he said provokingly.

===Chinese===
“我劝你把下巴放平了。它生得太圆太软，这样绷着可不好看。”他撩拨着说。

===Original===
I tried to extinguish him with a look, but it had not the desired effect.

===Chinese===
我试图拿眼色灭他的威风，却没收到指望的效果。

===Original===
“Now you had better be civil, for I have got the big end of the whip,” he said.

===Chinese===
“如今你最好放客气些，鞭子的大头可攥在我手里。”他说。

===Original===
“I reserve to myself the right of behaving as I think fit in my own uncle’s buggy. You are an intruder; it is yourself that should be civil.”

===Chinese===
“在我亲舅舅自己的马车里，我保留随心行事的权柄。你是个不速之客；该客气的恰恰是你自己。”

===Original===
I erected my parasol and held it so as to tease Harold. I put it down so that he could not see the horses. He quietly seized my wrist and held it out of his way for a time, and then loosing me said, “Now, behave.”

===Chinese===
我撑起阳伞，擎着它逗弄哈罗德。我把它压低，好挡住他看马的视线。他安安静静攥住我的手腕，把我的手挡开了一阵，随后一松，说：“放规矩些。”

===Original===
I flouted it now, so that his ears and eyes were endangered, and he was forced to hold his hat on.

===Chinese===
这回我索性乱挥一气，眼看他的耳朵眼睛都要遭殃，逼得他只好一手按住帽子。

===Original===
“I’ll give you three minutes to behave, or I’ll put you out,” he said with mock severity.

===Chinese===
“给你三分钟放规矩，不然就把你撂下车去。”他拿腔作势地板着脸说。

===Original===
“Shure it’s me wot’s behavin’ beautiful,” I replied, continuing my nonsense.

===Chinese===
“可不，俺才叫规矩得漂亮哩。”我答道，胡闹如故。

===Original===
He pulled rein, seized me in one arm, and lifted me lightly to the ground.

===Chinese===
他勒住缰，一条胳膊抄起我，轻轻把我放到了地上。

===Original===
“Now, you can walk till you promise to conduct yourself like a Christian!” he said, driving at a walk.

===Chinese===
“好啦，你就走着吧，几时答应像个基督徒似的规矩行事，几时再说！”他说着，驱车徐行。

===Original===
“If you wait till I promise anything, you’ll wait till the end of the century. I’m quite capable of walking home.”

===Chinese===
“你要等我心甘情愿应承什么，就直等到世纪末去吧。我完全走得回去。”

===Original===
“You’ll soon get tired of walking in this heat, and your feet will he blistered in a mile with those bits of paper.”

===Chinese===
“这么热的天，走不了多会儿你就腻了；穿着那两片纸，一英里路就得把你的脚磨出泡来。”

===Original===
The bits of paper to which he alluded were a pair of thin-soled white canvas slippers﻿—not at all fitted for walking the eight miles on the hard hot road ahead of me. I walked resolutely on, without deigning a glance at Harold, who had slowed down to a crawling walk.

===Chinese===
他指的那两片纸，是一双薄底的白帆布便鞋——全然不配走我前头这段又硬又烫的八英里路。我径自坚决前行，连眼角也不肯赏给哈罗德——他已放缓到蜗牛般的踱步。

===Original===
“Aren’t you ready to get up now?” he inquired presently.

===Chinese===
“这会儿肯上车了吗？”过了一会儿他问道。

===Original===
I did not reply. At the end of a quarter of a mile he jumped out of the buggy, seized upon me, lifted me in, and laughed, saying, “You’re a very slashing little concern, but you are not big enough to do much damage.”

===Chinese===
我不答话。走到四分之一英里尽头，他跳下车，一把逮住我，拎进车里，笑道：“你真是个泼辣痛快的小家伙，只是块头不够，闯不下多大的祸。”

===Original===
We were about halfway home when Barney gave a tremendous lurch, breaking a trace and some other straps. Mr. Beecham was at the head of the plunging horse in a twinkling. The harness seemed to be scattered everywhere.

===Chinese===
行到半路，巴尼猛地一个踉跄，拉断了一根挽绳和另外几条皮带。比彻姆先生一眨眼便抢到那匹发着性子的马头前。套具仿佛撒了满地。

===Original===
“I expect I had better walk on now,” I remarked.

===Chinese===
“我看这会儿我最好还是走下去吧。”我说。

===Original===
“Walk, be grannied! With two fat lazy horses to draw you?” returned Mr. Beecham.

===Chinese===
“走下去，见鬼去吧！不是有两匹肥肥胖胖的懒马拉着你吗？”比彻姆先生回敬道。

===Original===
Men are clumsy, stupid creatures regarding little things, but in their right place they are wonderful animals. If a buggy was smashed to smithereens, from one of their many mysterious pockets they would produce a knife and some string, and put the wreck into working order in no time.

===Chinese===
男人在小事上是笨手笨脚的蠢物，可放对了地方，他们又是了不得的动物。哪怕一辆马车摔成了齑粉，他们也能从身上那一堆神秘的口袋里掏出把小刀、几段绳子，转眼叫这堆残骸重新上路。

===Original===
Harold was as clever in this way as any other man with as much bushman ability as he had, so it was not long ere we were bowling along as merrily as ever.

===Chinese===
论这一手，哈罗德配着他那一身丛林本事，不输任何男人，不多时我们便又欢欢喜喜地辘辘上路了。

===Original===
Just before we came in sight of Caddagat he came to a standstill, jumped to the ground, untied Warrigal, and put the reins in my hand, saying:

===Chinese===
将到望得见卡达加特的地方，他停了车，跳下地，解开瓦里加尔，把缰绳放进我手里，说：

===Original===
“I think you can get home safely from here. Don’t be in such a huff﻿—I was afraid something might happen you if alone. You needn’t mention that I came with you unless you like. Goodbye.”

===Chinese===
“我想打这儿起，你自己稳稳当当到家了。别这么气鼓鼓的——我是怕你一个人路上出事。除非你自己乐意，不必提我送过你一程。再见。”

===Original===
“Goodbye, Mr. Beecham. Thank you for being so officious,” I said by way of a parting shot.

===Chinese===
“再见，比彻姆先生。多谢你这般热心越权。”我拿这话作临别一箭。

===Original===
“Old Nick will run away with you for being so ungrateful,” he returned.

===Chinese===
“你这样不知好歹，老尼克（Old Nick）要把你拐了去。”他回敬道。

===Original===
“Old Nick will have me anyhow,” I thought to myself as I drove home amid the shadows. The hum of the cicadas was still, and dozens of rabbits, tempted out by the cool of the twilight, scuttled across my path and hid in the ferns.

===Chinese===
“横竖老尼克总要把我弄到手的。”我一路在暮影里驱车回家，心里这般想。蝉声已经静了，几十只兔子受黄昏凉气的诱哄钻出洞来，簌簌窜过我的车路，躲进蕨草里。

===Original===
I wished the harness had not broken, as I feared it would put a clincher on my being allowed out driving alone in future.

===Chinese===
但愿那套具没断才好，我怕这一断，往后再想获准独自驾车出门，可就板上钉钉地没指望了。

===Original===
Joe Slocombe, the man who acted as groom and rouseabout, was waiting for me at the entrance gate.

===Chinese===
乔·斯洛科姆（Joe Slocombe）——那个充任马夫兼牧场杂工（rouse-about）的人——正在大门口等我。

===Original===
“I’m glad you come at last, Miss Sybyller. The missus has been in a dreadful stoo for fear something had happened yuz. She’s been runnin’ in an’ out like a gurrl on the lookout fer her lover, and was torkin’ of sendin’ me after yuz, but she went to her tea soon as she see the buggy come in sight. I’ll put all the parcels on the back veranda, and yuz can go in at woncest or yuz’ll be late fer yer tea.”

===Chinese===
“您总算回来啦，俺真高兴，西比拉尔小姐（Miss Sybyller）。太太生怕恁出了事，急得像热锅上的蚂蚁。她跑进跑出，活像个巴望情郎的闺女儿，还念叨着要打发俺去追恁；一瞧见马车进了眼，她这就喝茶去啦。俺把包裹全搁到后游廊上，恁这就进去吧，赶不上趟儿，茶点可要迟到哩。”

===Original===
“Joe, the harness broke and had to be tied up. That is what kept me so late,” I explained.

===Chinese===
“乔，套具断了，只好先捆上。这就是我回来这么晚的缘故。”我解释道。

===Original===
“The harness broke!” he exclaimed. “How the doose is that! Broke here in the trace, and that strap! Well, I’ll be hanged! I thought them straps couldn’t break only onder a tremenjous strain. The boss is so dashed partickler too. I believe he’ll sool me off the place; and I looked at that harness only yesterday. I can’t make out how it come to break so simple. The boss will rise the devil of a shine, and say you might have been killed.”

===Chinese===
“套具断了！”他嚷起来。“这可邪了门啦！挽绳这儿断的，还有那条皮带！嘿，我算见了活鬼！我原当这几条皮带，不使上老鼻子大的劲儿，断然断不了。东家又偏偏顶顶挑剔。我看他要撵俺滚蛋；昨儿个俺还刚查看过那套套具。俺闹不明白，它怎么就这样轻巧地断了。东家准要闹个天翻地覆，还要说您险些把命搭上呢。”

===Original===
This put a different complexion on things. I knew Joe Slocombe could mend the harness with little trouble, as it was because he was what uncle Jay-Jay termed a “handy divil” at saddlery that he was retained at Caddagat. I said carelessly:

===Chinese===
这一来事情的眉目便不同了。我知道乔·斯洛科姆修补套具不费什么手脚——杰杰舅舅留他守在卡达加特，正因他是舅舅口中的“巧手鬼”（handy divil），摆弄鞍具行当样样来得。我满不在乎地说：

===Original===
“If you mend the harness at once, Joe, uncle Julius need not be bothered about it. As it happened, there is no harm done, and I won’t mention the matter.”

===Chinese===
“乔，你这就把套具修好，朱利叶斯舅舅便不必为这事烦心。如今恰好没伤着什么，我也不提这茬。”

===Original===
“Thank you, miss,” he said eagerly. “I’ll mend it at once.”

===Chinese===
“谢谢您，小姐。”他忙不迭地说。“我这就修。”

===Original===
Now that I had that piece of business so luckily disposed of, I did not feel the least nervous about meeting grannie. I took the mail in my arms and entered the dining-room, chirping pleasantly:

===Chinese===
这桩事体这样凑巧地理料妥帖，我见外婆便没有半点心虚了。我把邮件抱在怀里，走进饭厅，口里欢快地唧唧喳喳：

===Original===
“Grannie, I’m such a good mail-boy. I have heaps of letters, and did not forget one of your commissions.”

===Chinese===
“外婆，我这送信的小差役多称职。信攒了老大一堆，您吩咐的事一件也没忘。”

===Original===
“I don’t want to hear that now,” she said, drawing her dear old mouth into a straight line, which told me I was not going to palm things off as easily as I thought. “I want a reason for your conduct this afternoon.”

===Chinese===
“这会儿我不爱听那个。”她说着，把那张亲爱的老嘴绷成一条直线——这告诉我，事情休想照我想的那样轻易搪塞过去。“我要你说说，今天下午这番行径作何道理。”

===Original===
“Explain what, grannie?” I inquired.

===Chinese===
“解释什么呀，外婆？”我问。

===Original===
“None of that pretence! Not only have you been most outrageously insulting to Mr. Hawden when I sent him with you, but you also deliberately and wilfully disobeyed me.”

===Chinese===
“少来这套装糊涂！我打发霍登先生陪你同去，你不光对他无礼到了张狂的地步，你还存心蓄意地违逆了我。”

===Original===
Uncle Julius listened attentively, and Hawden looked at me with such a leer of triumph that my fingers tingled to smack his cars. Turning to my grandmother, I said distinctly and cuttingly:

===Chinese===
朱利叶斯舅舅（Uncle Julius）凝神听着；霍登望着我，那副得胜的斜眼馋相，看得我手指发痒，恨不得掴他两记耳光。我转向外祖母，一字一句、又清楚又扎人地说：

===Original===
“Grannie, I did not intentionally disobey you. Disobedience never entered my head. I hate that thing. His presence was detestable to me. When he got out at the gate I could not resist the impulse to drive off and leave him there. He looked such a complete jackdaw that you would have laughed yourself to see him.”

===Chinese===
“外婆，我不是有意违逆您。‘违逆’二字从没进过我的脑子。我恨那种行径。有他在跟前，我嫌恶得受不住。他下车站去开那道门时，我按捺不住那股冲动，便把车赶走，把他撂在那儿了。他那副十足的寒鸦相，您要是亲眼瞧见，准得自己先笑坏。”

===Original===
“Dear, oh dear! You wicked hussy, what will become of you!” And grannie shook her head, trying to look stern, and hiding a smile in her serviette.

===Chinese===
“哎呀，哎呀！你这个坏丫头，往后可怎么得了！”外婆摇着头，竭力要板起脸，却把一抹笑意藏进了餐巾里。

===Original===
“Your manners are not improving, Sybylla. I fear you must be incorrigible,” said aunt Helen.

===Chinese===
“你的举止可不见长进，西比拉。我看你是不可救药了。”海伦姨妈说。

===Original===
When uncle Jay-Jay heard the whole particulars of the affair, he lay back in his chair and laughed fit to kill himself.

===Chinese===
杰杰舅舅听罢这桩事的全部始末，仰靠进椅子里，笑得险些背过气去。

===Original===
“You ought to be ashamed to always encourage her in her tomboyish ways, Julius. It grieves me to see she makes no effort to acquire a ladylike demeanour,” said grannie.

===Chinese===
“你老这么纵容她疯疯野野，也不害臊，朱利叶斯。眼看她全不学着点儿大家闺秀的仪态，我心里难过。”外婆说。

===Original===
Mr. Hawden had come off second-best, so he arose from his half-finished meal and stamped out, banging the door after him, and muttering something about “a disgustingly spoilt and petted tomboy,” “a hideous barbarian,” and so forth.

===Chinese===
霍登先生落了下风，便从那顿没吃完的饭食边起身，咚咚地跺着脚出去，砰地带上身后的门，嘴里还咕哝着些什么“娇宠得不像话的野丫头”、“丑八怪野蛮人”云云。

===Original===
Uncle Jay-Jay related that story to everyone, dwelling with great delight upon the fact that Frank Hawden was forced to walk four miles in the heat and dust.

===Chinese===
杰杰舅舅逢人便讲这段故事，讲得眉飞色舞，尤其津津乐道的是：弗兰克·霍登竟被迫顶着大热天和满路尘土走了四英里。

## XVIII As Short as I Wish Had Been the Majority of Sermons to Which I Have Been Forced to Give Ear / 第18章 但愿我被迫听过的布道大都这般短

===Original===
When alone I confessed to aunt Helen that Harold had accompanied me to within a short distance of home. She did not smile as usual, but looked very grave, and, drawing me in front of her, said:

===Chinese===
趁无人在旁，我向海伦姨妈（aunt Helen）坦白说，哈罗德（Harold）一路把我送到了离家不远的地方。她没有照常露出笑容，神色十分凝重，把我拉到身前，说道：

===Original===
“Sybylla, do you know what you are doing? Do you love Harry Beecham? Do you mean to marry him?”

===Chinese===
“西比拉，你可知道自己在做什么？你爱哈里·比彻姆（Harry Beecham）吗？你打算嫁给他吗？”

===Original===
“Aunt Helen, what a question to ask! I never dreamt of such a thing. He has never spoken a word of love to me. Marriage! I am sure he does not for an instant think of me in that light. I’m not seventeen.”

===Chinese===
“海伦姨妈，这问的是什么话！我做梦也不曾想过这种事。他从不曾对我说过半个情字。结婚！我敢说，他连一瞬间也不曾往那上头想过我。我还不到十七岁呢。”

===Original===
“Yes, you are young, but some people’s age cannot be reckoned by years. I am glad to see you have developed a certain amount of half-real and half-assumed youthfulness lately, but when the novelty of your present life wears away, your old mature nature will be there, so it is of no use feigning childishness. Harold Beecham is not given to speech﻿—action with him is the same thing. Can you look at me straight, Sybylla, and say that Harold has not extended you something more than common politeness?”

===Chinese===
“不错，你是年轻，可有些人的年岁是不能拿年头计算的。我很高兴，看你近来添了几分半真半假的少女天真，不过等你眼下这种生活的新鲜劲儿一消，你那副老成的性子便又会露出来，所以装孩子气是没有用的。哈罗德·比彻姆（Harold Beecham）不惯于言语——在他，行动便等于言语。西比拉，你能直直地望着我的眼睛，说哈罗德待你从来没有超出寻常的客气吗？”

===Original===
Had aunt Helen put that question to me a day before, I would have blushed and felt guilty. But today not so. The words of the jackeroo the night before had struck home. “A hideous barbarian,” he had called me, and it seemed to me he had spoken the truth. My life had been so pleasant lately that I had overlooked this fact, but now it returned to sting with redoubled bitterness. I had no lovable qualities to win for me the love of my fellows, which I so much desired.

===Chinese===
这话海伦姨妈若是早一天问我，我必定会红了脸、心里发虚。可是今天却不然。头天夜里那个牧场学徒（jackeroo）的话正说到了我心坎上。他骂我是个“丑八怪野人”，而依我看，他说的是实话。近来我的日子过得太快活了，把这个事实疏忽了过去，此刻它又回来蜇我，苦味加倍。我本来那样渴望同伴的爱怜，却没有一丝一毫可爱的品性，能替我赢得它。

===Original===
I returned aunt Helen a gaze as steady as her own, and said bitterly:

===Chinese===
我回望海伦姨妈，目光同她自己的目光一样镇定，苦涩地说：

===Original===
“Aunt Helen, I can truly say he has never, and will never extend to me more than common politeness. Neither will any other man. Surely you know enough of masculine human nature to see there is no danger of a man losing his heart to a plain woman like me. Love in fancy and song is a pretty myth, embracing unity of souls, congeniality of tastes, and suchlike commodities. In workaday reality it is the lowest of passions, which is set alight by the most artistic nose and mouth, and it matters not if its object is vile, low, or brainless to idiocy, so long as it has these attributes.”

===Chinese===
“海伦姨妈，我可以实实在在地说，他从不曾、将来也决不会待我超出寻常的客气。任何别的男人也不会。男人的天性你总该见识得够多了，该看得出，像我这样相貌平常的女人，绝无叫男人堕入情网的危险。幻想与歌吟里的爱情是个美丽的神话，包罗着灵魂的契合、趣味的相投之类的好货色；在谋生度日的现实里，它却是诸般情欲中最低下的一种，单凭一只顶艺术的鼻子、一张顶艺术的嘴便能点着，至于它的对象是不是卑劣、下贱、蠢得近于白痴，全都无关紧要，只要她生着这两样宝贝便罢。”

===Original===
“Sybylla, Sybylla,” said auntie sadly, as if to herself. “In the first flush of girlhood, and so bitter. Why is this?”

===Chinese===
“西比拉，西比拉。”姨妈忧伤地说，像是自言自语。“正当少女年华初绽，心肠却这样苦。这是为什么？”

===Original===
“Because I have been cursed with the power of seeing, thinking, and, worse than all, feeling, and branded with the stinging affliction of ugliness,” I replied.

===Chinese===
“因为我受了诅咒，生就了会看、会想、更糟的是还会感受的本领，又被打上丑陋这一刺人的烙印。”我答道。

===Original===
“Now, Sybylla, you are going to think of yourself again. Something has put you out. Be sensible for once in a way. What you have said of men’s love may be true in a sense, but it is not always so, and Harry is not that kind of man. I have known him all his life, and understand him, and feel sure he loves you truly. Tell me plainly, do you intend to accept him?”

===Chinese===
“好了，西比拉，你又要只顾自己了。有什么事惹恼了你。就这一回，讲讲道理吧。你方才说的男人的爱，就某种意义而言也许是实情，但并非一概如此，哈里就不是那种男人。打他出生起我就认识他，也懂得他，深信他是真心爱你。明白告诉我：你打算接受他吗？”

===Original===
“Intend to accept him!” I echoed. “I haven’t once thought of such a possibility. I never mean to marry anyone.”

===Chinese===
“打算接受他！”我接口道。“这种可能我连想都不曾想过一回。我压根儿不打算嫁给任何人。”

===Original===
“Don’t you care for Harold? Just a little? Think.”

===Chinese===
“你难道对哈罗德没有一点儿情意？就那么一丁点儿？想想看。”

===Original===
“How could I care for him?”

===Chinese===
“我怎么会对他有情意？”

===Original===
“For many, many reasons. He is young, and very kind and gentle. He is one of the biggest and finest-looking men you could find. He is a man whom no one could despise, for he has nothing despicable about him. But, best of all, he is true, and that, I think, is the bedrock of all virtues.”

===Chinese===
“理由有千条万条。他年轻，又非常和善温柔。他是你能寻见的最魁伟、最体面的男人之一。他是个谁也鄙薄不了的人，因为他身上没有一处可鄙的地方。而顶要紧的是，他真诚，我想，那是一切美德的基石。”

===Original===
“But he is so conceited,” I remarked.

===Chinese===
“可他那么自负。”我说。

===Original===
“That does not make him any the less lovable. I know another young person very conceited, and it does not prevent me from loving her dearly,” here aunt Helen smiled affectionately at me. “What you complain of in Harold will wear off presently﻿—life has been very easy for him so far, you see.”

===Chinese===
“那也不减他半分可爱。我还认得另一个非常自负的年轻人，这可一点儿也不妨碍我疼她疼得厉害。”说到这里，海伦姨妈充满疼爱地朝我一笑。“你挑剔哈罗德的这一条，不久便会磨掉——你要知道，他至今过的一直是顺遂日子。”

===Original===
“But, auntie, I’m sure he thinks he could have any girl for the asking.”

===Chinese===
“可是姨妈，我敢说，他准以为只要开口，哪个姑娘都能到手。”

===Original===
“Well, he has a great number to choose from, for they all like him.”

===Chinese===
“那倒不假，他有大把的姑娘好挑，因为她们个个都喜欢他。”

===Original===
“Yes, just for his money,” I said scornfully. “But I’ll surprise him if he thinks he can get me for the asking.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，还不是看中他的钱。”我不屑地说。“可他要是以为只要开口就能把我弄到手，我倒要叫他吃一惊。”

===Original===
“Sybylla, never flirt. To play with a man’s heart, I think, is one of the most horribly unwomanly actions our sex can be guilty of.”

===Chinese===
“西比拉，千万别卖弄风情。拿一个男人的心当玩意儿，我想，是我们女人所能犯下的最可怕、最没有女人味的行径之一。”

===Original===
“I would scorn to flirt with any man,” I returned with vigour. “Play with a man’s heart! You’d really think they had such a thing, aunt Helen, to hear you talk. Hurt their vanity for a few days is the most a woman could do with any of them. I am sick of this preach, preach about playing with men’s hearts. It is an old fable which should have been abolished long ago. It does not matter how a woman is played with.”

===Chinese===
“要我同任何男人调情，我还不屑于做呢。”我激昂地回道。“拿男人的心当玩意儿！听你说话的口气，海伦姨妈，人家真要当他们果真有这么一件东西。至多是伤一伤他们的虚荣心，疼上几天，这已是一个女人在他们随便哪一个身上能做的一切了。什么玩弄男人的心，这样一篇篇说教下去，我听腻了。那是个早该废除的老寓言。至于女人被人怎样玩弄，倒是无关紧要的。”

===Original===
“Sybylla, you talk at random. The shortcomings of men are no excuse for you to be unwomanly,” said aunt Helen.

===Chinese===
“西比拉，你信口乱说。男人的短处，可不能当你失掉女人样的借口。”海伦姨妈说。

## XIX The 9th of November 1896 / 第19章 1896 年 11 月 9 日

===Original===
The Prince of Wales’s birthday up the country was celebrated as usual thereaway by the annual horse-races on the Wyambeet course, about fourteen miles from Caddagat.

===Chinese===
在内地乡间，威尔士亲王的寿辰照例以一年一度的赛马来庆贺——赛场便设在怀安比特（Wyambeet）赛马场，离卡达加特约莫十四英里。

===Original===
The holding of these races was an elderly institution, and was followed at night by a servants’ ball given by one of the squatters. Last year it had been Beecham’s ball, the year before Bossier’s, and this year it was to take place in the woolshed of James Grant of Yabtree. Our two girls, the gardener, and Joe Slocombe the groom, were to be present, as also were all the other employees about. Nearly everyone in the district﻿—masters and men﻿—attended the races. We were going, Frank Hawden volunteering to stay and mind the house.

===Chinese===
这赛马是个上了年头的老例；赛罢入夜，照例由一位大牧场主（squatter）做东，开一场仆役舞会。去年做东的是比彻姆家，前年是博西耶家，今年则定在亚布特里（Yabtree）詹姆斯·格兰特（James Grant）的剪毛棚里举行。我们家的两个女仆、园丁和马夫乔·斯洛科姆（Joe Slocombe）都要到场，左近其余的雇工也一样。本区的人差不多全去看赛马——主人家和雇工们一道。我们也要去，弗兰克·霍登（Frank Hawden）自告奋勇留下看家。

===Original===
We started at nine o’clock. Grannie and uncle Boss sat in the front seat of the buggy, and aunt Helen and I occupied the back. Uncle always drove at a good round gallop. His idea was to have good horses, not donkeys, and not to spare them, as there were plenty more to be had any day. On this morning he went off at his usual pace. Grannie urged as remonstrance that the dust was fearful when going at that rate. I clapped my hands and exclaimed, “Go it, Mr. Bossier! Well done, uncle Jay-Jay! Hurrah for Clancy!”

===Chinese===
我们九点钟动身。外婆和博西耶舅舅坐在四轮轻便马车（buggy）的前座，海伦姨妈（aunt Helen）和我占了后座。舅舅赶车，向来是一路痛快的疾驰。他的道理是：要养好马，不养蠢驴，也绝不惜它们的力——反正日子长着，好马天天买得着。这天早上，他又照老样子放开了赶。外婆婉言劝阻，说照这个速度，尘土大得吓人。我拍着手嚷道：“跑哇，博西耶先生！好样的，杰杰舅舅（uncle Jay-Jay）！克兰西（Clancy）好哇！”

===Original===
Uncle first said he was glad to see I had the spirit of an Australian, and then threatened to put my nose above my chin if I failed to behave properly. Grannie remarked that I might have the spirit of an Australian, but I had by no means the manners of a lady; while aunt Helen ventured a wish that I might expend all my superfluous spirits on the way, so that I would be enabled to deport myself with a little decorum when arrived at the racecourse.

===Chinese===
舅舅先说，他很高兴看见我有一股澳大利亚人的精气神儿，接着又吓唬说，我若是不守规矩，他就要把我的鼻子挪到下巴上头去。外婆评说道，澳大利亚人的精气神儿我兴许有，淑女的仪态我却半分也没有；海伦姨妈则斗胆许了个愿：但愿我把这一身多余的劲头在半路上统统使唤干净，好歹到了赛马场，还能拿出些许端庄来。

===Original===
We went at a great pace; lizards and goannas scampered out of the way in dozens, and, clambering trees, eyed us unblinkingly as we passed. Did we see a person or vehicle a tiny speck ahead of us﻿—in a short time they were as far away in the background.

===Chinese===
我们一路疾驰；蜥蜴和巨蜥（goanna）成打成打地窜出路面，攀上树去，一眨不眨地瞪着我们走过。前方若望见一个人影或一辆车，起初小小一粒点——转眼间，他们已被远远抛进背后的景物里去了。

===Original===
“Please, uncle, let me drive,” I requested.

===Chinese===
“舅舅，求您让我赶一会儿吧。”我央告道。

===Original===
“Couldn’t now. Your grannie can’t sit in the backseat﻿—neither could I﻿—and look like a tame cockatoo while you sat in front. You ask Harry to let you drive him. I bet he’ll consent; he’s sure to be in a sulky with a spare seat on spec. We’re sure to overtake him in a few minutes.”

===Chinese===
“这会儿可不行。你外婆坐不进后座——换了我也坐不来——总不能坐在后头像只驯顺的凤头鹦鹉，倒叫你坐在前头赶车。你去求求哈里（Harry），叫他让你替他赶车吧。我打赌他准肯；他准驾着一辆双轮轻便马车（sulky），车上特备一个空座碰运气。不出几分钟我们准能追上他。”

===Original===
There was a vehicle in the distance which proved to be from Five-Bob Downs, but as we overhauled it, it was the drag, and not a sulky. Harold occupied the driver’s seat, and the other occupants were all ladies. I noticed the one beside him was wearing a very big hat, all ruffles, flowers, and plumes.

===Chinese===
远处有一辆车，看那方向是从五博丘（Five-Bob Downs）来的；待我们赶上去，才发现那是四驾大马车（drag），不是双轮轻便马车。哈罗德（Harold）坐在驭座上，车里其余的乘客全是女客。我留意到他身边那一位，戴着一顶极大的帽子，褶边、花朵、翎毛缀了个遍。

===Original===
“Shall I pull up and get you a seat?” inquired uncle Jay-Jay.

===Chinese===
“要不要我停车，替你讨个座儿？”杰杰舅舅问。

===Original===
“No, no, no.”

===Chinese===
“不要，不要，不要。”

===Original===
The boss of Five-Bob drew to his side of the road, and when we had passed uncle began to tease:

===Chinese===
五博丘的东家把车靠到他那一侧的路上；我们一过去，舅舅便开起了玩笑：

===Original===
“Got fainthearted, did you? The flower-garden on that woman’s hat corked your chances altogether. Never mind, don’t you funk; I’ll see that you have a fair show. I’ll get you a regular cartwheel next time I go to town, and we’ll trim it up with some of old Barney’s tail. If that won’t fetch him, I’m sure nothing will.”

===Chinese===
“泄气啦，是吧？那女人帽子上的花园，把你的指望全断送喽。别往心里去，怕什么；我保管叫你有场公平的较量。下趟进城，我给你弄一顶地道的大车轱辘回来，再拿老巴尼（Barney）的尾巴毛给你扎上几绺装点起来。要是那还钩不住他，我敢保天底下再没什么钩得住他了。”

===Original===
Before we got to the racecourse Barney went lame through getting a stone in his hoof; this caused a delay which enabled the Five-Bob trap to catch us, and we pulled rein a little distance apart at the same time, to alight.

===Chinese===
还没到赛马场，巴尼蹄子里硌进了块石子，走瘸了；这一耽搁，教五博丘的车赶上了我们；两车相隔一小段路，同时勒缰停下，各自下车。

===Original===
Mr. Beecham’s groom went to his horses’ heads while Harold himself assisted his carriageful of ladies to set foot on the ground. Aunt Helen and grannie went to talk to them, but I stayed with uncle Jay-Jay while he took the horses out. Somehow I was feeling very disappointed. I had expected Harold Beecham to be alone. He had attended on me so absolutely everywhere I had met him lately, that I had unconsciously grown to look upon him as mine exclusively; and now, seeing he would belong to his own party of ladies for the day, things promised to be somewhat flat without him.

===Chinese===
比彻姆先生的马夫赶去照管马头，哈罗德则亲自搀扶他满车的女客下地。海伦姨妈和外婆过去同她们攀谈，我却留在杰杰舅舅身边，看他卸马。不知怎么，我心里好生失望。我原指望哈罗德·比彻姆（Harold Beecham）是一个人来的。近来每到一处碰上他，他都寸步不离地殷勤照料，不知不觉间，我竟已把他看作我一个人的了；如今眼见他这一天要归他自己那一伙女客，没了他，这诸般热闹只怕都要寡淡几分。

===Original===
“I told that devil of a Joe to be sure and turn up as soon as I arrived. I wanted him to water the horses, but I can’t see him anywhere﻿—the infernal, crawling, doosed idiot!” ejaculated uncle Julius.

===Chinese===
“我嘱咐过乔那个活宝，务必我一到他就露面。我原要他来饮马，可哪儿也寻不见他——这个混账的、窝窝囊囊的、该死的蠢货！”朱利叶斯舅舅（uncle Julius）脱口骂道。

===Original===
“Never mind, uncle, let him have his holiday. I suppose he’d like to have time to spoon with his girl. I can easily water the horses.”

===Chinese===
“别计较啦，舅舅，让他过他的节吧。我猜他巴不得抽空同他的姑娘卿卿我我一番呢。饮马的活儿我轻易就干了。”

===Original===
“That would suit Joe, I have no doubt; but I don’t pay him to let you water the horses. I’ll water ’em myself.”

===Chinese===
“那样乔自然称心，我毫不怀疑；可我雇他，不是雇你来饮马的。我自己来。”

===Original===
He led one animal, I took the other, and we went in the direction of water a few hundred yards away.

===Chinese===
他牵一匹，我牵一匹，朝几百码外的水边走去。

===Original===
“You run along to your grannie and the rest of them, and I’ll go by myself,” said uncle, but I kept on with the horse.

===Chinese===
“你这就去吧，找你外婆她们去，我一个人走。”舅舅说；可我仍牵着马不放。

===Original===
“You mustn’t let a five-guinea hat destroy your hopes altogether,” he continued, with a mischievous twinkle in his eyes. “If you stick to your guns you have a better show than anyone to bag the boss of Five-Bob.”

===Chinese===
“你千万别叫一顶五基尼（guinea）的帽子，把指望毁了个干净吧。”他接着说，眼里闪着促狭的光。“只要你守住阵地，比起随便哪个，你都更有把握把五博丘的东家猎到手。”

===Original===
“I am at a loss to interpret your innuendo, Mr. Bossier,” I said stiffly.

===Chinese===
“您这话里有话，恕我无从索解，博西耶先生。”我板着脸说。

===Original===
“Now, little woman, you think you are very smart, but you can’t deceive me. I’ve seen the game you and Harry have been up to this last month. If it had been any other man, I would have restricted your capers long ago.”

===Chinese===
“得了，小丫头，你自以为机灵得很，可瞒不过我。这一个月来你同哈里在玩什么把戏，我全瞧在眼里。换了别的男人，我早就管住你这些蹦跶了。”

===Original===
“Uncle﻿—” I began.

===Chinese===
“舅舅——”我开了口。

===Original===
“Now, Sybylla, none of your crammers. There is no harm in being a bit gone on Harry. It’s only natural, and just what I’d expect. I’ve known him since he was born, and he’s a good all-round fellow. His head is screwed on the right way, his heart is in the right place, and his principles are tip-top. He could give you fal-de-rals and rubbish to no end, and wouldn’t be stingy either. You’ll never get a better man. Don’t you be put out of the running so cheaply: hold your own and win, that’s my advice to you. There is nothing against him, only temper﻿—old Nick himself isn’t a patch on him for temper.”

===Chinese===
“行了，西比拉，少来你那套鬼话。对哈里有点儿着迷，不算什么坏事。这再自然不过，也正合我的料想。他一落生我就认识他，是个样样拿得起的好小子。他脑袋安得正，心肠放得端，品行更是顶呱呱。他能没完没了地给你置办花哨小玩意儿和零碎东西，出手还大方。再好些的男人，你打灯笼也找不着了。别这么便宜地就出了局：站稳脚跟，赢了他——这是我给你的忠告。他身上挑不出毛病，唯独脾气一条——论脾气，老尼克（Old Nick）本尊也远不及他。”

===Original===
“Temper!” I exclaimed. “He is always so quiet and pleasant.”

===Chinese===
“脾气！”我叫起来。“他总是那么安静、和气。”

===Original===
“Yes, he controls it well. He’s a fellow with a will like iron, and that is what you want, as I find you have none of your own. But be careful of Harry Beecham in a temper. He is like a raging lion, and when his temper dies away is a sulking brute, which is the vilest of all tempers. But he is not vindictive, and is easy managed, if you don’t mind giving in and coaxing a little.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，他克制得好。他是个意志如铁的家伙——这恰恰是你缺的，据我看，你自己一点儿主见也没有。不过哈里·比彻姆动起脾气来，你可要当心。他像一头狂怒的狮子；脾气消下去之后，又是一头闷声赌气的畜生——那是诸般脾气里最下作的一种。好在他不记仇，也容易笼络，只要你不嫌让让步、温言哄一哄。”

===Original===
“Now, uncle, you have had your say, I will have mine. You seem to think I have more than a friendly regard for Mr. Beecham, but I have not. I would not marry him even if I could. I am so sick of everyone thinking I would marry any man for his possessions. I would not stoop to marry a king if I did not love him. As for trying to win a man, I would scorn any action that way; I never intend to marry. Instead of wasting so much money on me in presents and other ways, I wish you would get me something to do, a profession that will last me all my life, so that I may be independent.”

===Chinese===
“好了，舅舅，您说完了您的，该我说我的了。您似乎以为我对那位比彻姆先生怀着超出友谊的情意，其实并没有。就是能嫁，我也不嫁他。人人都当我会为着男人的家产嫁人，我腻烦透了。若是不爱，便是国王，我也不屑屈身去嫁。至于去赢取一个男人——那一路的行径我一概鄙夷；我从不打算嫁人。与其送这送那、在我身上糟蹋这么多钱，我倒愿您给我寻点儿事做，寻一门够我使一辈子的营生，好叫我自立。”

===Original===
“No mistake, you’re a rum youngster. You can be my companion till further orders. That’s a profession that will last you a goodish while.”

===Chinese===
“错不了，你真是个古怪的小东西。那你就来做我的女伴吧，直到另行通知。这也算一门营生，够你当上好一大阵子了。”

===Original===
With this I had to be contented, as I saw he considered what I had said as a joke.

===Chinese===
我只好拿这话聊以自足，因为我看得出，他把我方才那番话只当了个玩笑。

===Original===
I left uncle and went in quest of grannie, who, by this, was beyond the other side of the course, fully a quarter of a mile away. Going in her direction I met Joe Archer, one of the Five-Bob jackeroos, and a great chum of mine. He had a taste for literature, and we got on together like one o’clock. We sat on a log under a stringybark-tree and discussed the books we had read since last we met, and enjoyed ourselves so much that we quite forgot about the races or the flight of time until recalled from book-land by Harold Beecham’s voice.

===Chinese===
我撇下舅舅去找外婆；这时她已在赛场的另一头之外，足有四分之一英里远。朝她那方向走时，我遇上了乔·阿彻（Joe Archer）——五博丘的牧场学徒（jackeroo）之一，我的一个至交好友。他喜好文学，我们俩一拍即合。我们坐在一株条皮桉（stringybark）底下的木头上，畅谈自上次分手以来各自读过的书，谈得那样快活，把赛马和光阴的飞逝忘了个干净，直到哈罗德·比彻姆的嗓音把我们从书国里唤了回来。

===Original===
“Excuse me, Miss Melvyn, but your grannie has commissioned me to find you as we want to have lunch, and it appears you are the only one who knows the run of some of the tucker bags.”

===Chinese===
“对不住，梅尔文小姐，你外婆委托我来寻你，我们要开午饭了；而那几袋吃食的底细，看来只有你一个人摸得清。”

===Original===
“How do you do, Mr. Beecham? Where are they going to have lunch?”

===Chinese===
“你好吗，比彻姆先生？他们要在哪儿用午饭？”

===Original===
“Over in that clump of box-trees,” he replied, pointing in the direction of a little rise at a good distance.

===Chinese===
“就在那边那一丛黄杨桉（box-tree）里。”他答道，朝远处一座小土坡指了指。

===Original===
“How are you enjoying yourself?” he asked, looking straight at me.

===Chinese===
“你玩得怎么样？”他问道，直直地望着我。

===Original===
“Treminjous intoirely, sor,” I replied.

===Chinese===
“痛快得——不得了，先生。”我答道。

===Original===
“I suppose you know the winner of every race,” he remarked, quizzically watching Joe Archer, who was blushing and as uneasy as a schoolgirl when nabbed in the enjoyment of an illicit love-letter.

===Chinese===
“想必每场赛马的赢家，你都心里有数喽。”他说着，揶揄地打量乔·阿彻——他正红着脸，局促得像个偷读禁阅情书、读得正酣时被当场拿住的女学生。

===Original===
“Really, Mr. Beecham, Mr. Archer and I have been so interested in ourselves that we quite forgot there was such a thing as a race at all,” I returned.

===Chinese===
“真的，比彻姆先生，阿彻先生和我光顾着谈我们自己的事，把世上还有赛马这回事全忘干净了。”我回道。

===Original===
“You’d better see where old Boxer is. He might kick some of the other horses if you don’t keep a sharp lookout,” he said, turning to his jackeroo.

===Chinese===
“你最好去看看老博克瑟（old Boxer）在哪儿。不留神盯着点儿，它兴许要踢着旁的马。”他转向他的牧场学徒说。

===Original===
“Ladies before gentlemen,” I interposed. “I want Mr. Archer to take me to grannie, then he can go and look after old Boxer.”

===Chinese===
“女士优先。”我插嘴道。“我要请阿彻先生送我去外婆那儿，然后他再去照看老博克瑟。”

===Original===
“I’ll escort you,” said Beecham.

===Chinese===
“我送你去。”比彻姆说。

===Original===
“Thank you, but I have requested Mr. Archer to do so.”

===Chinese===
“谢谢你，可我已经请了阿彻先生。”

===Original===
“In that case, I beg your pardon, and will attend to Boxer while Joe does as you request.”

===Chinese===
“既然这样，请恕我冒失；乔照你的吩咐办，我去看博克瑟。”

===Original===
Raising his hat he walked swiftly away with a curious expression on his usually pleasant face.

===Chinese===
他举了举帽，快步走开，那张素来和悦的脸上，带着一种说不出的神情。

===Original===
“By Jove, I’m in for it!” ejaculated my escort. “The boss doesn’t get that expression on his face for nothing. You take my tip for it, he felt inclined to seize me by the scruff of the neck and kick me from here to Yabtree.”

===Chinese===
“天哪，我这回可有好果子吃了！”我的护送人脱口嚷道。“东家脸上摆出那副神情，从不会是无缘无故的。你记住我这话：他方才简直想一把揪住我的后颈窝，把我从这儿一路踢到亚布特里去。”

===Original===
“Go on!”

===Chinese===
“去你的吧！”

===Original===
“It’s a fact. He did not believe in me not going to do his bidding immediately. He has a roaring derry on disobedience. Everyone has to obey him like winkie or they can take their beds up and trot off quick and lively.”

===Chinese===
“是真事儿。他容不得我不立刻去办他的差遣。他对违抗恨得咬牙切齿。谁都得一眨眼工夫便听他的号令，不然就卷起铺盖，欢蹦乱跳地撒腿滚蛋。”

===Original===
“Mr. Beecham has sufficient sense to see I was the cause of your disobedience,” I replied.

===Chinese===
“比彻姆先生有足够的头脑，看得出是我害你抗了令。”我答道。

===Original===
“That’s where it is. He would not have cared had it been some other lady, but he gets mad if anyone dares to monopolize you. I don’t know how you are going to manage him. He is a pretty hot member sometimes.”

===Chinese===
“症结正出在这儿。换了旁的哪位女士，他满不在乎；可谁敢独占你，他就要发疯。我不知道你往后打算怎么驾驭他。他有时候，可是个相当火爆的角色。”

===Original===
“Mr. Archer, you presume! But throwing such empty banter aside, is Mr. Beecham really bad-tempered?”

===Chinese===
“阿彻先生，你放肆了！不过撇开这些空口玩笑，比彻姆先生的脾气当真坏得很吗？”

===Original===
“Bad-tempered is a tame name for it. You should have seen the dust he raised the other day with old Benson. He just did perform.”

===Chinese===
“‘脾气坏’这说法太绵软了。前几天他跟老本森（old Benson）掀起的那阵尘土，你真该见识见识。他可真来了场好戏。”

===Original===
I was always hearing of Harold Beecham’s temper, and wished I could see a little of it. He was always so imperturbably calm, and unfailingly good-tempered under the most trying circumstances, that I feared he had no emotions in him, and longed to stir him up.

===Chinese===
我老是听人讲哈罗德·比彻姆的脾气，巴不得能亲眼见识一二。他在最难堪的场合也总是那样临之泰然、好脾气得从不出岔，以致我疑心他体内根本就没有感情这东西，一心想激他一激。

===Original===
Grannie greeted me with, “Sybylla, you are such a tiresome girl. I don’t know how you have packed these hampers, and we want to have lunch. Where on earth have you been?”

===Chinese===
外婆一见我便迎头数落：“西比拉，你这孩子真磨人。这几提篮吃食你到底是怎么装的，我摸不着头脑，可我们要开饭了。你究竟跑哪儿去了？”

===Original===
Miss Augusta Beecham saluted me warmly with a kiss, and presented me to her sister Sarah, who also embraced me. I went through an introduction to several ladies and gentlemen, greeted my acquaintances, and then set to work in dead earnest to get our provisions laid out﻿—the Five-Bob Downs party had theirs in readiness. Needless to say, we were combining forces. I had my work completed when Mr. Beecham appeared upon the scene with two young ladies. One was a bright-faced little brunette, and the other a tall light blonde, whom, on account of her much trimmed hat, I recognized as the lady who had been sitting on the box-seat of the Beecham drag that morning.

===Chinese===
奥古斯塔小姐（Miss Augusta）热情地吻了我一下招呼我，又把我引见给她的妹妹莎拉（Sarah），莎拉也拥抱了我。我应付完同好几位女士先生的引见，同相识的人寒暄了一阵，便一本正经地动手把我们的吃食摆将出来——五博丘一行人的早已齐备。不消说，我们是两家合力并作一处。我刚把活计料理停当，比彻姆先生便携着两位年轻小姐到了场。一位是面色明艳的小个子黑发姑娘；另一位是高挑的金发白肤女子——凭那顶缀饰繁多的帽子，我认出她正是那天上午坐在比彻姆家四驾大马车驭座上的那位女士。

===Original===
Joe Archer informed me in a whisper that she was Miss Blanche Derrick from Melbourne, and was considered one of the greatest beauties of that city.

===Chinese===
乔·阿彻悄悄告诉我，她是墨尔本来的布兰奇·德里克小姐（Blanche Derrick），算得那座城里数一数二的美人。

===Original===
This made me anxious to examine her carefully, but I did not get an opportunity of doing so. In the hurry to attend on the party, I missed the honour of an introduction, and when I was at leisure she was sitting at some distance on a log, Harold Beecham shading her in a most religious manner with a dainty parasol. In the afternoon she strolled away with him, and after I had attended to the remains of the feast, I took Joe Archer in tow. He informed me that Miss Derrick had arrived at Five-Bob three days before, and was setting her cap determinedly at his boss.

===Chinese===
这一来我便急想细细端详她一番，却没得到机会。忙乱着张罗那一行人时，我错过了被引见的荣幸；等我得了空，她已坐在相当远的一根木头上，哈罗德·比彻姆毕恭毕敬地擎着一把精美的阳伞替她遮阳。下午，她同他散步去了；我照管完席上的残局，便拽上乔·阿彻。他告诉我，德里克小姐三天前到了五博丘，正拿定主意要擒他的东家。

===Original===
“Was she really very handsome?” I inquired.

===Chinese===
“她当真非常漂亮吗？”我问。

===Original===
“By Jove, yes!” he replied. “But one of your disdainful haughty beauties, who wouldn’t deign to say good day to a chap with less than six or seven thousand a year.”

===Chinese===
“天哪，当真！”他答道。“不过是你那种冷面倨傲的美人——年进项不上六七千镑的小子，她连声‘日安’也不屑赏。”

===Original===
I don’t know why I took no interest in the races. I knew nearly all the horses running. Some of them were uncle’s; though he never raced horses himself, he kept some swift stock which he lent to his men for the occasion.

===Chinese===
我不知道自己为什么对赛马提不起兴致。出赛的马我几乎全认得，有几匹还是舅舅的；他自己从不赛马，却养着几匹快马，到时候借给他的手下人出赛。

===Original===
Of more interest to me than the races was the pair strolling at a distance. They were fit for an artist’s models. The tall, broad, independent figure of the bushman with his easy gentlemanliness, his jockey costume enhancing his size. The equally tall majestic form of the city belle, whose self-confident fashionable style spoke of nothing appertaining to girlhood, but of the full-blown rose﻿—indeed, a splendid pair physically!

===Chinese===
比起赛马来，更教我留意的，是远处信步闲游的那一对儿。他们给画家当模特儿都够格了。丛林人高大魁伟、昂然自恃的身量，带着他那从容自在的绅士风度，骑师的装束愈发衬出他的块头；城里美人同样高挑、雍容的身影，那副自信时髦的做派，透不出半点儿属于少女的东西，只有盛放的玫瑰——论皮相，真真是光彩照人的一对！

===Original===
Then I thought of my lack of beauty, my miserable five-feet-one-inch stature, and I looked at the man beside me, small and round-shouldered, and we were both dependent children of indigence. The contrast we presented to the other pair struck me hard, and I laughed a short bitter laugh.

===Chinese===
接着我想起自己容貌的缺失，想起我那可怜的五英尺一英寸的个头，又看看身旁这个男人——矮小，微驼着背——我们俩都是仰人度日的穷孩子。同那一对两相对照，这反差狠狠击中了我，我短短地、苦涩地笑了一声。

===Original===
I excused myself to my companion, and acceded to the request of several children to go on a flower- and gum-hunting expedition. We were a long time absent, and returning, the little ones scampered ahead and left me alone. Harold Beecham came to meet me, looking as pleasant as ever.

===Chinese===
我向同伴告了罪，应下几个孩子的请求，领队远征采花拾桉果去了。我们出去了好半晌；回来时，小的们蹦跳着一窝蜂跑到前头，把我一个人撂在了后头。哈罗德·比彻姆迎上前来，脸色一如既往地和悦。

===Original===
“Am I keeping grannie and uncle waiting?” I inquired.

===Chinese===
“是我叫外婆和舅舅久等了吗？”我问。

===Original===
“No. They have gone over an hour,” he replied.

===Chinese===
“没有。他们走了一个多钟头了。”他答道。

===Original===
“Gone! How am I to get home? She must have been very angry to go and leave me. What did she say?”

===Chinese===
“走了！那我可怎么回家？她准是气极了，才这样走掉丢下我。她说了些什么？”

===Original===
“On the contrary, she was in great fiddle. She said to tell you not to kill yourself with fun, and as you are not going home, she left me to say good night. I suppose she kisses you when performing that ceremony,” he said mischievously.

===Chinese===
“恰恰相反，她乐着呢。她叫捎话给你，别乐过了头伤了身子；又说既然你不回家，就把道晚安的差事留给了我。我想她行这道仪式的时候，总少不了要亲亲你吧。”他促狭地说。

===Original===
“Where am I going tonight?”

===Chinese===
“那我今晚去哪儿？”

===Original===
“To Five-Bob Downs, the camp of yours truly,” he replied.

===Chinese===
“去五博丘，在下的大营盘。”他答道。

===Original===
“I haven’t got a dinner dress, and am not prepared. I will go home.”

===Chinese===
“我没有晚宴的裙子，什么也没备办。我要回家。”

===Original===
“We have plenty dinner dresses at Five-Bob without any more. It is Miss Melvyn we want,” he said.

===Chinese===
“五博丘的晚宴裙子已经够多了，不差再添一条。我们要的是梅尔文小姐。”他说。

===Original===
“Oh, bother you!” I retorted. “Men are such stupid creatures, and never understand about dress or anything. They think you could go to a ball in a wrapper.”

===Chinese===
“噢，你这个讨厌的家伙！”我回敬道。“男人都是些蠢物，裙子的事也好，别的什么也好，从来不懂。他们以为你裹件家常袍子就能去赴舞会。”

===Original===
“At all events, they are cute enough to know when they want a young lady at their place, no matter how she’s dressed,” he said good-humouredly.

===Chinese===
“无论如何，自家的地界上什么时候想要一位年轻小姐，这一点他们倒乖觉得很——管她穿得如何。”他和和气气地说。

===Original===
On reaching the racecourse I was surprised to see aunt Helen there. From her I learnt that grannie and uncle Jay-Jay had really gone home, but Mr. Beecham had persuaded them to allow aunt Helen and me to spend the night at Five-Bob Downs, our host promising to send or take us home on the morrow. Now that I was to have aunt Helen with me I was delighted at the prospect, otherwise I would have felt a little out of it. With aunt Helen, however, I was content anywhere, and built a castle in the air, wherein one day she and I were always to live together﻿—forever! Till death!

===Chinese===
回到赛马场，我惊讶地发现海伦姨妈还在那儿。从她口中我得知，外婆和杰杰舅舅当真回家了，但比彻姆先生劝得他们应允，让海伦姨妈和我到五博丘过夜；我们那位东道主还许下话，明日打发人送我们回去，或者亲自送回。既有海伦姨妈做伴，这前景便教我满心欢喜，不然我可要觉得自己是个多出来的了。有海伦姨妈在，我到哪儿都心安，还筑起一座空中楼阁：楼里有一天她和我要永远厮守在一起——永远！到死方休！

===Original===
Going home aunt Helen occupied a front seat with Harold and Miss Derrick, and I was crammed in at the back beside Miss Augusta, who patted my hand and said she was delighted to see me.

===Chinese===
回去时，海伦姨妈同哈罗德和德里克小姐坐在前座，我被塞进后座，挨着奥古斯塔小姐；她拍拍我的手，说见到我真高兴。

===Original===
A great concourse of young men and women in vehicles and on horseback, and in expectation of great fun, were wending their way to Yabtree﻿—nearly every trap containing a fiddle, concertina, flute, or accordion in readiness for the fray.

===Chinese===
一大伙青年男女，乘车的乘车，骑马的骑马，满怀着一场大热闹的指望，一路逶迤往亚布特里去——几乎每辆车里都装着一把小提琴、一只六角手风琴、一支长笛或一具手风琴，一切就绪，单等着投入那场鏖战。

## XX Same Yarn (Continued) / 第20章 故事续篇

===Original===
Every station hand from Five-Bob, male and female, had gone to the ball at Yabtree. Harold and his overseer had to attend to the horses, while the jackeroos started a fire in the kitchen, opened windows and doors which had been locked all day, and saw to the comfort of the gentlemen guests.

===Chinese===
五博丘（Five-Bob）的牧场人手，不论男女，全都上亚布特里（Yabtree）赴舞会去了。哈罗德（Harold）只得同他的监工（overseer）亲自照料马匹，几个牧场学徒（jackeroo）则在厨房里生起火，打开闩了一整天的门窗，把男宾们的舒妥一一打点停当。

===Original===
Aunt Helen and I shared the one bedroom. As we had not fresh dresses to put on we had to make the best of our present toilet.

===Chinese===
海伦姨妈（aunt Helen）和我合住同一间卧室。我们没有新衣裳可换，只好把手头这副妆束将就着拾掇到最好。

===Original===
I unplaited my hair (shook the dust out of it) and wore it flowing. We washed and dusted ourselves, and wore as adornment﻿—roses. Crimson and cream roses paid the penalty of peeping in the window. Aunt Helen plucked some of them, which she put in my hair and belt, and pinned carefully at my throat, and then we were ready. Miss Beecham assured us there was nothing to be done, as the maids had set the table and prepared the viands for a cold meal before leaving in the morning, so we proceeded to the drawing-room to await the arrival of the other visitors. They soon made their appearance. First, two stout old squatters with big laughs and bigger corporations, then Miss Augusta Beecham, next Joe Archer the overseer, and the two other jackeroos. After these appeared a couple of governesses, Mr., Mrs., and Miss Benson, a clergyman, an auctioneer, a young friend of Harold’s from Cootamundra, a horse-buyer, a wool-classer, Miss Sarah Beecham, and then Miss Derrick brought herself and her dress in with great style and airs. She was garbed in a sea-green silk, and had jewellery on her neck, arms, and hair. Her self-confident mien was suggestive of the conquest of many masculine hearts. She was a big handsome woman. Beside her, I in my crushed white muslin dress was as overshadowed as a little white handkerchief would be in comparison to a gorgeous shawl heavily wrought in silks and velvet. She was given the best seat as though she were a princess. She sat down with great indifference, twirled a bracelet round her wrist, languidly opened her fan, and closed her eyes as she wafted it slowly to and fro.

===Chinese===
我解开辫子（抖净发间的尘土），让一头长发披泻而下。我们洗了洗，掸了掸尘，拿玫瑰作了装饰——绯红的、乳白的玫瑰，只因探进窗来窥望，便付出了代价。海伦姨妈摘下几朵，簪进我的头发，插上我的腰带，又细心地别在我喉前，我们便妆扮停当了。比彻姆小姐叫我们不必操劳：女仆们清晨动身前已摆好餐桌、备下冷餐酒馔，于是我们移步客厅，静候其余宾客到来。他们很快就到了。先来了两位上了年纪的大牧场主（squatter），身躯粗壮，笑声洪亮，肚皮更洪亮；接着是奥古斯塔·比彻姆小姐（Miss Augusta Beecham），随后是监工乔·阿彻（Joe Archer）和另外两名牧场学徒；这批人之后，又来了两位家庭女教师，本森先生、本森太太和本森小姐（Mr., Mrs., and Miss Benson）一家，一位牧师，一位拍卖商，哈罗德一位从库塔曼德拉（Cootamundra）来的年轻朋友，一位贩马客，一位羊毛分级员，莎拉·比彻姆小姐（Miss Sarah Beecham）；最后，德里克小姐（Miss Derrick）连人带那身衣裳，派头十足、架子十足地款款而入。她一身海绿色绸衫，颈上、臂上、发间都缀着珠光宝气。她那副自信满满的气派，叫人想到被她攻陷的男人心不知凡几。她是个高大俊美的妇人。在她旁边，我裹着一身揉皱的白细布裙，就像一方小小的白手帕，同一条满绣丝绒、华丽厚重的披巾摆在了一处，那样黯然失色。她得了上座，俨然一位公主。她大模大样地落座，捻着手腕上的镯子，懒洋洋地打开折扇，阖上双眼，把扇儿缓缓地摇来摇去。

===Original===
“By Jove, isn’t she a splendid creature?” enthusiastically whispered a gentleman sitting beside me.

===Chinese===
“老天作证，她岂不是个光彩照人的尤物？”坐在我身旁的一位先生热情地悄声说。

===Original===
I looked at her critically. She was very big, and in a bony stiff way was much developed in figure. She had a nice big nose, and a long well-shaped face, a thin straight mouth, and empty light eyes. If my attention had not been called to her I would not have noticed her one way or the other, but being pointed to as a beauty, I weighed her according to my idea of facial charm, and pronounced her one of the most insipid-looking people I had set eyes upon.

===Chinese===
我挑剔地打量她。她生得十分高大，一身嶙峋僵直的骨架，身段却发育得颇为可观。她有一只端正的大鼻子，一张修长周正的脸，一张薄而直的嘴，一双空空洞洞的浅色眼睛。若不是有人特地指给我看，我是好是歹根本不会留意她；可既被人指为美人，我便拿自己对姿色的那套标准来掂量她，断她是平生所见的面孔里最索然寡味的一张。

===Original===
She was the kind of woman with whom men become much infatuated. She would never make a fool of herself by letting her emotions run away with her, because she had no emotions, but lived in a sea of unruffled self-consciousness and self-confidence. Any man would be proud to introduce her as his wife to his friends whom he had brought home to dinner. She would adorn the head of his table. She would never worry him with silly ideas. She would never act with impropriety. She would never become a companion to her husband. Bah, a man does not want his wife to be a companion! There were myths and fables in the old day; so there are now. The story that men like a companion as well as a wife is an up-to-date one.

===Chinese===
她正是那种男人会为之神魂颠倒的女人。她绝不会任感情牵着跑、闹出丢人现眼的蠢事，因为她压根儿没有感情，只活在一片波澜不兴的自我意识与自我信心里。随便哪个男人把这样的妻子引见给请到家吃饭的朋友，都与有荣焉。她装点得起他餐桌的首席。她绝不用傻念头去烦扰他。她绝无失礼的举止。她也绝不会成为丈夫的知己。呸，男人根本不要妻子做知己！古时有神话寓言，如今照样有。说男人除妻子外还喜欢个知己，不过是个时新的传说罢了。

===Original===
This train of thought was interrupted by our host, who appeared in the doorway, clad from sole to neck in white. We steered for the dining-room﻿—twenty-two all told﻿—thirteen men and nine representatives of the other sex.

===Chinese===
这串思绪被我们的东道打断了。他出现在门口，从脚板到脖颈裹着一身雪白。我们朝饭厅鱼贯而去——男女合计二十二位——男人十三个，另一性的代表九位。

===Original===
Aunt Helen got one seat of honour near the head of the table and Miss Derrick another. I drifted to the foot among the unimportant younger fry, where we had no end of fun and idle chatter. We had to wait on ourselves, and as all formality was dispensed with, it was something like a picnic.

===Chinese===
海伦姨妈得了靠近上首的一个荣座，德里克小姐得了另一个。我随波逐流，漂到桌子末尾，混在一群无足轻重的毛孩子中间——在那儿，我们的耍笑闲磕牙没了完。我们得自己伺候自己，又全免了客套，倒像是一场野餐。

===Original===
The heat was excessive. Every window and door were open, and the balmy, almost imperceptible, zephyrs which faintly rustled the curtains and kissed our perspiration-beaded brows were rich with many scents from the wide old flower-garden, which, despite the drought, brought forth a wealth of blossom.

===Chinese===
暑气逼人。门窗尽数敞着，一缕缕温软得几乎察觉不出的微风，轻轻拂响帘幔，吻着我们沁满汗珠的额头，饱含着那座开阔的老花园里飘来的种种香气——虽是大旱年头，它依旧捧出满园繁花。

===Original===
When done eating we had to wash the dishes. Such a scamper ensued back and forwards to the kitchen, which rang with noise, and merriment. Everyone was helping, hindering, laughing, joking, teasing, and brimming over with fun and enjoyment. When we had completed this task, dancing was proposed. Some of the elderly and more sensible people said it was too hot, but all the young folks did not care a rap for the temperature. Harold had no objections, Miss Derrick was agreeable, Miss Benson announced herself ready and willing, and Joe Archer said he was “leppin’ ” to begin, so we adjourned to the dancing-room and commenced operations.

===Chinese===
吃罢饭，我们还得洗盘子。于是乎一阵大折腾，人人在厨房里跑进跑出，厨房里喧声震耳，笑闹连天。人人都在帮忙，也都在帮倒忙，笑着、逗着、闹着，欢畅得从心里往外溢。这桩差事收了场，便有人提议跳舞。几位年长些、明白些的说天太热，可年轻人对这温度连一个子儿也不放在心上。哈罗德没有异议，德里克小姐深表赞同，本森小姐宣称自己随时奉候、满心愿意，乔·阿彻说他“急得脚底板直蹦跶”，恨不能立刻开场，于是我们移进舞厅，拉开了阵仗。

===Original===
I played the piano for the first quadrille, and aunt Helen for the second dance. It was most enjoyable. There was a table at one end of the room on which was any amount of cherries, lollies, cake, dainties, beers, syrups, and glasses, where all could regale themselves without ceremony or bother every time the inclination seized them. Several doors and windows of the long room opened into the garden, and, provided one had no fear of snakes, it was delightful to walk amid the flowers and cool oneself between dances.

===Chinese===
头一支四方舞由我弹琴，第二支舞归海伦姨妈弹。实在是快活极了。屋子一头摆着一张桌子，上头的樱桃、糖果、糕点、各色细巧吃食、啤酒、果子露、杯盏，应有尽有；谁兴头上来，随时可以不拘礼数、不费周章地自取自享。长厅的好几道门窗都开向花园，只要不怕蛇，舞与舞之间到花丛里走走、凉快凉快，再可心不过。

===Original===
A little exertion on such a night made us very hot. After the third dance the two old squatters, the horse-buyer, the clergyman, and Mr. Benson disappeared. Judging from the hilarity of their demeanour and the killing odour of their breaths when they returned an hour or so later, during their absence they must have conscientiously sampled the contents of every whisky decanter on the dining-room sideboard.

===Chinese===
这样的夜晚，稍一动弹便热得够呛。第三支舞过后，那两位上了年纪的大牧场主、贩马客、牧师和本森先生都不见了踪影。约莫一个钟头后他们回来，瞧那副乐陶陶的做派，闻那股冲鼻子的酒气，可知他们不在的这一阵，准是把饭厅餐具柜上每一只威士忌细颈壶里的存货，都尽心尽责地逐一品鉴过了。

===Original===
I could not dance, but had no lack of partners, as, ladies being in the minority, the gentlemen had to occasionally put up with their own sex in a dance.

===Chinese===
我不会跳舞，舞伴却从不短少——女客占少数，先生们只得偶尔凑合着同自己的性别共舞一场。

===Original===
“Let’s take a breeze now and have a song or two, but no more dancing for a while,” said some of them; but Harold Beecham said, “One more turn, and then we will have a long spell and a change of programme.”

===Chinese===
“咱们这会儿出去透透风，唱一两支歌，舞且停一停吧。”他们中间有几位这么说。哈罗德·比彻姆（Harold Beecham）却说：“再转一圈，然后我们好好歇上一场，换一换节目。”

===Original===
He ordered Joe Archer to play a waltz, and the floor soon held several whirling couples. Harold “requested the pleasure” of me﻿—the first time that night. I demurred. He would not take a refusal.

===Chinese===
他吩咐乔·阿彻弹一支圆舞曲，舞场上很快旋起了好几对人儿。哈罗德向我“敬请赏光”——当晚这是头一回。我推辞。他不容回绝。

===Original===
“Believe me, if I felt competent, Mr. Beecham, I would not refuse. I cannot dance. It will be no pleasure to you.”

===Chinese===
“请相信我，比彻姆先生，我若跳得来，是不会拒绝的。我不会跳舞。同我跳，您得不着半点乐趣。”

===Original===
“Allow me to be the best judge of what is a pleasure to me,” he said, quietly placing me in position.

===Chinese===
“什么于我是乐趣，请容我自己来裁断。”他说着，不动声色地把我安放进舞步的位置。

===Original===
He swung me once round the room, and then through an open window into the garden.

===Chinese===
他拥着我绕场旋转了一遭，随即从一扇敞开的窗子转进了花园。

===Original===
“I am sorry that I haven’t had more time to look after you today. Come round into my room. I want to strike a bargain with you,” were his words.

===Chinese===
“很抱歉，今天我没多腾出工夫照应你。到我屋里来一趟吧。我要同你做成一笔交易。”他这样说。

===Original===
I followed him in the direction of a detached building in the garden. This was Harold’s particular domain. It contained three rooms﻿—one a library and office, another an arsenal and deed-room, and the third, into which he led me, was a sort of sitting-room, containing a piano, facilities for washing, a table, easy-chairs, and other things. As we entered I noticed the lamp, burning brightly on the table, gleamed on the face of a clock on the wall, which pointed to half past ten.

===Chinese===
我跟着他朝花园里一座独立的屋子走去。这是哈罗德的自家天地。里头有三间房——一间是书房兼办公室，一间是兵器房兼契据房，第三间便是他领我进去的那间，算是间起居室，屋里有一架钢琴、盥洗的家什、一张桌子、几张安乐椅，还有些别物。进门时我注意到，桌上亮堂堂燃着的灯光，正映在墙上一面钟的钟面上——钟指着十点半。

===Original===
We stood beside the table, some distance apart, and, facing me, he said:

===Chinese===
我们站在桌旁，隔着一段距离，他面朝着我，说道：

===Original===
“It is no use of me making a long yarn about nothing. I’m sure you know what I want to say better than I do myself. You always are wonderfully smart at seeing through a fellow. Tell me, will it be yes or no?”

===Chinese===
“我犯不上没事找事，绕一大篇闲话。我想说什么，你保准比我自己还清楚。你向来有本事一眼把一个男人看穿。告诉我，答应，还是不答应？”

===Original===
This was an experience in love. He did not turn red or white, or yellow or green, nor did he tremble or stammer, or cry or laugh, or become fierce or passionate, or tender or anything but just himself, as I had always known him. He displayed no more emotion than had he been inviting me to a picnic. This was not as I had pictured a man would tell his love, or as I had read of it, heard of it, or wished it should be. A curious feeling﻿—disappointment, perhaps﻿—stole over me. His matter-of-fact coolness flabbergasted me.

===Chinese===
这便是一场求爱的经历。他不变红，不变白，也不发青发绿；不颤抖，不结巴；不哭，不笑；不激烈，不痴狂，也不温柔——什么都不是，只是他自己，同我素来认得的他一般无二。他表露的情愫，不比邀我去野餐时多一丝一毫。这同我想象中一个男人吐露爱情的模样全不相干，同我书上读来的、耳朵听来的、心里盼着的也全不相干。一种古怪的感觉——兴许是失望——悄悄袭上我的心头。他这副就事论事的冷静，把我惊得瞠目结舌。

===Original===
“Is this not rather sudden? You have given me no intimation of your intentions,” I stammered.

===Chinese===
“这岂不太仓促了些？您的意向，我从没得到过半点风声。”我讷讷地说。

===Original===
“I didn’t think it wise to dawdle any longer,” he replied. “Surely you have known what I’ve been driving at ever since I first clapped eyes on you. There’s plenty of time. I don’t want to hurry you, only I want you to be engaged to me for safety.”

===Chinese===
“我觉着再磨蹭下去就不聪明了。”他答道。“打我头一眼见着你，我打的什么主意，你必定早知道了。时间有的是。我不想催你，只是想叫你先同我订下婚约，图个稳妥。”

===Original===
He spoke as usual in his slow twangy drawl, which would have proclaimed his Colonial nationality anywhere. No word of love was uttered to me and none requested from me.

===Chinese===
他照旧操着那副慢吞吞、拖腔拖调的土腔说话——这口音无论走到哪里，都会替他亮明殖民地人的出身。没有一句情话对我说出口，也没有向我讨一句。

===Original===
I put it down to his conceit. I thought that he fancied he could win any woman, and me without the least palaver or trouble. I felt annoyed. I said aloud, “I will become engaged to you;” to myself I added, “Just for a little while, the more to surprise and take the conceit out of you when the time comes.”

===Chinese===
我把这归咎于他的自负。我想，他准以为自己不费半句殷勤、半点周章，便能赢得随便哪个女人——连我也不例外。我心头着恼。我出声说道：“我愿意同你订婚。”心里又补上一句：“不过订一小阵子，好等时候一到，加倍地叫你吃惊，煞尽你的自负。”

===Original===
Now that I understand his character I know that it was not conceit, but just his quiet unpretending way. He had meant all his actions towards me, and had taken mine in return.

===Chinese===
如今我懂得了他的性子，才知道那并不是自负，只是他那安静而不事张扬的做派罢了。他待我的一举一动都是当真的，也把我回敬的举动一一当了真。

===Original===
“Thank you, Sybylla, that is all I want. We will talk about the matter more some other time. I will go up to Caddagat next Sunday. You have surprised me nearly out of my wits,” here he laughed. “I never dreamt you would say yes so easily, just like any other girl. I thought I would have a lot of trouble with you.”

===Chinese===
“谢谢你，西比拉，我要的就是这一句。这件事我们改日再细谈。下个礼拜天我就动身到卡达加特去。你险些把我惊得魂儿都出了窍。”说到这里他笑了。“我做梦也不曾想到，你会这么轻易地就答应了，同随便哪个姑娘一模一样。我原打算在你身上费好一番周折呢。”

===Original===
He approached me and was stooping to kiss me. I cannot account for my action or condemn it sufficiently. It was hysterical﻿—the outcome of an overstrung, highly excitable, and nervous temperament. Perhaps my vanity was wounded, and my tendency to strike when touched was up in arms. The calm air of ownership with which Harold drew near annoyed me, or, as Sunday-school teachers would explain it, Satan got hold of me. He certainly placed a long strong riding-whip on the table beneath my hand! As Harold stooped with the intention of pressing his lips to mine, I quickly raised the whip and brought it with all my strength right across his face. The instant the whip had descended I would have smashed my arm on the doorpost to recall that blow. But that was impossible. It had left a great weal on the healthy suntanned skin. His moustache had saved his lips, but it had caught his nose, the left cheek, had blinded the left eye, and had left a cut on the temple from which drops of blood were rolling down his cheek and staining his white coat. A momentary gleam of anger shot into his eyes and he gave a gasp, whether of surprise, pain, or annoyance, I know not. He made a gesture towards me. I half expected and fervently wished he would strike. The enormity of what I had done paralysed me. The whip fell from my fingers and I dropped on to a low lounge behind me, and placing my elbows on my knees crouchingly buried my face in my hands; my hair tumbled softly over my shoulders and reached the floor, as though to sympathetically curtain my humiliation. Oh, that Harold would thrash me severely! It would have infinitely relieved me. I had done a mean unwomanly thing in thus striking a man, who by his great strength and sex was debarred retaliation. I had committed a violation of self-respect and common decency; I had given a man an ignominious blow in the face with a riding-whip. And that man was Harold Beecham, who with all his strength and great stature was so wondrously gentle﻿—who had always treated my whims and nonsense with something like the amused tolerance held by a great Newfoundland for the pranks of a kitten.

===Chinese===
他朝我走近，俯下身来要吻我。我这举动，我自己既解说不了，也贬斥得不够。那是一阵歇斯底里——一副过度紧绷、一点就着、神经兮兮的性子结出的果子。兴许是我的虚荣心着了伤，我一挨碰便要动手还击的老脾气便全副披挂起来。哈罗德走近时那副气定神闲的占有者气派惹恼了我；或者，照主日学老师们的讲法，是撒旦攫住了我。他可确实把一根长大结实的马鞭搁在了桌上，就搁在我手底下！哈罗德俯身要把嘴唇印上我的嘴唇，我倏地举起鞭子，使尽全身力气，一鞭正正抽过他的脸。鞭子落下去的那一瞬，我甘愿把这条胳膊在门柱上撞断，好收回那一击。可是不能够。健康晒黑的面皮上已经隆起一道长长的鞭痕。胡子护住了他的嘴唇，鞭梢却扫着了鼻子、左边面颊，打得他左眼睁不开，太阳穴上还豁开一道口子，血珠一颗颗滚下面颊，玷污了他的白衣。一星怒火从他眼里倏然掠过，他猛抽一口气——是惊，是痛，还是恼，我不知道。他朝我这边做了一个手势。我半是料到、又热切巴望他会还手打来。我闯下的滔天大祸把我吓瘫了。鞭子从指间脱落，我跌坐到身后的矮榻上，手肘支着膝盖，蜷缩着把脸埋进双手；头发软软地泼洒在肩头，一直垂到地上，仿佛要给我这场羞辱挂一道怜恤的帘。唉，哈罗德要是狠狠地揍我一顿该多好！那会叫我松快到万倍。我干了件卑劣、没有女人样的事——这样去打一个男人，而他凭着一身伟力、凭着他的性别，是不得还手的。我犯下了对自尊与起码体面的亵渎；我拿一根马鞭，打了一个男人一记叫他蒙羞的耳光。而那个男人，是哈罗德·比彻姆——他空有那一身力气、那样魁伟的身量，却温存得出奇——他一向待我那些任性胡闹，怀着的好比一条大纽芬兰犬看小猫崽儿撒泼时那种含笑的宽容。

===Original===
The clock struck eleven.

===Chinese===
钟敲了十一下。

===Original===
“A less stinging rebuke would have served your purpose. I had no idea that a simple caress from the man whose proposal of marriage you had just accepted would be considered such an unpardonable familiarity.”

===Chinese===
“轻些扎人的训斥，原也尽够达到你的目的了。我真想不到，一个你刚刚应允了婚事的男人，给你一个普普通通的爱抚，竟会被看作这般不可饶恕的放肆。”

===Original===
Harold’s voice fell clearly, calmly, cuttingly on the silence. He moved away to the other end of the room and I heard the sound of water.

===Chinese===
哈罗德的嗓音清晰、平静、尖利，一字字落在寂静上。他踱到屋子另一头去了，我听见一阵水声。

===Original===
A desire filled me to tell him that I did not think he had attempted a familiarity, but that I had been mad. I wished to say I could not account for my action, but I was dumb. My tongue refused to work, and I felt as though I would choke. The splash of the water came from the other end of the room. I knew he must be suffering acute pain in his eye. A far lighter blow had kept me sleepless a whole night. A fear possessed me that I might have permanently injured his sight. The splash of water ceased. His footfall stopped beside me. I could feel he was within touching distance, but I did not move.

===Chinese===
我心里涨满一股想望，想告诉他：我并不认为他存过什么放肆的企图，是我疯了。我想说我解释不清自己的举动，可我哑了。舌头不肯听差，只觉得一口气噎在喉咙里。哗哗的水声从屋子那头传来。我知道他那只眼睛必定痛得厉害——轻得多的挨一下，还曾叫我整夜整夜合不上眼。一种恐惧攫住了我：我怕是把他的眼力永远伤坏了。泼水声停了。他的脚步在我身旁停住。我觉得出他就在伸手可及的地方，可我一动不动。

===Original===
Oh, the horrible stillness! Why did he not speak? He placed his hand lightly on my head.

===Chinese===
啊，这可怕的岑寂！他怎么不说话？他把一只手轻轻搁在我头上。

===Original===
“It doesn’t matter, Syb. I know you didn’t mean to hurt me. I suppose you thought you couldn’t affect my dark, old, saddle-flap-looking phiz. That is one of the disadvantages of being a big lumbering concern like I am. Jump up. That’s the girl.”

===Chinese===
“不要紧的，西比。我知道你不是存心伤我。我猜你是想着，我这么一张黑黢黢、老巴巴、马鞍皮似的脸膛，怎么打也打不出个好歹。做我这样一个大而笨的家伙，吃亏处之一便在这里。起来吧。这才是好姑娘。”

===Original===
I arose. I was giddy, and would have fallen but for Harold steadying me by the shoulder. I looked up at him nervously and tried to ask his forgiveness, but I failed.

===Chinese===
我起身。我头晕眼花，若不是哈罗德扶住我的肩，早就要栽倒。我怯怯地仰头望他，想求他饶恕，却没能说出口。

===Original===
“Good heavens, child, you are as white as a sheet! I was a beast to speak harshly to you.” He held a glass of water to my lips and I drank.

===Chinese===
“天哪，孩子，你白得像一张纸！我方才话说得重，真是个畜生。”他把一杯水送到我唇边，我喝了。

===Original===
“Great Jupiter, there’s nothing to worry about! I know you hadn’t the slightest intention of hurting me. It’s nothing﻿—I’ll be right in a few moments. I’ve often been amused at and have admired your touch-me-not style. You only forgot you had something in your hand.”

===Chinese===
“我的老天爷，没什么好犯愁的！我知道你丝毫没有伤我的意思。不碍事——过一会儿就好了。你那副碰不得的做派，我时常看得有趣，心里也佩服。你只是一时忘了手里还拿着东西。”

===Original===
He had taken it quite as a matter of fact, and was excusing me in the kindest possible terms.

===Chinese===
他把这件事全然当作稀松平常，还用着顶顶和软的字眼替我开脱。

===Original===
“Good gracious, you mustn’t stew over such a trifling accident! It’s nothing. Just tie this handkerchief on for me, please, and then we’ll go back to the others or there will be a search-party after us.”

===Chinese===
“哎呀，这么一桩芝麻绿豆的意外，你千万犯不着熬煎！没事的。劳驾拿这块手帕替我扎上，然后我们就回到大家那儿去，不然人家要派出搜寻队来找我们了。”

===Original===
He could have tied the handkerchief just as well himself﻿—it was only out of kindly tact he requested my services. I accepted his kindness gratefully. He sank on his knee so that I could reach him, and I tied a large white handkerchief across the injured part. He could not open his eye, and hot water poured from it, but he made light of the idea of it paining. I was feeling better now, so we returned to the ballroom. The clock struck the half-hour after eleven as we left the room. Harold entered by one door and, I by another, and I slipped into a seat as though I had been there some time.

===Chinese===
这手帕他自己原也照样扎得——他请我效这份劳，全出于一片体贴的机心。我感激地领受了他的好意。他单膝跪下去，好让我够得着，我便拿一条大白手帕替他扎住伤处。他那只眼睛睁不开了，眼里滚热的泪水直往外涌，他却把疼字说得轻如鸿毛。我这时已缓过些来，我们便回到舞厅。出屋时，钟敲了十一下半。哈罗德打一道门进去，我打另一道门进去，我悄悄溜进一个座位，仿佛早就在那儿坐了半晌。

===Original===
There were only a few people in the room. The majority were absent﻿—some lovemaking, others playing cards. Miss Beecham was one who was not thus engaged. She exclaimed at once:

===Chinese===
屋里只剩寥寥几个人。大多数都不见了——有的谈情说爱去了，有的打牌去了。比彻姆小姐是没去干这些营生的一位。她当即叫了起来：

===Original===
“Good gracious, boy, what have you done to yourself?”

===Chinese===
“老天爷，孩子，你把自己弄成什么样子啦？”

===Original===
“Looks as if he had been interviewing a belligerent tramp,” said aunt Helen, smilingly.

===Chinese===
“瞧这模样，像是会见过一个好斗的流浪汉。”海伦姨妈笑吟吟地说。

===Original===
“He’s run into the clothesline, that’s what he’s done,” said Miss Augusta confidently, after she had peeped beneath the bandage.

===Chinese===
“他是撞上晾衣绳了，就是这么回事。”奥古斯塔小姐朝绷带底下瞄了一眼，满有把握地说。

===Original===
“You ought to get a bun for guessing, aunt Gus,” said Harold laughing.

===Chinese===
“姑妈这一猜，该赏块甜面包才是。”哈罗德笑着说。

===Original===
“I told them to put the clotheslines up when they had done with them. I knew there would be an accident.”

===Chinese===
“我早吩咐过他们，晾衣绳用完了就该收上去。我就知道早晚要出事。”

===Original===
“Perhaps they were put up high enough for ordinary purposes,” remarked her nephew.

===Chinese===
“兴许那绳子的高度，对付寻常的个头儿原是尽够用的。”她的侄子搭了一句。

===Original===
“Let me do something for you, dear.”

===Chinese===
“让我替你做点什么吧，亲爱的。”

===Original===
“No, thank you, aunt Gus. It is nothing,” he said carelessly, and the matter dropped.

===Chinese===
“不必了，谢谢你，格西姑妈。没的事。”他漫不经心地说，这桩事便就此揭过。

===Original===
Harold Beecham was not a man to invite inquiry concerning himself.

===Chinese===
哈罗德·比彻姆不是个会招惹旁人对自己刨根问底的人。

===Original===
Seeing I was unobserved by the company, I slipped away to indulge in my foolish habit of asking the why and the wherefore of things. Why had Harold Beecham (who was a sort of young sultan who could throw the handkerchief where he liked) chosen me of all women? I had no charms to recommend me﻿—none of the virtues which men demand of the woman they wish to make their wife. To begin with, I was small, I was erratic and unorthodox, I was nothing but a tomboy﻿—and, cardinal disqualification, I was ugly. Why, then, had he proposed matrimony to me? Was it merely a whim? Was he really in earnest?

===Chinese===
瞧着没有人留意我，我便溜了出去，纵容自己那点愚蠢的老习惯——凡事都要问一个为什么、凭什么。哈罗德·比彻姆（他好比一位年轻的苏丹，手帕爱抛给谁便抛给谁）为什么偏偏在天下女人中间挑中了我？我没有任何动人之处方堪自荐——男人指望他们想娶的女人具备的诸般品性，我一样也不占。头一条，我个子小；我行事乖张，离经叛道；我十足是个疯野的假小子——而头号大忌：我生得丑。那么，他为什么偏向我求婚？难道只是一时兴起？他到底是当真的么？

===Original===
The night was soft and dark; after being out in it for a time I could discern the shrubs dimly silhouetted against the light. The music struck up inside again. A step approached me on the gravelled walk among the flowers, and Harold called me softly by name. I answered him.

===Chinese===
夜色温柔而浓黑；在外头待了一阵，我便辨得出花木衬着灯光的淡淡剪影。屋里乐声又起。花间砾石小径上，一阵脚步向我走近，哈罗德柔声唤我的名字。我应了他。

===Original===
“Come,” he said, “we are going to dance; will you be my partner?”

===Chinese===
“来吧，”他说，“我们要跳舞了；你肯做我的舞伴吗？”

===Original===
We danced, and then followed songs and parlour games, and it was in the small hours when the merry goodnights were all said and we had retired to rest. Aunt Helen dropped to sleep in a short time; but I lay awake listening to the soft distant call of the mopokes in the scrub beyond the stables.

===Chinese===
我们跳了舞，随后是唱歌、客厅游戏；等一路欢声的道别统统道罢、我们各自归寝，已是凌晨时分。海伦姨妈不多时便睡熟了；我却醒着躺在床上，听马厩那头灌木林里袋鸮（mopoke）一声声柔远的啼唤。

## XXI My Unladylike Behaviour Again / 第21章 我又不淑女了

===Original===
Joe Archer was appointed to take us home on the morrow. When our host was seeing us off﻿—still with his eye covered﻿—he took opportunity of whispering to me his intention of coming to Caddagat on the following Sunday.

===Chinese===
次日，派定由乔·阿彻送我们回家。男主人送我们上路时——那只眼还蒙着——趁机会对我悄声说，他打算下个星期天到卡达加特来。

===Original===
Early in the afternoon of that day I took a book, and, going down the road some distance, climbed up a broad-branched willow-tree to wait for him.

===Chinese===
到了那天午后不久，我带上一本书，顺着大路走出一程，爬上一棵枝丫开阔的大柳树，坐等他来。

===Original===
It was not long before he appeared at a smart canter. He did not see me in the tree, but his horse did, and propping, snorted wildly, and gave a backward run. Harold spurred him, he bucked spiritedly. Harold now saw me and sang out:

===Chinese===
没等多久，他便踏着利落的跑步来了。他没有看见树上的我，他那匹马却看见了——猛地定住前蹄，狂野地打着响鼻，一个劲儿倒退。哈罗德用马刺一催，它便起劲地尥起蹶子。哈罗德这才望见我，扬声喊道：

===Original===
“I say, don’t frighten him any more or he’ll fling me, saddle and all. I haven’t got a crupper or a breastplate.”

===Chinese===
“我说，别再吓唬它了，不然它要把我连人带鞍甩出去的。我可没上鞧带，也没上胸革。”

===Original===
“Why haven’t you, then? Hang on to him. I do like the look of you while the horse is going on like that.”

===Chinese===
“那你为什么不装呢？坐稳了抓牢它。它这么撒野的时候，你那副模样我实在爱看。”

===Original===
He had dismounted, and had thrown the bridle rein over a post of the fence.

===Chinese===
他已下了马，把缰绳搭在栅栏的一根桩上。

===Original===
“I came with nothing but a girth, and that loose, as it was so hot; and I was as near as twopence to being off, saddle and all. You might have been the death of me,” he said good-humouredly.

===Chinese===
“来的时候天太热，我光系了根肚带，还松松垮垮的；方才差一丁点儿，我就连人带鞍摔下去了。你差点儿要了我的命。”他好脾气地说。

===Original===
“Had I been, my fortune would have been made,” I replied.

===Chinese===
“你若真要了我的命，我的好运可就赚到手了。”我答道。

===Original===
“How do you make that out? You’re as complimentary as ever.”

===Chinese===
“这话怎么讲？你恭维人的功夫倒是一点没退步。”

===Original===
“Everyone would be wanting to engage me as the great noxious weedkiller and poisonous insect exterminator if I made away with you,” I answered. I gave him an invitation to take a seat with me, and accepting, he swung up with easy grace. There was any amount of accommodation for the two of us on the good-natured branches of the old willow-tree.

===Chinese===
“我要是结果了你，人人都要抢着聘我当大名鼎鼎的毒草铲除家兼毒虫扑灭师了。”我答道。我邀他上来同我并坐，他应了，轻巧潇洒地攀了上来。老柳树那些宽厚的枝干上，容我们两个绰绰有余。

===Original===
When he had settled himself, my companion said, “Now, Syb, I’m ready for you. Fire away. But wait a minute, I’ve got something here for you which I hope you’ll like.”

===Chinese===
他坐定身子，我的同伴说道：“好了，西比，我候着你呢。有话尽管放马过来。不过且慢——我这儿有样东西给你，希望你会喜欢。”

===Original===
As he searched in his pockets, I noticed that his eye had quite recovered, though there was still a slight mark on his cheek. He handed me a tiny morocco case, which on being opened disclosed a costly ring. I have about as much idea of the prices of things as a turkey would have. Perhaps that ring cost thirty pounds or possibly fifty guineas, for all I know. It was very heavy, and had a big diamond supported on either side by a large sapphire, and had many small gems surrounding it.

===Chinese===
他往口袋里摸索的当口，我注意到他那只眼睛已经好利索了，只是面颊上还留着一点浅浅的印子。他递给我一只小小的摩洛哥皮盒子，打开来，里面是一枚贵重的戒指。要论我对东西价目的见识，同一只火鸡不相上下。那戒指也许值三十镑，也许值五十几尼，天晓得。它沉甸甸的，当中一粒大钻石，两旁各托着一粒大蓝宝石，四周还镶着许多小宝石。

===Original===
“Let me see if it fits,” he said, taking my hand; but I drew it away.

===Chinese===
“让我瞧瞧合不合尺寸。”他说着来拉我的手，我却把手抽开了。

===Original===
“No; don’t you put it on. That would make us irrevocably engaged.”

===Chinese===
“不行；你别给我戴上。那我们就等于订死了终身，再无翻悔。”

===Original===
“Isn’t that what we intend to be?” he said in a tone of surprise.

===Chinese===
“我们本不就是打算订婚的吗？”他语带惊讶地说。

===Original===
“Not just yet; that is what I want to say to you. We will have three months’ probation to see how we get on. At the end of that time, if we manage to sail along smoothly, we’ll have the real thing; until then we will not be any more than we have been to each other.”

===Chinese===
“眼下还不到时候；这正是我要对你说的话。我们先试三个月的考验期，看看处得怎么样。期满之时，若是我们顺顺当当地处下来，就来真的；在那之前，我们彼此间不比先前多一分。”

===Original===
“But what am I to do in the meantime?” he asked, with amusement curving the corners of his mouth.

===Chinese===
“可这期间我该做些什么呢？”他问，嘴角弯起好玩的笑意。

===Original===
“Do! Do the usual thing, of course; but don’t pay me any special attentions, or I’ll be done with you at once.”

===Chinese===
“做什么！当然是照平常那样做；只是别对我献什么特别的殷勤，不然我立刻同你断了。”

===Original===
“What’s your idea for this?”

===Chinese===
“你这又是打的什么主意？”

===Original===
“It is no use making fools of ourselves; we might change our minds.”

===Chinese===
“犯不着白白叫我们自己变成傻子；我们说不定会变卦呢。”

===Original===
“Very well; so be it,” he said laughing. “I might have known you would have things arranged different from any other girl. But you’ll take the ring and wear it, won’t you? Let me put it on.”

===Chinese===
“很好；就这么办。”他笑着说。“我早该料到，凡事到了你手里，安排得必定同别的姑娘两样。不过戒指你且收下戴着，好不好？让我给你戴上。”

===Original===
“No; I won’t let you put a finger on me till the three months are up. Then, if we definitely make up our minds, you can put it on; but till then, don’t for the life of you hint by word or sign that we have any sort of an arrangement between us. Give me the ring and I’ll wear it sometimes.”

===Chinese===
“不；三个月期满之前，你一根指头也别想碰我。到那时，若是我们拿定了主意，你再给我戴上；在那之前，打死也不许你用言语或神色点破半分，叫人看出我们之间有丝毫默契。戒指给我，我有时会戴一戴。”

===Original===
He handed it to me again, and I tried it on. It was a little large. Harold took it, and tried to put it on one of his fingers. It would fit on none but the very top of his little finger. We laughed heartily at the disparity in the size of our hands.

===Chinese===
他又把戒指递给我，我试戴了一下。稍稍大了些。哈罗德接过去，往自己的手指上套。套遍诸指，只有小指最末一节才容得下。我们看着两只手尺寸这般悬殊，不由放声大笑。

===Original===
“I’ll agree to your bargain,” he said. “But you’ll be really engaged to me all the same.”

===Chinese===
“你这桩买卖，我应下了。”他说。“不过无论如何，你终究算是真订了给我的。”

===Original===
“Yes; under those conditions. Then it will not matter if we have a tiff. We can part, and no one will be the wiser.”

===Chinese===
“好；就依这些条件。这样，即便我们拌几句嘴也不碍事了。尽可散伙，谁也瞧不出半点儿破绽。”

===Original===
On my suggesting that it was now time to go to the house, he swung himself down by a branch and turned to assist me. Descending from that tree was a feat which presented no difficulties to me when no one was by, but now it seemed an awkward performance.

===Chinese===
我说此刻该回宅子去了，他攀着一根树枝一悠落地，回身来扶我。从那棵树上下来，这本是我独个儿时全不费事的一桩功夫，此刻却显得狼狈别扭起来。

===Original===
“Just lead your horse underneath, so that I can get on to his back, thence to the ground quite easily,” I said.

===Chinese===
“只消把你的马牵到树底下来，我好踩上马背，再从马背上轻轻松松落了地。”我说。

===Original===
“No fear! Warrigal wouldn’t stand that kind of dodge. Won’t I do? I don’t think your weight will quite squash me,” he returned, placing himself in leapfrog position, and I stepped on to his back and slid from there to the ground quite easily.

===Chinese===
“才不呢！瓦里加尔才不吃这一套把戏。有我不行吗？谅你这点分量压不扁我。”他答着，弯下身子摆出跳山羊的架势；我便踩上他的背，顺势溜到地上，轻轻松松。

===Original===
That afternoon, when leaving the house, I had been followed by one of the dogs, which, when I went up the willow-tree, amused himself chasing water lizards along the bank of the creek. He treed one, and kept up a furious barking at the base of its refuge. The yelping had disturbed grannie where she was reading on the veranda, and coming down the road under a big umbrella to see what the noise was about, as luck would have it she was in the nick of time to catch me standing on Harold Beecham’s back. Grannie frequently showed marked displeasure regarding what she termed my larrikinism, but never before had I seen her so thoroughly angry. Shutting her umbrella, she thrust at me with it, saying, “shame! shame! You’ll come to some harm yet, you immodest, bold, bad hussy! I will write to your mother about you. Go home at once, miss, and confine yourself in your room for the remainder of the day, and don’t dare eat anything until tomorrow. Spend the time in fasting, and pray to God to make you better. I don’t know what makes you so forward with men. Your mother and aunt never gave me the slightest trouble in that way.”

===Chinese===
那天午后出门时，家里的一条狗跟了我来；待我上了柳树，它便在溪岸边追水蜥蜴自遣。它把一只撵上了树，对着那逃命的所在在树底下狂吠个不了。那阵吠声搅扰了正在游廊上读书的外婆。她撑着一柄大伞顺路下来看个究竟，偏巧来得正是时候，正撞见我站在哈罗德·比彻姆的背上。外婆对于她所谓的我那股“野小子作风”，常要明白地板起脸子，可我从未见她这样怒透心底。她收拢伞，拿伞尖直戳我，说道：“不害臊！不害臊！你早晚要闯出祸来，你这个不知廉耻、轻狂放肆的坏丫头！我要写信告诉你母亲。马上给我回家去，小姐，今天余下的工夫关在自己屋里，明天之前不许进口一点东西。就这样斋戒着，求上帝叫你学好。我不知道你怎么在男人跟前这样轻狂。你母亲和你姨妈当年在这上头，从没给过我一丝一毫的麻烦。”

===Original===
She pushed me from her in anger, and I turned and strode housewards without a word or glancing behind. I could hear grannie deprecating my conduct as I departed, and Harold quietly and decidedly differing from her.

===Chinese===
她怒冲冲把我一推。我转身朝宅子大步走去，一言不发，也不回头。走开时我听得见外婆数落我的行止，也听得见哈罗德平心静气却毫不含糊地不以为然。

===Original===
From the time of my infancy punishment of any description never had a beneficial effect upon me. But dear old grannie was acting according to her principles in putting me through a term of penance, so I shut myself in my room as directed, with goodwill towards her at my heart. I was burning with shame. Was I bold and immodest with men, as accused of being? It was the last indiscretion I would intentionally have been guilty of. In associating with men I never realize that the trifling difference of sex is sufficient to be a great wall between us. The fact of sex never for an instant enters my head, and I find it as easy to be chummy with men as with girls: men in return have always been very good, and have treated me in the same way.

===Chinese===
打从襁褓时起，无论什么样的惩罚，从来不曾对我有过半点儿益处。可是亲爱的老外婆是在照她的原则行事，罚我领受一功补赎，我便遵命把自己关进房里，心里对她仍怀着好意。我羞得浑身火烧。难道我真如在男人跟前轻狂、不知检点——如她所指责的那样？那是我断断不会有意去犯的一桩失检。同男人相处时，我从不曾意识到性别上那点微不足道的差异，就足以在我们中间砌起一道高墙。性别这回事从没有一瞬间走进过我的脑子；我发觉同男人亲热相处，同同姑娘们相处一样容易：男人呢，也一向待我极好，同样拿同伴的情分待我。

===Original===
On returning from her walk grannie came to my room, brought me some preachy books to read, and held out to me the privilege of saying I was sorry, and being restored to my usual place in the society of the household.

===Chinese===
外婆散步回来，到我房里，带来几本满纸说教的书给我读，又向我敞开一项恩典：只消说一声知错了，便可恢复我在家中众人之间的原位。

===Original===
“Grannie, I cannot say I am sorry and promise to reform, for my conscience does not reproach me in the least. I had no evil﻿—not even a violation of manners﻿—in my intentions; but I am sorry that I vexed you,” I said.

===Chinese===
“外婆，我说不出‘我错了’，也担保不了改过，因为我的良心一点儿也没有责备我。我的用意里没有恶意——连失礼都谈不上；只是我惹恼了你，这个我难过。”我说。

===Original===
“Vexing me is not the sinful part of it. It is your unrepentant heart that fills me with fears for your future. I will leave you here to think by yourself. The only redeeming point about you is, you do not pretend to be sorry when you are not.”

===Chinese===
“惹恼我还算不得罪过。叫我为你的将来心惊的，是你那颗不肯悔改的心。我把你留在这儿，自己想想吧。你身上唯一可取的一点是：不难过的时候，你不假装难过。”

===Original===
The dear old lady shook her head sorrowfully as she departed.

===Chinese===
这位亲爱的老太太摇着头，神色凄然地出去了。

===Original===
The afternoon soon ran away, as I turned to my bookcase for entertainment and had that beautiful ring to admire.

===Chinese===
一个下午就这样很快溜了过去——我向书橱讨消遣，又有那枚美丽的戒指可以把玩。

===Original===
I heard them come in to tea, and I thought Harold had gone till I heard uncle Jay-Jay address him:

===Chinese===
我听见他们进来用晚茶，还当哈罗德已经走了，忽听得杰杰舅舅同他搭话：

===Original===
