“Joe Archer told me you ran into a clothesline on race-night, and ever since then mother has kept up a daddy of a fuss about ours. We’ve got props about a hundred feet long, and if you weren’t in the know you’d think we had a telegraph wire to old St. Peter up above.”

===Chinese===
“乔·阿彻告诉我，赛马那晚你一头撞上了晾衣绳。打那以后，我妈就为咱家那根绳子大惊小怪个没完。如今撑竿竖得约莫一百英尺高，你要是不知底细，还当咱们扯了根直通天上老圣彼得的电报线呢。”

===Original===
I wondered what Harold thought of the woman he had selected as his future wife being shut up for being a “naughty girl.” The situation amused me exceedingly.

===Chinese===
我不知道哈罗德作何感想：他挑作未来妻子的女人，竟因做了“顽皮姑娘”给关了禁闭。这处境逗得我不亦乐乎。

===Original===
About nine o’clock he knocked at my window and said:

===Chinese===
九点来钟，他敲了敲我的窗子，说：

===Original===
“Never mind, Syb. I tried to get you off, but it was no go. Old people often have troublesome straitlaced ideas. It will blow over by tomorrow.”

===Chinese===
“别往心里去，西比。我替你讨过情了，可是不成的。上了年纪的人常有些恼人的古板念头。到明天就烟消云散了。”

===Original===
I did not answer; so he passed on with firm regular footfall, and presently I heard his horse’s hoof-beats dying away in the darkness, and the closing and locking of doors around me as the household retired for the night.

===Chinese===
我没有答话；他便迈着沉稳匀整的脚步走过去了。不多时，我听见他的马蹄声在黑暗里渐渐消逝，又听见一家人各自就寝，四下里门扇一扇扇关合、落锁。

===Original===
During the following fortnight I saw Harold a good many times at cricket-matches, hare-drives, and so forth, but he did not take any particular notice of me. I flirted and frolicked with my other young men friends, but he did not care. I did not find him an ardent or a jealous lover. He was so irritatingly cool and matter-of-fact that I wished for the three months to pass so that I might be done with him, as I had come to the conclusion that he was barren of emotion or passion of any kind.

===Chinese===
此后两个星期，我在板球赛、猎野兔会等等场合碰见哈罗德许多回，他却不曾对我有什么特别的理会。我同别的青年男友调笑耍闹，他也毫不在意。我不曾见他是个热切的或爱吃醋的情人。他冷静务实得叫人冒火，我只盼那三个月快快过去，好同他一刀两断——因为我已经断定，此人身上无论哪种情感、哪种激情，一概荒芜不生。

## XXII Sweet Seventeen / 第22章 芳龄十七

===Original===
Monday arrived﻿—last day of November and seventeenth anniversary of my birth﻿—and I celebrated it in a manner which I capitally enjoyed.

===Chinese===
星期一到了——十一月最后的一天，我降生后的第十七个周年——我用一种自己顶顶痛快的法子把它庆祝了一番。

===Original===
It was the time of the annual muster at Cummabella﻿—a cattle-station seventeen miles eastward from Caddagat﻿—and all our men were there assisting. Word had been sent that a considerable number of beasts among those yarded bore the impress of the Bossier brand on their hides; so on Sunday afternoon uncle Jay-Jay had also proceeded thither to be in readiness for the final drafting early on Monday morning. This left us manless, as Frank Hawden, being incapacitated with a dislocated wrist, was spending a few weeks in Gool-Gool until he should be fit for work again.

===Chinese===
正赶上卡马贝拉（Cummabella）一年一度的牲口大清点（muster）——那是卡达加特（Caddagat）以东十七英里的一处牧牛场——我们全部的人手都在那边帮工。有话传来，说围场里的牲口中有一大批，皮上烙着博西耶（Bossier）家的印记；于是礼拜天下午，杰杰舅舅（uncle Jay-Jay）也动身赶了过去，好从礼拜一一早便伺候着最后的分栏拣选。这一来家里就没了男人——弗兰克·霍登（Frank Hawden）腕骨脱臼干不得活，正在古尔古尔（Gool-Gool）将养几个礼拜，等养好了再回来上工。

===Original===
Uncle had not been gone an hour when a drover appeared to report that twenty thousand sheep would pass through on the morrow. Grass was precious. It would not do to let the sheep spread and dawdle at their drovers’ pleasure. There was not a man on the place; grannie was in a great stew; so I volunteered my services. At first she would not hear of such a thing, but eventually consented. With many injunctions to conduct myself with proper stiffness, I started early on Monday morning. I was clad in a cool blouse, a holland riding-skirt, and a big straw hat; was seated on a big bay horse, was accompanied by a wonderful sheepdog, and carried a long heavy stock-whip. I sang and cracked my stock-whip as I cantered along, quite forgetting to be reserved and proper. Presently I came upon the sheep just setting out for their day’s tramp, with a black boy ahead of them, of whom I inquired which was the boss. He pointed towards a man at the rear wearing a donkey-supper hat. I made my way through the sheep in his direction, and asked if he were in charge of them. On being answered in the affirmative, I informed him that I was Mr. Bossier’s niece, and, as the men were otherwise engaged, I would see the sheep through.

===Chinese===
舅舅走了还不到一个钟头，便来了个赶牲口人（drover），报告说有两万只羊明天要打这儿过。牧草金贵，断不能由着羊群散开去，随赶羊人的性子磨蹭。宅子里一个男人也没有；外婆（grannie）急得团团转；我便自告奋勇。起初她说什么也不肯依，末了还是应了。她千叮咛万嘱咐，要我举止端端庄庄、拿足架子，我礼拜一一早便动身了。我穿一件凉快的罩衫、一条荷兰麻布骑马裙，戴一顶大草帽；骑一匹高大的骝色马，带着一条顶出色的牧羊犬，手里拎一条又长又沉的牧鞭（stock-whip）。我一路催马小跑，唱着歌，把牧鞭甩得噼啪响，什么端庄架子，早忘到九霄云外去了。不多时我便赶上了那群羊，它们正要动身做一天的跋涉，前头走着个土著男孩，我便问他谁是头儿。他朝队尾一个戴“驴晚饭”帽的男人一指。我从羊群里朝那个人挤过去，问他可是管事的。他应了一声是，我便告诉他，我是博西耶先生的外甥女，男人们都有差事绊住了脚，由我来把这群羊送过境。

===Original===
“That’s all right, miss. I will look out that you don’t have much trouble,” he replied, politely raising his hat, while a look of amusement played on his face.

===Chinese===
“没要紧的，小姐。我会留神，叫您不致碰上多少麻烦。”他答道，彬彬有礼地举了举帽，脸上浮起一丝觉得有趣的神情。

===Original===
He rode away, and shouted to his men to keep the flock strictly within bounds and make good travelling.

===Chinese===
他骑马走开，朝他的伙计们扬声喊话，叫他们把羊群严格圈在界内，赶出个痛快的脚程来。

===Original===
“Right you are, boss,” they answered; and returning to my side he told me his name was George Ledwood, and made some remarks about the great drought and so on, while we rode in the best places to keep out of the dust and in the shade. I asked questions such as whence came the sheep? whither were they bound? and how long had they been on the road? And having exhausted these orthodox remarks, we fell a-talking in dead earnest without the least restraint. I listened with interest to stories of weeks and weeks spent beneath the sun and stars while crossing widths of saltbush country, mulga and myall scrubs, of encounters with blacks in Queensland, and was favoured with a graphic description of a big strike among the shearers when the narrator had been boss-of-the-board out beyond Bourke. He spoke as though well educated, and a gentleman﻿—as drovers often are. Why, then, was he on the road? I put him down as a scapegrace, for he had all the winning pleasant manner of a ne’er-do-well.

===Chinese===
“遵命，头儿！”他们应道。他回到我身边，告诉我他名叫乔治·莱德伍德（George Ledwood），又扯了些大旱年景之类的闲话；我们拣着尘土最少、荫凉最多的好位置并辔而行。我问了些诸如此类的照例话：羊是从哪里来的？要往哪里去？在路上走了多久了？这几句照例的客套一说完，我们便正正经经攀谈起来，没有半点拘束。我听得津津有味：他们一连几礼拜头顶烈日、夜宿星空，穿越一片片滨藜（saltbush）荒原、穆尔加（mulga）和相思木（myall）灌丛；在昆士兰（Queensland）同土著黑人打交道；还有幸听他绘声绘色讲了一场剪毛工大罢工——那时叙述者正在伯克（Bourke）城外当剪毛台领班（boss-of-the-board）。他谈吐像个受过良好教育的人，像个绅士——赶牲口人里头往往颇有这样的。那么，他怎么还在路上奔波？我断定他是个出门胡混的浪荡子：他浑身上下那副讨人喜欢的和气劲儿，正是那些不成器的败家子共有的。

===Original===
At noon﻿—a nice, blazing, dusty noon﻿—we halted within a mile of Caddagat for lunch. I could have easily ridden home for mine, but preferred to have it with the drovers for fun. The men boiled the billy and made the tea, which we drank out of tin pots, with tinned fish and damper off tin plates as the completion of the menu, Mr. Ledwood and I at a little distance from the men. Tea boiled in a billy at a bush fire has a deliciously aromatic flavour, and I enjoyed my birthday lunch immensely. Leaving the cook to collect the things and put them in the spring-cart, we continued on our way, lazily lolling on our horses and chewing gum-leaves as we went.

===Chinese===
正午——一个妙极了、火辣辣、灰扑扑的正午——我们在离卡达加特不到一英里的地方停下打尖。我原可以轻轻松松赶回家去吃饭，却宁可图个新鲜，同赶牲口人们搭伙。伙计们烧上比尔壶（billy）沏了茶，我们用锡罐喝，就着锡盘里的罐头鱼和篝火面饼（damper），这席面便告圆满——莱德伍德先生同我坐得离伙计们略远些。在灌木篝火上的比尔壶里烧出来的茶，有一股芬芳馥郁的香味，我这一顿生日午餐吃得痛快极了。我们把收拾家什、装上弹簧轻马车的活计留给厨子，继续赶路，懒洋洋地歪在马背上，一路嚼着桉树叶。

===Original===
When the last of the sheep got off the Caddagat run it was nearing two o’clock.

===Chinese===
等最后一只羊走出卡达加特的放牧地，已快两点钟了。

===Original===
Mr. Ledwood and I shook hands at parting, each expressing a wish that we might meet again some day.

===Chinese===
莱德伍德先生同我握手道别，彼此都说，但愿日后有缘再会。

===Original===
I turned and rode homewards. I looked back and saw the drover gazing after me. I waved my hand; he raised his hat and smiled, displaying his teeth, a gleam of white in his sun-browned face. I kissed my hand to him; he bowed low; I whistled to my dog; he resumed his way behind the crawling sheep; I cantered home quickly and dismounted at the front gate at 2:30 p.m., a dusty, heated, tired girl.

===Chinese===
我拨转马头往家走。回头一望，见那赶牲口人正目送着我。我朝他挥手；他举帽微笑，露出一口牙齿，在他晒成褐色的脸上一道白光。我吻了吻自己的手，向他挥去；他深深一躬；我吹哨唤我的狗；他重新跟在蠕蠕而行的羊群后面上了路；我一路小跑赶回家，下午 2:30 在前门口下了马——一个满身尘土、热烘烘、乏透了的女孩子。

===Original===
Grannie came out to question me regarding the sex, age, condition, and species of the sheep, what was their destination, whether they were in search of grass or were for sale, had they spread or eaten much grass, and had the men been civil?

===Chinese===
外婆迎出来盘问：那群羊的公母、口齿、膘情、品类，它们要往哪里去，是寻草场还是待售的，散没散开、吃掉了多少草，还有那些人客气不客气？

===Original===
When I had satisfactorily informed her on all these points, she bade me have something to eat, to bathe and dress, and gave me a holiday for the remainder of the day.

===Chinese===
我把这些事一一回禀周全，她便吩咐我吃点东西，洗个澡换身衣裳，又赏了我半天假，这天剩下的时辰随我玩乐。

===Original===
My hair was grey with dust, so I washed all over, arrayed myself in a cool white dress, and throwing myself in a squatter’s chair in the veranda, spread my hair over the back of it to dry. Copies of Gordon, Kendall, and Lawson were on my lap, but I was too physically content and comfortable to indulge in even these, my sworn friends and companions. I surrendered myself to the mere joy of being alive. How the sunlight blazed and danced in the roadway﻿—the leaves of the gum-trees gleaming in it like a myriad gems! A cloud of white, which I knew to be cockatoos, circled over the distant hilltop. Nearer they wheeled until I could hear their discordant screech. The thermometer on the wall rested at 104 degrees despite the dense shade thrown on the broad old veranda by the foliage of creepers, shrubs, and trees. The gurgling rush of the creek, the scent of the flower-laden garden, and the stamp, stamp of a horse in the orchard as he attempted to rid himself of tormenting flies, filled my senses. The warmth was delightful. Summer is heavenly, I said﻿—life is a joy.

===Chinese===
我的头发叫尘土蒙得灰白，便周身洗了个遍，换上一袭凉快的白裙，往游廊上一张牧场主宽椅（squatter's chair）里一躺，把头发披散在椅背上晾干。戈登（Gordon）、肯德尔（Kendall）、劳森（Lawson）几卷诗集就摊在我膝头，可我浑身舒坦、通体熨帖，连这几位誓同生死的朋友伙伴也懒怠去亲近，只一味把自己交给单单活着的欢喜。阳光在大路上烧得多旺、舞得多欢——桉树的叶子在光里闪烁，像千万粒宝石！远处山顶上盘旋着一团白影，我知道那是凤头鹦鹉。它们兜着圈子越飞越近，近得我听得见那不谐和的尖叫。墙上温度表停在 104 度，尽管藤蔓、花木、乔木的浓荫遮着这宽阔的老游廊。溪水汩汩奔流的声响，满园花气熏人，果园里一匹马为甩开恼人的苍蝇不停刨着蹄子——咚，咚——这一切灌满了我的感官。这暖意真叫人受用。夏天是天堂，我说——人生是欢喜。

===Original===
Aunt Helen’s slender fingers looked artistic among some pretty fancywork upon which she was engaged. Bright butterflies flitted round the garden, and thousands of bees droned lazily among the flowers. I closed my eyes﻿—my being filled with the beauty of it all.

===Chinese===
海伦姨妈（aunt Helen）纤细的手指埋在一幅精巧的绣活里，看着真有画意。明媚的蝴蝶在园中翻飞，千万只蜜蜂在花间懒懒地嗡鸣。我阖上眼——满心满眼都被这一派美填满了。

===Original===
I could hear grannie’s pen fly over the paper as she made out a list of Christmas supplies on a table near me.

===Chinese===
我听得见外婆的笔在纸上刷刷地飞——她正在我近旁一张桌上开列圣诞节采买的单子。

===Original===
“Helen, I suppose a hundredweight of currants will be sufficient?”

===Chinese===
“海伦，我想一英担无核葡萄干该够用了罢？”

===Original===
“Yes; I should think so.”

===Chinese===
“够了；我想是够了。”

===Original===
“Seven dozen yards of unbleached calico be enough?”

===Chinese===
“七打码本色粗平布够不够？”

===Original===
“Yes; plenty.”

===Chinese===
“够；绰绰有余。”

===Original===
“Which tea-service did you order?”

===Chinese===
“你订的是哪一套茶具？”

===Original===
“Number two.”

===Chinese===
“第二号。”

===Original===
“Do you or Sybylla want anything extra?”

===Chinese===
“你或西比拉还要添些什么？”

===Original===
“Yes; parasols, gloves, and some books.”

===Chinese===
“要；阳伞、手套，还有几本书。”

===Original===
“Books! Can I get them at Hordern’s?”

===Chinese===
“书！在霍尔登商店（Hordern's）买得着么？”

===Original===
“Yes.”

===Chinese===
“买得着。”

===Original===
Grannie’s voice faded on my ears, my thoughts ran on uncle Jay-Jay. He had promised to be home in time for my birthday spread, and I was sure he had a present for me. What would it be?﻿—something nice. He would be nearly sure to bring someone home with him from Cummabella, and we would have games and fun to no end. I was just seventeen, only seventeen, and had a long, long life before me wherein to enjoy myself. Oh, it was good to be alive! What a delightful place the world was!﻿—so accommodating, I felt complete mistress of it. It was like an orange﻿—I merely had to squeeze it and it gave forth sweets plenteously. The stream sounded far away, the sunlight blazed and danced, grannie’s voice was a pleasant murmur in my ear, the cockatoos screamed over the house and passed away to the west. Summer is heavenly and life is a joy, I reiterated. Joy! Joy! There was joy in the quit! quit! of the green-and-crimson parrots, which swung for a moment in the rosebush over the gate, and then whizzed on into the summer day. There was joy in the gleam of the sun and in the hum of the bees, and it throbbed in my heart. Joy! Joy! A jackass laughed his joy as he perched on the telegraph wire out in the road. Joy! joy! Summer is a dream of delight and life is a joy, I said in my heart. I was repeating the one thing over and over﻿—but ah! it was a measure of happiness which allowed of much repetition. The cool murmur of the creek grew far away, I felt my poetry books slip off my knees and fall to the floor, but I was too content to bother about them﻿—too happy to need their consolation, which I had previously so often and so hungrily sought. Youth! Joy! Warmth!

===Chinese===
外婆的声音在我耳边淡下去，我的心念转到了杰杰舅舅身上。他答应过赶回来吃我的生日席，我拿准他给我备了礼物。会是什么呢？——总是样好东西。他八成还会从卡马贝拉捎个客人回来，那我们便有玩不完的游戏、闹不完的趣。我才十七岁，不过十七岁，前头还有长长的、长长的一生，尽够我受用。啊，活着真好！这世界是个多么可心的地方！——它这样随和听话，我觉得自己俨然是它全权的主妇。它像一只橙子——我只消一捏，它便涌出满口的甜来。溪声听着远了，阳光烧着、舞着，外婆的絮语在耳边好听得像低唱，凤头鹦鹉掠过屋顶，尖叫着往西去了。夏天是天堂，人生是欢喜，我一遍又一遍地说。欢喜！欢喜！红绿鹦鹉在门首玫瑰丛里荡了一荡，“奎特！奎特！”啼着，一忽儿便嗖地飞进夏日里去了——那啼声里有欢喜。太阳的光芒里有欢喜，蜜蜂的嗡鸣里有欢喜，它还在我心口突突地跳。欢喜！欢喜！一只笑翠鸟栖在大路上的电报线上，格格大笑，笑出它的欢喜。欢喜！欢喜！夏天是一场欢喜的梦，人生是一场欢喜，我在心里说。我把同一句话翻来覆去地念——可是啊！这样的幸福，正禁得起千遍的重复。溪水清凉的絮语越听越远，我觉得膝上的诗集滑下去、落到了地上，可我舒坦得懒得去管——我快活得不需它们的慰藉了，虽然从前我曾那样常常地、那样如饥似渴地向它们讨慰藉。青春！欢喜！温暖！

===Original===
The clack of the garden gate, as it swung to, awoke me from a pleasant sleep. Grannie had left the veranda, and on the table where she had been writing aunt Helen was filling many vases with maidenhair fern and La France roses. A pleasant clatter from the dining-room announced that my birthday tea was in active preparation. The position of the yellow sunbeams at the far end of the wide veranda told that the dense shadows were lengthening, and that the last of the afternoon was wheeling westward. Taking this in, in an instant I straightened the piece of mosquito-netting, which, to protect me from the flies, someone﻿—auntie probably﻿—had spread across my face, and feigned to be yet asleep. By the footsteps which sounded on the stoned garden walk, I knew that Harold Beecham was one of the individuals approaching.

===Chinese===
花园的门哐啷一声阖上，把我从一场好睡里惊醒。外婆已离开游廊，在她方才写字的那张桌上，海伦姨妈正往许多花瓶里插铁线蕨和法兰西玫瑰（La France）。饭厅里传来一片好听的锅盘磕碰声，宣告我的生日茶点正在大张旗鼓地筹备。从宽阔游廊尽头那几道金黄日光的位置看得出，浓荫正在拉长，下半晌最后的一段光阴正朝着西边转去。我把这一切看在眼里，一转眼便把那块蚊帐纱抚正——那是不知哪一位、多半是姨妈，为替我挡苍蝇，罩在我脸上的——装出犹自酣睡的模样。碎石园径上响起脚步声，听那脚步我便知道，来人当中有一个是哈罗德·比彻姆（Harold Beecham）。

===Original===
“How do you do, Mrs. Bell? Allow me to introduce my friend, Archie Goodchum. Mrs. Bell, Mr. Goodchum. Hasn’t it been a roaster today? Considerably over 100 degrees in the shade. Terribly hot!”

===Chinese===
“您好吗，贝尔太太（Mrs. Bell）？容我介绍我的朋友，阿奇·古德查姆（Archie Goodchum）。贝尔太太，这位是古德查姆先生。今天岂不是个大火炉？背阴处也足足超了 100 度。热得吓人！”

===Original===
Aunt Helen acknowledged the introduction, and seated her guests, saying:

===Chinese===
海伦姨妈应过了引见，请客人落座，说道：

===Original===
“Harry, have you got an artistic eye? If so, you can assist me with these flowers. So might Mr. Goodchum, if he feels disposed.”

===Chinese===
“哈里，你有没有一双审美的慧眼？有的话，可以帮我插这些花。古德查姆先生若是有兴，也不妨搭一把手。”

===Original===
Harold accepted the proposal, and remarked:

===Chinese===
哈罗德应承了，说道：

===Original===
“What is the matter with your niece? It is the first time I ever saw her quiet.”

===Chinese===
“你这位外甥女是怎么啦？我头一回见她这样安安静静的。”

===Original===
“Yes; she is a noisy little article﻿—a perfect whirlwind in the house﻿—but she is a little tired this afternoon; she has been seeing those sheep through today.”

===Chinese===
“正是；她是个聒噪的小东西——在这宅子里活脱一阵旋风——不过今天下午她有点乏了；她今天押送那群羊来着。”

===Original===
“Don’t you think it would be a good lark if I get something and tickle her?” said Goodchum.

===Chinese===
“我去寻个什么物件来胳肢她，岂不是桩妙事？”古德查姆说。

===Original===
“Yes, do,” said Harold; “but look out for squalls. She is a great little fizzer.”

===Chinese===
“好哇，去罢，”哈罗德说；“不过要提防风暴。她可是瓶了不得的小汽水，一晃就要冒泡。”

===Original===
“Then she might be insulted.”

===Chinese===
“那她可要觉得受了冒犯。”

===Original===
“Not she,” interposed auntie. “No one will enjoy the fun more than herself.”

===Chinese===
“她才不会呢，”姨妈插嘴道。“这场玩笑，再没有谁比她自己更受用的了。”

===Original===
I had my eyes half open beneath the net, so saw him cautiously approach with a rose-stem between his fingers. Being extremely sensitive to tickling, so soon as touched under the ear I took a flying leap from the chair somewhat disconcerting my tormentor.

===Chinese===
我在纱底下把眼睛睁开一半，瞧见他指间夹着一根玫瑰枝，蹑手蹑脚地挨近来。我天生最经不得痒，耳朵底下刚一触到，便从椅子里一跃而起，把我的折磨者闹了个措手不及。

===Original===
He was a pleasant-looking young fellow somewhere about twenty, whose face was quite familiar to me.

===Chinese===
他是个模样讨喜的年轻人，二十上下年纪，那张脸我熟得很。

===Original===
He smiled so good-humouredly at me that I widely did the same in return, and he came forward with extended hand, exclaiming, “At last!”

===Chinese===
他朝我好脾气地咧嘴一笑，我也不由得大大回了他一个笑，他便迎上前来，伸出手，喊道：“可算啦！”

===Original===
The others looked on in surprise, Harold remarking suspiciously, “You said you were unacquainted with Miss Melvyn, but an introduction does not seem necessary.”

===Chinese===
旁人都讶然望着，哈罗德犯着疑心搭话说：“你说你同梅尔文小姐素不相识，可眼下瞧着，引见似乎大可不必了。”

===Original===
“Oh, yes it is,” chirped Mr. Goodchum. “I haven’t the slightest idea of the young lady’s name.”

===Chinese===
“哦，还是需要的。”古德查姆先生啾啾地说。“这位小姐姓甚名谁，我可丝毫不知。”

===Original===
“Don’t know each other!” ejaculated Harold; and grannie, who had appeared upon the scene, inquired stiffly what we meant by such capers if unacquainted.

===Chinese===
“互不相识！”哈罗德脱口喊道。外婆这时也到了跟前，板起脸盘问：既然素不相识，我们这是演的哪一出。

===Original===
Mr. Goodchum hastened to explain.

===Chinese===
古德查姆先生连忙解释。

===Original===
“I have seen the young lady on several occasions in the bank where I am employed, and I had the good fortune to be of a little service to her one day when I was out biking. Her harness, or at least the harness on the horse she was driving, broke, and I came to the rescue with my pocketknife and some string, thereby proving, if not ornamental, I was useful. After that I tried hard to find out who she was, but my inquiries always came to nothing. I little dreamt who Miss Melvyn was when Harry, telling me she was a Goulburn girl, asked if I knew her.”

===Chinese===
“这位小姐，我在我供职的那家银行里见过好几回。有一天我骑车出门，又凑巧替她效过一点小劳，实在三生有幸。她那车的挽具——至少是她赶的那匹马的挽具——断了，我凭着一把随身小折刀和几段细绳赶去救急，就此证明我纵然当不得摆设，到底还中点用。打那以后，我千方百计打听她是谁，却总是打听不出。哈里说她是个古尔本（Goulburn）姑娘、问我认不认得时，我哪里想得到这位梅尔文小姐竟是她。”

===Original===
“Quite romantic,” said aunt Helen, smiling; and a great thankfulness overcame me that Mr. Goodchum had been unable to discover my identity until now. It was right enough to be unearthed as Miss Melvyn, granddaughter of Mrs. Bossier of Caddagat, and great friend and intimate of the swell Beechams of Five-Bob Downs station. At Goulburn I was only the daughter of old Dick Melvyn, broken-down farmer-cockatoo, well known by reason of his sprees about the commonest pubs in town.

===Chinese===
“真是罗曼蒂克。”海伦姨妈含笑说；古德查姆先生直到今天才揭出我的底细，我心头涌起一阵大大的庆幸。被揭穿是梅尔文小姐——卡达加特博西耶太太的外孙女、五博丘（Five-Bob）显赫比彻姆家的至交密友——原也无伤大雅。可在古尔本，我不过是老迪克·梅尔文的女儿——一个穷途末路的小农（cocky），因为常在城里顶下等的酒馆里灌得烂醉而远近闻名。

===Original===
Mr. Goodchum told us it was his first experience of the country, and therefore he was enjoying himself immensely. He also mentioned that he was anxious to see some of the gullies around Caddagat, which, he had heard, were renowned for the beauty of their ferns. Aunt Helen, accordingly, proposed a walk in the direction of one of them, and hurried off to attend to a little matter before starting. While waiting for her, Harold happened to say it was my birthday, and Mr. Goodchum tendered me the orthodox wishes, remarking, “It is surely pardonable at your time of life to ask what age you have attained today?”

===Chinese===
古德查姆先生告诉我们，这是他头一遭下乡，因此玩得快活极了。他又提到，他巴望去看看卡达加特周围的几条沟谷，听人说那一带的蕨类以秀美出名。海伦姨妈于是提议往其中一条走去，动身之前先匆匆进去料理一桩小事。等她的当口，哈罗德偶然说起今天是我的生日，古德查姆先生便向我致了一番照例的祝贺，说道：“照您这样的年纪，问一声您今天芳龄几何，总还是可以恕罪的罢？”

===Original===
“Seventeen.”

===Chinese===
“十七。”

===Original===
“Oh! oh! ‘sweet seventeen, and never been kissed’; but I suppose you cannot truthfully say that, Miss Melvyn?”

===Chinese===
“哦！哦！‘芳龄十七，尚无人吻过’；不过我想，这句话您总不能照实说了罢，梅尔文小姐？”

===Original===
“Oh yes, I can.”

===Chinese===
“哦，我能，照实说得的。”

===Original===
“Well, you won’t be able to say it much longer,” he said, making a suggestive move in my direction. I ran, and he followed, grannie reappearing from the dining-room just in time to see me bang the garden gate with great force on my pursuer.

===Chinese===
“那么，这句话您也说不了一会子了。”他说着，朝我这边意味深长地挪近一步。我撒腿就跑，他紧追不舍，外婆恰在这时从饭厅里再度出来，正赶上看见我砰的一声，把花园的大门狠狠地关在我那追赶者的面前。

===Original===
“What on earth is the girl doing now?” I heard her inquire.

===Chinese===
“这丫头这会儿又在弄什么名堂？”我听见她问。

===Original===
However, Mr. Goodchum did not execute his threat; instead we walked along decorously in the direction of the nearest ferns, while Harold and aunt Helen followed, the latter carrying a sunbonnet for me.

===Chinese===
不过古德查姆先生终究没把他的恐吓付诸实行；我们规规矩矩地朝最近的蕨丛走去，哈罗德和海伦姨妈跟在后头，姨妈手里替我拿着一顶遮阳帽。

===Original===
After we had climbed some distance up a gully aunt Helen called out that she and Harold would rest while I did the honours of the fern grots to my companion.

===Chinese===
爬上沟谷一程之后，海伦姨妈扬声说，她同哈罗德要在原地歇脚，由我领着我的同伴去游那几处蕨穴，尽一尽地主之谊。

===Original===
We went on and on, soon getting out of sight of the others.

===Chinese===
我们愈走愈远，不多时便望不见其余的人了。

===Original===
“What do you say to my carving our names on a gum-tree, the bark is so nice and soft?” said the bank clerk; and I seconded the proposal.

===Chinese===
“这桉树的皮又细又软，我把咱俩的名字刻上去，你说好不好？”这位银行职员说；我附议了这项提议。

===Original===
“I will make it allegorical,” he remarked, setting to work.

===Chinese===
“我要刻出一番寓意来。”他说着，动起手来。

===Original===
He was very deft with his penknife, and in a few minutes had carved S. P. M. and A. S. G., encircling the initials by a ring and two hearts interlaced.

===Chinese===
他使起铅笔刀来十分灵巧，不消几分钟便刻好了 S. P. M. 和 A. S. G.，又拿一个圆环把这几组起首字母圈住，环上交扣着两颗心。

===Original===
“That’ll do nicely,” he remarked, and turning round, “Why, you’ll get a sunstroke; do take my hat.”

===Chinese===
“这就很好了。”他说着，转过身来，“哎呀，你要中暑的；千万戴上我的帽子。”

===Original===
I demurred, he pressed the matter, and I agreed on condition he allowed me to tie his handkerchief over his head. I was wearing his hat and tying the ends of a big silk handkerchief beneath his chin when the cracking of a twig caused me to look up and see Harold Beecham with an expression on his face that startled me.

===Chinese===
我推辞，他再三敦劝，我便应承了，条件是准我把他的手帕扎在他头上。我正戴着他那顶帽子、把一条大绸手帕的两只角在他下巴颏底下打结，忽听得一根小树枝喀的一声，叫我抬起头来——哈罗德·比彻姆站在那里，脸上那副神情把我吓了一跳。

===Original===
“Your aunt sent me on with your hood,” he said jerkily.

===Chinese===
“你姨妈叫我给你把兜帽送来。”他没头没脑地蹦出一句。

===Original===
“You can wear it﻿—I’ve been promoted,” I said flippantly, raising my headgear to him and bowing. He did not laugh as he usually did at my tricks, but frowned darkly instead.

===Chinese===
“您自管戴着罢——我如今高升了。”我轻薄俏皮地说，朝他举了举我头上的帽子，又对他一躬。他并不像平日见了我的把戏那样发笑，反倒阴沉沉地皱起眉头。

===Original===
“We’ve been carving our names﻿—at least, I have,” remarked Goodchum.

===Chinese===
“我们方才在刻名字——至少，是我刻了。”古德查姆搭话说。

===Original===
Harold tossed my sunbonnet on the ground, and said shortly, “Come on, Goodchum, we must be going.”

===Chinese===
哈罗德把我的遮阳帽往地上一摔，简短地说：“走罢，古德查姆，我们该回去了。”

===Original===
“Oh, don’t go, Mr. Beecham. I thought you came on purpose for my birthday tea. Auntie has made me a tremendous cake. You must stay. We never dreamt of you doing anything else.”

===Chinese===
“哦，别走呀，比彻姆先生。我还当你是专程来吃我的生日茶点的呢。姨妈给我做了一个好大的蛋糕。你务必要留下。我们做梦也没想到你会不留下。”

===Original===
“I’ve changed my mind,” he replied, striding on at such a pace that we had difficulty in keeping near him. As we resumed our own head-wear, Goodchum whispered, “A bulldog ant must have stung the boss. Let’s ask him.”

===Chinese===
“我改了主意。”他答道，大步流星地往前赶，我们好容易才跟得上他。我们各自把帽戴回头上时，古德查姆悄声道：“准是斗犬蚁（bulldog ant）蜇着老板了。咱们问问他去。”

===Original===
On reaching the house we found other company had arrived in the persons of young Mr. Goodjay from Cummabella, his sister, her governess, and a couple of jackeroos. They were seated on the veranda, and uncle Jay-Jay, attired in his shirtsleeves, was appearing through the dining-room door with half a dozen bottles of homemade ginger ale in his arms. Dumping them down on the floor, he produced a couple of tots from his shirt-pockets, saying, “Who votes for a draw of beer? Everyone must feel inclined for a swig. Harry, you want some; you don’t look as though the heat was good for your temper. Hullo, Archie! Got up this far. Take a draw out of one of these bottles. If there had been a dozen pubs on the road, I’d have drunk every one of em dry today. I never felt such a daddy of a thirst on me before.”

===Chinese===
回到宅子，我们发觉又来了别的客人：卡马贝拉来的小古德杰伊先生（Goodjay）、他的妹妹、她的女家庭教师，还有两个牧场学徒（jackeroo）。他们都坐在游廊上，杰杰舅舅只穿着衬衫，正抱着半打自家酿的姜汁汽水，从饭厅门里现身。他把瓶子往地上一墩，又从衬衫口袋里摸出两只小酒盅，说道：“谁投票赞成来一口啤酒？这样的天气，谁不想来上一大口？哈里，你是非来一点不可的；瞧你这副模样，这暑气于你的脾气没什么好处。喂，阿奇！居然跑到这么远来了。从这几瓶里抿上一口罢。今儿这一路，哪怕开上一打酒馆，我也能把它们喝个一滴不剩。这样大得冒尖的渴，我从前可从来没觉过。”

===Original===
“Good gracious, Julius!” exclaimed grannie, as he offered the governess a pot full of beer, “Miss Craddock can’t drink out of that pint.”

===Chinese===
“老天爷，朱利叶斯（Julius）！”外婆失声喊道——他正把满满一罐“啤酒”递给那位家庭教师——“克拉多克小姐（Miss Craddock）可没法就着那样一个一品脱的大罐子喝。”

===Original===
“Those who don’t approve of my pints, let ’em bring their own,” said that mischievous uncle Jay-Jay, who was a great hand at acting the clown when he felt that way inclined.

===Chinese===
“看不惯我这品脱罐的，就请自备家伙。”那位爱恶作剧的杰杰舅舅说——每逢兴致上来，他最拿手的一手就是扮小丑逗乐。

===Original===
I was dispatched for glasses, and after emptying the bottles uncle proposed a game of tennis first, while the light lasted, and tea afterwards. This proposition being carried with acclamation, we proceeded to the tennis court. Harold came too﻿—he had apparently altered his intention of going home immediately.

===Chinese===
我被差去取玻璃杯。几瓶汽水倾空之后，舅舅提议趁天光未暗先打一场网球，随后再用茶点。这项提议在欢呼声中通过，我们便向网球场进发。哈罗德也去了——他那马上回家的主意，显然改掉了。

===Original===
There were strawberries to be had in the orchard, also some late cherries, so uncle ordered me to go and get some. I procured a basket, and willingly agreed to obey him. Mr. Goodchum offered to accompany me, but Harold stepped forward saying he would go, in such a resolute tragic manner that Goodchum winked audaciously, saying waggishly, “Behold, the hero descends into the burning mine!”

===Chinese===
果园里有草莓可摘，还有些晚熟的樱桃，舅舅便吩咐我去采一些来。我寻了一只篮子，欣然从命。古德查姆先生提出陪我同去，哈罗德却抢上一步，说他去——那副斩钉截铁、悲壮凛然的架势，惹得古德查姆放肆地挤了挤眼，谐谑道：“看哪，英雄下燃烧的矿井去喽！”

## XXIII “Ah, for One Hour of Burning Love, ’Tis Worth an Age of Cold Respect!” / 第23章 “啊，哪怕一小时的烈火之爱，也胜过一世纪的冷清敬意！”

===Original===
We walked in perfect silence, Harold not offering to carry my little basket. I did not dare lift my eyes, as something told me the face of the big man would not be pleasant to look upon just then. I twirled the ring he had given me round and round my finger. I occasionally put it on, wearing the stones on the palm-side of my finger, so that it would not be taken for other than one of two or three aunt Helen had lent me, saying I was at liberty to use them while at Caddagat, if it gave me any pleasure.

===Chinese===
我们一路走着，一片死寂，哈罗德也不来替我拎那只小篮子。我不敢抬眼，因为有种感觉告诉我，此刻这大个子男人那张脸是不会好看的。我把他给我的那枚戒指在手指上转了又转。我不时把它戴上，把宝石转到贴掌心的一面，好教人只当它是海伦姨妈借给我的两三枚戒指之一——她说过，我在卡达加特住着，这些戒指尽可以随意取用，只要能给我添点儿乐趣。

===Original===
The Caddagat orchard contained six acres, and being a narrow enclosure, and the cherries growing at the extreme end from the house, it took us some time to reach them. I led the way to our destination﻿—a secluded nook where grapevines clambered up fig-trees, and where the top of gooseberry bushes met the lower limbs of cherry-trees. Blue and yellow lupins stood knee-high, and strawberries grew wild among them. We had not uttered a sound, and I had not glanced at my companion. I stopped; he wheeled abruptly and grasped my wrist in a manner which sent the basket whirling from my hand. I looked up at his face, which was blazing with passion, and dark with a darker tinge than Nature and the sun had given it, from the shapely swelling neck, in its soft well-turned-down collar, to where the stiff black hair, wet with perspiration, hung on the wide forehead.

===Chinese===
卡达加特的果园有六英亩大，园地狭长，樱桃又长在离宅子最远的一头，我们走了好一阵才到。我在头前领路，直奔我们的去处——一个幽僻的角落，那里葡萄藤攀缘上无花果树，醋栗丛的顶梢挨着樱桃树低垂的枝桠。蓝的黄的羽扇豆齐膝高，草莓野野地长在其间。我们不曾出一点声，我也不曾瞥一眼我的同伴。我停住脚；他猛地转过身来，一把攥住我的手腕，那股劲道把篮子从我手里甩得飞了出去。我仰头看他那张脸——一脸燃烧的激情，罩着一层比天性和太阳赋给他的肤色更深的暗色；从生得端正丰隆的脖颈，连同那软软翻下的领子，直到汗湿的硬黑发覆在宽阔的额前。

===Original===
“Unhand me, sir!” I said shortly, attempting to wrench myself free, but I might as well have tried to pull away from a lion.

===Chinese===
“放开我，先生！”我简短地说道，一面拼命想挣脱，可这好比想从一头狮子爪下挣开。

===Original===
“Unhand me!” I repeated.

===Chinese===
“放开我！”我又说了一遍。

===Original===
For answer he took a firmer hold, in one hand seizing my arm above the elbow, and gripping my shoulder with the other so tightly that, through my flimsy covering, his strong fingers bruised me so severely that in a calmer moment I would have squirmed and cried out with pain.

===Chinese===
作为回答，他攥得更紧了，一只手扣住我肘弯以上的手臂，另一只手死死钳住我的肩头，隔着薄薄的衣衫，他那有力的手指掐得那样狠，若在心平气和的当儿，我早就会疼得扭着身子叫出来了。

===Original===
“How dare you touch me!” He drew me so closely to him that, through his thin shirt﻿—the only garment on the upper part of his figure﻿—I could feel the heat of his body, and his big heart beating wildly.

===Chinese===
“你怎么敢碰我！”他把我拽得紧紧贴在身上，隔着那件薄衬衫——他上身唯一的衣物——我感觉得到他躯体的热力，感觉得到他那颗宽大的心在狂野地跳动。

===Original===
At last! at last! I had waked this calm silent giant into life. After many an ineffectual struggle I had got at a little real love or passion, or call it by any name﻿—something wild and warm and splendidly alive that one could feel, the most thrilling, electric, and exquisite sensation known.

===Chinese===
终于！终于！我把这个沉静无言的巨人唤醒活了。多少回的用劲都落了空，这一回，我终于搅出了一点真正的爱恋或激情——随你怎么称呼它——某种狂野、温热、活灵灵地焕发着、教人感觉得到的东西，一种人间已知最惊心动魄、最如电彻体、最妙不可言的滋味。

===Original===
I thoroughly enjoyed the situation, but did not let this appear. A minute or two passed and he did not speak.

===Chinese===
这处境我受用极了，却一点不露在脸上。一两分钟过去，他一言不发。

===Original===
“Mr. Beecham, I’ll trouble you to explain yourself. How dare you lay your hands upon me?”

===Chinese===
“比彻姆先生，劳你的驾，请你解释解释自己的行止。你怎么敢对我动手动脚？”

===Original===
“Explain!” he breathed rather than spoke, in a tone of concentrated fury. “I’ll make *you* explain, and I’ll do what I like with you. I’ll touch you as much as I think fit. I’ll throw you over the fence if *you* don’t explain to *my* satisfaction.”

===Chinese===
“解释！”他与其说是说话，不如说是吁出几个字，声调里凝着一腔狂怒。“我要叫*你*解释；我要拿你随心处置。我爱碰你就碰你，爱碰多少碰多少。要是*你*解释得不合*我*的意，我就把你扔过栅栏去。”

===Original===
“What is there that I can explain?”

===Chinese===
“我有什么可解释的？”

===Original===
“Explain your conduct with other men. How dare you receive their attentions and be so friendly with them!”

===Chinese===
“解释你同旁的男人的行止。你怎么敢领受他们的殷勤，同他们那样亲热！”

===Original===
“How dare you speak to me like that! I reserve the right of behaving as I please without your permission.”

===Chinese===
“你怎么敢这样同我说话！我保留一份权利：行事只凭我乐意，无须你准许。”

===Original===
“I won’t have a girl with my engagement ring on her finger going on as you do. I think I have a right to complain, for I could get any amount of splendid women in every way to wear it for me, and behave themselves properly too,” he said fiercely.

===Chinese===
“我不能容忍一个指头上戴着我订婚戒指的姑娘像你这般行事。我想我有权抱怨——样样都出众的女人，我能弄到多少有多少，个个肯替我戴上它，还个个行止规矩。”他恶狠狠地说。

===Original===
I tossed my head defiantly, saying, “Loose your hold of me, and I’ll quickly explain matters to my own satisfaction and yours, Harold Beecham.”

===Chinese===
我倔强地一甩头，说道：“撒开手，我就立刻把事情解释得叫我自己满意、也叫你满意，哈罗德·比彻姆。”

===Original===
He let me go, and I stepped a pace or two away from him, drew the costly ring from my finger, and, with indifference and contempt, tossed it to his feet, where the juice of crushed strawberries was staining the ground, and facing him, said mockingly:

===Chinese===
他撒开了手。我退开一两步，从指头上捋下那枚贵重的戒指，带着冷淡与轻蔑，丢到他脚边——被踩烂的草莓的汁水正染着那里的地面——然后我面朝着他，讥诮地说道：

===Original===
“Now, speak to the girl who wears your engagement ring, for I’ll degrade myself by wearing it no more. If you think I think you as great a catch as you think yourself, just because you have a little money, you are a trifle mistaken, Mr. Beecham, that is all. Ha ha ha! So you thought you had a right to lecture me as your future slave! Just fancy! I never had the slightest intention of marrying you. You were so disgustingly conceited that I have been attempting to rub a little of it out of you. Marry you! Ha ha! Because the social laws are so arranged that a woman’s only sphere is marriage, and because they endeavour to secure a man who can give them a little more ease, you must not run away with the idea that it is yourself they are angling for, when you are only the bothersome appendage with which they would have to put up, for the sake of your property. And you must not think that because some women will marry for a home they all will. I trust I have explained to your satisfaction, Mr. Beecham. Ha ha ha!”

===Chinese===
“现在，你只管去同那位戴着你的订婚戒指的姑娘说话吧——我是再不肯戴它了，戴它就辱没了我自己。你若以为，就因为你有几个钱，我在心里便把你当成同你自己看自己一样了不得的良配，那你可就有点儿弄错了，比彻姆先生，仅此而已。哈哈哈！原来你以为你有权把我当作未来的奴隶来教训！亏你想得出！我从来没有一丝一毫要嫁给你的意思。是你自负得叫人恶心，我才一直想法子替你磨掉一点儿。嫁给你！哈哈！社会的法度既是这样安排的——女人唯一的天地就是婚姻；她们既千方百计要抓牢一个能让她们多松快一点儿的男人——你就万不可得意忘形，当人家钓的是你这个人：你不过是人家看在你产业的份上，不得不将就凑合着的那件讨厌的附属物。你也休想因为有些女人肯为着一个家嫁人，便当个个女人都肯。我想我已经解释得叫你满意了吧，比彻姆先生。哈哈哈！”

===Original===
The jealous rage had died out of his face and was succeeded by trembling and a pallor so ghastly, that I began to have a little faith in descriptions of love which I had hitherto ridiculed.

===Chinese===
他脸上那股妒恨的狂怒熄了下去，继之而来的是颤抖，和一种惨厉得吓人的苍白，叫我开始对那些一向被我嘲笑的关于爱情的描写信了几分。

===Original===
“Are you in earnest?” he asked in a deadly calm voice.

===Chinese===
“你这话是当真的？”他问，声音平静得怕人。

===Original===
“Most emphatically I am.”

===Chinese===
“千真万确，是当真的。”

===Original===
“Then all I can say is that I haven’t much respect for you, Miss Melvyn. I always considered that there were three classes of women﻿—one, that would marry a blackfellow if he had money; another, that were shameless flirts, and who amuse themselves by flirting and disgracing the name of woman; and a third class that were pure and true, on whom a man could stake his life and whom he could worship. I thought you belonged to this class, but I have been mistaken. I know you always try to appear heartless and worthless, but I fancied it was only your youth and mischief, and imagined you were good underneath; but I have been mistaken,” he repeated with quiet contempt.

===Chinese===
“那我只能说，我对你没有多少敬意了，梅尔文小姐。我一向认为女人有三类——一类，只要有钱，连土著黑人也肯嫁；一类，是无耻的卖弄风情者，拿调情取乐，糟蹋女人这个名字；还有第三类，纯净而真挚，男人能把性命押在她们身上，能把她们当神明敬奉。我以为你属于这一类，是我看错了。我知道你总设法显得没心没肝、一无可取，可我只当那是你年幼淘气，想着你底子是好的；是我看错了。”他又说了一遍，声气里带着安静的轻蔑。

===Original===
His face had regained its natural colour, and the well-cut pleasant mouth, clearly seen beneath the soft drooping moustache, had hardened into a sullen line which told me he would never be first to seek reconciliation﻿—not even to save his life.

===Chinese===
他脸上恢复了本色，那张轮廓姣好、招人喜欢的嘴——在软软下垂的髭须底下看得分明——绷成了一条愠怒的直线，告诉我他绝不会抢先去求和解——哪怕为救自己的命也不会。

===Original===
“Bah!” I exclaimed sarcastically. “It appears that we all labour under delusions. Go and get a beautiful woman to wear your ring and your name. One that will be able to say yes and no at the right time; one who will know how to dress properly; one who wouldn’t for the world do anything that other women did not also; one who will know where to buy the best groceries and who will readily sell herself to you for your wealth. That’s the sort of woman that suits men, and there are plenty of them; procure one, and don’t bother with me. I am too small and silly, and have nothing to recommend me. I fear it speaks little for your sense or taste that you ever thought of me. Ta-ta, Mr. Beecham,” I said over my shoulder with a mocking smile, and walked away.

===Chinese===
“呸！”我挖苦地喊道。“看样子，我们人人都在错觉里讨生活。去找个漂亮女人来戴你的戒指、姓你的姓吧。要找一个在该说‘是’的时候说是、该说‘不’的时候说不的；一个懂得怎样穿得合乎体统的；一个别的女人不做的事她死也不做的；一个知道上哪儿买上等的杂货、情愿为着你的家财把自己卖给你的。那才是合男人心意的女人，这样的人遍地都是；去弄一个来，别再来缠我。我又小又傻，毫无可取之处。你竟会想到过我，只怕这于你的见识或品味，都不是什么好话。回头见，比彻姆先生。”我隔着肩头丢下这句，带着一脸讥诮的笑，走开了。

===Original===
When about halfway down the orchard reflection pulled me up shortly under an apple-tree.

===Chinese===
走到果园半途，一番思量把我拦在一棵苹果树下。

===Original===
I had said what I had said because, feeling bitter for the want of love, and because full of pain myself, I rejoiced with a sort of revenge to see the same feeling flash across another’s face. But now I was cool, and, forgetting myself, thought of Harold.

===Chinese===
我方才那样说，是因为苦于爱情的匮乏，因为自己满腔是痛，看见同样的痛楚掠过另一张脸，便怀着一种报复似的快意。可如今我冷静了，忘却了自己，惦念起哈罗德来。

===Original===
I had led him on because his perpetually calm demeanour had excited in me a desire to test if it were possible to disturb him. I had thought him incapable of emotion, but he had proved himself a man of strong and deep emotion; might he not also be capable of feeling﻿—of love? He had not been mean or nasty in his rage, and his anger had been righteous. By accepting his proposal of marriage, I had given him the right of expressing his objection to any of my actions of which he disapproved. I on my part had the liberty of trying to please him or of dissolving our engagement. Perhaps in some cases there was actually something more than wounded vanity when a man’s alleged love was rejected or spurned. Harold had seemed to suffer, to really experience keen disappointment. I was clearly in the wrong, and had been unwomanly beyond a doubt, as, granting that Harold Beecham was conceited, what right had I to constitute myself his judge or to take into my own hands the responsibility of correcting him? I felt ashamed of my conduct; I was sorry to have hurt anyone’s feelings. Moreover, I cannot bear to be at ill-will with my fellows, and am ever the first to give in after having quarrelled. It is easier than sulking, and it always makes the other party so self-complacent that it is amusing as well as convenient, and﻿—and﻿—and﻿—I found I was very, very fond of Harold Beecham.

===Chinese===
我先前之所以挑逗他，是因为他那副永远平静的做派激起了我一试的欲望，要看他究竟扰得动扰不动。我原以为他无从动情，可他已经证明自己是个情感强烈而深沉的男人；那么他会不会也有深情——有爱？他盛怒之下并不刻毒下作，他的恼怒是理直气壮的。我既接受了他的求婚，就已经给了他权利，对他不以为然的我的任何行止表示异议。而我这一面，也有自由去讨他欢心，或者解除我们的婚约。也许在某些情形下，一个男人的所谓爱情遭到拒绝、遭到践踏时，受挫的当真不止是虚荣心。哈罗德像是受了苦，像是真尝到了刻骨的失望。分明是我错了，而且毫无疑问很没有女人样——因为，就算哈罗德·比彻姆自负，我又有什么权柄自立为他的法官，把规正他的担子揽到自己手里？我为自己的行止羞愧；伤了人的心，我难过。再说，我受不了同同伴结怨，吵过架后永远是我第一个服软。这比怄气省力，又总把对方弄得那样顾影自得，既便宜又有趣，而且——而且——而且——我发觉我非常、非常喜欢哈罗德·比彻姆。

===Original===
I crept noiselessly up the orchard. He had his back to me, and had moved to where a post of the fence was peeping out among the greenery. He had his elbow placed thereon, and his forehead resting on his hand. His attitude expressed dejection. Maybe he was suffering the torture of a broken ideal.

===Chinese===
我蹑手蹑脚地往果园上头走。他背对着我，挪到了绿荫里探出的一根栅栏桩子跟前，肘弯支在桩上，额头抵在手上。那副姿态说尽了颓丧。也许他正在受刑——受一个破灭的理想的酷刑。

===Original===
His right hand hung limply by his side. I do not think he heard me approach.

===Chinese===
他的右手软软地垂在身侧。我想他没有听见我走近。

===Original===
My heart beat quickly, and a fear that he would snub me caused me to pause. Then I nerved myself with the thought that it would be only fair if he did. I had been rude to him, and he had a right to play tit-for-tat if he felt so disposed. I expected my action to be spurned or ignored, so very timidly slipped my fingers into his palm. I need not have been nervous, for the strong brown hand, which had never been known to strike a cowardly blow, completely enfolded mine in a gentle caressing clasp.

===Chinese===
我的心跳得飞快，生怕他抢白我，不由得迟疑了一下。转念一想：他若真抢白我，那也是再公道不过。我先前对他无礼，他若高兴，尽有权利针锋相对地还敬。我料定这一举动会被甩开、或干脆不被理睬，于是怯生生地把手指塞进他的掌心。其实我用不着紧张——那只结实的棕色大手，从不曾听说它打出过卑怯的一击——把我这只手整个儿拢住，温存地、爱抚地握紧了。

===Original===
“Mr. Beecham, Harold, I am so sorry I was so unwomanly, and said such horrible things. Will you forgive me, and let us start afresh?” I murmured. All flippancy, bitterness, and amusement had died out of me; I was serious and in earnest. This must have expressed itself in my eyes, for Harold, after gazing searchingly right there for a time, seemed satisfied, and his mouth relaxed to its habitually lovable expression as he said:

===Chinese===
“比彻姆先生——哈罗德——我方才那样不像个女人，说了那样可怕的话，真对不起。你肯饶恕我，让我们重新开始吗？”我低声说。一切轻浮、怨毒、戏谑都已在我身上死绝；我是郑重的、诚恳的。这层意思必定从我眼睛里透露了出来，因为哈罗德对着那双眼睛细细探究地望了一阵，似乎满意了，嘴也松回了惯常那副可爱的模样，说道：

===Original===
“Are you in earnest? Well, that is something more like the little woman.”

===Chinese===
“你是当真的？唔，这才有点儿小女人的样子了。”

===Original===
“Yes, I’m in earnest. Can you forgive me?”

===Chinese===
“是，我是当真的。你能饶恕我吗？”

===Original===
“There is nothing to forgive, as I’m sure you didn’t mean and don’t remember the blood curdling sentiments you aired.”

===Chinese===
“没有什么可饶恕的。我敢说你抛出的那些叫人毛骨悚然的高见，你说时既不曾存心，此刻也已忘干净了。”

===Original===
“But I did mean them in one sort of a way, and didn’t in another. Let us start afresh.”

===Chinese===
“可那些话，从一个方面讲我是存心的，从另一个方面讲又不是。让我们重新开始吧。”

===Original===
“How do you mean to start afresh?”

===Chinese===
“你打算怎么个重新开始法？”

===Original===
“I mean for us to be chums again.”

===Chinese===
“我是说，让我们重新做好伙伴。”

===Original===
“Oh, chums!” he said impatiently; “I want to be something more.”

===Chinese===
“哦，好伙伴！”他不耐烦地说；“我要的是比这更进一步。”

===Original===
“Well, I will be something more if you will try to make me,” I replied.

===Chinese===
“那好，只要你肯下功夫争取，我就做那更进一步。”我答道。

===Original===
“How? What do you mean?”

===Chinese===
“怎么个争取法？你是什么意思？”

===Original===
“I mean you never try to make me fond of you. You have never uttered one word of love to me.”

===Chinese===
“我是说，你从来不想法子叫我喜欢你。你从来没有对我说过一个爱字。”

===Original===
“Why, bless me!” he ejaculated in surprise.

===Chinese===
“哎呀，我的老天！”他惊得脱口喊道。

===Original===
“It’s a fact. I have only flirted to try and see if you cared, but you didn’t care a pin.”

===Chinese===
“这是实话。我先前同人调笑，不过是想试试你在不在乎，可你半点儿也不在乎。”

===Original===
“Why, bless me, didn’t you say I was not to show any affection yet awhile? And talk about not caring﻿—why, I have felt fit to kill you and myself many a time the last fortnight, you have tormented me so; but I have managed to keep myself within bounds till now. Will you wear my ring again?”

===Chinese===
“哎呀，我的老天！不是你吩咐过我，眼下还不许我露半点情意吗？还说什么不在乎——哼，这两个礼拜，你把我折磨成那样，有多少回我都恨不得杀了你、再杀了我自己；可我一直把自己管束住了，直到今天。你肯重新戴上我的戒指吗？”

===Original===
“Oh no; and you must not say I am flirting if I cannot manage to love you enough to marry you, but I will try my best.”

===Chinese===
“哦，不戴；还有，万一我怎么也爱不到肯嫁给你的地步，你也不许说我是在调笑——不过我一定尽力。”

===Original===
“Don’t you love me, Syb? I have thought of nothing else but you night and day since I saw you first. Can it be possible that you don’t care a straw for me?” and a pained expression came upon his face.

===Chinese===
“你不爱我吗，西比？打头一回见着你，我日夜想的就没有别的，只有你。难道你对我竟连一根草的分量也不放在心上？”他脸上浮起一层痛楚的神色。

===Original===
“Oh, Harold, I’m afraid I very nearly love you, but don’t hurry me too much! You can think me sort of secretly engaged to you if you like, but I won’t take your ring. Keep it till we see how we get on.” I looked for it, and finding it a few steps away, gave it to him.

===Chinese===
“哦，哈罗德，我怕我差不多是爱上你了，只是你别催我催得太急！你高兴的话，只当我是暗地里同你订了婚，可我不收你的戒指。先收着，等我们看看处得怎么样。”我四下里找那戒指，在几步外寻着了，便递给了他。

===Original===
“Can you really trust me again after seeing me get in such a vile beast of a rage? I often do that, you know,” he said.

===Chinese===
“亲眼看见我发了那样一通恶畜生似的怒火，你当真还能再信我？你知道，我这毛病常犯。”他说。

===Original===
“Believe me, Hal, I liked it so much I wish you would get in a rage again. I can’t bear people who never let themselves go, or rather, who have nothing in them to carry them away﻿—they cramp and bore me.”

===Chinese===
“相信我，哈尔，我太喜欢它了，恨不得你再发一回怒才好。我受不了那种从不放任自己的人——或者不如说，心里没有一点东西能带着他忘情的人——这种人拘得我发闷，闷得我发烦。”

===Original===
“But I have a frightful temper. Satan only knows what I will do in it yet. Would you not be frightened of me?”

===Chinese===
“可我的脾气凶得吓人。只有撒旦知道，我这脾气将来还会闹出什么来。你不怕我吗？”

===Original===
“No fear,” I laughed; “I would defy you.”

===Chinese===
“才不怕呢。”我笑着说；“我还要同你较量较量。”

===Original===
“A tomtit might as well defy me,” he said with amusement.

===Chinese===
“一只山雀（tomtit）也照样能同我较量。”他觉着好笑地说。

===Original===
“Well, big as you are, a tomtit having such superior facilities for getting about could easily defy you,” I replied.

===Chinese===
“哼，你块头再大，一只山雀飞来飞去的本事那样高强，想同你叫板还不容易。”我答道。

===Original===
“Yes, unless it was caged,” he said.

===Chinese===
“是啊——除非把它关进了笼子。”他说。

===Original===
“But supposing you never got it caged,” I returned.

===Chinese===
“可假定你永远没能把它关进笼子呢？”我回敬道。

===Original===
“Syb, what do you mean?”

===Chinese===
“西比，你这是什么意思？”

===Original===
“What could I mean?”

===Chinese===
“我能是什么意思？”

===Original===
“I don’t know. There are always about four or five meanings in what you say.”

===Chinese===
“我不知道。你的话里头，总归藏着大约四五个意思。”

===Original===
“Oh, thanks, Mr. Beecham! You must be very astute. I am always thankful when I am able to dish one meaning out of my idle gabble.”

===Chinese===
“哦，多谢夸奖，比彻姆先生！你必定精明得很。我这张嘴信口闲扯出来的话，能倒腾出一整个意思来，我就已经谢天谢地了。”

===Original===
The glorious summer day had fallen asleep on the bosom of the horizon, and twilight had merged into dusk, as, picking up the basket, Harold and I returned cherry- and strawberry-less to the tennis court. The players had just ceased action, and the gentlemen were putting on their coats. Harold procured his, and thrust his arms into it, while we were attacked on all sides by a flood of banter.

===Chinese===
壮丽的夏日已睡倒在天际的胸怀里，暮色已融成了昏暝。哈罗德拾起篮子，我们回到网球场来——樱桃也没摘着，草莓也没摘着。打球的人刚歇了手，先生们正在穿上外衣。哈罗德取来自己那件，两臂往袖子里一插；我们这边，则被四面八方涌来的一片戏谑围攻了。

===Original===
My birthday tea was a great success, and after it was done we enjoyed ourselves in the drawing-room. Uncle Jay-Jay handed me a large box, saying it contained a present. Everyone looked on with interest while I hurriedly opened it, when they were much amused to see﻿—nothing but a doll and materials to make it clothes! I was much disappointed, but uncle said it would be more in my line to play with that than to worry about tramps and politics.

===Chinese===
我的生日晚茶大获成功，吃罢，大家在客厅里尽欢。杰杰舅舅递给我一只大盒子，说里头是一件礼物。人人都饶有兴致地看着我急急打开，一见里头——不过是一个洋娃娃，外加给它做衣裳的料子——个个乐不可支！我大失所望，舅舅却说，同操心流浪汉和政治比起来，玩这个更合我的行当。

===Original===
I took care to behave properly during the evening, and when the goodbyes were in full swing had an opportunity of a last word with Harold, he stooping to hear me whisper:

===Chinese===
那天晚上我处处留心行止得体，到道别正进行得热火朝天时，得了机会同哈罗德讲最后一句话；他俯下身来，听我悄声说：

===Original===
“Now that I know you care, I will not annoy you any more by flirting.”

===Chinese===
“如今我知道你在乎了，我再不拿调笑来惹你恼了。”

===Original===
“Don’t talk like that. I was only mad for the moment. Enjoy yourself as much as you like. I don’t want you to be like a nun. I’m not quite so selfish as that. When I look at you and see how tiny you are, and how young, I feel it is brutal to worry you at all, and you don’t detest me altogether for getting in such an infernal rage?”

===Chinese===
“别那样说。我不过一时发了疯。你尽管尽情地玩。我不要你做个修女。我还没有自私到那个地步。我望着你，见你这么小、这么年轻，只觉得搅扰你本身就是件狠心的事——我发了那样一通恶魔似的狂怒，你没有把我恨透了吧？”

===Original===
“No. That is the very thing I liked. Good night!”

===Chinese===
“没有。我喜欢的恰恰是那个。晚安！”

===Original===
“Good night,” he replied, taking both my hands in his. “You are the best little woman in the world, and I hope we will spend all your other birthdays together.”

===Chinese===
“晚安。”他答道，把我的双手都握进他手里。“你是世上最好的小女人。我盼望你其余的每一个生日，我们都一同度过。”

===Original===
“It’s to be hoped you’ve said something to make Harry a trifle sweeter than he was this afternoon,” said Goodchum. Then it was:

===Chinese===
“但愿你方才说了句什么话，好叫哈里比今天下午甜和上一点儿。”古德查姆（Goodchum）说。接着便是：

===Original===
“Good night, Mrs. Bossier! Good night, Harry! Good night, Archie! Good night, Mr. Goodchum! Goodbye, Miss Craddock! Ta-ta, Miss Melvyn! So long, Jay-Jay! Goodbye, Mrs. Bell! Goodbye, Miss Goodjay! Good night, Miss Melvyn! Good night, Mr. Goodjay! Good night, Mrs. Bossier! Goodbye, Miss Melvyn! Good night all!”

===Chinese===
“晚安，博西耶太太！晚安，哈里！晚安，阿奇！晚安，古德查姆先生！再见，克拉多克（Craddock）小姐！回头见，梅尔文小姐！再会，杰杰！再见，贝尔太太！再见，古德杰伊（Goodjay）小姐！晚安，梅尔文小姐！晚安，古德杰伊先生！晚安，博西耶太太！再见，梅尔文小姐！祝大家晚安！”

===Original===
I sat long by my writing-table that night﻿—thinking long, long thoughts, foolish thoughts, sad ones, merry ones, old-headed thoughts, and the sweet, sweet thoughts of youth and love. It seemed to me that men were not so invincible and invulnerable as I had imagined them﻿—it appeared they had feeling and affections after all.

===Chinese===
那天夜里，我在写字台前久久坐着——想着长长的、长长的念头：愚蠢的念头，忧伤的念头，快活的念头，少年老成的念头，还有青春与爱情那甜甜的、甜甜的念头。在我看来，男人并不如我想象的那样不可征服、刀枪不入——他们到底也是有感情、有柔肠的。

===Original===
I laughed a joyous little laugh, saying, “Hal, we are quits,” when, on disrobing for the night, I discovered on my soft white shoulders and arms﻿—so susceptible to bruises﻿—many marks, and black.

===Chinese===
夜里就寝宽衣时，我发现自己柔软白皙的肩膀和手臂上——那么经不起掐碰的地方——有许多印子，而且青黑，便快活地轻笑一声，说：“哈尔，咱们两讫了。”

===Original===
It had been a very happy day for me.

===Chinese===
这于我，真是极其快活的一天。

## XXIV Thou Knowest Not What a Day May Bring Forth / 第24章 祸福旦夕难料

===Original===
The next time I saw Harold Beecham was on Sunday the 13th of December. There was a hammock swinging under a couple of trees in an enclosure, half shrubbery, partly orchard and vegetable garden, skirting the road. In this I was gently swinging to and fro, and very much enjoying an interesting book and some delicious gooseberries, and seeing Harold approaching pretended to be asleep, to see if he would kiss me. But no, he was not that style of man. After tethering his horse to the fence and vaulting himself over it, he shook me and informed me I was as sound asleep as a log, and had required no end of waking.

===Chinese===
我再见到哈罗德·比彻姆，是在十二月十三日，星期天。大路旁有一方园地，一半是灌木丛，一半是果园兼菜畦，几株树底下悬着一张吊床。我便在里头悠悠地荡来荡去，一册有趣的书、一把可口的醋栗，正享用得惬意；望见哈罗德走近，我便装作睡熟，看他会不会吻我。可是没有——他不是那一路的男人。他把马拴上栅栏，一个翻身跃了过来，摇了摇我，说我睡得同木头一样死，好一番折腾才叫得醒。

===Original===
My hair tumbled down. I accused him of disarranging it, and ordered him to repair the damage. He couldn’t make out what was the matter with it, only that “It looks a bit dotty.”

===Chinese===
我的头发散落了下来。我怪罪他弄乱了它，命令他把损失修补好。他看不出它究竟出了什么毛病，只说得出一句：“瞧着有点儿乱蓬蓬。”

===Original===
“Men are queer creatures,” I returned. “They have the most wonderful brains in some ways, but in little things they are as stupid as owls. It is no trouble to them to master geology, mineralogy, anatomy, and other things, the very name of which gives me a headache. They can see through politics, mature mighty water reservoir schemes, and manage five stations at once, but they couldn’t sew on a button or fix one’s hair to save their life.”

===Chinese===
“男人是种古怪的造物，”我回敬道。“他们在某些方面长着顶顶神奇的脑袋，可是在小事上，蠢得同猫头鹰一样。地质学、矿物学、解剖学，还有别的种种学问，单是名目就叫我头痛，他们学起来却全不费事。政治，他们瞧得透彻；浩大的蓄水库工程，他们筹划得周全；五处牧场，他们能一气兼管；可是叫他们缝一粒纽扣、替人抿好头发，就是要了他们的命也办不来。”

===Original===
I cannot imagine how the news had escaped me, for the story with which Harold Beecham surprised and startled me on that long hot afternoon had been common talk for some time.

===Chinese===
那消息竟会漏过我的耳目，我真想不明白——因为在那个漫长炎热的下午，哈罗德·比彻姆拿来叫我又惊又愕的那段情由，早已是众人嘴上谈论多时的闲话了。

===Original===
He had come to Caddagat purposely to explain his affairs to me, and stated as his reason for not having done so earlier that he had waited until the last moment thinking he might pull himself up.

===Chinese===
他这趟是专程到卡达加特来向我交代他的境况的；至于为什么不早说，他陈明缘由：他一直捱到最后一刻，指望自己兴许还能翻过身来。

===Original===
Business to me is a great mystery, into which I haven’t the slightest desire to penetrate. I have no brains in that direction﻿—so will not attempt to correctly reproduce all that Harold Beecham told me on that afternoon while leaning against a tree at my feet and looking down at me as I reclined in the hammock.

===Chinese===
生意经于我是一大玄奥，我一丝一毫也不想去参透。我在那上头没有脑筋——所以，那天下午哈罗德·比彻姆倚着我脚畔的一棵树、低头俯看躺在吊床里的我，所说的那一番话，我也不打算一字不差地照样复述。

===Original===
There was great mention of bogus bonds, bad investments, liabilities and assets and personal estates, and of a thing called an official assignee﻿—whatever that is﻿—voluntary sequestration, and a jargon of such terms that were enough to mither a Barcoo lawyer.

===Chinese===
满耳朵无非是假债券哪、蚀本的投资哪、负债呀、资产呀、动产呀，还有什么一样唤作官方受托人的东西——管它是什么——还有自愿清算哪；这一大串行话黑话，足以把巴库（Barcoo）的律师都搅得头昏脑涨。

===Original===
The gist of the matter, as I gathered it, was that Harold Beecham, looked upon as such a “lucky beggar,” and envied as a pet of fortune, had been visited by an unprecedented run of crushing misfortunes. He had not been as rich and sound in position as the public had imagined him to be. The failure of a certain bank two or three years previously had given him a great shaking. The tick plague had ruined him as regarded his Queensland property, and the drought had made matters nearly as bad for him in New South Wales. The burning of his wool last year, and the failure of the agents in whose hands he had placed it, this had pushed him farther into the mire, and now the recent “going bung” of a building society﻿—his sole remaining prop﻿—had run him entirely ashore.

===Chinese===
据我领会的要旨，事情是这样：哈罗德·比彻姆，那个人人眼里的“幸运儿”、众口艳羡的命运宠儿，遭了一连串史无前例的倾覆性灾殃。他并不像公众想象的那样家底殷实、根基稳固。两三年前一家银行倒闭，已狠狠摇撼了他一回；壁虱瘟疫毁了他昆士兰的产业；大旱又叫他在新南威尔士的境况近乎同样糟糕。去年羊毛遭了火焚，托售羊毛的代理商又告倒闭，把他往泥沼里又推进一层；如今一家建屋互助会近来的“完蛋”——他仅存的最后一根支柱——更把他整个儿抛上了浅滩。

===Original===
He had sequestrated his estate, and as soon as practicable was going through the courts as an insolvent. The personal estate allowed him from the debris of his wealth he intended to settle on his aunts, and he hoped it might be sufficient to support them. Himself, he had the same prospects as the boundary-riders on Five-Bob Downs.

===Chinese===
他已把自己的产业交付清算，一俟手续可行，便要以无力偿债者的身份过堂法院。从他家财的残骸里获准留给他的那份动产，他打算划归两位姑奶奶名下，并盼望它足以供养她们。至于他自己，前程同五博丘上的巡栏员一般无二。

===Original===
I had nothing to say. Not that Harold was a much-to-be-pitied man when one contrasted his lot with that of millions of his fellows as deserving as he; but, on the other hand, considering he had been reared in wealth and as the master of it since his birth, to be suddenly rendered equal with a labourer was pretty hard lines.

===Chinese===
我无话可说。倒不是说，同千百万与他同样值得同情的世人一比，哈罗德就算个多么可怜的人；可是话说回来，念着他生在富贵里、落地便是财富的主人，一朝忽然降得同雇工平起平坐，也实在是够狠的运气。

===Original===
“Oh, Harold, I am so sorry for you!” I managed to stammer at last.

===Chinese===
“哦，哈罗德，我真替你难过！”我好容易才结结巴巴吐出这一句。

===Original===
“Don’t worry about me. There’s many a poor devil, crippled and ill, though rolling in millions, who would give all his wealth to stand in my boots today,” he said, drawing his splendid figure to its full height, while a look of stern pride settled on the strong features. Harold Beecham was not a whimpering cur. He would never tell anyone his feelings on the subject; but such a sudden reverse of fortune, tearing from him even his home, must have been a great blow to him.

===Chinese===
“别替我操心。有的是可怜虫，残废的、害病的，哪怕泡在百万家财里，也甘愿舍尽全部财富，来换今日站进我这双靴子里的光景。”他说着，把他那副堂堂的身躯挺到十足的高度，一片冷峻的傲色落定在他刚强的五官上。哈罗德·比彻姆不是一条呜呜哀鸣的癞皮狗。他在这桩事上的心绪，永远不会向任何人吐露；可是这般急转直下的败运，连他的家都从他手里夺了去，于他必是一记沉重的打击。

===Original===
“Syb, I have been expecting this for some years; now that it is done with, it is a sort of grim relief. The worst of all is that I’ve had to give up all hope of winning you. That is the worst of all. If you didn’t care for me when I was thought to be in a position to give you all that girls like, you could never look at me now that I’m a pauper. I only hope you will get some fellow who will make you as happy as I would have tried to had you let me.”

===Chinese===
“西比，这场祸事我已经提防了好些年；如今尘埃落定，倒有一种冷冷的宽慰。最糟的是，我不得不断了赢得你的一切指望。这才是最糟的。人家当我处境优裕、姑娘们喜欢的东西样样给得起的时候，你尚且不曾把我放在心上；如今我做了穷光蛋，你就更不会看我一眼了。我只盼你日后遇上一个男人，他给你的幸福，抵得上我——倘若你容过我——原本会尽力给你的。”

===Original===
I sat and wondered at the marvellous self-containment of the man before me. With this crash impending, just imagine the worry he must have gone through! But never had the least suspicion that he was troubled found betrayal on his brow.

===Chinese===
我坐在那儿，惊叹眼前这个男人惊人的自持。这样一场倾覆悬在头顶，想想他必得熬过多少忧煎！可是他额头上从不曾露出过最细微的破绽，叫人疑心他心里有事。

===Original===
“Goodbye, Syb,” he said; “though I’m a nobody now, if I could ever be of use to you, don’t be afraid to ask me.”

===Chinese===
“再会，西比，”他说；“我如今是个无名小卒了，可若有朝一日能对你派上用场，别怕开口来找我。”

===Original===
I remember him wringing the limp hand I mechanically stretched out to him and then slowly revaulting the fence. The look of him riding slowly along with his broad shoulders drooping despondently waked me to my senses. I had been fully engrossed with the intelligence of Harold’s misfortune﻿—that I was of sufficient importance to concern him in any way had not entered my head; but it suddenly dawned on me that Harold had said that I was, and he was not in the habit of uttering idle nothings.

===Chinese===
我记得他紧紧握了握我机械地伸出去的那只绵软的手，随后慢慢翻过栅栏去了。望着他缓缓骑行、宽阔的肩膀颓然垂着的背影，我这才惊醒过来。方才我一门心思全叫哈罗德的不幸占满了——我自己在他心里竟有那般要紧，这层压根儿不曾进过我的脑子；可我蓦然明白过来：哈罗德方才分明这样说了，而他素来没有说空话的习惯。

===Original===
While fortune smiled on him I had played with his manly love, but now that she frowned had let him go without even a word of friendship. I had been poor myself, and knew what awaited him in the world. He would find that they who fawned on him most would be first to turn their backs on him now. He would be rudely disillusioned regarding the fables of love and friendship, and would become cynical, bitter, and sceptical of there being any disinterested good in human nature. Suffering the cold heart-weariness of this state myself, I felt anxious at any price to save Harold Beecham from a like fate. It would be a pity to let one so young be embittered in that way.

===Chinese===
命运向他微笑的时候，我拿他男子汉的爱情耍着玩儿；如今命运朝他皱了眉，我竟连一句友情的话也不曾给，便由他去了。我自己穷过，知道这世道等着他的是什么。他会发现，从前对他趋奉得最殷勤的，如今头一个背过身去不认他。关于爱情与友谊的种种佳话，他会遭逢粗暴的幻灭，从此变得玩世不恭、心怀怨毒，再不信人性里还有什么不掺私心的善。这种心境那份冰凉的心灰意懒，我自己正受着，便急切地不惜任何代价，要把哈罗德·比彻姆从同样的命运里救出来。这样年轻的人，任他那样变怨恨了，未免可惜。

===Original===
There was a shortcut across the paddocks to a point of the road where he would pass; and with these thoughts flashing through my mind, hatless and with flying hair, I ran as fast as I could, scrambling up on the fence in a breathless state just as he had passed.

===Chinese===
穿过围场有条近道，直通他必经的那段大路；这些念头在脑中飞闪，我不戴帽子、头发翻飞，撒腿能跑多快跑多快，气喘吁吁地爬上栅栏时，恰恰他才走过去。

===Original===
“Hal, Hal!” I called. “Come back, come back! I want you.”

===Chinese===
“哈尔！哈尔！”我喊道。“回来，回来！我要你。”

===Original===
He turned his horse slowly.

===Chinese===
他缓缓拨转马头。

===Original===
“Well, Syb, what is it?”

===Chinese===
“怎么，西比，什么事？”

===Original===
“Oh, Hal, dear Hal! I was thinking too much to say anything; but you surely don’t think I’d be so mean as to care a pin whether you are rich or poor﻿—only for your own sake? If you really want me, I will marry you when I am twenty-one if you are as poor as a crow.”

===Chinese===
“哦，哈尔，亲爱的哈尔！我心里翻腾得太满，一句话也说不出来；可你总不至于把我看得那样卑俗，以为你富也罢穷也罢，我会放在心上一个子儿——我惦记的只是你本人？你要是当真要我，哪怕你穷得像只乌鸦，我满二十一岁也嫁给你。”

===Original===
“It is too good to be true. I thought you didn’t care for me. Sybylla, what do you mean?”

===Chinese===
“这好得不像真话了。我只当你心里没有我。西比拉，你这是什么意思？”

===Original===
“Just what I say,” I replied, and without further explanation, jumping off the fence I ran back as fast as I had come.

===Chinese===
“就是我说的意思。”我答道，再不作一句多余的解释，跳下栅栏，撒腿往回跑，跑得同来时一样快。

===Original===
When halfway home I stopped, turned, looked, and saw Harold cantering smartly homewards, and heard him whistling a merry tune as he went.

===Chinese===
跑到离家半路，我停住脚，回身望去，只见哈罗德踏着轻快的跑步奔回家去，一路还吹着一支欢快的曲子。

===Original===
After all, men are very weak and simple in some ways.

===Chinese===
说到底，男人在某些方面实在又软弱又简单。

===Original===
I laughed long and sardonically, apostrophizing myself thus:

===Chinese===
我笑了许久，笑得冷嘲，对着自己这般呼告：

===Original===
“Sybylla Penelope Melvyn, your conceit is marvellous and unparalleled! So you actually imagined that you were of sufficient importance to assist a man through life﻿—a strong, healthy young man too, standing six feet three and a half in his socks, a levelheaded business man, a man of high connections, spotless character, and influential friends, an experienced bushman, a man of sense, and, above all, a man﻿—a man! The world was made for men.

===Chinese===
“西比拉·佩内洛普·梅尔文（Sybylla Penelope Melvyn），你的自负真是妙绝寰宇、举世无俦！你竟当真以为，你一个人有那么要紧，能扶持一个男人走过一世——而且是个强壮健康的年轻男人，只穿袜子量身也高六英尺三英寸半，一个头脑冷静的生意人，一个交游显贵、品行无瑕、朋友得势的男人，一个老练的丛林人，一个明白事理的男人，而首要的是，是个男人——男人！这世界原是为男人们造的。

===Original===
“Ha ha! You, Sybylla, thought this! You, a chit in your teens, an ugly, poor, useless, unimportant, little handful of human flesh, and, above, or rather below, all, a woman﻿—only a woman! It would indeed be a depraved and forsaken man who would need your services as a stay and support! Ha ha! The conceit of you!”

===Chinese===
“哈哈！你，西比拉，竟这样想过！你，一个十几岁的小丫头，一小把丑陋、贫穷、无用、无足轻重的人间血肉，而高过这一切的——不，是垫在这一切底下的——是个女人，不过是个女人！当真得是一个堕了道、遭了弃的男人，才需要你来充当他的支柱与倚靠！哈哈！瞧你这份自负哟！”

## XXV Because? / 第25章 缘何？

===Original===
The Beechams were vacating Five-Bob almost immediately﻿—before Christmas. Grannie, aunt Helen, and uncle Jay-Jay went down to say goodbye to the ladies, who were very heartbroken about being uprooted from Five-Bob, but they approved of their nephew settling things at once and starting on a clean sheet. They intended taking up their residence﻿—hiding themselves, they termed it﻿—in Melbourne. Harold would be detained in Sydney some time during the settling of his affairs, after which he intended to take anything that turned up. He had been offered the management of Five-Bob by those in authority, but could not bring himself to accept managership where he had been master. His great desire, now that Five-Bob was no longer his, was to get as far away from old associations as possible.

===Chinese===
比彻姆一家几乎立刻就要腾出五博丘了——赶在圣诞节以前。外婆、海伦姨妈和杰杰舅舅下去向两位姑奶奶道别；被连根拔离五博丘，两位老小姐伤心得很，却都赞许侄儿当机立断、把诸事料理停当，在一张干净账页上从头另起。她们打算把住处安到墨尔本——用她们自己的话说，是“把自己藏起来”。了结事务期间，哈罗德要在悉尼耽搁些时日，此后碰到什么出路便接什么出路。当权的那些人曾提出把五博丘交给他管理，可他无法说服自己，在自己当过主人的地方屈就管家之职。五博丘既已不再归他，他如今最大的心愿，便是离旧日的一切人事越远越好。

===Original===
He had seen his aunts off, superintended the muster of all stock on the place, dismissed all the female and most of the male employees, and surrendered the reins of government, and as Harold Augustus Beecham, boss of Five-Bob, on Monday, the 21st of December 1896, was leaving the district forever. On Sunday, the 20th of December, he came to bid us goodbye and to arrive at an understanding with me concerning what I had said to him the Sunday before. Grannie, strange to say, never suspected that there was likely to be anything between us. Harold was so undemonstrative, and had always come and gone as he liked at Caddagat: she overlooked the possibility of his being a lover, and in our intercourse allowed us almost the freedom of sister and brother or cousins.

===Chinese===
他送走了两位姑母，督办了场上全部牲口的大清点，辞退了全部女雇工和大半男雇工，交出了当家的权柄；而身为五博丘之主的哈罗德·奥古斯都·比彻姆，将于 1896 年 12 月 21 日，星期一，永远离开这一带。12 月 20 日，星期天，他来向我们辞行，并要就我上一个星期天对他说的话，同我达成一个明白的谅解。说来奇怪，外婆从不曾疑心我们之间兴许有什么。哈罗德是那样不形于色，在卡达加特又一向如在自己家里般来去随心：她压根儿不曾想到他也可能是位求婚者，待我们往来相处，几乎给了我们如同姐弟或表兄妹一般的自由。

===Original===
On this particular afternoon, after we had talked to grannie for a little while, knowing that he wished to interview me, I suggested that he should come up the orchard with me and get some gooseberries. Without demur from anybody we set off, and were scarcely out of hearing before Harold asked me had I really meant what I said.

===Chinese===
这天下午，我们同外婆谈了一小会儿话，我知道他有话要同我说，便提议他随我上果园里去摘些醋栗。谁也没有二话，我们便动身了；才刚走得出了能叫人听见的范围，哈罗德就问我，我说的话是不是当真。

===Original===
“Certainly,” I replied. “That is, if you really care for me, and think it wise to choose me of all my sex.”

===Chinese===
“当然是当真，”我答道。“那就是说，你得当真疼我，并且当真觉得，在全天下的女人里挑中我是明智的。”

===Original===
Ere he put it in words I read his answer in the clear brown eyes bent upon me.

===Chinese===
他还没把话诉诸言语，我已经从他俯望着我的那双清朗的褐色眼睛里，读出了他的回答。

===Original===
“Syb, you know what I feel and would like, but I think it would be mean of me to allow you to make such a sacrifice.”

===Chinese===
“西比，你知道我的心思，也知道我愿意什么；可是任由你做这样的牺牲，我想自己就太卑鄙了。”

===Original===
I knew I was not dealing with a booby, but with a sensible clear-sighted man, and so studied to express myself in a way which would not for an instant give him the impression that I was promising to marry him because﻿—what I don’t know and it doesn’t matter much, but I said:

===Chinese===
我知道对付的不是个笨蛋，而是个明白而有眼光的人，因此字斟句酌，措辞务求不叫他有一瞬间生出这样的印象，以为我答应嫁他是因为——因为什么呢，我不知道，这也无关紧要——可我还是说道：

===Original===
“Hal, don’t you think it is a little selfish of you to want to throw me over just because you have lost your money? You are young, healthy, have good character and influential connections, and plenty of good practical ability and sense, so, surely, you will know no such thing as failure if you meet the world bravely. Go and be the man you are; and if you fail, when I am twenty-one I will marry you, and we will help each other. I am young and strong, and am used to hard work, so poverty will not alarm me in the least. If you want me, I want you.”

===Chinese===
“哈尔，只因为你丢了钱，就想着把我抛开，你不觉得这有点儿自私吗？你年轻，健康，品行好，亲友情面广，实干的本事和见识都足足有余；只要你勇敢地面对世人，断不会知道什么叫失败。去，做你本来就是的那个男人；倘若你败了，等我满二十一岁，我就嫁给你，我们彼此扶持。我年轻力壮，苦活儿做惯了，贫穷一点儿也吓不着我。你要是要我，我也要你。”

===Original===
“Syb, you are such a perfect little brick that I couldn’t be such a beggarly cur as to let you do that. I knew you were as true as steel under your funny little whims and contrariness; and could you really love me now that I am poor?”

===Chinese===
“西比，你是这么个十全十美的好姑娘，我总不能贱得像条癞皮狗，由着你这样做。我早知道，在你那些滑稽的小性子和别扭底下，你真得像钢一样；可如今我穷了，你当真能爱我吗？”

===Original===
I replied with vigour:

===Chinese===
我理直气壮地答道：

===Original===
“Do you think I am that sort, that cares for a person only because he has a little money? Why! that is the very thing I am always preaching against. If a man was a lord or a millionaire I would not have him if I loved him not, but I would marry a poor cripple if I loved him. It wasn’t because you owned Five-Bob Downs that I liked you, but because you have a big heart in which one would have room to get warm, and because you are true, and because you are kind and big and﻿—” Here I could feel my voice getting shaky, and being afraid I would make a fool of myself by crying, I left off.

===Chinese===
“你把我当成那路人了吗——只因一个人有几个钱，才把他放在心上？嘿！那恰恰是我平日里口口声声反对的东西。一个男人，哪怕他是勋爵、是百万富翁，我不爱他就不要他；可我若爱他，哪怕他是个穷瘸子，我也嫁。我喜欢你，从来不是因为你坐拥五博丘，而是因为你有一颗宽大的心，人靠过去有地方取暖；因为你真，因为你仁慈、宽厚，还因为——”说到这里，我觉得自己的声音发起颤来，生怕哭出来丢人现眼，便住了口。

===Original===
“Syb, I will try and fix matters up a bit, and will claim you in that time if I have a home.”

===Chinese===
“西比，我要尽力把局面稍稍收拾起来；到那时，只要我有了个家，就来娶你。”

===Original===
“Claim me, home or not, if you are so disposed, but I will make this condition. Do not tell anyone we are engaged, and remember you are perfectly free. If you see a woman you like more than me, promise me on your sacred word that you will have none of those idiotic unjust ideas of keeping true to me. Promise.”

===Chinese===
“那就来娶我吧，有没有家都罢，只要你愿意；不过我要定下一个条件。不要告诉任何人我们订了婚，还要记着，你是完全自由的。倘若你遇上一个比我更合意的女人，我要你凭你的神圣信义起誓：绝不存那些愚蠢又不讲理的念头，觉得非对我守着不可。答应我。”

===Original===
“Yes, I will promise,” he said easily, thinking then, no doubt, as many a one before him has thought, that he would never be called upon to fulfil his word.

===Chinese===
“好，我答应。”他轻描淡写地说，心里想的，无疑同他之前的许多人想过的一样：这话是永远不会被要求兑现的。

===Original===
“I will promise in return that I will not look at another man in a matrimonial way until the four years are up, so you need not be jealous and worry yourself; for, Hal, you can trust me, can you not?”

===Chinese===
“我也回敬一个诺言：四年期满之前，我绝不拿挑丈夫的眼光去瞧别的男人，所以你不必吃醋，也不必自寻烦恼；哈尔，你是信得过我的，对不对？”

===Original===
Taking my hand in his and looking at me with a world of love in his eyes, which moved me in spite of myself, he said:

===Chinese===
他把我的手握进他手里，眼里盛着满世界的爱意望着我，我不由得为之动容。他说：

===Original===
“I could trust you in every way to the end of the world.”

===Chinese===
“无论在哪一件事上，我都信得过你，直信到天涯海角。”

===Original===
“Thank you, Harold. What we have said is agreed upon﻿—that is, of course, as things appear now: if anything turns up to disturb this arrangement it is not irrevocable in the least degree, and we can lay out more suitable plans. Four years will not be long, and I will be more sensible at the end of that time﻿—that is, of course, if I ever have any sense. We will not write or have any communication, so you will be perfectly free if you see anyone you like better than me to go in and win. Do you agree?”

===Chinese===
“谢谢你，哈罗德。咱们说定的就算说定了——自然，这是照眼下的光景而论：倘有什么变故搅了这个安排，它丝毫也不是不可更改的，咱们尽可以另订更合适的计划。四年不会长，到那时候我也会更懂事了——自然，这还得看我日后到底有没有懂事的那一天。咱们不写信、不通音讯，这样，你若遇上比我更称心的人，便完全可以放开手去赢得她。你同意吗？”

===Original===
“Certainly; any little thing like that you can settle according to your fancy. I’m set up as long as I get you one way or another, that’s all I want. It was a bit tough being cleared out from all the old ways, but if I have you to stand by me it will be a great start. Say what you said last Sunday, again. Syb, say you will be my wife.”

===Chinese===
“当然；这类小事，随你高兴怎么定就怎么定。只要不管怎样能得着你，我就万事齐备，我要的仅此而已。从旧日的一切里被扫地出门，滋味是有点儿苦；可有你站在我身边，便是个了不起的起点。把上星期天说的话，再说一遍。西比，说你愿意做我的妻子。”

===Original===
I had expected him to put it in that way, and believing in doing all or nothing, had laid out that I would put my hand in his and promise what he asked. But now the word wife finished me up. I was very fond of Harold﻿—fond to such an extent that had I a fortune I would gladly have given it all to him: I felt capable of giving him a life of servitude, but I loved him﻿—big, manly, lovable, wholesome Harold﻿—from the crown of his head to the sole of his foot he was good in my sight, but lacking in that power over me which would make me desirous of being the mother of his children.

===Chinese===
我料到他会这样提出来，而且抱着要么全给、要么全无的宗旨，早已打定主意要把手放进他手里，把所求的应下。可到了这一步，“妻子”这个词却把我击垮了。我深爱哈罗德——爱到这般地步：我若有一份家财，会欢欢喜喜地全部给他；我觉得自己尽可以为他操劳一世；说到爱——魁伟、有男子气、可爱、健全的哈罗德——从头顶到脚底，他在我眼里样样都好，独独缺了那一种慑服我的力量，那种能叫我甘愿去做他孩子们的母亲的力量。

===Original===
As for explaining my feelings to him﻿—ha! He would laughingly call them one of my funny little whims. With his orthodox, practical, plain, commonsense views of these things, he would not understand me. What was there to understand? Only that I was queer and different from other women. But he was waiting for me to speak. I had put my hand to the plough and could not turn back. I could not use the word wife, but I put my hand in his, looked at him steadily, and said﻿—

===Chinese===
至于把我的心事解说给他听——哈！他只会笑着把它唤作我又一个小小的怪念头。凭着他对这些事那套正统、实际、直白、凭常识的见解，他是不会懂我的。又有什么可懂的呢？无非是我这个人古怪，跟别的女人不一样罢了。可他正等着我开口。我既已把手扶上了犁，就再不能回头。“妻子”这个词我说不出口，但我把手放进他的手里，定定地望着他，说道——

===Original===
“Harold. I meant what I said last Sunday. If you want me﻿—if I am of any use to you﻿—I will marry you when I attain my majority.”

===Chinese===
“哈罗德。上星期天我说的话是当真的。你要我——我若对你还有点儿用处——我一到成年就嫁给你。”

===Original===
He was satisfied.

===Chinese===
他满足了。

===Original===
He bade us goodbye early that afternoon, as he intended departing from Five-Bob when the morrow was young, and had two or three little matters to attend to previous to his departure.

===Chinese===
那天下午他早早向我们道了别，因为他打算次日天一亮就动身离开五博丘，行前还有两三件小事要料理。

===Original===
I accompanied him a little way, he walking and leading his horse. We parted beneath the old willow-tree.

===Chinese===
我送了他一程，他步行着，牵着他的马。我们在那株老柳树下分了手。

===Original===
“Goodbye, Harold. I mean all I have said.”

===Chinese===
“再会，哈罗德。我说的话，句句当真。”

===Original===
I turned my face upwards; he stooped and kissed me once﻿—only once﻿—one light, gentle, diffident kiss. He looked at me long and intently without saying a word, then mounted his horse, raised his hat, and rode away.

===Chinese===
我仰起脸来；他俯下身，吻了我一次——只此一次——轻轻的、温存而怯生生的一吻。他久久地、定定地望着我，一言不发，随后上了马，举了举帽子，策马而去。

===Original===
I watched him depart along the white dusty road, looking like a long snake in the glare of the summer sun, until it and he who travelled thereon disappeared among the messmate- and hickory-trees forming the horizon.

===Chinese===
我望着他沿着那条白尘漫漫的大路远去——盛夏烈日的强光下，那路望过去像一条长蛇；直到路，连同走在路上的他，一齐消失在构成天际的那一带斜叶桉和山核桃树（hickory）的林影里。

===Original===
I stood gazing at the hills in the distance on which the blue dreaming mists of evening were gathering, until tears stole down my cheeks.

===Chinese===
我伫立着，凝望远处的群山——黄昏那些如梦的蓝雾正往山头聚拢——直到泪水悄悄爬下我的脸颊。

===Original===
I was not given to weeping. What brought them? I hardly knew. It was not because Harold was leaving, though I would miss him much. Was it because I was disappointed in love? I persuaded myself that. I loved Harold as much as I could ever love anyone, and I could not forsake him now that he needed me. But, but, but, I did not want to marry, and I wished that Harold had asked anything of me but that, because﻿—because, I don’t know what, and presently felt ashamed for being such a selfish coward that I grudged to make a little sacrifice of my own inclinations to help a brother through life.

===Chinese===
我不是个惯于落泪的人。这泪是哪里来的？我简直说不上来。并不是因为哈罗德要走，虽然我会很想念他。是因为我情场失意吗？我说服自己是这样。我爱哈罗德，爱到了我此生爱一个人的极限；如今他正需要我，我不能撇下他。可是，可是，可是，我不想嫁人；哈罗德哪怕向我求什么都好，偏偏别求这个——因为——因为什么，我不知道；随后我便觉出羞惭，羞自己竟是这样自私的懦夫，舍不得为扶持一个兄弟走过一世，稍稍委屈一点自己的心愿。

===Original===
“I used to feel sure that Harry meant to come up to the scratch, but I suppose he’s had plenty to keep him going lately without bothering his head about a youngster in short frocks and a pigtail,” remarked uncle Jay-Jay that night.

===Chinese===
“我原先还拿得准，哈里早晚要上得阵来把话挑明；可我估摸着，他近来够他忙的了，犯不着再为一个穿短裙、拖小辫的黄毛丫头伤神。”当天晚上，杰杰舅舅这般议论。

===Original===
“Well, Sybylla, poor Harry has gone: we will all﻿—even you included﻿—miss him very much, I am sure. I used to think that he cared for you. It may be that he has not spoken to us on account of his financial failure, and it may be that I made a mistake,” said aunt Helen when she was bidding me good night.

===Chinese===
“唉，西比拉，可怜的哈里走了：我们大家——连你也在内——都会很想念他的，这一点我拿得准。我从前一直以为他心里有你。也许是碍着他破了产，才没向我们开口；也许是我看错了。”海伦姨妈向我道晚安时说。

===Original===
I held my peace.

===Chinese===
我缄默不言。

## XXVI Boast Not Thyself of Tomorrow / 第26章 莫向明日夸海口

===Original===
We felt the loss of the Beechams very, very much. It was sad to think of Five-Bob﻿—pleasant, hospitable Five-Bob﻿—as shut up, with no one but a solitary caretaker there pending the settling of the Beecham insolvency; with flowers running to seed unheeded in the wide old garden, grass yellowing on the lawns, fruit wasting in wain-loads in the great orchard, kennels, stables, fowl-houses, and cow-yards empty and deserted. But more than all, we missed the quiet, sunburnt, gentlemanly, young giant whose pleasant countenance and strapping figure were always welcome at Caddagat.

===Chinese===
比彻姆一家的离去，我们感念非常、非常之深。一想到五博丘——那宜人好客的五博丘——如今门户紧闭，只剩一个孤零零的看守人守着，等待比彻姆家破产案子的了结；一想到宽敞的老花园里，花儿听任结籽、无人经心，草坪上的草日渐枯黄，大果园里整车的果子白白糟蹋，犬舍、马厩、鸡棚、牛栏一概空空落落、荒废无人——怎不叫人黯然。而比这一切更甚的，是我们想念那个沉静、晒得黢黑、绅士派头的年轻巨人，他那愉快的面容和魁伟的身躯，在卡达加特向来是最受欢迎的。

===Original===
Fortunately, Christmas preparations gave us no rest for the soles of our feet, and thus we had little time to moon about such things: in addition, uncle Jay-Jay was preparing for a trip, and fussed so that the whole place was kept in a state of ferment.

===Chinese===
幸而圣诞节的种种操办叫我们脚板不得消停，因此难得有工夫为这些事痴痴出神；加之杰杰舅舅正打点一趟远行，忙乱得整个宅子沸反盈天。

===Original===
We had fun, feasting, and company to no end on Christmas Day. There were bank clerks and young fellows out of offices from Gool-Gool, jackeroos and governesses in great force from neighbouring holdings, and we had a merry time.

===Chinese===
圣诞节那天，我们的玩乐、盛宴和宾客没有个穷尽。到场的有古尔古尔来的银行职员和写字楼里的年轻人，还有左近各牧场蜂拥而至的大批牧场学徒和家庭女教师，我们过得快活极了。

===Original===
On Boxing Day uncle Jay-Jay set out on a tour to New Zealand, intending to combine business with pleasure, as he meant to bring back some stud stock if he could make a satisfactory bargain. Boxing Day had fallen on a Saturday that year, and the last of our guests departed on Sunday morning. It was the first time we had had any quietude for many weeks, so in the afternoon I went out to swing in my hammock and meditate upon things in general. Taking with me a bountiful supply of figs, apricots, and mulberries, I laid myself out for a deal of enjoyment in the cool dense shade under the leafy kurrajong- and cedar-trees.

===Chinese===
节礼日（Boxing Day）那天，杰杰舅舅动身去新西兰游历，打算把买卖同游乐合在一趟——倘若谈得成一笔合意的买卖，他还要带些种畜回来。那年的节礼日恰逢星期六，我们的客人直到星期天早晨才走尽。这是我们许多个星期以来头一回得着清静，于是下午我便出去，荡我的吊床，漫想世间种种。我带了满满一份无花果、杏子和桑葚，预备在枝叶扶疏的瓶木和雪松（cedar）之下、清凉浓密的树荫里，大大享受一番。

===Original===
To begin with, Harold Beecham was gone, and I missed him at every turn. I need not worry about being engaged to be married, as four years was a long, long time. Before that Harold might take a fancy to someone else, and leave me free; or he might die, or I might die, or we both might die, or fly, or cry, or sigh, or do one thing or another, and in the meantime that was not the only thing to occupy my mind: I had much to contemplate with joyful anticipation.

===Chinese===
头一件，哈罗德·比彻姆不在了，我处处都觉得少了他。订了婚要出嫁这件事，倒不必我操心——四年是一段很长、很长的光阴。在那之前，哈罗德说不定会看中别人，还我自由；或者他没了，或者我没了，或者我们俩都没了，或者飞了，哭了，叹了，或者干出件把别的什么事来；况且那也不是盘踞我心思的唯一一桩：我还有好些事情，可以欢欢喜喜地悬想期盼哩。

===Original===
Towards the end of February a great shooting and camping party, organized by grannie, was to take place. Aunt Helen, grannie, Frank Hawden, myself, and a number of other ladies and gentlemen, were going to have ten days or a fortnight in tents among the blue hills in the distance, which held many treasures in the shape of lyrebirds, musk, ferns, and such scenery as would make the thing perfection. After this auntie and I were to have our three months’ holiday in Sydney, where, with Everard Grey in the capacity of showman, we were to see everything from Manly to Parramatta, the Cyclorama to the Zoo, the theatres to the churches, the restaurants to the jails, and from Anthony Hordern’s to Paddy’s Market. Who knows what might happen then? Everard had promised to have my talents tested by good judges. Might it not be possible for me to attain one of my ambitions﻿—enter the musical profession? joyful dream! Might I not be able to yet assist Harold in another way than matrimony?

===Chinese===
二月末，外婆张罗的一场盛大的打猎露营聚会就要举行。海伦姨妈、外婆、弗兰克·霍登、我，还有好些别的女士先生们，要到远处蓝色的群山里去，在帐篷中过上十天或半个月；那山里藏着许多珍宝——琴鸟、麝香灌木（musk）、蕨草，还有那样的景致，保管叫这桩事十全十美。这之后，姨妈和我要去悉尼度我们的三个月假期；在那里，有埃弗拉德·格雷充任领路的看客，从曼利（Manly）到帕拉马塔（Parramatta）、从环景画馆到动物园、从剧院到教堂、从餐馆到监狱、从霍尔登商店到帕迪市场（Paddy's Market），我们要把一切看个遍。谁知道那时候会出什么事呢？埃弗拉德答应过，要请高明的行家来品鉴我的才艺。我岂不是有可能得着我的抱负之一——进身音乐这一行吗？快活的梦！我岂不还能另辟一条婚姻之外的路子，帮衬哈罗德吗？

===Original===
Yes, life was a pleasant thing to me now. I forgot all my wild unattainable ambitions in the little pleasures of everyday life. Such a thing as writing never entered my head. I occasionally dreamt out a little yarn which, had it appeared on paper, would have brimmed over with pleasure and love﻿—in fact, have been redolent of life as I found it. It was nice to live in comfort, and among ladies and gentlemen﻿—people who knew how to conduct themselves properly, and who paid one every attention without a bit of fear of being twitted with “laying the jam on.”

===Chinese===
是啊，如今生活于我是一件愉快的事了。日常生活的种种小乐趣，把我那些狂野而高不可攀的抱负全数淹没。写作这样的事，压根儿不曾进过我的头脑。我偶尔在幻想里编排出一个小故事，倘若落到纸上，准会满纸盈盈地漫溢着快乐与爱——说实在的，会处处散发着我自己所寻见的那种生活的芬芳。日子过得舒舒服服，周旋于女士先生们之间——这些人懂得怎样举止合宜，样样殷勤待你，又全不怕被人打趣一句“抹蜜奉承”——这实在是好事。

===Original===
I ate another fig and apricot, a mulberry or two, and was interrupted in the perusal of my book by the clatter of galloping hoofs approaching along the road. I climbed on to the fence to see who it could be who was coming at such a breakneck pace. He pulled the rein opposite me, and I recognized a man from Dogtrap. He was in his shirtsleeves; his horse was all in a lather, and its scarlet nostrils were wide open, and its sides heaving rapidly.

===Chinese===
我又吃了一只无花果、一只杏子，外加一两颗桑葚；正读着书，大路上渐近的马蹄疾响把我打断了。我爬上栅栏，看是谁这样不要命地飞奔而来。他在我对面收住缰，我认出是狗陷来的一个人。他只穿着件衬衫；那匹马浑身汗沫淋漓，通红的鼻孔张得老大，两肋急促地起伏。

===Original===
