“I say, miss, hunt up the men quickly, will you?” he said hurriedly. “There’s a tremenjous fire on Wyambeet, and we’re short-handed. I’m goin’ on to knock them up at Bimbalong.”

===Chinese===
“我说小姐，快些把工人都唤起来，行不行？”他急匆匆地说。“怀安比特着了一场不得了的大火，俺们人手不够。俺这就赶去宾巴隆（Bimbalong），敲门把人叫起来哩。”

===Original===
“Hold hard,” I replied. “We haven’t a man on the place, only Joe Slocombe, and I heard him say he would ride down the river and see what the smoke was about; so he will be there. Mr. Hawden and the others have gone out for the day. You go back to the fire at once; I’ll rouse them up at Birribalong.”

===Chinese===
“慢着，”我答道。“庄上一个男人也没有，只有乔·斯洛科姆，我听他说要骑马沿河下去瞧瞧那烟是怎么回事，所以他准在那儿。霍登先生和其他人出去一整天了。你这就回火场去；比里巴隆（Birribalong）那边由我去把人叫起来。”

===Original===
“Right you are, miss. Here’s a couple of letters. My old moke flung a shoe and went dead lame at Dogtrap; an’ wile I was saddlun another, Mrs. Butler stuffed ’em in me pocket.”

===Chinese===
“成嘞，小姐。这儿有两封信。俺那口老牲口在狗陷崩掉了个蹄铁，瘸得一步也走不得咧；俺备另一匹马的当儿，巴特勒太太把信塞进了俺兜里。”

===Original===
He tossed them over the fence, and, wheeling his mount, galloped the way he had come. The letters fell, address upwards, on the ground﻿—one to myself and one to grannie, both in my mother’s handwriting. I left them where they lay. The main substance of mother’s letters to me was a hope that I was a better girl to my grannie than I had been to her﻿—a sentiment which did not interest me.

===Chinese===
他把信从栅栏上头抛过来，拨转坐骑，顺原路飞驰而去。两封信跌落在地上，地址朝天——一封给我的，一封给外婆的，都是我母亲的笔迹。我由它们躺在那儿。母亲写给我的信，要旨无非是盼我在外婆跟前做个比在她跟前乖些的女儿——这样一份情意，引不起我半点兴致。

===Original===
“Where are you off to?” inquired grannie, as I rushed through the house.

===Chinese===
“你这是上哪儿去？”我冲进屋时，外婆问道。

===Original===
I explained.

===Chinese===
我说明了缘由。

===Original===
“What horse are you going to take?”

===Chinese===
“你打算骑哪匹马？”

===Original===
“Old Tadpole. He’s the only one available.”

===Chinese===
“老塔德波尔（old Tadpole）。眼下只有它使得。”

===Original===
“Well, you be careful and don’t push him too quickly up that pinch by Flea Creek, or he might drop dead with you. He’s so fat and old.”

===Chinese===
“那么你当心，上跳蚤溪（Flea Creek）旁边那道陡坡时别催它太紧，不然它兴许当场倒毙，把你一起摔翻。它又肥又老了。”

===Original===
“All right,” I replied, snatching a bridle and running up the orchard, where old Tadpole had been left in case of emergency. I clapped a sidesaddle on his back, a hat on my head, jumped on just as I was, and galloped for my life in the direction of Bimbalong, seven miles distant. I eased my horse a little going up Flea Creek pinch, but with this delay reached my destination in half an hour, and sent the men galloping in the direction of the fire. I lingered for afternoon tea, and returned at my leisure.

===Chinese===
“好嘞，”我答道，抓起一副笼头就往果园跑——老塔德波尔为应急起见，一直留在那儿。我往它背上撂了一副女式侧鞍，往头上扣了顶帽子，一身家常打扮便蹿上马背，朝着七英里外的宾巴隆没命地飞驰。上跳蚤溪那道陡坡时，我略略松了松它，饶是这般耽搁，半个钟头便到了地头，打发那些人朝火场的方向疾驰而去。我流连着用了下午茶，才从从容容往回走。

===Original===
It was sundown when I got in sight of Caddagat. Knowing the men would not be home for some time, I rode across the paddock to yard the cows. I drove them home and penned the calves, unsaddled my horse and returned him to the orchard, then stood upon the hillside and enjoyed the scene. It had been a fearfully hot day, with a blasting, drought-breathed wind; but the wind had dropped to sleep with the sunlight, and now the air had cooled. Blue smoke wreathed hill and hollow like a beauteous veil. I had traversed drought-baked land that afternoon, but in the immediate vicinity of Caddagat house there was no evidence of an unkind season. Irrigation had draped the place with beauty, and I stood ankle-deep in clover. Oh, how I loved the old irregularly built house, with here and there a patch of its low iron roof peeping out of a mass of greenery, flowers, and fruit﻿—the place where I was born﻿—home! Save for the murmur of the creek, the evening was wrapped in silence﻿—sweet-breathed, balmy-browed, summer quietude. I stretched out my hand and stained my fingers, next my lips and teeth, with the sweet dark fruit of a mulberry-tree beside me. The shadows deepened; I picked up my saddle, and, carrying it housewards, put it in its place in the harness-room among the fig- and apricot-trees﻿—laden to breaking point with ripe and ripening fruit. The two servant girls had departed on their Christmas holiday that morning, so grannie and auntie were the only members of the family at home. I could not see or hear them anywhere, so, presuming they were out walking, I washed my hands, lit a lamp, and sat down to my tea, where it had been left for me on the dining-table. I remembered﻿—wonderful aberration from my usual thoughtlessness﻿—that the book I had left in the hammock had a beautiful cover which the dew would spoil, so I left my tea to bring it in. Two little white squares struck my eye in the gathering dusk. I picked them up also, and, bringing them to the light, opened the one addressed to me, and read:

===Chinese===
望见卡达加特时，已是日落时分。我知道男人们一时半会儿回不来，便骑马穿过围场去圈牛。我把牛赶回家关进栏，把牛犊另圈起来，卸下马鞍，把马送回果园，然后站在山坡上赏玩眼前景致。这一日热得怕人，刮着旱魃般灼人的风；可那风已随着日光一同睡去，此刻空气凉了下来。青烟像一幅美丽的面纱，缭绕着山峦和洼地。那天下午我跑过的尽是旱得烤焦的土地，可在卡达加特宅子左近，却看不出一点无情时令的痕迹。灌溉给这地方披上了美的衣装，我站在没踝深的三叶草里。啊，我多么爱这座盖得参差错落的老宅子——低矮的铁皮屋顶东一块西一块，从满目的葱茏、花卉与果实间探出头来——这是我出生的地方——是家啊！除却溪水的低语，暮色便裹在寂静里——气息甘美、眉额温软的夏日清幽。我伸出手，从身旁一株桑树上摘下甜黑的果子吃，染污了手指，接着又染了嘴唇和牙齿。暗影渐深；我拾起马鞍，朝宅子抱去，放进无花果树与杏树之间那间鞍具房里它原在的位置——两样树上熟透的、将熟的果子，压得枝条几乎要断。两个女佣那天早晨已动身去过圣诞假期，所以家里只剩外婆和姨妈。我在哪儿也望不见、听不见她们，料想她们散步去了，便洗了手，点起一盏灯，坐下用我的茶——茶点留在餐桌上候着我呢。我想起来——素来粗心的我，这回真是破天荒的异举——落在吊床里的那本书有个漂亮的封皮，夜露会毁了它，便撇下茶去取它。渐浓的暮色里，两个白色的小方块撞进我的眼帘。我把它们也拾起，拿到灯下，拆开写着我名字的那一封，读道：

===Original===
> No doubt what I have to write will not be very palatable to you; but it is time you gave up pleasuring and began to meet the responsibilities of life. Your father is lazier if anything, and drinks more than ever. He has got himself into great debt and difficulties, and would have been sold off again but for Peter M’Swat. You will remember Peter M’Swat? Well, he has been good enough to lend your father 500 pounds at 4 percent, which means 20 pounds per year interest. Your father would have no more idea of meeting this amount than a cat would have. But now I am coming to the part of the matter which concerns you. Out of friendship to your father, Mr. M’Swat is good enough to accept your services as governess to his children, in lieu of interest on the money. I have told him you will be in Yarnung In Friday the 8th of January 1897, where he will meet you. Be careful to remember the date. I am sorry I could not give you more notice; but he wants his children to commence school as soon as possible, and he deserves every consideration in the matter. Perhaps you will not find it as pleasant as Caddagat; but he has been very good, and offers you a fair number of holidays, and what he will give you is equal to 20 pounds. That is a lot in these times, when he could easily get so many better girls than you are in every way for half the money, and make your father pay the interest, and thereby be 10 pounds in pocket. You will have to help Mrs. M’Swat with the work and sewing; but that will do you good, and I hope you will try hard to give every satisfaction. I have also written to your grandmother.

===Chinese===
> 毫无疑问，我要写下的这些话，你读来不会怎么受用；可是也到了你收起玩乐、开始担起生活责任的时候了。你父亲比先前更懒了些，酒也喝得比先前更凶。他背上了一屁股债，陷进了难处，若不是彼得·麦斯沃特（Peter M'Swat），早又被连锅端去拍卖了。你总记得彼得·麦斯沃特吧？好吧，承他的情，借给你父亲 500 镑，年息四厘，就是说一年 20 镑的利息。你父亲对偿还这笔钱的心思，不会比一只猫多上一丝一毫。现在，我要说到这桩事里与你相干的部分了。看在你父亲的情分上，麦斯沃特先生承情愿意收下你，去给他的孩子们当家庭女教师，抵这笔钱的利息。我已告诉他，你将于 1897 年 1 月 8 日星期五抵达雅农（Yarnung），他会在那儿接你。千万记牢日子。抱歉不能早些知会你；可他要孩子们尽早开课，在这桩事上，他理应得到方方面面的体谅。兴许你在那儿不会觉得像卡达加特这样惬意；可他一直待我们很好，给你的假期也公道，他给你的东西抵得上 20 镑。这在眼下年月不是小数目——他满可以只花一半的钱，轻易雇到多少样样胜过你的姑娘，再叫你父亲照付利息，自己净赚 10 镑落腰包。你得帮麦斯沃特太太做家务、做针线；不过那于你有益，我望你尽心竭力，事事叫人满意。我也已写信给你的外婆。

===Original===
That letter wiped away ever vestige of my appetite for the dainties before me. M’Swat’s! Send﻿—me﻿—to M’Swat’s! I could not believe it! It must be a nightmare! M’Swat’s!

===Chinese===
这封信把我对着眼前精美茶点仅剩的一点胃口，扫荡得干干净净。麦斯沃特家！把——我——送去麦斯沃特家！我不能相信！这准是一场噩梦！麦斯沃特家！

===Original===
Certainly, I had never been there; but all those who had gave graphic descriptions of the total ignorance of Mrs. M’Swat. Why, the place was quite tabooed on account of its squalor and dirt!

===Chinese===
不错，我从没去过那儿；但凡去过的人，都把麦斯沃特太太那彻头彻尾的无知描摹得活灵活现。可不是么，那地方就因为又穷又脏，简直成了人人避而不提的禁忌之地！

===Original===
The steel of my mother’s letter entered my soul. Why had she not expressed a little regret at the thing she was imposing on me? Instead, there was a note of satisfaction running through her letter that she was able to put an end to my pleasant life at Caddagat. She always seemed to grudge me any pleasure. I bitterly put it down as accruing from the curse of ugliness, as, when mentioning Gertie, it was ever, “I have let Gertie go to such and such an entertainment. We could not very well afford it, but the poor little girl does not have many pleasures for her years.” I was smaller than Gertie, and only eleven months older; but to me it was “You must think of something besides pleasure.”

===Chinese===
母亲信里的刀锋直刺入我的灵魂。她把这样一件事强加于我，怎么竟不流露一丝惋惜？恰恰相反，那封信自始至终透着一股快意，庆幸她终能了结我在卡达加特的快活日子。无论什么乐趣，她似乎都见不得我沾。我恨恨地把这归到丑陋的诅咒账上——因为一提起格蒂，她永远是：“我让格蒂去了这样那样的游艺会。我们实在供不起，可这可怜的小姑娘，在她这样的年纪，实在也没多少可乐的。”我个子比格蒂小，只比她大十一个月；轮到我，便是：“你必须想想玩乐以外的事了。”

===Original===
The lot of ugly girls is not joyful, and they must be possessed of natures very absurdly sanguine indeed ever to hope for any enjoyment in life.

===Chinese===
丑姑娘的命运并不欢乐，她们的天性非得乐观到荒唐的地步，才敢指望人生里还有什么乐趣可言。

===Original===
It was cruel, base, horrible of my mother to send me to M’Swat’s. I would not go﻿—not for 50 pounds a day! I would not go! I would not! not for any consideration.

===Chinese===
母亲把我送到麦斯沃特家去，狠心，卑劣，可怖。我不去——一天给 50 镑也不去！我不去！我不去！给什么条件都不去！

===Original===
I stamped about in a fever of impatience until grannie appeared, when I handed both letters to her, and breathlessly awaited her verdict.

===Chinese===
我焦灼难耐，跺着脚走来走去，直到外婆露面，才把两封信一并递到她手里，屏息等她的裁断。

===Original===
“Well, child, what do you say?”

===Chinese===
“怎么样，孩子，你怎么说？”

===Original===
“Say? I won’t go! I can’t! I won’t! Oh, grannie, don’t send me there﻿—I would rather die.”

===Chinese===
“怎么说？我不去！我去不了！我不去！哦，外婆，别送我去那儿——我宁肯死。”

===Original===
“My dear child, I would not be willing to part with you under any circumstances, but I cannot interfere between a mother and her child. I would not have allowed anyone to do it with me, and believe in acting the same towards any other mother, even though she is my own daughter. However, there is time to get a reply before you would have to start, so I will write and see what can be done.”

===Chinese===
“亲爱的孩子，无论什么情形我都不愿同你分开，可我不能插手母女之间的事。换作是我，我也不许旁人这样待我；我信奉待人如己，对任何一位母亲都当如此，哪怕她是我自己的女儿。好在离你动身还有些日子，来得及等一封回信，我这就写信，看看还有什么法子可想。”

===Original===
The dear old lady, with her prompt businesslike propensities, sat down and wrote there and then. I wrote also﻿—pleaded with my mother against her decree, begged her to leave me at Caddagat, and assured her I could never succeed at M’Swat’s.

===Chinese===
这位可亲可敬的老太太，秉性行事干脆麻利，当即坐下，当场就写。我也写了——向母亲恳求收回成命，求她把我留在卡达加特，并向她担保，我在麦斯沃特家是万万成不了事的。

===Original===
I did not sleep that night, so arose betimes to await the first traveller, whom I asked to post the letters.

===Chinese===
那一夜我不曾合眼，天一亮便起身守着，等头一个过路的旅客，托他把信寄走。

===Original===
We got an answer to them sooner than we expected﻿—at least grannie did. Mother did not deign to write to me, but in her letter to grannie I was described as an abominably selfish creature, who would not consider her little brothers and sisters. I would never be any good; all I thought of was idleness and ease. Most decidedly I could not get out of going to M’Swat’s, as mother had given her word.

===Chinese===
回信来得比我们料想的快——至少外婆收到了。母亲不屑赏我一封信，但在给外婆的信里，我被描画成一个可憎自私的东西，全不顾念她的弟弟妹妹；我永远成不了什么气候；满脑子想的唯有安逸与清闲。至于躲开麦斯沃特家不去，那是断断不必想了——母亲已经许下了话。

===Original===
“I am sorry for you,” said grannie, “but it cannot be helped. You can stay there for two or three years, and then I can have you here again.”

===Chinese===
“我替你难过，”外婆说，“可是没法子。你在那儿待上两三年，往后我就又能把你接回身边来了。”

===Original===
I was inconsolable, and would not listen to reason. Ah! that uncle Jay-Jay had been at home to rescue me from this. Then aunt Helen brought her arguments to bear upon me, and persuaded me to think it was necessary for the benefit of my little brothers and sisters that I should take up this burden, which I knew would be too much for me.

===Chinese===
我伤心得无可慰藉，什么道理也听不进。唉！要是杰杰舅舅在家，能把我从这桩事里搭救出来，那该多好！后来海伦姨妈拿她的道理来劝我，说得我相信：为了我弟弟妹妹们的益处，我理应挑起这副担子——虽然我明知道它非我力所能任。

===Original===
It was a great wrench to be torn away from Caddagat﻿—from refinement and comfort﻿—from home! As the days till my departure melted away, how I wished that it were possible to set one’s weight against the grim wheel of time and turn it back! Nights I did not sleep, but drenched my pillow with tears. Ah, it was hard to leave grannie and aunt Helen, whom I worshipped, and turn my back on Caddagat!

===Chinese===
从卡达加特被生生拽开——从精致与安逸——从家——这是怎样的一番撕扯！动身前的日子一天天消融，我多么巴望能拿自己的身子去抵住时间那狰狞的车轮，把它扳转回去！夜里我睡不着，泪水把枕头浸得透湿。啊，撇下我敬爱的外婆和海伦姨妈，背转身去离开卡达加特，这有多难！

===Original===
I suppose it is only a fancy born of the wild deep love I bear it, but to me the flowers seem to smell more sweetly there; and the shadows, how they creep and curl! oh, so softly and caressingly around the quaint old place, as the great sun sets amid the blue peaks; and the never-ceasing rush of the crystal fern-banked stream﻿—I see and hear it now, and the sinking sun as it turns to a sheet of flame the mirror hanging in the backyard in the laundry veranda, before which the station hands were wont to comb and wash themselves. Oh, the memories that crowd upon me! Methinks I can smell the roses that clamber up the veranda posts and peep over the garden gate. As I write my eyes grow misty, so that I cannot see the paper.

===Chinese===
我料想这不过是我对它怀的那份狂深热爱生出的痴念，可在我闻来，那儿的百花似乎吐着更甜的芬芳；还有那些暗影，瞧它们怎样潜行、怎样舒卷！哦，那样轻柔、那样爱抚地缠绕着那座古雅的老宅，正当一轮巨日沉入蓝色的群峰；还有那条两岸蕨生、晶莹透亮的溪流永不停歇的奔涌——此刻我仍看得见、听得见它；还有那西沉的太阳，把洗衣游廊下挂在后院的那面镜子烧成一面烈焰——牧场的工人们从前惯常对着它梳头盥洗。啊，纷纷沓来涌上我心头的记忆！我想我闻得到那攀上游廊柱子、从园门上探头张望的玫瑰的花香。写到此处，我的双眼蒙上了雾，连纸面都望不清了。

===Original===
The day for my departure arrived﻿—hot, 110 degrees in the shade. It was a Wednesday afternoon. Frank Hawden was to take me as far as Gool-Gool that evening, and see me on to the coach next day. I would arrive in Yarnung about twelve or one o’clock on Thursday night, where, according to arrangement, Mr. M’Swat would be waiting to take me to a hotel, thence to his home next day.

===Chinese===
我动身的日子到了——酷热，背荫处 110 度。那是一个星期三的下午。当晚弗兰克·霍登要送我到古尔古尔，第二天再送我上长途马车。我将在星期四夜里十二点到一点之间抵达雅农；照约定，麦斯沃特先生会在那儿等着，先带我去一家旅馆，第二天再带我去他的家。

===Original===
My trunks and other belongings were stowed in the buggy, to which the fat horses were harnessed. They stood beneath the dense shade of a splendid kurrajong, and lazily flicked the flies off themselves while Frank Hawden held the reins and waited for me.

===Chinese===
我的箱子和其他行装都装上了马车，两匹肥壮的马套在车前。它们立在一株挺拔的瓶木浓浓密密的荫凉底下，懒懒地甩尾驱蝇，弗兰克·霍登执缰坐等着我。

===Original===
I rushed frantically round the house taking a last look at nooks and pictures dear to me, and then aunt Helen pressed my hand and kissed me, saying:

===Chinese===
我发狂似的绕着宅子奔走，把心爱的角落和图画最后看上一眼；随后海伦姨妈紧握我的手，吻了吻我，说道：

===Original===
“The house will be lonely without you, but you must brighten up, and I’m sure you will not find things half as bad as you expect them.”

===Chinese===
“你走了，这宅子要冷清了，可你务必振作起来；我敢保，事情决不会像你想的那样糟，一半也及不上。”

===Original===
I looked back as I went out the front gate, and saw her throw herself into a chair on the veranda and cover her face with her hands. My beautiful noble aunt Helen! I hope she missed me just a little, felt just one pang of parting, for I have not got over that parting yet.

===Chinese===
出了前门我回头望去，见她跌坐进游廊上的一把椅子，双手掩住了脸。我美丽高贵的海伦姨妈！但愿她也有一点想念我，也尝到哪怕一下离别的心痛——因为那一场别离，我到如今还没有缓过来。

===Original===
Grannie gave me a warm embrace and many kisses. I climbed on to the front seat of the buggy beside my escort, he whipped the horses﻿—a cloud of dust, a whirr of wheels, and we were gone﻿—gone from Caddagat!

===Chinese===
外婆给了我一个热热的拥抱和许多亲吻。我爬上马车前座，在我这位护送人身旁坐下；他扬鞭催马——一股尘土，一阵轮响，我们去了——从卡达加特去了！

===Original===
We crossed the singing stream: on either bank great bushes of blackthorn﻿—last native flower of the season﻿—put forth their wealth of magnificent creamy bloom, its rich perfume floating far on the hot summer air. How the sunlight blazed and danced and flickered on the familiar and dearly loved landscape! Over a rise, and the house was lost to view, then goodbye to the crystal creek. The trees of Five-Bob Downs came within eye-range far away on our left. What merry nights I had spent there amid music, flowers, youth, light, love, and summer warmth, when the tide of life seemed full! Where now was Harold Beecham and the thirty or more station hands, who but one short month before had come and gone at his bidding, hailing him boss?

===Chinese===
我们驶过那条歌唱着的溪流：两岸丛丛的黑刺李（blackthorn）——本季最后开放的乡土花——绽出满树丰盈华美的乳白花朵，馥郁的香气在炎夏的空气里飘出老远。阳光在那熟悉而心爱的风景上，怎样地燃烧、曼舞、闪跳！翻过一道坡，宅子便望不见了，接着便向那晶莹的溪流道了别。五博丘的树林远远进入眼帘，在我们左手边。曾在那儿，在音乐、鲜花、青春、光明、爱情和仲夏的暖意中间，我度过多少欢畅的夜晚——那时生命的潮水仿佛正涨得满满！如今哈罗德·比彻姆和那三十多名牧场工人又都在哪儿呢？不过短短一个月之前，他们还凭他一声吩咐来来去去，口口声声称他老板哩。

===Original===
It was all over! My pleasant life at Caddagat was going into the past, fading as the hills which surrounded it were melting into a hazy line of blue.

===Chinese===
全都完了！我在卡达加特那段快活的日子正沉入过去，渐渐消隐，正如环抱着它的群山，正融成一线朦胧的蓝。

## XXVII My Journey / 第27章 我的旅程

===Original===
The coach was a big vehicle, something after the style of a bus, the tilt and seats running parallel with the wheels. At the rear end, instead of a door, was a great tailboard, on the principle of a spring-cart. This was let down, and, after we scrambled over it into our seats, it was fixed half-mast, all the luggage piled thereon, and firmly roped into position. When this was completed, to anyone on the ground only the heads of passengers were visible above the pile. Had the coach capsized we would have been in a nice fix, as the only means of exit was by crawling up through the back of the box-seat, which rose breast-high﻿—an awkward feat.

===Chinese===
那辆长途马车是个大家伙，样式有几分像公共马车，车篷和座席顺着车轮平行排开。车尾不设车门，只有一块大尾板，原理同弹簧轻马车一个样。板子先放下来，等我们手脚并用地翻过它、爬进各自的座位，再把它支到半截，全部行李都码在上头，用绳索牢牢捆定。这一套完毕，站在地上的人望去，乘客们便只剩一颗颗脑袋露在行李堆上头。马车若是翻了车，我们的处境可就“妙”了——唯一的出口是打车夫座背后爬出去，那座儿升得齐胸高——一桩别扭的功夫。

===Original===
Frank Hawden and I parted good friends. I leant out and waved my handkerchief, until a bend of the road hid him from sight.

===Chinese===
弗兰克·霍登同我和和气气地分了手。我探出身子挥动手帕，直到道路一个弯儿把他遮出我的视线。

===Original===
It was noon, the thermometer registered 112 degrees in the shade, and the dust was simply awful. It rose in such thick grey clouds that often it was impossible to discern the team of five which pulled us, and there was danger of colliding with passing vehicles. We were very much crowded, there being sixteen passengers. When we settled down and got started, I discovered that I was the only representative of my sex, and that I was sandwiched between a perky youth in his teens and a Chinaman, while a black fellow and a man with a red beard sat opposite. A member of Parliament, farther up the seat, who had been patronizing New Year’s Day races in a portion of his electorate, bawled loudly to his companion about “the doin’s of the ’Ouse.” In the perky youth I discovered a professional jockey; and when he found that I was a daughter of Dick Melvyn, the onetime great horse-breeder, he became very friendly. He gave me a couple of apples out of his tin box under the seat, from whence he also produced his whip for my inspection, and was good enough to say:

===Chinese===
时当正午，背阴处的温度计指着华氏 112 度，尘土更是糟得没话说。它腾起一团团浓灰的云，常常连拉着我们走的那五匹马都辨不出来，还有同迎面车辆相撞的危险。车上挤得很，一共十六名乘客。待大家坐定、车子启动，我才发现自己是本性别在车上唯一的代表，被夹在一个十几岁、神气活现的小子和一个中国人中间，对面则坐着一个土著黑人和一个红胡子男人。座位再往上头是一位议员，他方才到选区的一处地方去光临了元旦赛马会，此刻正扯着嗓门向同伴大讲“‘议院’里干的那些事儿”。那个神气活现的小子，我发觉是个职业骑师；待他得知我是迪克·梅尔文的女儿——当年那位鼎鼎大名的养马人——便对我亲热起来。他从座位底下的铁皮匣子里掏出两只苹果给我，又从同一处取出他的马鞭来请我鉴赏，还大发好心地说：

===Original===
“If you can’t stand the stink of that bloomin’ chow, miss, just change seats with me. I’ve knocked about, so that I can easy stand some tough smells without much inconvenience.”

===Chinese===
“小姐，你要是受不住那该死中国佬的臭味，只管同我换座儿。我在外头闯荡惯了，多呛人的味儿也受得住，没多大相干。”

===Original===
I cautioned him to talk lower for fear of hurting the Chinaman’s feelings: this amused him immensely. He laughed very much, and, leaning over to the red-bearded man, repeated the joke:

===Chinese===
我嘱咐他说话放低声些，怕伤了那中国人的心：这话把他逗得乐不可支。他哈哈大笑，又凑向那红胡子男人，把笑话重讲了一遍：

===Original===
“I say, this young lady is afraid I might hurt the chow’s feelin’s. Golly! Fancy a bloomin’ chow havin’ any!”

===Chinese===
“我说，这位小姐怕我伤了那中国佬的心呢。老天爷！亏你想得出——一个该死的中国佬，居然还有心！”

===Original===
The other man also thought it a great joke. I changed seats with the jockey, which put me beside a young gentleman of a literary turn of mind, with whom I had some conversation about books when the dust, rumble of wheels, and turf talk of my other neighbour permitted. They were all very kind to me﻿—gave me fruit, procured me drinks of water, and took turns in nursing a precious hat, for which, on account of the crush, no safe place could be found among the other luggage.

===Chinese===
那一位也把这当成一桩绝妙的笑话。我同骑师换了座位，于是便挨着了一位雅好文学的青年绅士；只要尘土、车轮的轰隆和我另一侧邻座的赛马经容许，我就同他谈谈书。他们待我全都非常和气——给我水果，替我张罗水喝，还轮流看护着我那顶宝贝帽子——车里挤成这样，其余行李当中实在寻不出一处安放它的稳妥地方。

===Original===
Before we had gone half our journey the horses knocked up. All the men were forced to walk up hills for miles and miles in the dust and heat, which did not conduce to their amiability, and many and caustic were the remarks and jokes made upon the driver. He wore out two whips upon his team, until the labour and excessive heat sent the perspiration rolling in rivulets down his face, leaving muddy tracks in the thick coating of dust there. The jockey assisted with his loaded instrument of trade, some of the passengers thrashed with sticks, and all swore under their breath, while a passing bullock-driver used his whip with such deadly effect, that the sweat which poured off the poor beasts was mingled with blood.

===Chinese===
路程未走一半，马匹先垮了下来。上坡时男人们全被迫下车，顶着尘土和酷热一英里又一英里地走，这对他们的和气大有损减；冲着车夫发的议论和笑话又多又刻毒。他在那队马身上抽断了两条鞭子，劳苦加酷热，汗珠子顺着他的脸一道道往下淌，在脸上厚厚的尘垢里冲出一道道泥沟。骑师操起他吃饭的家伙——那柄灌了铅的鞭子——助阵，几位乘客拿棍子抽，人人压着嗓子咒骂；一个过路的赶牛车人也抡起鞭子，下手狠到那几匹可怜的牲口身上淌下的汗都和了血。

===Original===
“Why the deuce don’t you have proper horses?” demanded the red-bearded passenger.

===Chinese===
“你干吗他妈的不配几匹像样的马？”红胡子乘客质问道。

===Original===
The man explained that a ministerial party had chartered his best team to go on a tour of inspection to a mine; a brother coachman had been “stuck up” for horses, and borrowed a couple from him, whereupon he was forced to do with animals which had been turned out for a spell, and the heat and overloading accounted for a good part of the contretemps. However, we managed to catch our train, but had to rush for it without waiting for refreshments. Nice articles we looked﻿—our hair grey with dust, and our faces grimy. The men took charge of me as carefully as though I had been specially consigned to their care. One procured my ticket, another secured me a seat, while a third took charge of my luggage; and they were just as thoughtful when we had to change trains. Off we went. Grannie had packed me quite a large box full of dainties. I produced it, the men provided drinks, and we had quite a pleasant picnic, with all the windows down to catch a little air.

===Chinese===
车夫解释说，一位部长的一行人包下了他顶好的一班马，去视察一座矿井；又有一位同行车夫遭人“拦路打劫”失了马，从他这里借走两匹，于是他只好将就着用那几匹歇了好一阵子工的牲口，再者这般酷热加上超载，也是这场岔子的大半缘由。好在我们总算赶上了火车，只是茶点也顾不得等，拔腿就狂奔。我们这批“货色”的模样真够体面——头发叫尘土蒙得灰白，脸上乌一道泥一道。男人们照管我照管得细心极了，倒像我是一件专门托运给他们、责成他们照料的行李。一位替我买车票，一位给我占座位，第三位照管我的行李；到得换车，他们照样想得这样周到。车开了。外婆给我装了满满一大匣子精美的吃食。我把它拿将出来，男人们供上酒水，我们竟痛痛快快野餐了一场，车窗全放下来，好透一点风。

===Original===
I love the rush and roar of the train, and wished on this occasion that it might go on and on for over, never giving me time to think or stop. But, alas, at 1:20 we pulled up at Yarnung, where a man came inquiring for a young lady named Melvyn. My fellow passengers collected my belongings, and I got out.

===Chinese===
我爱火车的疾驰与轰鸣，这一回更巴望它就这么一直开下去、永远开下去，永远不给我留出思考或停顿的工夫。可是，唉，1:20 我们便在雅农（Yarnung）停了车，一个人上车来，寻问一位名叫梅尔文的年轻小姐。同车的乘客们替我收拢了行李，我下了车。

===Original===
“Goodbye, gentlemen; thank you very much for your kindness.”

===Chinese===
“再会，先生们；多谢你们的好意。”

===Original===
“Goodbye, miss; you’re welcome. Some of us might meet again yet. Ta-ta!”

===Chinese===
“再会，小姐；不用谢。我们这伙人往后兴许还能再碰上呢。回头见！”

===Original===
A shriek, a jerk, and the great train rushed on into the night, leaving me there on the insignificant little platform, feeling how lonely and unhappy, no one knew or cared.

===Chinese===
一声尖啸，车身一挫，那庞然的大火车便一头冲进夜色里去了，把我撂在那个不起眼的小站台上，只觉着心里那份孤寂、那份愁苦有多深——没有人知道，也没有人在乎。

===Original===
Mr. M’Swat shouldered most of my luggage, I took the remainder, and we trudged off in the dark without a word on either side. The publican had given M’Swat the key, so that we might enter without disturbing the household, and he escorted me to a bedroom, where I tumbled into bed with expedition.

===Chinese===
麦斯沃特先生（Mr. M'Swat）扛起我大部分的行李，我拿着剩下的，两人摸着黑一路跋涉，彼此一言不发。旅店老板已把钥匙交给了麦斯沃特，好教我们进门时不必惊动他家里的人；他把我送到一间卧室，我便以麻利至极的身手一头栽上了床。

## XXVIII To Life / 第28章 活下去

===Original===
It is indelibly imprinted on my memory in a manner which royal joy, fame, pleasure, and excitement beyond the dream of poets could never efface, not though I should be cursed with a life of fivescore years. I will paint it truthfully﻿—letter for letter as it was.

===Chinese===
那一幕烙在我记忆里的印迹，任凭王者般的欢乐、名望、声色之娱，任凭超乎诗人梦想的兴奋刺激，都再无法磨灭分毫，哪怕老天咒我活上一百岁也不能。我要把它如实描画出来——一字一母，照它本来的样子。

===Original===
It was twenty-six miles from Yarnung to Barney’s Gap, as M’Swat’s place was named. He had brought a light wagonette and pair to convey me thither.

===Chinese===
从雅农到巴尼山口——麦斯沃特家的地方就叫这名字——有二十六英里路。他赶了一辆双马的轻便四轮马车来，载我前去。

===Original===
As we drove along, I quite liked my master. Of course, we were of calibre too totally unlike ever to be congenial companions, but I appreciated his sound common sense in the little matters within his range, and his bluntly straightforward, fairly good-natured, manner. He was an utterly ignorant man, with small ideas according to the sphere which he fitted, and which fitted him; but he was “a man for a’ that, an’ a’ that.”

===Chinese===
一路行去，我倒相当喜欢我的东家。当然，我们两人的质地差得太远，永远成不了意气相投的伴儿；但凡他见识所及的小事上，他那扎实的常识，连同他那直统统、还算和气的做派，我都看得上。他是个十足无知的人，见识短浅，倒正配他所处的那个圈子，那圈子也正配他这样的人；可他到底是“一个响当当的汉子，管他这一套、那一套”。

===Original===
He and my father had been boys together. Years and years ago M’Swat’s father had been blacksmith on my father’s station, and the little boys had played together, and, in spite of their then difference in station, had formed a friendship which lived and bore fruit at this hour. I wished that their youthful relations had been inimical, not friendly.

===Chinese===
他和我父亲小时候是在一处的。许多许多年以前，麦斯沃特的父亲在我父亲的牧场上当铁匠，两个小男孩一块儿玩耍，尽管那时身份有别，却结下了一段交情，这段交情一直活到如今，恰在这个当口结出了果子。我倒宁愿他们少年时的交往是冤家，不是朋友。

===Original===
We left the pub in Yarnung at nine, and arrived at our destination somewhere about two o’clock in the afternoon.

===Chinese===
我们九点钟离开雅农的旅馆，下午两点来钟到了目的地。

===Original===
I had waxed quite cheerful, and began to look upon the situation in a sensible light. It was necessary that I should stand up to the guns of life at one time or another, and why not now? M’Swat’s might not be so bad after all. Even if they were dirty, they would surely be willing to improve if I exercised tact in introducing a few measures. I was not afraid of work, and would do many things. But all these ideas were knocked on the head, like a dairyman’s surplus calves, when on entering Barney’s Gap we descended a rough road to the house, which was built in a narrow gully between two steep stony hills, which, destitute of grass, rose like grim walls of rock, imparting a desolate and prison-like aspect.

===Chinese===
我原本已经提振起相当的精神，开始用明达的眼光看待自己的处境。人生的炮火，人早晚总得硬起头皮去顶一阵，如今顶便是了，何乐而不为？麦斯沃特家兴许到底没那么糟。就算他们脏，只要我使点手腕、推行几条章程，他们必定也肯改进。我不怕干活，也情愿做许多事。可是这些念头统统被敲掉了脑袋——像奶农打发多余牛犊那样——因为一进巴尼山口，我们便顺着一条崎岖的路下到宅子跟前。宅子盖在两座陡峭多石的山包之间一道窄窄的沟谷里；那两座山寸草不生，像两堵狰狞的石墙般拔地而起，透出一派荒凉、一座牢狱似的气象。

===Original===
Six dogs, two pet lambs, two or three pigs, about twenty fowls, eight children which seemed a dozen, and Mrs. M’Swat bundled out through the back door at our approach. Those children, not through poverty﻿—M’Swat made a boast of his substantial banking account﻿—but on account of ignorance and slatternliness, were the dirtiest urchins I have ever seen, and were so ragged that those parts of them which should have been covered were exposed to view. The majority of them had red hair and wide hanging-open mouths. Mrs. M’Swat was a great, fat, ignorant, pleasant-looking woman, shockingly dirty and untidy. Her tremendous, flabby, stockingless ankles bulged over her unlaced hobnailed boots; her dress was torn and unbuttoned at the throat, displaying one of the dirtiest necks I have seen. It did not seem to worry her that the infant she held under her arm like a roll of cloth howled killingly, while the other little ones clung to her skirts, attempting to hide their heads in its folds like so many emus. She greeted me with a smacking kiss, consigned the baby to the charge of the eldest child, a big girl of fourteen, and seizing upon my trunks as though they were featherweight, with heavy clodhopping step disappeared into the house with them. Returning, she invited me to enter, and following in her wake, I was followed by the children through the dirtiest passage into the dirtiest room, to sit upon the dirtiest chair, to gaze upon the other dirtiest furniture of which I have ever heard. One wild horrified glance at the dirt, squalor, and total benightedness that met me on every side, and I trembled in every limb with suppressed emotion and the frantic longing to get back to Caddagat which possessed me. One instant showed me that I could never, never live here.

===Chinese===
我们的车一到，六条狗、两只养熟的羊羔、两三口猪、二十来只鸡、八个瞧着倒像一打的娃娃，还有麦斯沃特太太，一窝蜂从后门拥了出来。这些孩子——倒不是因为穷，麦斯沃特还夸口过自己在银行里有笔殷实的存款——而是因为无知加邋遢，是我此生见过的最脏的小顽童，衣裳破烂到身上本该遮着的部位都露了出来。他们多半长着红头发，嘴巴老是大张着合不拢。麦斯沃特太太是个高大肥胖、无知无识、模样倒还和气的女人，脏得吓人，也邋遢得吓人。她那双肥厚松垮、光着不穿袜的脚踝，鼓在一双没系鞋带的钉齿靴外头；衣裳破了，领口敞着不扣，露出我此生见过最脏的一条脖子。她胳肢窝下像夹一匹布似的夹着个奶娃娃，娃娃哭得死去活来，她却似乎全不在意；其余的小家伙一个个揪住她的裙子，活像一群小鸸鹋，都想把脑袋藏进裙褶里去。她咂着嘴响亮地亲了我一下，算是见礼，把娃娃移交最大的孩子——一个十四岁的大姑娘——照管，随即拎起我的箱子，倒像拎的是几根鸡毛，迈开沉重笨大的脚步，拎着它们没进屋去。她回出来请我进屋；我跟在她身后，身后又缀着那群孩子，穿过我此生走过的最脏的过道，走进最脏的屋子，坐上最脏的椅子，见识我平生有所耳闻的其余那些最脏的家具。我只狂乱而惊骇地朝四下扫了一眼——满目污秽、穷酸和彻头彻尾的蒙昧——登时便因强抑的情绪和攫住我心的那股回卡达加特去的疯狂渴望，四肢百骸一齐发抖。只这一眼便叫我看明白：我永远、永远不可能在这儿住下去。

===Original===
“Have ye had yer dinner?” my future mistress inquired in a rough uncultivated voice. I replied in the negative.

===Chinese===
“你吃过晌午饭没有？”我这位未来的女主人用一条粗粝未加调教的嗓子问道。我回答没有。

===Original===
“Sure, ye’ll be dyin’ of hunger; but I’ll have it in a twinklin’.”

===Chinese===
“可不，你准要饿坏了；我眨眼工夫就给你弄来。”

===Original===
She threw a crumpled and disgustingly filthy cloth three-cornered ways on to the dusty table and clapped thereon a couple of dirty knives and forks, a pair of cracked plates, two poley cups and chipped saucers. Next came a plate of salt meat, red with saltpetre, and another of dark, dry, sodden bread. She then disappeared to the kitchen to make the tea, and during her absence two of the little boys commenced to fight. One clutched the tablecloth, and over went the whole display with a bang﻿—meat-dish broken, and meat on the dusty floor; while the cats and fowls, ever on the alert for such occurrences, made the most of their opportunities. Mrs. M’Swat returned carrying the tea, which was spilling by the way. She gave those boys each a clout on the head which dispersed them roaring like the proverbial town bull, and alarmed me for the safety of their eardrums. I wondered if their mother was aware of their having eardrums. She grabbed the meat, and wiping it on her greasy apron, carried it around in her hand until she found a plate for it, and by that time the children had collected the other things. A cup was broken, and another, also a poley, was put in its stead.

===Chinese===
她把一块皱巴巴、脏得叫人作呕的桌布，歪三角地往积尘的桌子上一撂，又啪啪地摆上一对脏刀叉、两只有裂纹的盘子、两只无柄杯和几只磕了边的茶托。接着上来一盘腌肉，叫硝石渍得通红，另一盘是又黑又干又泡囊的面包。随后她进厨房沏茶去了；她不在的当儿，两个小男孩打起架来。一个揪住桌布，哗啦一声，满桌的排场全翻了——肉盘打碎了，肉躺在积尘的地上；猫和鸡向来时刻提防着这类事变，自然把这机会用了个十足。麦斯沃特太太端着茶回来，一路上一路淌。她给那两个男孩一人头上一个巴掌，打得他们像谚语里那头镇上的公牛般吼叫着四散，倒叫我为他们耳鼓的安危捏一把汗。我纳闷他们的母亲是否晓得他们长着耳鼓。她抓起那块肉，在油腻的围裙上抹了抹，拿在手里擎了一路，直到寻出一只盘子来盛它；到那时，孩子们已把其余的东西拾掇起来了。摔碎了一只杯子，另换上一只——也是只无柄的。

===Original===
Mr. M’Swat now appeared, and after taking a nip out of a rum bottle which he produced from a cupboard in the corner, he invited me to sit up to dinner.

===Chinese===
麦斯沃特先生这时露了面，先从墙角一只食柜里摸出一瓶朗姆酒抿了一口，然后请我入席用饭。

===Original===
There was no milk. M’Swat went in entirely for sheep, keeping only a few cows for domestic purposes: these, on account of the drought, had been dry for some months. Mrs. M’Swat apologized for the lack of sugar, stating she was quite out of it and had forgotten to send for a fresh supply.

===Chinese===
没有牛奶。麦斯沃特一心全扑在羊身上，只养几头奶牛供家用：因为天旱，这几头牛已经好几个月是干的了。麦斯沃特太太为缺糖赔不是，说糖全用尽了，忘了打发人去添购。

===Original===
“You damned fool, to miss such a chance wen I was goin’ to town with the wagonette! I mightn’t be groin’ in again for munce [months]. But sugar don’t count much. Them as can’t do without a useless luxury like that for a spell will never make much of a show at gettin’ on in the wu-r-r-r-ld,” concluded Mr. M’Swat, sententiously.

===Chinese===
“你这个该死的蠢货，我赶着车进城那阵儿，你倒把这么个机会放过了！兴许好几个月里我进不着一趟城。不过糖顶不了什么。连这么件没用的奢侈品都缺不得一阵子的人，在这个世——界——上头，永远混不出个模样。”麦斯沃特先生煞有介事地作了结。

===Original===
The children sat in a row and, with mouths open and interest in their big wondering eyes, gazed at me unwinkingly till I felt I must rush away somewhere and shriek to relieve the feeling of overstrained hysteria which was overcoming me. I contained myself sufficiently, however, to ask if this was all the family.

===Chinese===
孩子们坐成一排，嘴巴张着，一双双瞪圆的眼睛里满是好奇，一眨也不眨地盯着我，盯得我觉着自己非得跑到什么地方去尖叫一声不可，好舒散那正把我压倒的、绷得要发狂的情绪。不过我还是把自己按捺住了，只问这一家子人是否全在此地。

===Original===
“All but Peter. Where’s Peter, Mary Ann?”

===Chinese===
“就差彼得。彼得哪儿去了，玛丽·安（Mary Ann）？”

===Original===
“He went to the Red Hill to look after some sheep, and won’t be back till dark.”

===Chinese===
“他上红山（Red Hill）照看羊去了，天黑才回得来。”

===Original===
“Peter’s growed up,” remarked one little boy, with evident pride in this member of the family.

===Chinese===
“彼得长大成人了。”一个小男孩说，对家里这位成员显见得十分自豪。

===Original===
“Yes; Peter’s twenty-one, and hes a mustatche and shaves,” said the eldest girl, in a manner indicating that she expected me to be struck dumb with surprise.

===Chinese===
“可不；彼得二十一了，蓄着小胡子，还刮脸呢。”大姑娘说，那副口气，只等着我惊得说不出话来。

===Original===
“She’ll be surprised wen she sees Peter,” said a little girl in an audible whisper.

===Chinese===
“她见了彼得，准得吓一大跳。”一个小女孩用旁人都听得见的嗓门悄声说。

===Original===
Mrs. M’Swat vouchsafed the information that three had died between Peter and Lizer, and this was how the absent son came to be so much older than his brothers and sisters.

===Chinese===
麦斯沃特太太惠然见告：彼得和莱泽（Lizer）中间夭折过三个，所以这位不在家的儿子才比弟弟妹妹们大了这么多。

===Original===
“So you have had twelve children?” I said.

===Chinese===
“这么说，你生过十二个孩子？”我说。

===Original===
“Yes,” she replied, laughing fatly, as though it were a joke.

===Chinese===
“是啊。”她答道，肥胖和气地笑着，倒好像这是句玩笑话。

===Original===
“The boys found a bees’ nest in a tree an’ have been robbin’ it the smornin’,” continued Mrs. M’Swat.

===Chinese===
“男孩子们在棵树上寻着个蜂窝，一早起就掏蜜来着。”麦斯沃特太太接着说。

===Original===
“Yes; we have ample exemplification of that,” I responded. It was honey here and honey there and honey everywhere. It was one of the many varieties of dirt on the horrible foul-smelling tablecloth. It was on the floor, the door, the chairs, the children’s heads, and the cups. Mrs. M’Swat remarked contentedly that it always took a couple of days to wear “off of” things.

===Chinese===
“可不是；这事的实证有的是。”我应道。这儿是蜜，那儿是蜜，到处都是蜜。它是那块可怕恶臭的桌布上许多种污渍里的一种。地板上有，门上有，椅子上有，孩子们的头上有，杯子上也有。麦斯沃特太太心满意足地说，这些东西上的蜜，总要两三天才“蹭落”得掉。

===Original===
After “dinner” I asked for a bottle of ink and some paper, and scrawled a few lines to grannie and my mother, merely reporting my safe arrival at my destination. I determined to take time to collect my thoughts before petitioning for release from Barney’s Gap.

===Chinese===
“午饭”过后，我讨了一瓶墨水、几张纸，潦潦草草给外婆和母亲各写了几行，只报告我平安抵达了目的地。我打定主意，先缓一缓、理清思绪，再去恳求把我从巴尼山口开释出去。

===Original===
I requested my mistress to show me where I was to sleep, and she conducted me to a fairly respectable little bedroom, of which I was to be sole occupant, unless I felt lonely and would like Rose Jane to sleep with me. I looked at pretty, soft-eyed, dirty little Rose Jane, and assured her kindhearted mother I would not be the least lonely, as the sickening despairing loneliness which filled my heart was not of a nature to be cured by having as a bedmate a frowzy wild child.

===Chinese===
我请女主人指给我睡处，她领我到一间还算像样的小卧室，说我可以独占——除非我嫌孤单，愿意让罗丝·简（Rose Jane）来陪我睡。我瞧了瞧那漂亮、眼睛柔柔、脏乎乎的小罗丝·简，向她那位好心的母亲担保，我一点儿也不会孤单——因为充塞我心中的那种令人作呕、叫人绝望的孤寂，可不是拉一个蓬头垢面的野孩子来同床共枕治得好的。

===Original===
Upon being left alone I barred my door and threw myself on the bed to cry﻿—weep wild hot tears that scalded my cheeks, and sobs that shook my whole frame and gave me a violent pain in the head.

===Chinese===
剩我一个人时，我闩上门，扑倒在床上哭——淌着滚烫的泪水灼我的双颊，抽噎得我浑身乱颤，头也剧痛起来。

===Original===
Oh, how coarse and grating were the sounds to be heard around me! Lack, nay, not lack, but utter freedom from the first instincts of cultivation, was to be heard even in the great heavy footfalls and the rasping sharp voices which fell on my ears. So different had I been listening in a room at Caddagat to my grannie’s brisk pleasant voice, or to my aunt Helen’s low refined accents; and I am such a one to see and feel these differences.

===Chinese===
啊，我四下里听见的声响，何其粗粝刺耳！欠缺——不，哪里是欠缺，分明是全然没有一丝教化的初心，单是落到我耳朵里的那沉沉的重脚步声、刮耳尖利的说话声里，便听得出来。从前在卡达加特的房间里，我听的是外婆那爽利悦耳的嗓音，或是海伦姨妈低柔文雅的声口，何啻天壤；而我偏偏又是个把这类差异看在眼里、感在心头的人。

===Original===
However, I pulled together in a little while, and called myself a fool for crying. I would write to grannie and mother explaining matters, and I felt sure they would heed me, as they had no idea what the place was like. I would have only a little while to wait patiently, then I would be among all the pleasures of Caddagat again; and how I would revel in them, more than ever, after a taste of a place like this, for it was worse than I had imagined it could be, even in the nightmares which had haunted me concerning it before leaving Caddagat.

===Chinese===
不过，不多时我便抖擞起来，骂自己哭得傻气。我要写信给外婆和母亲，把情由说明白；我笃定她们会理会我，因为她们全然想不到这地方是什么光景。我只需耐心等上短短一阵，便又能置身卡达加特的种种乐事之中了；到那时我可要纵情受用，比从前更甚——尝过这么一个地方的滋味之后——因为这地方比我所能想象的还要糟，比离开卡达加特之前纠缠过我的那些噩梦里的它还要糟。

===Original===
The house was of slabs, unlimed, and with very low iron roof, and having no sign of a tree near it, the heat was unendurable. It was reflected from the rocks on either side, and concentrated in this spot like an oven, being 122 degrees in the veranda now. I wondered why M’Swat had built in such a hole, but it appears it was the nearness of the point to water which recommended it to his judgment.

===Chinese===
房子是板皮搭的，没刷石灰水，铁皮屋顶压得极低，近处连一棵树的影儿也没有，那热真叫人没法忍受。热气从两侧的岩石上反照下来，全聚在这一处，像座烤炉，此刻游廊上已是 122 度。我纳闷麦斯沃特干吗把房子盖在这么个坑里，可看来是这地方离水近这一条，入了他的法眼。

===Original===
With the comforting idea that I would not have long to bear this, I bathed my eyes, and walked away from the house to try and find a cooler spot. The children saw me depart but not return, to judge from a discussion of myself which I heard in the dining-room, which adjoined my bedchamber.

===Chinese===
抱着这苦头受不了多久的宽慰念头，我洗了洗眼睛，离了宅子信步走去，想寻一处凉快些的所在。孩子们瞧见我出去，却没见我回来——这是我从一场关于我本人的谈论里推想出来的，那谈论就发生在我卧室隔壁的饭厅里。

===Original===
Peter came home, and the children clustered around to tell the news.

===Chinese===
彼得回了家，孩子们围拢上去报告新闻。

===Original===
“Did she come?”

===Chinese===
“她来了没有？”

===Original===
“Yes.”

===Chinese===
“来了。”

===Original===
“Wot’s she like?”

===Chinese===
“她长啥样儿？”

===Original===
“Oh, a rale little bit of a thing, not as big as Lizer!”

===Chinese===
“哦，一小不点儿人儿，个头还没莱泽大哩！”

===Original===
“And, Peter, she hes teeny little hands, as wite as snow, like that woman in the picter ma got off of the tea.”

===Chinese===
“还有呢彼得，她那双手小小的，白得跟雪似的，就跟妈从茶叶里得来的画片上那个女人一个样。”

===Original===
“Yes, Peter,” chimed in another voice; “and her feet are that little that she don’t make no nise wen she walks.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，彼得，”另一个声音搭腔；“她那脚也小得很，走起道儿一点声儿都没有。”

===Original===
“It ain’t only becos her feet are little, but cos she’s got them beautiful shoes like wot’s in picters,” said another.

===Chinese===
“不光是她脚小，”又一个说，“还因为她穿着画片里那种漂亮鞋哩。”

===Original===
“Her hair is tied with two great junks of ribbing, one up on her head an’ another near the bottom; better than that bit er red ribbing wot Lizer keeps in the box agin the time she might go to town some day.”

===Chinese===
“她头发上扎着两大截缎带，一截在头顶上，一截在发梢底下；比莱泽收在匣子里那点儿红缎带强多啦——那可是攒着等哪天兴许进城才用的。”

===Original===
“Yes,” said the voice of Mrs. M’Swat, “her hair is near to her knees, and a plait as thick as yer arm; and wen she writ a couple of letters in a minute, you could scarce see her hand move it was that wonderful quick; and she uses them big words wot you couldn’t understand without bein’ eddicated.”

===Chinese===
“可不，”这是麦斯沃特太太的嗓音，“她那头发快垂到膝盖了，一条辫子有你们胳膊那么粗；她一分钟能写好几封信，那手快得你简直瞧不见它动；她净使那些大字眼儿，不念过书的人根本懂不了。”

===Original===
“She has tree brooches, and a necktie better than your best one wots you keeps to go seeing Susie Duffy in,” and Lizer giggled slyly.

===Chinese===
“她有三枚胸针，还有条领带，比你那条顶好的还强——你那条，不是留着去会苏西·达菲（Susie Duffy）才舍得戴的么。”莱泽狡黠地咯咯直笑。

===Original===
“You shut up about Susie Duffy, or I’ll whack yuz up aside of the ear,” said Peter angrily.

===Chinese===
“你给我闭嘴，少提苏西·达菲，不然我一巴掌乎你耳朵上。”彼得怒气冲冲地说。

===Original===
“She ain’t like ma. She’s fat up here, and goes in like she’d break in the middle, Peter.”

===Chinese===
“她跟妈不一个样。她上头这儿是胖的，到当腰又掐进去，跟要从中间折断似的，彼得。”

===Original===
“Great scissors! she must be a flyer,” said Peter. “I’ll bet she’ll make you sit up, Jimmy.”

===Chinese===
“了不得的大剪刀！她准是个出挑的人物，”彼得说。“我打赌她准给你好看，吉米（Jimmy）。”

===Original===
“I’ll make her sit up,” retorted Jimmy, who came next to Lizer.﻿—“She thinks she’s a toff, but she’s only old Melvyn’s darter, that pa has to give money to.”

===Chinese===
“我给她好看才对，”吉米反唇相讥——他挨着莱泽排下来。“她自当自己是个阔小姐，其实不过是老梅尔文的丫头，还得咱们爸倒贴钱给他的。”

===Original===
“Peter,” said another, “her face ain’t got them freckles on like yours, and it ain’t dark like Lizer’s. It’s reel wite, and pinky round here.”

===Chinese===
“彼得，”又一个说，“她脸上没长你那号雀斑，也不像莱泽那么黑。她那张脸是真白，这儿一圈儿粉扑扑的。”

===Original===
“I bet she won’t make me knuckle down to her, no matter wot colour she is,” returned Peter, in a surly tone.

===Chinese===
“管她长什么颜色，我打赌她休想叫我服软。”彼得回嘴，一副悻悻的腔调。

===Original===
No doubt it was this idea which later in the afternoon induced him to swagger forward to shake hands with me with a flash insolent leer on his face. I took pains to be especially nice to him, treating him with deference, and making remarks upon the extreme heat of the weather with such pleasantness that he was nonplussed, and looked relieved when able to escape. I smiled to myself, and apprehended no further trouble from Peter.

===Chinese===
无疑正是这个念头，才引得他当天下午晚些时候大摇大摆踱上前来同我握手，脸上挂着一副放肆无礼的斜睨。我特意待他格外和气，对他恭恭敬敬，又那般和颜悦色地议论天气热得厉害，说得他没了辙，等到能够脱身时，一脸如释重负。我暗自好笑，料想彼得不会再寻我的麻烦。

===Original===
The table for tea was set exactly as it had been before, and was lighted by a couple of tallow candles made from bad fat, and their odour was such as my jockey travelling companion of the day before would have described as a tough smell.

===Chinese===
晚茶的桌子还照先前那样原模原样地摆着，点着两支劣脂做的牛油蜡烛；那气味，用我前一天旅途上那位骑师伙伴的话说，可真“够呛”。

===Original===
“Give us a toon on the peeany,” said Mrs. M’Swat after the meal, when the dishes had been cleared away by Lizer and Rose Jane. The tea and scraps, of which there was any amount, remained on the floor, to be picked up by the fowls in the morning.

===Chinese===
“给俺们来个曲儿，弹弹那钢琴。”饭后麦斯沃特太太说；那时碗碟已由莱泽和罗丝·简收拾了下去。茶水和残羹——多的是呢——都留在地板上，等着明儿一早鸡来收拾。

===Original===
The children lay on the old sofa and on the chairs, where they always slept at night until their parents retired, when there was an all-round bawl as they were wakened and bundled into bed, dirty as they were, and very often with their clothes on.

===Chinese===
孩子们横躺在旧沙发和椅子上，夜里他们总在那儿睡，直到父母去歇息；那时便响起一片此起彼伏的嚎哭，因为他们被叫醒、被塞进被窝——一身泥也不洗，还常常衣裳都不脱。

===Original===
I acceded to Mrs. M’Swat’s request with alacrity, thinking that while forced to remain there I would have one comfort, and would spend all my spare time at the piano. I opened the instrument, brushed a little of the dust from the keys with my pocket-handkerchief, and struck the opening chords of Kowalski’s “Marche Hongroise.”

===Chinese===
我爽爽利利地应承了麦斯沃特太太的请求，心想既然被迫暂留此地，好歹算有一桩安慰，我可以把全部闲工夫都消在这钢琴上。我掀开琴盖，用随身手帕掸去琴键上的浮尘，敲响了科瓦尔斯基《匈牙利进行曲》（Marche Hongroise）的开头几组和弦。

===Original===
I have heard of pianos sounding like a tin dish, but this was not as pleasant as a tin dish by long chalks. Every note that I struck stayed down not to rise, and when I got them up the jarring, clanging, discordant clatter they produced beggars description. There was not the slightest possibility of distinguishing any tune on the thing. Worthless to begin with, it had stood in the dust, heat, and wind so long that every sign that it had once made music had deserted it.

===Chinese===
我也听说过有钢琴响得像洋铁盘子，可这一架，比洋铁盘子差得远去了。我按下去的每个音都趴下不再起来，等我把它们一一撬起，它们发出的那种轧轧铮铮、走调刺耳的哐啷声，简直非笔墨所能形容。要在这物件上辨出任何曲调，是半分指望也没有的。它起初就是件废物，又在尘土、酷热和风里立了这么久，连它曾经出过音乐的一切形迹都荡然无存了。

===Original===
I closed it with a feeling of such keen disappointment that I had difficulty in suppressing tears.

===Chinese===
我合上琴盖，那阵失望锋利如割，我好不容易才把眼泪忍了回去。

===Original===
“Won’t it play?” inquired Mr. M’Swat.

===Chinese===
“它响不了？”麦斯沃特先生问。

===Original===
“No; the keys stay down.”

===Chinese===
“响不了；琴键趴下去不起来。”

===Original===
“Then, Rose Jane, go ye an’ pick ’em up while she tries again.”

===Chinese===
“那就这样，罗丝·简，你去，她再弹的时候，你就在旁边把琴键一个个拾起来。”

===Original===
I tried again, Rose Jane fishing up the keys as I went along. I perceived instantly that not one had the least ear for music or idea what it was; so I beat on the demented piano with both hands, and often with all fingers at once, and the bigger row I made the better they liked it.

===Chinese===
我又试了一遍，我一路弹，罗丝·简一路把琴键捞起来。我登时看穿，这一家子没有一个有半点音乐的耳朵，也不知音乐为何物；于是我双手在这架疯癫的钢琴上一通乱敲，还常常十指齐落，我闹出的动静越大，他们越是乐。

## XXIX To Life (Continued) / 第29章 活下去（续）

===Original===
Mr. M’Swat very kindly told me I need not begin my duties until Monday morning, and could rest during Saturday and Sunday. Saturday, which was sickeningly hot and sultry, and which seemed like an eternity, I spent in arranging my belongings, brushing the dust from my travelling dress, and in mending a few articles. Next morning rain started to fall, which was a great Godsend, being the first which had fallen for months, and the only rain I saw during my residence at Barney’s Gap.

===Chinese===
麦斯沃特先生和气得很，说我尽可以到星期一早晨再开始履职，星期六、星期天只管歇着。星期六那天闷热得叫人发呕，长得像一整个永恒，我便拿来收拾随身衣物，掸去旅行裙上的尘土，再缝补几件东西。第二天早晨落起雨来，真是天大的恩赐——一连好几个月，这是头一场雨，也是我住在巴尼山口期间见到的唯一一场雨。

===Original===
That was a hideous Sabbath. Without a word of remonstrance from their parents, the children entertained themselves by pushing each other into the rain, the smaller ones getting the worst of it, until their clothing was saturated with water. This made them very cold, so they sat upon the floor and yelled outrageously.

===Chinese===
那是一个丑恶的安息日。孩子们把互相往雨里推当作消遣，爹娘一句也不喝止，年纪越小的越吃亏，直到浑身的衣裳全叫水浸透。这一来他们冷得受不住，便坐在地板上没命地嚎。

===Original===
It was the custom of Peter to spend his Sundays in riding about, but today, being deterred by the rain, he slept some of the time, and made a muzzle for one of his dogs, between whiles.

===Chinese===
小彼得的规矩是逢星期天骑马四处游荡，今天叫雨拦住了，便睡上一阵，间歇里又给他的一条狗做了个口套。

===Original===
From breakfast to the midday meal I shut myself in my bedroom and wrote letters to my mother and grandmother. I did not rant, rave, or say anything which I ought not to have said to my elders. I wrote those letters very coolly and carefully, explaining things just as they were, and asked grannie to take me back to Caddagat, as I could never endure life at Barney’s Gap. I told my mother I had written thus, and asked her if she would not let grannie take me again, would she get me some other situation? What I did not care, so long as it brought emancipation from the M’Swat’s. I stamped and addressed these missives, and put them by till a chance of posting should arise.

===Chinese===
从早饭到午饭，我把自己关在卧室里，给母亲和外婆写信。我没有咆哮，没有撒泼，没有对长辈说不该说的话。这几封信我写得极其冷静、极其仔细，把事情原原本本讲明白，恳求外婆接我回卡达加特，因为巴尼山口的日子我一天也熬不下去。我又告诉母亲我已这样写了，问她若不肯让外婆再收留我，肯不肯另外给我寻个去处？是什么差事我全不在乎，只要能从麦斯沃特家解脱出来就好。我把这几封信贴好邮票、写上地址收在一旁，等有寄出的机会。

===Original===
Mr. M’Swat could read a little by spelling the long words and blundering over the shorter ones, and he spent the morning and all the afternoon in perusal of the local paper﻿—the only literature with which Barney’s Gap was acquainted. There was a long list of the prices of stock and farm produce in this edition, which perfectly fascinated its reader. The ecstasy of a man of fine, artistic, mental calibre, when dipping for the first time into the work of some congenial poet, would be completely wiped out in comparison to the utter soul-satisfaction of M’Swat when drinking in the items of that list.

===Chinese===
麦斯沃特先生能读一点书——长单词靠一个字母一个字母地拼，短单词反倒磕磕绊绊——他把一个上午连同整个下午，都消磨在研读本地报纸上，那是巴尼山口唯一结识的文学。这一期上登着一长列牲口与农产品的行情，把它的读者迷得神魂颠倒。一位心智细腻、富于艺术气质的人，头一回捧读某位性之所近的诗人的作品时那份心醉神迷，比起麦斯沃特痛饮那行情单上一条条条目时的彻底的灵魂满足来，也要被碾得干干净净。

===Original===
“By damn, pigs was up last Toosday! Thames the things to make prawfit on,” he would excitedly exclaim; or﻿—“Wheat’s rose a shillun a bushel! By dad, I must double my crops this year.” When he had plodded to the end, he started at the beginning again.

===Chinese===
“嘿他妈的，上个礼拜二猪价涨啦！这才四有赚头滴东西，”他会激动万分地嚷嚷；要不就是——“麦子一蒲式耳涨乐一先令！老天爷，今年俺非把庄稼翻上一倍不可。”他吭哧吭哧读到末尾，又从头读起。

===Original===
His wife sat the whole afternoon in the one place, saying and doing nothing. I looked for something to read, but the only books in the house were a Bible, which was never opened, and a diary kept most religiously by M’Swat. I got permission to read this, and opening it, saw:

===Chinese===
他老婆一整个下午坐在老地方一动不动，一言不发，一事不做。我想找点东西读读，可这户人家仅有的书籍，是一部从来没人翻开过的《圣经》，和一本麦斯沃特顶虔诚地记着的日记。我得了许可翻开来，头一眼便看见：

===Original===
> “September
> “1st. Fine. Wint to boggie creak for a cow. 2nd. Fine. Got the chestnut mair shode. 3rd. Fine. On the jury. 4th. Fine. Tail the lams 60 yeos 52 wethers. 5th. Cloudy. Wint to Duffys. 6th. Fine. Dave Duffy called. 7th. Fine. Roped the red filly. 8th. Showery. Sold the gray mair’s fole. 9th. Fine. Wint to the Red hill after a horse. 10th. Fine, Found tree sheap ded in sqre padick.”

===Chinese===
> “九月
> “1日。晴。去波吉溪牵牛。2日。晴。栗色母马上乐蹄掌。3日。晴。去当陪审。4日。晴。给羊糕断尾，60 只母洋 52 只羯洋。5日。阴。去达菲家。6日。晴。戴夫·达菲来坐。7日。晴。套住红母驹。8日。阵雨。卖乐灰母马滴驹子。9日。晴。去红山寻马。10日。晴，方快围厂里发现三之死洋。”

===Original===
I closed the book and put it up with a sigh. The little record was a perfect picture of the dull narrow life of its writer. Week after week that diary went on the same﻿—drearily monotonous account of a drearily monotonous existence. I felt I would go mad if forced to live such a life for long.

===Chinese===
我合上本子，叹口气把它放了回去。这份小小的记录，把它主人的沉闷狭隘的生活描画得纤毫毕现。那日记一星期又一星期全是老样子——对一种阴郁单调的生存所作的阴郁单调的流水账。我觉得，若逼着我长久过这样的日子，我会发疯的。

===Original===
“Pa has lots of diaries. Would I like to read them?”

===Chinese===
“爸的日记多着呢。要不要拿来给你看看？”

===Original===
They were brought and put before me. I inquired of Mr. M’Swat which was the liveliest time of the year, and being told it was shearing and threshing, I opened one first in November:

===Chinese===
日记一本本抱来，堆在我面前。我问麦斯沃特先生一年里哪段时光最热闹，听他说是剪毛和打场的时节，便先翻开一本十一月的：

===Original===
> “November 1896
> “1st. Fine. Started to muster sheap. 2nd. Fine. Counten sheap very dusty 20 short. 3rd. Fine. Started shering. Joe Harris cut his hand bad and wint hoam. 4th. Showery. Shering stoped on account of rane.”

===Chinese===
> “1896 年 11 月
> “1日。晴。动手清点洋群。2日。晴。数洋，灰特大，短乐 20 只。3日。晴。动手剪毛。乔·哈里斯割破乐手，回家去乐。4日。阵雨。剪毛活儿因雨停乐。”

===Original===
Then I skipped to December:

===Chinese===
接着我跳到十二月：

===Original===
> “December 1896
> “1st. Fine and hot. Stripped the weet 60 bages. 2nd. Fine. Killed a snake very hot day. 3rd. Fine. Very hot alle had a boagy in the river. 4th. Fine. Got returns of woll 7½ fleece 5¼ bellies. 5th. Fine. Awful hot got a serkeler from Tatersal by the poast. 6th. Fine. Saw Joe Harris at Duffys.”

===Chinese===
> “1896 年 12 月
> “1日。晴热。收小卖 60 代。2日。晴。打死一条蛇，热得很。3日。晴。热极，大伙在河里跑了个澡。4日。晴。得乐羊毛款：7½套毛、5¼腹毛。5日。晴。热得要命，打邮局来乐一张塔特索尔滴传单。6日。晴。在达菲家见着乔·哈里斯。”

===Original===
There was no entertainment to be had from the diaries, so I attempted a conversation with Mrs. M’Swat.

===Chinese===
这些日记里榨不出半点乐趣，我便试着同麦斯沃特太太攀谈。

===Original===
“A penny for your thoughts.”

===Chinese===
“一个便士，卖不卖你的心事？”

===Original===
“I wuz jist watchin’ the rain and thinkin’ it would put a couple a bob a head more on sheep if it keeps on.”

===Chinese===
“俺正瞅着雨呢，寻思这雨要四老这么下，每只羊兴许能多值俩先令。”

===Original===
What was I to do to pass the day? I was ever very restless, even in the midst of full occupation. Uncle Jay-Jay used to accuse me of being in six places at once, and of being incapable of sitting still for five minutes consecutively; so it was simply endurance to live that long, long day﻿—nothing to read, no piano on which to play hymns, too wet to walk, none with whom to converse, no possibility of sleeping, as in an endeavour to kill a little of the time I had gone to bed early and got up late. There was nothing but to sit still, tormented by maddening regret. I pictured what would be transpiring at Caddagat now; what we had done this time last week, and so on, till the thing became an agony to me.

===Chinese===
这一天可怎么挨过去？我一向坐不定，哪怕手头的事情满满当当。杰杰舅舅从前老数落我，说我一个人能同时待在六个地方，连安安静静连坐五分钟都做不到；于是那漫漫长长的一天，纯然成了一场苦熬——没有书读，没有钢琴弹圣歌，天湿地滑出不了门，没有一个可以说话的人，觉也没得睡——为消磨掉一点时间，我头天早早上了床，这天又起得极迟。除了干坐着，任凭悔恨疯狂地撕咬，再没有别的法子。我描画着此刻卡达加特正在发生些什么；上星期的这个时候我们做了些什么，如此等等，直到这件事变成对我的一场酷刑。

===Original===
Among my duties before school I was to set the table, make all the beds, dust and sweep, and “do” the girls’ hair. After school I had to mend clothes, sew, set the table again, take a turn at nursing the baby, and on washing-day iron. This sounds a lot, but in reality was nothing, and did not half occupy my time. Setting the table was a mere sinecure, as there was nothing much to put on it; and the only ironing was a few articles outside my own, as Mr. M’Swat and Peter did not wear white shirts, and patronised paper collars. Mrs. M’Swat did the washing and a little scrubbing, also boiled the beef and baked the bread, which formed our unvaried menu week in and week out. Most peasant mothers with a family of nine have no time for idleness, but Mrs. M’Swat managed things so that she spent most of the day rolling on her frowsy bed playing with her dirty infant, which was as fat and good-tempered as herself.

===Chinese===
上课之前，我的职分有摆饭桌、铺全家人的被褥、掸尘扫地，再给女孩子们“梳”头。放学后我得缝补衣裳、做针线、再摆一回饭桌、轮着抱一抱娃娃，洗衣日还要熨衣。这些活计听着不少，其实算不得什么，连我一半的时光都占不满。摆饭桌纯属闲差，桌上本没有多少东西可摆；熨衣活儿除我自己那几件之外也寥寥无几，因为麦斯沃特先生和小彼得不穿白衬衫，惯用的是纸领子。洗衣服和一点擦擦抹抹归麦斯沃特太太，煮牛肉、烤面包也归她——那便是一星期又一星期从不更改的菜单。寻常庄户人家的母亲拉扯着九个儿女，多半没有偷闲的工夫，麦斯沃特太太却把事情安排得妥妥帖帖，一天的大好时光都滚在她那张油腻腻的床上，逗她那个脏兮兮的娃娃——娃娃同她本人一样，胖乎乎、好脾气。

===Original===
On Monday morning I marshalled my five scholars (Lizer, aged fourteen; Jimmy, twelve; Tommy, Sarah, and Rose Jane, younger) in a little back skillion, which was set apart as a schoolroom and store for flour and rock-salt. Like all the house, it was built of slabs, which, erected while green, and on account of the heat, had shrunk until many of the cracks were sufficiently wide to insert one’s arm. On Monday﻿—after the rain﻿—the wind, which disturbed us through them, was piercingly cold, but as the week advanced summer and drought regained their pitiless sway, and we were often sunburnt by the rough gusts which filled the room with such clouds of dust and grit that we were forced to cover our heads until it passed.

===Chinese===
星期一早晨，我把五名学生（莱泽，十四岁；吉米，十二岁；汤米（Tommy）、萨拉（Sarah）和罗丝·简更小些）整队带进屋后一间小披屋——那是划出来兼作教室和面粉、岩盐储藏间的屋子。同整座房子一样，它用生板皮搭成；板皮上墙时还带着青气，又经不得暑热，缩得许多裂缝宽得能塞进一条胳膊。星期一——雨后——打缝里钻进来搅扰我们的风冷得刺骨；可随着这一个星期过去，夏天和旱灾重新夺回了它们无情的统治，阵阵狂风刮得我们皮肤生疼，卷进屋来的尘土沙砾腾起满室烟尘，我们只得蒙住脑袋，等它刮过去。

===Original===
A policeman came on Tuesday to take some returns, and to him I entrusted the posting of my letters, and then eagerly waited for the reply which was to give me glorious release.

===Chinese===
星期二来了个警察，收几份统计表，我便把那几封信托他代寄，随后便眼巴巴地盼着那将赐我光荣解脱的回音。

===Original===
The nearest post-office was eight miles distant, and thither Jimmy was dispatched on horseback twice a week. With trembling expectancy every mail-day I watched for the boy’s return down the tortuous track to the house, but it was always, “No letters for the school-missus.”

===Chinese===
最近的邮局在八英里外，每星期打发吉米骑马跑两趟。每个邮日，我都怀着颤巍巍的盼望，望着那孩子顺着通到家门口的九曲十八弯的小道折返回来，可回回都是一句：“女先生没有信。”

===Original===
A week, a fortnight, dragged away. Oh, the slow horror of those never-ending days! At the end of three weeks Mr. M’Swat went to the post unknown to me, and surprised me with a couple of letters. They bore the handwriting of my mother and grandmother﻿—what I had been wildly waiting for﻿—and now that they had come at last I had not the nerve to open them while anyone was observing me. All day I carried them in my bosom till my work was done, when I shut myself in my room and tore the envelopes open to read first my grannie’s letter, which contained two:

===Chinese===
一星期，两星期，磨磨蹭蹭地挨了过去。唉，那些没有尽头的日子，慢慢煎熬的恐怖！到第三个星期末尾，麦斯沃特先生趁我不知道去了趟邮局，带回两封信给了我一个惊喜。信上是母亲和外婆的笔迹——正是我望眼欲穿等着的——可如今当真到了手，当着旁人的面我竟没有勇气拆开。一整天我把它们揣在怀里，直到活计干完，才把自己关进房里，撕开信封，先读外婆的信——信里附着两封：

===Original===
> I have been a long time answering your letter on account of waiting to consult your mother. I was willing to take you back, but your mother is not agreeable, so I cannot interfere between you. I enclose your mother’s letter, so you can see how I stand in the matter. Try and do good where you are. We cannot get what we would like in this world, and must bow to God’s will. He will always, etc.

===Chinese===
> 你上封信我迟迟未复，只因要等你母亲的示下。我原愿意接你回来，可你母亲不肯，我便不能插手你们母女之间的事。随信附上你母亲的信，你自会明白我在这件事上的处境。且安心在你如今的地方尽力行善吧。人活在世上，难求事事如愿，唯有顺服上帝的旨意。他必永远看顾，云云。

===Original===
Mother’s Letter to Grannie

===Chinese===
母亲写给外婆的信

===Original===
> I am truly grieved that Sybylla should have written and worried you. Take no notice of her; it is only while she is unused to the place. She will soon settle down. She has always been a trial to me, and it is no use of taking notice of her complaints, which no doubt are greatly exaggerated, as she was never contented at home. I don’t know where her rebellious spirit will eventually lead her. I hope M’Swat’s will tame her; it will do her good. It is absolutely necessary that she should remain there, so do not say anything to give her other ideas etc.

===Chinese===
> 西比拉竟会写信去搅扰您，我实在痛心。您千万别理会她；她不过是还没习惯那地方，很快就安顿下来了。她一向是我的一桩磨炼，她的那些抱怨大可不必当真——十有八九言过其实，她在家时就从没有个知足的时候。我不知道她这副反骨到头来会把她带向何处。我指望麦斯沃特家能磨去她的性子；这于她有好处。她绝对必须留在那里，所以请您什么也别说，免得她又生出别的念头，云云。

===Original===
Mother’s Letter to Me

===Chinese===
母亲写给我的信

===Original===
> I wish you would not write and worry your poor old grandmother, who has been so good to you. You must try and put up with things; you cannot expect to find it like holidaying at Caddagat. Be careful not to give offence to anyone, as it would be awkward for us. What is wrong with the place? Have you too much work to do? Do you not get sufficient to eat? Are they unkind to you, or what? Why don’t you have sense and not talk of getting another place, as it is utterly impossible; and unless you remain there, how are we to pay the interest on that money? I’ve always been a good mother to you, and the least you might do in return is this, when you know how we are situated. Ask God etc.

===Chinese===
> 我但愿你别再写信去搅扰你那可怜的老外婆，她待你一向多好。你得想法子将就着过；你总不能指望那里像在卡达加特度假一般。千万仔细，别开罪了任何人，那会叫我们难堪。那地方到底有什么不好？你是活计太多了吗？是吃得不够吗？是他们亏待了你，还是怎么的？你为何不放明白些，别再提另换个地方的话——那根本办不到；再说，你若不留在那里，那笔钱的利息我们拿什么去付？我一直都是个好母亲，如今我们的处境你也知道，你至少可以做到这一点来报答我。祈求上帝，云云。

===Original===
Full of contempt and hatred for my mother, I tore her letters into tiny pieces and hurled them out the window. Oh, the hard want of sympathy they voiced! She had forced me to this place: it would have been different had I wanted to come of my own accord, and then sung out for a removal immediately; but no, against my earnest pleadings she had forced me here, and now would not heed my cry. And to whom in all the world can we turn when our mother spurns our prayer?

===Chinese===
满心对母亲的轻蔑与憎恨，我把她的信撕成碎片，抡出窗外。唉，字里行间那份冷硬的、毫无体恤！是她逼我到这地方来的：倘若是我自己情愿来，来了又立刻喊受不了，那另当别论；可是不，她不顾我苦苦哀求，硬把我塞到这里，如今又不肯理会我的哭喊。当母亲的都践踏我们的祈求了，普天之下，我们还能投奔谁去？

===Original===
There never was any sympathy between my mother and myself. We are too unlike. She is intensely matter-of-fact and practical, possessed of no ambitions or aspirations not capable of being turned into cash value. She is very ladylike, and though containing no spice of either poet or musician, can take a part in conversation on such subjects, and play the piano correctly, because in her young days she was thus cultivated; but had she been born a peasant, she would have been a peasant, with no longings unattainable in that sphere. She no more understood me than I understand the works of a watch. She looked upon me as a discontented, rebellious, bad child, possessed of evil spirits, which wanted trouncing out of me; and she would have felt that she was sinning had she humoured me in any way, so after cooling I did not blame her for her letters. She was doing her duty according to her lights. Again, it was this way, grannie did not come to my rescue on this occasion on account of her attitude towards my father. The Bossiers were not at enmity with him, but they were so disgusted with his insobriety that they never visited Possum Gully, and did not assist us as much as they would have done had my father’s failure been attributable to some cause more deserving of sympathy.

===Chinese===
母亲和我之间，从来不曾有过心意相通。我们太不相像了。她是个彻头彻尾讲究实际的人，凡不能折成现钱的心气与抱负，她一概没有。她极有大家闺秀的风范，虽说骨子里没有半分诗人或音乐家的气息，却也能在这样的题目里插得上话，钢琴也弹得四平八稳，因为她年少时受过这番调教；可她倘若生为农妇，也便会安分守己地做个农妇，在那个天地里不会有任何够不着的想望。她之不能懂得我，恰如我之不能懂得一只钟表的内脏。她把我看作一个怨天怨地、犯上作乱的坏孩子，着了邪祟，非把这邪祟从我身上痛打出去不可；她若顺着我的性子迁就半分，便要觉得自己是在造罪——所以待我冷静下来，也就不为那两封信怪她了。她是照她那盏灯所照见的，尽她的本分。再者，事情又是这样：外婆这一回没有来搭救我，是因为她对我父亲的态度。博西耶家同他并无仇隙，只是对他的贪杯烂醉厌恶透顶，从来不去袋熊谷走动，也不曾像原本会帮衬的那样帮衬我们——倘若我父亲的败落，是出于某种更值得同情的缘由。

===Original===
After reading my letters I wept till every atom of my body writhed with agonized emotion. I was aroused by Mrs. M’Swat hammering at my door and inquiring:

===Chinese===
读罢信，我直哭到浑身上下每一粒原子都随着痛楚的情感扭绞。麦斯沃特太太擂响我的房门，把我惊醒，她问：

===Original===
“What ails ye, child? Did ye git bad noos from home?”

===Chinese===
“咋啦，孩子？家里来坏信儿乐？”

===Original===
I recovered myself as by a miracle, and replied, no; that I was merely a little homesick, and would be out presently.

===Chinese===
我像蒙了一场奇迹似的定下神来，回答说没事，不过是有点想家，马上就出去。

===Original===
I wrote again to my mother, but as I could not truthfully say I was hungry or ill-treated, for, according to their ability, the M’Swats were very kind to me, she took no notice of my plaint, but told me that instead of complaining of monotony, it would suit me better if I cleared up the house a little.

===Chinese===
我又给母亲写了信，可我没法昧着良心说自己挨饿受虐——凭他们的能力，麦斯沃特一家待我实在很和气——于是她对我的哀告置之不理，只说与其抱怨日子单调，不如把屋子收拾得清爽些，那于我更相宜。

===Original===
Acting upon this advice, I asked Mr. M’Swat to put a paling fence round the house, as it was useless trying to keep the house respectable while the fowls and pigs ran in every time the door was opened.﻿—

===Chinese===
依着这条教导，我请麦斯沃特先生绕屋子扎一道板条栅栏，因为每逢一开门，鸡呀猪呀便一股脑涌进屋里，想撑住一份体面，纯属徒劳。——

===Original===
He was inclined to look with favour upon this proposition, but his wife sat upon it determinedly﻿—said the fowls would lose the scraps. “Would it not be possible to throw them over the fence to the fowls?” I asked; but this would cause too much waste, she considered.

===Chinese===
他本有几分乐意成全这桩提议，他老婆却斩钉截铁地一屁股坐死——说鸡就吃不上残羹剩饭了。“把剩饭隔着栅栏扔过去给鸡，岂不两全？”我问；可据她想来，那样未免太糟蹋东西。

===Original===
Next I suggested that the piano should be tuned, but they were united in their disapproval of such a fearful extravagance. “The peeany makes a good nise. What ails it?”

===Chinese===
接着我提议把钢琴调一调音，两口子却齐心一气，不赞成这等骇人听闻的铺张。“琴响得好好的，它碍着什么乐？”

===Original===
Then I suggested that the children should be kept tidier, for which I was insulted by their father. I wanted them to be dressed up like swells, and if he did that he would soon be a pauper like my father. This I found was the sentiment of the whole family regarding me. I was only the daughter of old hard-up Melvyn, consequently I had little weight with the children, which made things very hard for me as a teacher.

===Chinese===
再往后，我提议孩子们该穿得齐整些，为这个我反倒挨了他们父亲一顿奚落。原来我是想叫他们打扮得跟阔少爷似的，他真要依了我，用不了多久就得变成我父亲那样的穷光蛋。我这才发现，这便是他们全家看待我的心思。我不过是穷酸老梅尔文的女儿，因此在孩子们跟前没有多少斤两，这叫我这个当教师的实在难做。

===Original===
One day at lunch I asked my mistress if she would like the children to be instructed in table-manners. “Certainly,” her husband replied, so I commenced.

===Chinese===
一天午饭时，我问女主人愿不愿意让孩子们学学餐桌上的规矩。“当然愿意。”她丈夫答道，我便开了课。

===Original===
“Jimmy, you must never put your knife in your mouth.”

===Chinese===
“吉米，刀子千万不可以放进嘴里。”

===Original===
“Pa does at any rate,” replied Jimmy.

===Chinese===
“反正爸就常放。”吉米答道。

===Original===
“Yes,” said pa; “and I’m a richer man today than them as didn’t do it.”

===Chinese===
“可不，”爸爸说；“俺今天可比那些不这么干的人阔多乐。”

===Original===
“Liza, do not put a whole slice of bread to your mouth like that, and cram so. Cut it into small pieces.”

===Chinese===
“莱泽，面包不要整片整片这样往嘴上送，也不要这样死塞。要切成小块。”

===Original===
“Ma doesn’t,” returned Liza.

===Chinese===
“妈就不切。”莱泽回嘴。

===Original===
“Ye’ll have yer work cut out with ’em,” laughed Mrs. M’Swat, who did not know how to correct her family herself, and was too ignorant to uphold my authority.

===Chinese===
“这帮小祖宗，够你忙活滴。”麦斯沃特太太笑着说。她自己不晓得怎么管教家里人，又糊涂得不懂该护着我的威信。

===Original===
That was my only attempt at teaching manners there. In the face of such odds it was a bootless task, and as there were not enough knives and forks to go round, I could not inculcate the correct method of handling those implements.

===Chinese===
那是我在这家教授礼貌的唯一一次尝试。面对这样的阵仗，这本是徒劳无功的事，何况刀叉又不够轮流分派，连握刀叉的正确手法我也无从传授。

===Original===
Mrs. M’Swat had but one boiler in which to do all her cooking, and one small tub for the washing, and there was seldom anything to eat but bread and beef; and this was not because they were poor, but because they did not know, or want to know, any better.

===Chinese===
麦斯沃特太太煮饭烧菜统共只有一口大锅，洗衣裳只有一只小木盆，吃的东西难得有面包牛肉之外的物色；这倒不是因为他们穷，而是因为他们不晓得——也压根不想要晓得——更好的过法。

===Original===
Their idea of religion, pleasure, manners, breeding, respectability, love, and everything of that ilk, was the possession of money, and their one idea of accumulating wealth was by hard sordid dragging and grinding.

===Chinese===
在他们的观念里，宗教也好，乐趣也好，规矩、教养、体面、爱情，以及诸如此类的一切，无非就是手里有钱；而他们积攒钱财的唯一法门，便是又狠又脏地死磨硬抠。

===Original===
A man who rises from indigence to opulence by business capabilities must have brains worthy of admiration, but the man who makes a fortune as M’Swat of Barney’s Gap was making his must be dirt mean, grasping, narrow-minded, and soulless﻿—to me the most uncongenial of my fellows.

===Chinese===
一个人凭经商的本事从赤贫爬到豪富，那副头脑必定值得钦佩；可是像巴尼山口的麦斯沃特这样攒家当的人，必定卑污下作、贪婪攫取、心胸狭隘、全无灵魂——在我看来，是我的同类中最气味不投的一种人。

===Original===
I wrote once more to my mother, to receive the same reply. One hope remained. I would write to aunt Helen. She understood me somewhat, and would know how I felt.

===Chinese===
我又一次写信给母亲，得到的还是同样的回音。只剩下一线指望了。我要写信求海伦姨妈。她多少懂得我一些，会明白我此刻的滋味。

===Original===
Acting on this inspiration, I requested her to plead for me. Her answer came as a slap in the face, as I had always imagined her above the common cant of ordinary religionists. She stated that life was full of trials. I must try and bear this little cross patiently, and at the end of a year they might have me back at Caddagat. A year! A year at Barney’s Gap! The possibility of such a thing made me frantic. I picked up my pen and bitterly reproached my aunt in a letter to which she did not deign to reply; and from that day to this she has rigidly ignored me﻿—never so much as sending me the most commonplace message, or casually using my name in her letters to my mother.

===Chinese===
依着这个念头，我恳求她替我说情。她的回信却像一记耳光，因为我一向把她想得高出寻常教徒那套滥调。她说，人生满是磨难，我必须试着耐心背起这小小的十字架，熬满一年，他们或许会接我回卡达加特。一年！在巴尼山口熬一年！单是这件事的可能性就叫我发起狂来。我提起笔，写了一封信，把姨妈狠狠责备了一顿；她不屑于回信，而且从那一天直到今天，她把我冷冷地撇在一边——连最寻常的一句口信也不曾捎给我，连在写给我母亲的信里顺口提我一笔也从不曾有过。

===Original===
Aunt Helen, is there such a thing as firm friendship when even yours﻿—best of women﻿—quibbled and went under at the hysterical wail from the overburdened heart of a child?

===Chinese===
海伦姨妈，连你的友谊——女人当中最好的女人——都会在一个孩子那不堪重负的心发出的歇斯底里的哀号面前支吾其词、败下阵来，这世上还有坚贞的友谊这回事吗？

===Original===
My predecessor, previous to her debut at Barney’s Gap, had spent some time in a lunatic asylum, and being a curious character, allowed the children to do as they pleased, consequently they knew not what it meant to be ruled, and were very bold. They attempted no insubordination while their father was about the house, but when he was absent they gave me a dog’s life, their mother sometimes smiling on their pranks, often lazily heedless of them, but never administering any form of correction.

===Chinese===
我的前任在巴尼山口登场之前，曾在疯人院里住过一阵，又是个古怪性子，任凭孩子们爱怎么便怎么，于是他们全不晓得“受人管教”是什么滋味，胆大包天。他们的父亲在屋里时，他们不敢公然作乱；他一出门，就把我作践得猪狗不如——他们的母亲有时瞧着他们的胡闹发笑，多半时候懒得理会，却从不行任何方式的惩戒。

===Original===
If I walked away from the house to get rid of them, they would follow and hoot at me; and when I reproved them they informed me they were not going “to knuckle under to old Melvyn’s darter, the damnedest fool in the world, who’s lost all his prawperty, and has to borry money off of pa.”

===Chinese===
我若走开躲他们，他们便跟在后头冲我怪叫；我一喝斥，他们便向我宣告，说他们才不打算“给老梅尔文滴丫头低头——那四天底下头一号大傻瓜，破家当赔了个精光，还得倒过来跟我爸借钱”。

===Original===
Did I shut myself in my room, they shoved sticks in the cracks and made grimaces at me. I knew the fallacy of appealing to their father, as they and their mother would tell falsehoods, and my word would not be taken in contradiction of theirs. I had experience of this, as the postmistress had complained of Jimmy, to be insulted by his father, who could see no imperfection in his children.

===Chinese===
我若把自己关进房里，他们便把棍子从缝里捅进来，冲我挤眉弄眼。我明白向他们父亲申诉是靠不住的：他们母子会撒谎，而我的话抵不得他们的话。这方面我有过来人的经验——女邮政局长告过吉米的状，反挨了他父亲一顿羞辱，在他眼里，自己的儿女没有一丝一毫不齐全的地方。

===Original===
M’Swat was much away from home at that time. The drought necessitated the removal of some of his sheep, for which he had rented a place eighty miles coastwards. There he left them under the charge of a man, but he repaired thither frequently to inspect them. Sometimes he was away from home a fortnight at a stretch. Peter would be away at work all day, and the children took advantage of my defenceless position. Jimmy was the ringleader. I could easily have managed the others had he been removed. I would have thrashed him well at the start but for the letters I constantly received from home warning me against offence to the parents, and knew that to set my foot on the children’s larrikinism would require measures that would gain their mother’s ill-will at once. But when M’Swat left home for three weeks Jim got so bold that I resolved to take decisive steps towards subjugating him. I procured a switch﻿—a very small one, as his mother had a great objection to corporal punishment﻿—and when, as usual, he commenced to cheek me during lessons, I hit him on the coat-sleeve. The blow would not have brought tears from the eyes of a toddler, but this great calf emitted a wild yope, and opening his mouth let his saliva pour on to his slate. The others set up such bloodcurdling yells in concert that I was a little disconcerted, but I determined not to give in. I delivered another tap, whereupon he squealed and roared so that he brought his mother to his rescue like a ton of bricks on stilts, a great fuss in her eyes which generally beamed with a cowful calm.

===Chinese===
那段日子麦斯沃特常常不在家。旱灾逼得他挪走一部分羊，为此他在朝海岸方向八十英里外租下了一处地方。羊群留在那儿交给一个人照管，他自己却三天两头赶去查看。有时他一离家便是整半个月。小彼得成天在外头做活，孩子们便趁着我孤立无援，好作践我。吉米是领头作乱的；若去了他，其余几个我轻易便能收服。要不是家里接连来信，告诫我千万不可开罪孩子们的爹娘，我早在一开头就把他狠狠抽一顿了；我也明白，要镇住孩子们的小无赖行径，非动立刻招来他们母亲记恨的手段不可。可是等到麦斯沃特一走三个星期，吉米愈发胆大包天，我决意采取决定性的步骤把他降服。我备下一根细条——很细的一根，因为他母亲顶反对体罚——待他照例在课上开始顶撞我，我朝他的衣袖抽了一下。那一下，就是抽在刚学步的小娃娃身上也打不出眼泪来，这半大的蠢牛犊却发出一声狂嚎，咧开嘴巴，任口水泼到石板上。其余的孩子齐声嚎出叫人毛骨悚然的尖叫，我心里微微一乱，但打定了主意决不让步。我又轻轻拍了一下，他顿时又尖又嚎，把他母亲搬来救驾——她来得像踩着高跷砸下来的一吨砖，平日里泛着母牛般安详的那双眼睛，此刻满是煞有介事的大惊小怪。

===Original===
Seizing my arm she shook me like a rat, broke my harmless little stick in pieces, threw it in my face, and patting Jimmy on the shoulder, said:

===Chinese===
她一把攥住我的胳膊，拎耗子似的把我乱抖一阵，把我那根不伤人的小细条折成几段，摔在我脸上，又拍着吉米的肩膀说：

===Original===
“Poor man! She sharn’t touch me Jimmy while I know. Sure you’ve got no sense. You’d had him dead if I hadn’t come in.”

===Chinese===
“可怜滴孩子！有俺在一天，她就休想再碰俺吉米一根毫毛。你真是没脑子。俺要四不进来，他都被她弄死乐。”

===Original===
I walked straight to my room and shut myself in, and did not teach any more that afternoon. The children rattled on my door-handle and jeered:

===Chinese===
我径直走回自己的房间，把门关严，那天下午再没有上课。孩子们哗啷哗啷地摇我的门把手，嘲骂道：

===Original===
“She thought she’d hit me, but ma settled her. Old poor Melvyn’s darter won’t try no more of her airs on us.”

===Chinese===
“她当她能打俺，叫妈收拾乐。穷酸老梅尔文滴丫头，再也别想到咱们跟前摆架子乐。”

===Original===
I pretended not to hear. What was I to do? There was no one to whom I could turn for help. M’Swat would believe the story of his family, and my mother would blame me. She would think I had been in fault because I hated the place.

===Chinese===
我装作没听见。我能怎么办呢？没有一个可以求助的人。麦斯沃特只会信他家里那套说辞，母亲只会怪我。她会认定错处在我，因为我恨那个地方。

===Original===
Mrs. M’Swat called me to tea, but I said I would not have any. I lay awake all night and got desperate. On the morrow I made up my mind to conquer or leave. I would stand no more. If in all the wide world and the whole of life this was the only use for me, then I would die﻿—take my own life if necessary.

===Chinese===
麦斯沃特太太喊我吃茶点，我说不吃了。我睁着眼躺了一整夜，横下一条心。第二天早晨，我打定主意：不降服他们，就走人。我再也受不住了。倘若茫茫世界、漫漫一生，留给我的用场只有这个，那我宁可死——必要的话，就自己了断。

===Original===
Things progressed as usual next morning. I attended to my duties and marched my scholars into the schoolroom at the accustomed hour. There was no decided insubordination during the morning, but I felt Jimmy was waiting for an opportunity to defy me. It was a fearful day, possessed by a blasting wind laden with red dust from Riverina, which filled the air like a fog. The crockery ware became so hot in the kitchen that when taking it into the dining-room we had to handle it with cloths. During the dinner-hour I slipped away unnoticed to where some quince-trees were growing and procured a sharp rod, which I secreted among the flour-bags in the schoolroom. At half-past one I brought my scholars in and ordered them to their work with a confident air. Things went without a ripple until three o’clock, when the writing lesson began. Jimmy struck his pen on the bottom of the bottle every time he replenished it with ink.

===Chinese===
第二天早晨，诸事照常。我料理完分内的活计，按惯常的钟点把学生带进教室。上午没有明目张胆的犯上，可我觉出吉米正等着机会公然抗拒我。那是个可怕的日子，一股挟着里弗赖纳红尘的灼热狂风霸住了天气，把空气填得像一场大雾。厨房里的陶器烫得厉害，往饭厅端的时候，我们得垫着布才敢上手。午饭时分，我趁人不备溜出去，走到几棵榅桲树跟前，抽了一根锋利的枝条，藏进教室的面粉袋中间。一点半，我把学生带进来，一副胸有成竹的样子，吩咐他们各归功课。风平浪静直到三点钟写字课开始。吉米每回蘸罢墨水，都把笔杆朝墨水瓶底狠狠一戳。

===Original===
“Jimmy,” I gently remonstrated, “don’t jab your pen like that﻿—it will spoil it. There is no necessity to shove it right to the bottom.”

===Chinese===
“吉米，”我温和地劝道，“别那样戳你的笔——要戳坏的。用不着一直捅到瓶底。”

===Original===
Jab, jab, went Jimmy’s pen.

===Chinese===
戳，戳，吉米的笔一戳再戳。

===Original===
“Jimmy, did you hear me speak to you?”

===Chinese===
“吉米，你听见我跟你说话了吗？”

===Original===
Jab went the pen.

===Chinese===
笔又是一戳。

===Original===
“James, I am speaking to you!”

===Chinese===
“詹姆斯，我在跟你说话！”

===Original===
Jab went the pen again.

===Chinese===
笔再度戳下去。

===Original===
“James,” I said sternly, “I give you one more chance.”

===Chinese===
“詹姆斯，”我严厉地说，“我再给你一次机会。”

===Original===
He deliberately defied me by stabbing into the ink-bottle with increased vigour. Liza giggled triumphantly, and the little ones strove to emulate her. I calmly produced my switch and brought it smartly over the shoulders of my refractory pupil in a way that sent the dust in a cloud from his dirty coat, knocked the pen from his fingers, and upset the ink.

===Chinese===
他不慌不忙，用加倍的狠劲朝墨水瓶里戳下去，存心抗命。莱泽得意地咯咯直笑，几个小的争相效仿。我不动声色地抽出我的细条，利利索索抽在那不听话的学生肩上——这一记，抽得他脏外套上的尘土腾起一团云，笔从指缝里打落，墨水也掀翻了。

===Original===
He acted as before﻿—yelled eardrum-breakingly, letting the saliva from his distended mouth run on his copybook. His brothers and sisters also started to roar, but bringing the rod down on the table, I threatened to thrash every one of them if they so much as whimpered; and they were so dumbfounded that they sat silent in terrified surprise. Jimmy continued to bawl. I hit him again.

===Chinese===
他的做派一仍旧贯——嚎出震破耳膜的尖叫，咧开的嘴巴里任口水淌到习字簿上。他的弟弟妹妹们也跟着嚎起来，我把枝条往桌上一拍，喝道谁敢哼一声就挨个儿抽；他们吓得呆了，坐在那里大气也不敢出。吉米还在嚎。我又抽了他一下。

===Original===
“Cease instantly, sir.”

===Chinese===
“立刻住口，先生。”

===Original===
Through the cracks Mrs. M’Swat could be seen approaching. Seeing her, Jimmy hollered anew. I expected her to attack me. She stood five feet nine inches, and weighed about sixteen stones; I measured five feet one inch, and turned the scale at eight stones﻿—scarcely a fair match; but my spirit was aroused, and instead of feeling afraid, I rejoiced at the encounter which was imminent, and had difficulty to refrain from shouting “Come on! I’m ready, physically and mentally, for you and a dozen others such.”

===Chinese===
透过板缝，望得见麦斯沃特太太正走过来。一见她，吉米重新嚎得山响。我料定她要扑上来打我。她身高五英尺九英寸，体重约十六英石（stone，英制重量单位）；我身高五英尺一英寸，秤上八英石——算不得一场公平的较量；可我的斗志已经点燃，非但不怕，反倒为这场一触即发的交锋满心欢喜，好不容易才忍住没有喊出声：“来吧！我身心都备好了，单等着你，再加你这样的一打也不嫌多。”

===Original===
My curious ideas regarding human equality gave me confidence. My theory is that the cripple is equal to the giant, and the idiot to the genius. As, if on account of his want of strength the cripple is subservient to the giant, the latter, on account of that strength, is compelled to give in to the cripple. So with the dolt and the man of brain, so with Mrs. M’Swat and me.

===Chinese===
我那些关于人类平等的奇想给了我胆气。我的理论是：跛者与巨人平等，白痴与天才平等。因为，倘若跛者因力气不济而屈从于巨人，那么巨人便因这份力气而被强制向跛者低头。蠢人与智者之间是如此，麦斯沃特太太与我之间也是如此。

===Original===
The fact of not only my own but my family’s dependence on M’Swat﻿—sank into oblivion. I merely recognized that she was one human being and I another. Should I have been deferential to her by reason of her age and maternity, then from the vantage which this gave her, she should have been lenient to me on account of my chit-ship and inexperience. Thus we were equal.

===Chinese===
我本人乃至全家都仰仗着麦斯沃特这一事实——沉入了遗忘。我只认得她是一个人，我是另一个人。倘若我该因她的年岁和做母亲的身分而敬她三分，那么她也该凭这一重便宜，看在我黄毛丫头、没经见过世面的分上宽让我三分。如此，我们便是平等的。

===Original===
Jimmy hollered with renewed energy to attract his mother, and I continued to rain blows across his shoulders. Mrs. M’Swat approached to within a foot of the door, and then, as though changing her mind, retraced her steps and entered the hot low-roofed kitchen. I knew I had won, and felt disappointed that the conquest had been so easy. Jimmy, seeing he was worsted, ceased his uproar, cleaned his copybook on his sleeve, and sheepishly went on with his writing.

===Chinese===
吉米拿出加倍的力气嚎哭，好把他母亲招来，我手里的枝条也雨点般不住落在他肩头。麦斯沃特太太走到离门口不到一英尺的地方，忽然像改了主意，转身折回，进了那间低顶闷热的厨房。我知道自己赢了，反倒为这场征服来得这样容易而感到扫兴。吉米见自己败下阵来，住了嚎，拿袖子擦净习字簿，讪讪地接着写他的字。

===Original===
Whether Mrs. M’Swat saw she had been in fault the day before I know not; certain it is that the children ever after that obeyed me, and I heard no more of the matter; neither, as far as I could ascertain, did the “ruction” reach the ears of M’Swat.

===Chinese===
麦斯沃特太太是否明白前一天错在她自己，我不得而知；可以断定的是，孩子们从此服我管了，这事再没有下文；就我所能查探的，那场“乱子”也始终没传进麦斯沃特的耳朵。

===Original===
“How long, how long!” was my cry, as I walked out ankle-deep in the dust to see the sun, like a ball of blood, sink behind the hills on that February evening.

===Chinese===
“还要多久，还要多久！”——二月里那个黄昏，我踩着没踝的尘土走出去，望着太阳像一团血球沉落到山背后，喊出的便是这一声。

## XXX Where Ignorance Is Bliss, ’Tis Folly to Be Wise / 第30章 无知是福，聪明反被聪明误

===Original===
When by myself, I fretted so constantly that the traces it left upon me became evident even to the dull comprehension of Mrs. M’Swat.
