===Chinese===
独自一人时，我无时无刻不在烦恼，那烦恼在我身上留下的印痕，连麦斯沃特太太那迟钝的悟性也看得分明。

===Original===
“I don’t hold with too much pleasure and disherpation, but you ain’t had overmuch of it lately. You’ve stuck at home pretty constant, and ye and Lizer can have a little fly round. It’ll do yous good,” she said.

===Chinese===
“寻欢作乐、‘散心耗神’的事儿太多，我是不赞成的，不过你近来也实在没沾过多少。你在家守得够久也够安分了，你跟莱泽（Lizer）可以出去小小地撒个欢儿。这对你们有好处，”她说。

===Original===
The dissipation, pleasure, and flying round allotted to “Lizer” and me were to visit some of the neighbours. Those, like the M’Swats, were sheep-farming selectors. They were very friendly and kind to me, and I found them superior to my employers, in that their houses were beautifully clean; but they lived the same slow life, and their soul’s existence fed on the same small ideas. I was keenly disappointed that none of them had a piano, as my hunger for music could be understood only by one with a passion for that art.

===Chinese===
派给“莱泽”和我的那点儿作乐、享受和撒欢儿，不过是去拜访几家邻居。这些人家同麦斯沃特家一样，都是养羊的选地农。他们待我非常友好和善，而且我发觉他们比我的东家强——强在屋子里拾掇得漂漂亮亮、干干净净；可他们过的同样是那种慢吞吞的日子，灵魂的生计照旧靠同样狭小的念头喂养。他们谁家都没有钢琴，这叫我大失所望——我对音乐的饥渴，唯有对这门艺术怀有一腔热恋的人才懂得。

===Original===
I borrowed something to read, but all that I could get in the way of books were a few *Young Ladies’ Journals*, which I devoured ravenously, so to speak.

===Chinese===
我借了些东西读，可弄到手的书报无非几册《青年女士杂志》（*Young Ladies’ Journals*），不妨说，我是把它们如饥似渴地狼吞虎咽了一气。

===Original===
When Lizer’s back would be turned, the girls would ask me how I managed to live at Barney’s Gap, and expressed themselves of the opinion that it was the most horrible hole in the world, and Mrs. M’Swat the dirtiest creature living, and that they would not go there for £50 a week. I made a point of never saying anything against Mrs. M’Swat; but I fumed inwardly that this life was forced upon me, when girls with no longings or aspirations beyond being the wife of a Peter M’Swat recoiled from the thought of it.

===Chinese===
每当莱泽转过背去，姑娘们便问我，我到底是怎么在巴尼山口活得下去的，还纷纷发表高见，说那是天底下最可怕的一个窟窿，说麦斯沃特太太是世上顶腌臜的活物，说一个星期给 50 镑她们也不肯去。我给自己立下规矩，从不说麦斯沃特太太一句坏话；可我心里暗暗冒火：那些除了嫁给彼得·麦斯沃特再无半点渴望、半点志气的姑娘，尚且一想起来就打怵，这样的生活却硬是压到了我头上。

===Original===
My mother insisted upon my writing to her regularly, so once a week I headed a letter “Black’s Camp,” and condemned the place, while mother as unfailingly replied that these bad times I should be thankful to God that I was fed and clothed. I knew this as well as anyone, and was aware there were plenty of girls willing to jump at my place; but they were of different temperament to me, and when one is seventeen, that kind of reasoning does not weigh very heavily.

===Chinese===
母亲坚持要我按时给她写信，于是每星期我都在信头上题作“黑人营”（Black's Camp），把那地方数落一顿；母亲也从不失约地回信说，这样艰难的年月，我有吃有穿，就该感谢上帝。这个道理我同谁一样明白，也知道多的是姑娘巴不得顶我的缺；可她们同我脾性不同，况且一个人十七岁的时候，这类道理是压不了多重的。

===Original===
My eldest brother, Horace, twin brother of my sister Gertie, took it upon himself to honour me with the following letter:

===Chinese===
我的大弟霍勒斯——妹妹格蒂的孪生哥哥——竟自作主张赏脸，给我来了下面这样一封信：

===Original===
> Why the deuce don’t you give up writing those letters to mother? We get tongue-pie on account of them, and it’s not as if they did you any good. It only makes mother more determined to leave you where you are. She says you are that conceited you think you ought to have something better, and you’re not fit for the place you have, and she’s glad it is such a place, and it will do you the world of good and take the nonsense out of you﻿—that it’s time you got a bit of sense. Sullivan’s Ginger. After she gets your letters she does jaw, and wishes she never had a child, and what a good mother she is, and what bad devils we are to her. You are a fool not to stay where you are. I wish I could get away to M’Swat or Mack Pot, and I would jump at the chance like a good un. The boss still sprees and loafs about town till someone has to go and haul him home. I’m about full of him, and I’m going to leave home before next Christmas, or my name ain’t what it is. Mother says the kiddies would starve if I leave; but Stanley is coming on like a haystack, I tell him, and he does kick up, and he ought to be able to plough next time. I ploughed when I was younger than him. I put in fourteen acres of wheat and oats this year, and I don’t think I’ll cut a wheelbarrow-load of it. I’m full of the place. I never have a single penny to my name, and it ain’t father’s drinking that’s all to blame; if he didn’t booze it wouldn’t be much better. It’s the slowest hole in the world, and I’ll chuck it and go shearing or droving. I hate this dairying, it’s too slow for a funeral: there would be more life in trapping possums out on Timlinbilly. Mother always says to have patience, and when the drought breaks and good seasons come round again things will be better, but it’s no good of trying to stuff me like that. I remember when the seasons were wet. It was no good growing anything, because everyone grew so much that there was no market, and the sheep died of foot-rot and you couldn’t give your butter away, and it is not much worse to have nothing to sell than not be able to sell a thing when you have it. And the long and short of it is that I hate dairying like blue murder. It’s as tame as a clucking hen. Fancy a cove sitting down every morning and evening pulling at a cow’s tits fit to bust himself, and then turning an old separator, and washing it up in a dish of water like a blooming girl’s work. And if you go to a picnic, just when the fun commences you have to nick off home and milk, and when you tog yourself on Sunday evening you have to undress again and lay into the milking, and then you have to change everything on you and have a bath, or your best girl would scent the cow-yard on you, and not have you within cooee of her. We won’t know what rain is when we see it; but I suppose it will come in floods and finish the little left by the drought. The grasshoppers have eaten all the fruit and even the bark off the trees, and the caterpillars made a croker of the few tomatoes we kept alive with the suds. All the cockeys round here and dad are applying to the Government to have their rents suspended for a time. We have not heard yet whether it will be granted, but if Gov. doesn’t like it, they’ll have to lump it, for none of us have a penny to bless ourselves with, let alone dub up for taxes. I’ve written you a long letter, and if you growl about the spelling and grammar I won’t write to you any more, so there, and you take my tip and don’t write to mother on that flute any more, for she won’t take a bit of notice.
> Yr loving brother,
> Horace.

===Chinese===
> 你到底干吗不放掉给妈写信这档子事？为着这些信，我们净领受妈的呲儿，再说写信对你也没半点儿好处，只会叫她越发拿定主意把你撂在原地不动。她说你自大得没边儿，觉得自个儿理该摊上更好的去处，眼下的位置又委屈了你；她还说，亏得是这么个地方，正好叫你大大受益，把你满脑袋的糊涂念头给磨掉——说你早该长点儿脑子了。苏利文家的姜汁儿！（Sullivan's Ginger）她每回接到你的信就数落个没完，悔不该生儿育女，又念叨她这个当妈的多么贤良，我们这些做儿女的对她是何等的小魔鬼。你傻就傻在不安分待着。我巴不得能脱身去麦斯沃特家或是麦克波特（Mack Pot）那号地方，有这么个机会，我保准麻利儿地一口应承。老头子照旧在镇上喝酒胡混、游手好闲，直到非得派人去把他拖回家。我实在受够他了，来年圣诞节前我必定离家，不然我就不姓我这个姓。妈说我一走，小娃娃们得饿肚子；可我跟她说，斯坦利正跟草垛似的一天一个样地往上长，他也能撒欢踢腾了，下回就该能扶犁了。我比他小的年纪就扶过犁。今年我种了十四英亩小麦和燕麦，我看收下来连独轮车都装不满一车。这地方我腻烦透了。我名下从来摸不着一个子儿，这也不全怪老爸贪杯；他就是滴酒不沾，也好不到哪儿去。这是天底下顶闷的一个窟窿，我要撂挑子走人，去剪羊毛或者赶牲口。我恨透了养奶牛这营生，闷得比出殡还胜三分：到蒂姆林比利（Timlinbilly）山里下套子捉负鼠，都比这更有活气。妈总是说要耐住性子，等旱灾一过、好年景转回来，日子就会好起来；可想拿这一套填喂我，白搭。我还记得多雨的年景。那时候种什么也没用，因为人人种得太多，东西没处卖，羊得腐蹄病死掉，白搭的黄油白送都送不出去；手里无货可卖，同有货却一件也卖不掉，也相差无几。长话短说，我就是恨养奶牛恨得要死。它闷得像只扒窝咯咯叫的老母鸡。你想啊，一个大老爷们儿每天早晚坐下拽奶牛的奶头，憋足了劲差点儿把自己挣爆，完了还得摇那台老分离机，拿一盆水把它涮洗干净，跟个大姑娘家的活计似的。你要是去野餐，正玩到兴头上，就得撒丫子溜回家挤奶；星期天晚上你刚把自己拾掇得周吴郑王，又得脱了衣裳扑到牛跟前挤奶，挤完还得浑身上下换个遍、洗个澡，不然你的意中人一闻你一身牛栏味儿，连让你沾她半步边儿都不肯。等真下了雨，咱们见了都不认得喽；不过我料着它一来准是洪水，把旱灾剩下的最后那点儿家底也一发收拾干净。蚂蚱把果子啃了个精光，连树皮都啃掉了；毛毛虫又把咱们拿肥皂水救活的那几棵西红柿祸害了个稀烂。这一带的小农们连老爸一起，都在向政府递呈子，求暂缓缴一阵子租。批不批还没个信儿；可政府要是不乐意，也只好受着，因为咱们谁兜里连一个子儿都没有，更别提凑钱缴税了。我给你写了封长信，你要是嫌我的拼写文法，往后我就再不给你写信了，就这么着；你也听我一句，别再拿那个调调给妈写信了，反正她一星半点儿也不会往心里去。
> 爱你的弟弟，
> 霍勒斯。

===Original===
So! Mother had no pity for me, and the more I pleaded with her the more determined she grew upon leaving me to suffer on, so I wrote to her no more. However, I continued to correspond with grannie, and in one of her letters she told me that Harry Beecham (that was in February) was still in Sydney settling his affairs; but when that was concluded he was going to Queensland. He had put his case in the hands of squatters he had known in his palmy days, and the first thing that turned up in managing or overseeing he was to have; but for the present he had been offered the charge of 1,600 head of bullocks from a station up near the Gulf of Carpentaria overland to Victoria. Uncle Jay-Jay was not home yet: he had extended his tour to Hong Kong, and grannie was afraid he was spending too much money, as in the face of the drought she had difficulty in making both ends meet, and feared she would be compelled to go on the banks. She grieved that I was not becoming more reconciled to my place. It was dull, no doubt, but it would do my reputation no harm, whereas, were I in a lively situation, there might be numerous temptations hard to resist. Why did I not try to look at it in that way?

===Chinese===
瞧！母亲对我没有半分怜惜，我越是恳求，她越拿定主意把我撂在那儿受罪，于是我索性不再给她写信。不过我照旧同外婆通信。她在其中一封信里告诉我，哈里·比彻姆（那是二月里的事）还在悉尼料理他的事务；等事情办结，他便去昆士兰。他已把自己的事托付给他风光年月里结识的那些大牧场主，但凡有经管牧场或监工的差事冒头，就先尽着他；至于眼下，人家给了他一桩活计：把北边卡奔塔利亚湾（Gulf of Carpentaria）左近一座牧场的 1,600 头阉牛，由陆路赶往维多利亚（Victoria）。杰杰舅舅还没回家：他把游程延长到了香港，外婆怕他花钱太过，因为顶着这场旱灾，她要把日子凑合得收支相抵已属不易，生怕到头来不得不求到银行头上去。她痛心的是，我还不曾渐渐安于自己的境地。那地方固然沉闷，可于我的名声无损；反过来，倘若我置身一处热闹去处，只怕诱惑多得不招架不住。我何不也试着这样看待它呢？

===Original===
She sent a copy of the *Australasian*, which was a great treat to me, also to the children, as they were quite ignorant of the commonest things in life, and the advent of this illustrated paper was an event to be recorded in the diary in capital letters. They clustered round me eagerly to see the pictures. In this edition there chanced to be a page devoted to the portraits of eleven Australian singers, and our eyes fell on Madame Melba, who was in the middle. As what character she was dressed I do not remember, but she looked magnificent. There was a crown upon her beautiful head, the plentiful hair was worn flowing, and the shapely bosom and arms exposed.

===Chinese===
她寄来一份《澳大拉西亚报》（*Australasian*），这于我是件大大的享受，于孩子们也是——他们连人生里最寻常的事物都一概不知，这份画报的降临，是桩值得拿大写字母记进日记里的大事。他们急切地围拢过来，贴着我看图画。这一期上恰巧有一整版刊着十一位澳大利亚歌唱家的肖像，我们的目光落在正中间的梅尔巴夫人身上。她扮的是什么角色我已不记得，只记得她看上去华彩照人。她那颗美丽的头上戴着一顶冠冕，丰密的头发披垂如流，线条姣好的胸脯与手臂俱都袒露着。

===Original===
“Who’s that?” they inquired.

===Chinese===
“这是谁？”他们问。

===Original===
“Madame Melba; did you ever hear her name?”

===Chinese===
“梅尔巴夫人；你们听说过她的名字吗？”

===Original===
“Who’s Madame Melba? What’s she do? Is she a queen?”

===Chinese===
“梅尔巴夫人是谁？她是干什么的？她是女王吗？”

===Original===
“Yes, a queen, and a great queen of song;” and being inspired with great admiration for our own Australian cantatrice, who was great among the greatest prima-donnas of the world, I began to tell them a little of her fame, and that she had been recently offered £40,000 to sing for three months in America.

===Chinese===
“是的，一位女王，一位伟大的歌坛女王；”想到我们自家的澳大利亚歌姬——她在全世界最了不起的女歌唱家里也是了不起的一位——我登时满怀仰慕，开始对他们讲一点她的声名，说人家新近出 40,000 镑请她去美洲唱三个月。

===Original===
They were incredulous. Forty thousand pounds! Ten times as much as “pa” had given for a paid-up selection he had lately bought. They told me it was no use of me trying to tell them fibs. No one would give a woman anything to sing, not even one pound. Why, Susie Duffy was the best singer on the Murrumbidgee, and she would sing for anyone who asked her, and free of charge.

===Chinese===
他们怎么也不肯信。四万镑！顶得上“爸”前些天买下那块付清了地价的选地所付价钱的十倍。他们对我说，再想拿瞎话哄他们也是白搭。谁也不会给一个女人钱叫她唱歌，一个镑也别想。可不是嘛，苏西·达菲就是马兰比吉河一带唱得最好的，谁开口她都肯唱，还分文不取。

===Original===
At this juncture Jimmy, who had been absent, came to see the show. After gazing for a few seconds he remarked what the others had failed to observe, “Why, the woman’s naked!”

===Chinese===
正当这当口，先前不在跟前的吉米（Jimmy）过来看热闹。他瞪眼端详了几秒钟，便说出了旁人谁都没瞧出来的一点：“咦，这女人光着身子哩！”

===Original===
I attempted to explain that among rich people in high society it was customary to dress like that in the evening, and that it looked very pretty.

===Chinese===
我试着解释说，在阔气的上流社会里，晚上照例是这样穿戴的，而且看上去非常好看。

===Original===
Mrs. M’Swat admonished me for showing the children low pictures.

===Chinese===
麦斯沃特太太训了我一顿，怪我给孩子们看下流的画片。

===Original===
“She must be a very bold woman,” said Jimmy; and Lizer pronounced her mad because, as she put it, “It’s a wonder she’d be half-undressed in her photo; you’d think she oughter dress herself up complete then.”

===Chinese===
“她准是个不害臊的浪荡女人，”吉米说；莱泽则断定她是个疯子，因为照莱泽的说法：“照相照得半赤着身子，真是一桩怪事；你总想着，那种时候她总该把自己穿戴得齐全齐整才是。”

===Original===
Lizer certainly acted upon this principle, as a photo of her, which had been taken by a travelling artist, bore evidence that for the occasion she had arrayed herself in two pairs of ill-fitting cuffs, Peter’s watch and chain, strings, jackets, flowers, and other gewgaws galore.

===Chinese===
莱泽确确实实是奉行这一条原则的：她有一张由走方照相师傅拍下的照片可以为证——为了那个场合，她给自己披挂上两副不合尺寸的活袖口、小彼得的怀表和表链，还有缎带、外褂、花枝，以及琳琅满目一应花哨的小零碎，足足一大身。

===Original===
“There ain’t no such person as Madame Melber; it’s only a fairytale,” said Mrs. M’Swat.

===Chinese===
“压根儿没有梅尔波夫人这么个人；那不过是编出来的童话罢了，”麦斯沃特太太说。

===Original===
“Did you ever hear of Gladstone?” I inquired.

===Chinese===
“你们听说过格莱斯顿吗？”我问道。

===Original===
“No; where is that place?”

===Chinese===
“没听说过；那地方在哪儿？”

===Original===
“Did you ever hear of Jesus Christ?”

===Chinese===
“你们听说过耶稣基督吗？”

===Original===
“Sure, yes; he’s got something to do with God, ain’t he?”

===Chinese===
“那还用说，听说过；他跟上帝有点儿什么关系，是不？”

===Original===
After that I never attempted to enlighten them regarding our celebrities.

===Chinese===
打那以后，我们那些名人大贤的事，我再不试着给他们开蒙了。

===Original===
Oh, how I envied them their ignorant contentment! They were as ducks on a duck-pond; but I was as a duck forced forever to live in a desert, ever wildly longing for water, but never reaching it outside of dreams.

===Chinese===
啊，我多么羡慕他们那无知的知足！他们好比鸭池里的鸭子；我却是一只被强行永远安置在沙漠里的鸭子，时时刻刻发狂似的渴念着水，却除了在梦里，永远也够不着它。

## XXXI Mr. M’Swat and I Have a Bust-Up / 第31章 麦斯沃特先生和我大吵一场

===Original===
Men only, and they merely on business, came to Barney’s Gap﻿—women tabooed the place. Some of them told me they would come to see me, but not Mrs. M’Swat, as she always allowed the children to be as rude to them as they pleased. With the few individuals who chanced to come M’Swat would sit down, light his pipe, and vulgarly and profusely expectorate on the floor, while they yarned and yarned for hours and hours about the price of wool, the probable breeding capacity of the male stock they kept, and of the want of grass﻿—never a word about their country’s politics or the events of the day; even the news of the “Mountain Murders” by Butler had not penetrated here. I wondered if they were acquainted with the names of their Governor and Prime Minister.

===Chinese===
来巴尼山口的只有男人，而且纯粹为着生意——女人们把此地视作禁忌。他们当中有几位对我说，往后要来看我，却不去看望麦斯沃特太太，因为孩子们爱对来客多没礼就多没礼，她从来放任不管。偶或碰上来那么几个人，麦斯沃特便坐下来点着烟斗，粗鄙而痛畅地朝地板上一口接一口地吐痰，他们则闲扯了又闲扯，一连好几个钟头，扯的无非羊毛的行情、自家所养公畜大概的配种能力、草料的短缺——关于本国的政治、时下的世事，从来不曾有过半句话；连巴特勒（Butler）犯下的“山地谋杀案”的新闻，也不曾渗透到这个地方。我不免疑惑，他们究竟晓不晓得本国总督和总理的名姓。

===Original===
It was not the poor food and the filthy way of preparing it that worried me, or that Mr. M’Swat used “damn” on an average twice in five minutes when conversing, or that the children forever nagged about my father’s poverty and tormented me in a thousand other ways﻿—it was the dead monotony that was killing me.

===Chinese===
折磨我的，不是粗劣的饭食和烹制它的龌龊手法，不是麦斯沃特先生同人交谈时平均每五分钟要骂两声“该死”，也不是孩子们没完没了地数落我父亲的穷酸、再用千百种别的法子作弄我——是那死气沉沉的单调，正在一寸一寸要我的命。

===Original===
I longed feveredly for something to happen. Agony is a tame word wherewith to express what that life meant to me. Solitary confinement to a gipsy would be something on a par.

===Chinese===
我发着烧似的巴望出点什么事。“苦痛”是个太温驯的字眼，配不上那种生活之于我的分量。把一个吉卜赛人关进单身囚牢，那滋味庶几近之。

===Original===
Every night unfailingly when at home M’Swat sat in the bosom of his family and speculated as to how much richer he was than his neighbours, what old Recce lived on, and who had the best breed of sheep and who was the smartest at counting these animals, until the sordidness of it turned me dizzy, and I would steal out under the stars to try and cool my heated spirit. This became a practice with me, and every night I would slip away out of hearing of the household to sing the songs I had heard at Caddagat, and in imagination to relive every day and hour there, till the thing became too much for me, and I was scarcely responsible for my actions. Often I knelt on the parched ground beneath the balmy summer sky to pray﻿—wild passionate prayers that were never answered.

===Chinese===
每天晚上，只要在家，麦斯沃特雷打不动地坐在他那一窝家人中间，盘算自己比左邻右舍阔多少，老里斯（old Recce）靠什么过活，谁家的羊种最好，谁点数起这些牲口来手脚最麻利——那份腌臜劲儿直熏得我头晕，我便悄悄溜出门，站到星星底下，设法冷却我滚烫的心。这成了我的定课：每夜我都溜到听不见这一家人声气的地方，唱我在卡达加特听过的歌，在想象里把那里的每一日、每一时重新活一遍，直到这事儿把我涨得受不了，连自己的举动都几乎管不住。我常常跪在焦渴的地上，在温软的夏夜天空下祷告——那些狂野而炽热的、从来不曾得到回应的祷告。

===Original===
I was under the impression that my nightly ramble was not specially noticed by anyone, but I was mistaken. Mr. M’Swat, it appears, suspected me of having a lover, but was never able to catch me red-handed.

===Chinese===
我原以为我每夜的游荡没有谁特别留意，可是我想岔了。看情形，麦斯沃特先生疑心我有了相好的，只是一直没能当场拿住我。

===Original===
The possibility of a girl going out at night to gaze at the stars and dream was as improbable a thought for him as flying is to me, and having no soul above mud, had I attempted an explanation he would have considered me mad, and dangerous to have about the place.

===Chinese===
一个女孩子夜里出门去望星星、做做梦——这念头在他看来，同“人能飞上天”在我看来一样地荒诞不经；他的灵魂没有一寸高出泥淖，我若试图解释，他只会当我疯了，而且疯得不宜留在这地方走动。

===Original===
Peter, junior, had a sweetheart, one Susie Duffy, who lived some miles on the other side of the Murrumbidgee. He was in the habit of courting her every Sunday and two or three nights during the week, and I often heard the clang of his stirrup-irons and the clink of hobble-chain when he returned late; but on one occasion I stayed out later than usual, and he passed me going home. I stood still and he did not see me, but his horse shied violently. I thought he would imagine I was a ghost, so called out:

===Chinese===
小彼得有个相好，是苏西·达菲，住在马兰比吉河对岸几英里外。他的老规矩是每个星期天，外加平日里两三个夜晚，去同她谈情；他深夜归来时，我常听见马镫的锒铛声和绊脚链的叮当声；可是有一回我在外面待得比往常更晚，他回家时从我身边经过。我站着没动，他没有看见我，他的马却猛地受惊。我怕他把我当作鬼魂，便喊出声：

===Original===
“It is I.”

===Chinese===
“是我。”

===Original===
“Well, I’ll be hanged! What are ye doin’ at this time ev night. Ain’t yuz afraid of ghosts?”

===Chinese===
“嗬，活见鬼！这么晚滴时辰你在外头干啥？你就不怕撞上鬼？”

===Original===
“Oh dear no. I had a bad headache and couldn’t sleep, so came out to try if a walk would cure it,” I explained.

===Chinese===
“哎呀不怕。我头疼得厉害，睡不着，所以出来走走，看能不能把它走散，”我解释道。

===Original===
We were a quarter of a mile or so from the house, so Peter slackened his speed that I might keep pace with him. His knowledge of etiquette did not extend as far as dismounting. There is a great difference between rudeness and ignorance. Peter was not rude; he was merely ignorant. For the same reason he let his mother feed the pigs, clean his boots, and chop wood, while he sat down and smoked and spat. It was not that he was unmanly, as that this was the only manliness he had known.

===Chinese===
我们离屋子还有四分之一英里上下，小彼得便放慢了马，好让我跟得上他。他的礼数学问，远不及“下马”这一条。无礼与无知之间大有分别。小彼得并非无礼；他只是无知。也是出于同一个缘故，他任凭他母亲喂猪、替他刷靴子、劈柴，自己却坐下来抽烟吐痰。与其说他不像个男人，不如说这就是他见识过的唯一的男子气概。

===Original===
I was alone in the schoolroom next afternoon when Mr. M’Swat sidled in, and after stuttering and hawing a little, delivered himself of:

===Chinese===
第二天下午，教室里只有我一个人，麦斯沃特先生侧着身子蹭了进来，结结巴巴、吭吭哧哧了一阵，发表出这么一通：

===Original===
“I want to tell ye that I don’t hold with a gu-r-r-r-l going out of nights for to meet young men: if ye want to do any coortin’ yuz can do it inside, if it’s a decent young man. I have no objections to yer hangin’ yer cap up to our Peter, only that ye have no prawperty﻿—in yerself I like ye well enough, but we have other views for Peter. He’s almost as good as made it sure with Susie Duffy, an’ as ole Duffy will have a bit ev prawperty I want him to git her, an’ wouldn’t like ye to spoil the fun.”

===Chinese===
“俺要告诉你，俺不赞成一个大姑——娘——家黑天半夜跑出去会年轻男人：你要谈情说爱，尽可以在屋里头谈，只要那四个体面滴年轻男人。俺不反对你打俺们彼得滴主意，只可惜你名下没有家当——就你这人本身，俺对你倒蛮喜欢，可俺们对彼得另有打算。他跟苏西·达菲差不多已经算四拿定乐，老达菲往后又有一份家当，俺要他娶她，可不愿意叫你来搅了这桩好事。”

===Original===
Peter was “tall and freckled and sandy, face of a country lout,” and, like Middleton’s rouse-about, “hadn’t any opinions, hadn’t any ideas,” but possessed sufficient instinct and common bushcraft with which, by hard slogging, to amass money. He was developing a moustache, and had a “gu-r-r-r-l;” he wore tight trousers and long spurs; he walked with a sidling swagger that was a cross between shyness and flashness, and took as much pride in his necktie as any man; he had a kind heart, honest principles, and would not hurt a fly; he worked away from morning till night, and contentedly did his duty like a bullock in the sphere in which God had placed him; he never had a bath while I knew him, and was a man according to his lights. He knew there was such a thing as the outside world, as I know there is such a thing as algebra; but it troubled him no more than algebra troubles me.

===Chinese===
小彼得“高个子、雀斑脸、一头黄毛，一副乡下粗坯的面相”，又同米德尔顿（Middleton）笔下的牧场杂工一样，“没有什么主张，没有什么见识”，却凭着足够的本能和寻常的丛林本事，靠死命苦熬去积攒钱财。他正蓄起一撇小胡子，有了一个“姑——娘——”；他穿绷紧的裤子，戴长长的马刺；走起路来带着一种侧身摇摆的大摇大摆，是腼腆与浮滑杂交出来的品种，对领巾的得意不输任何男子；他心地厚道，做人的规矩诚实，连一只苍蝇也不肯伤害；他从早到晚做活不停，像一头公牛，在上帝安放他的那个位置上安分守己地尽他的本分；从我认识他起，他不曾洗过一回澡，并且，照他那盏灯看来，他是条汉子。他知道世上有“外面的世界”这么个东西，好比我知道世上有“代数”这么个东西；可它烦扰他的程度，也并不比代数烦扰我的更多。

===Original===
This was my estimation of Peter M’Swat, junior. I respected him right enough in his place, as I trust he respected me in mine, but though fate thought fit for the present to place us in the one groove, yet our lives were unmixable commodities as oil and water, which lay apart and would never meet until taken in hand by the omnipotent leveller﻿—death.

===Chinese===
这就是我对小彼得·麦斯沃特的估量。各安其位，我尽可以敬他，正如我信他在他的位置上也敬我；可是命运虽一时觉得合宜，把我们放进了同一道辙，我们的生命却是油和水那样掺不拢的货色，各自躺着，永不相混，直到被那位万能的拉平者——死亡——接手处置。

===Original===
Marriage with Peter M’Swat!

===Chinese===
嫁给小彼得·麦斯沃特！

===Original===
Consternation and disgust held me speechless, and yet I was half inclined to laugh at the preposterousness of the thing, when Peter’s father continued:

===Chinese===
惊愕与憎恶噎得我说不出话来，可这桩事情的荒诞，又逗得我险些笑出声——正在这时，彼得的父亲接着说道：

===Original===
“I’m sorry if you’ve got smitten on Peter, but I know you’ll be sensible. Ye see I have a lot of children, and when the place is divided among ’em it won’t be much. I tell ye wot, old Duffy has a good bit of money and only two children, Susie and Mick. I could get you to meet Mick﻿—he mayn’t be so personable as our Peter,” he reflected, with evident pride in his weedy firstborn, and he got no farther, for I had been as a yeast-bottle bubbling up, and now went off bang!

===Chinese===
“你要四当真看上乐彼得，俺心里也过意不去，可俺晓得你四个明白人。你瞧，俺有一大窝孩子，这份田产分到他们头上，就剩不下多少乐。俺告诉你，老达菲有老大一笔钱，儿女就苏西和米克（Mick）两个。俺可以安排你跟米克见见面——他兴许不及俺们彼得生得齐整，”他盘算着说，对自家那个瘦长条的长子透着明明白白的得意。他说不下去了，因为我早已像一瓶酵母似的咕嘟咕嘟往上冒泡，这会儿砰地一声炸了！

===Original===
“Silence, you ignorant old creature! How dare you have the incomparable impertinence to mention my name in conjunction with that of your boor of a son. Though he were a millionaire I would think his touch contamination. You have fallen through for once if you imagine I go out at night to meet anyone﻿—I merely go away to be free for a few minutes from the suffocating atmosphere of your odious home. You must not think that because you have grasped and slaved and got a little money that it makes a gentleman of you; and never you *dare* to again mention my name in regard to matrimony with anyone about here;” and with my head high and shoulders thrown back I marched to my room, where I wept till I was weak and ill.

===Chinese===
“住口，你这个无知的老东西！你哪里来的这份天下无双的胆大包天，竟敢把我的名字同你那乡下粗坯的儿子相提并论。就算他是百万富翁，我也当他一碰便是玷污。你若以为我夜里出门是会什么人，你这回可是失算到家了——我不过是走开几分钟，好从你这可憎的家那闷死人的空气里透一口气。你可休想凭着死抠死熬攒下几个钱，就把自己熬成了一位绅士；你也永远不*敢*再把我的名字，同这一带任何人的婚事扯到一处；”说罢，我昂着头，双肩向后一挺，开步走回我的房间，在那里直哭到浑身发软、遍体不适。

===Original===
This monotonous sordid life was unhinging me, and there was no legitimate way of escape from it. I formed wild plans of running away, to do what I did not care so long as it brought a little action, anything but this torturing maddening monotony; but my love for my little brothers and sisters held me back. I could not do anything that would put me forever beyond the pale of their society.

===Chinese===
这单调龌龊的生活正在把我的神志搞垮，而又没有一条光明正道的逃脱之路。我拟定了一个个狂乱的出走计划——出去干什么我全不在乎，只要能带来一点动静，什么都行，只是不要这凌迟人、逼疯人的单调；可是对弟弟妹妹们的爱把我拽住了。我不能做任何把我永远逐出他们身边的事。

===Original===
I was so reduced in spirit that had Harold Beecham appeared then with a matrimonial scheme to be fulfilled at once, I would have quickly erased the fine lines I had drawn and accepted his proposal; but he did not come, and I was unacquainted with his whereabouts or welfare. As I remembered him, how lovable and superior he seemed in comparison with the men I met nowadays: not that he was any better than these men in their place and according to their lights, but his lights﻿—at least not his lights, for Harold Beecham was nothing of a philosopher, but the furniture of the drawing-room which they illuminated﻿—was more artistic. What a prince of gentlemanliness and winning gallantries he was in his quiet way!

===Chinese===
我的精神已委顿到这般田地：倘若哈罗德·比彻姆那时带着一桩即刻兑现的婚约方案出现，我会飞快抹掉自己先前画下的那些细线，接受他的求婚；可是他没有来，他的行踪与安否，我也一概不知。在我的记忆里，比起我如今日日遇见的这些男人，他显得多么可爱、多么高出一头：倒不是各就其位、照各自那盏灯说来，他比这些人好到哪里去，而是他那盏灯——至少说不得是他的灯，因为哈罗德·比彻姆全然算不得什么哲学家，倒该说是被那些灯照亮的那间客厅里的家具——更具艺术气。以他那种安安静静的做派，他何尝不是一位绅士风度与动人殷勤的王子！

===Original===
This information concerning him was in a letter I received from my grandmother at Easter:

===Chinese===
关于他的这些消息，装在复活节时我收到的外婆的一封信里：

===Original===
> Who should surprise us with a visit the other day but Harold Beecham. He was as thin as a whipping-post, and very sunburnt [I smiled, imagining it impossible for Harold to be any browner than of yore]. He has been near death’s door with the measles﻿—caught them in Queensland while droving, and got wet. He was so ill that he had to give up charge of that 1,600 head of cattle he was bringing. He came to say goodbye to us, as he is off to Western Australia next week to see if he can mend his fortunes there. I was afraid he was going to be like young Charters, and swear he would never come back unless he made a pile, but he says he will be back next Christmas three years for certain, if he is alive and kicking, as he says himself.
> Why he intends returning at that stipulated time I don’t know, as he never was very communicative, and is more unsociable than ever now. He is a man who never shows his feelings, but he must feel the loss of his old position deeply. He seemed surprised not to find you here, and says it was a pity to set you teaching, as it will take all the life and fun out of you, and that is the first time I ever heard him express an opinion in anyone’s business but his own. Frank Hawden sends kind regards, etc.

===Chinese===
> 前几天来看我们、叫我们又惊又喜的，不是别人，正是哈罗德·比彻姆。他瘦得像根鞭刑柱，晒得黑黢黢的［我笑了，心想哈罗德要比往日再黑上一分，断无可能］。他害麻疹差点儿敲开了鬼门关——是在昆士兰赶牲口时传染上的，又淋了雨。他病得那样重，只好把正赶的那 1,600 头牛撇下不管了。他是来向我们辞行的，下星期就要动身去西澳大利亚（Western Australia），看能不能在那里把家运修补起来。我原本担心他要学年轻的查特斯（Charters）的样，赌咒发誓挣不出一份大家业便永不回来，可他说，只要他自己还活蹦乱跳——这是他的原话——三年后的圣诞节一定回来。
> 他为什么定要挑那个日子回来，我不知道，他一向不大肯把心里的事说给人听，如今比从前愈发不声不响。他是个从不外露感情的人，可是失掉了原先的地位，他心里一定很不好受。他见你不在家，似乎很吃惊，还说让你去教书可惜了，说这会把你一身的生气和快活都磨掉——他议论别人家的事，这还是我头一回听见。弗兰克·霍登附问安好，云云。

===Original===
Teaching certainly had the effect upon me anticipated by Harold Beecham, but it was not the teaching but the place in which I taught which was doing the mischief﻿—good, my mother termed it.

===Chinese===
教书固然在我身上起了哈罗德·比彻姆所预料的那种作用，可是作孽的不是教书，而是我教书所在的这个地方——这份孽，我母亲给它的名目是“好事”。

===Original===
I was often sleepless for more than forty-eight hours at a stretch, and cried through the nights until my eyes had black rings round them, which washing failed to remove. The neighbours described me as “a sorrowful lookin’ delicate creetur’, that couldn’t larf to save her life”﻿—quite a different character to the girl who at Caddagat was continually chid for being a romp, a hoyden, a boisterous tomboy, a whirlwind, and for excessive laughter at anything and everything. I got into such a state of nervousness that I would jump at the opening of a door or an unexpected footfall.

===Chinese===
我常常一连四十八个钟头以上合不上眼，整夜整夜地哭，哭得眼圈乌黑，洗也洗不掉。邻居们形容我，说我是“一个愁眉苦脸、弱不禁风滴小东西，为着救命她也笑不出一声”——同那个姑娘判若两人：那个在卡达加特因为疯野、因为撒泼、因为是个吵吵闹闹的假小子、一阵旋风，因为不管对什么、对一切都笑得过了分，而不断挨训斥的姑娘。我落到那样一种神经质的地步：门一开，或是冷不丁一阵脚步声，都会叫我惊跳起来。

===Original===
When cooling down, after having so vigorously delivered Mr. M’Swat a piece of my mind, I felt that I owed him an apology. According to his lights (and that is the only fair way of judging our fellows) he had acted in a kind of fatherly way. I was a young girl under his charge, and he would have in a measure been responsible had I come to harm through going out in the night. He had been good-natured, too, in offering to help things along by providing an eligible, and allowing us to “spoon” under his surveillance. That I was of temperament and aspirations that made his plans loathsome to me was no fault of his﻿—only a heavy misfortune to myself. Yes; I had been in the wrong entirely.

===Chinese===
待我冷静下来，想起自己那样慷慨激昂地痛陈了麦斯沃特先生一顿，便觉得欠他一个道歉。照他那盏灯（这也正是评判我们同类的唯一公道法子）行事，他原是一片父辈的心肠。我是他照管下的年轻姑娘，倘若我夜里出门遭了什么不测，多多少少他是有干系的。他肯出力玉成，备下一个合格的人选，还许我们在他的眼皮底下卿卿我我，也算得一片好意。我的脾性与抱负使他的打算在我眼里面目可憎，这怪不得他——那只是落在我自己头上的一桩沉重的不幸。是的；从头到尾，错的都是我。

===Original===
With this idea in my head, sinking ankle-deep in the dust, and threading my way through the pigs and fowls which hung around the back door, I went in search of my master. Mrs. M’Swat was teaching Jimmy how to kill a sheep and dress it for use; while Lizer, who was nurse to the baby and spectator of the performance, was volubly and ungrammatically giving instructions in the art. Peter and some of the younger children were away felling stringybark-trees for the sustenance of the sheep. The fall of their axes and the murmur of the Murrumbidgee echoed faintly from the sunset. They would be home presently and at tea; I reflected it would be “The old yeos looks terrible skinny, but the hoggets is fat yet. By crikey! They did go into the bushes. They chawed up stems and all﻿—some as thick as a pencil.”

===Chinese===
揣着这个念头，我踩着没踝的尘土，从围拢在后门口的猪和鸡群中间穿行过去，寻我的东家。麦斯沃特太太正教吉米怎样宰一只羊、收拾停当以供食用；身兼娃娃保姆与这场热闹的看客的莱泽，在旁边滔滔不绝、不成文法地指点着这门手艺。小彼得和几个小些的孩子到外头砍条皮桉去了，给羊群添口粮。他们的斧声和马兰比吉河的水声，从落日那边隐隐约约地传回来。他们眼看就要回家吃茶点了；我料想那开场白必定是：“老母洋一个个瘦得吓人，可那帮周岁滴羊羔还肥着哩。好家伙！它们真能往树棵子里钻。连秆带芯嚼了个精光——有的秆子有铅笔那么粗。”

===Original===
This information in that parlance had been given yesterday, the day before, would be given today, tomorrow, and the next day. It was the boss item on the conversational programme until further orders.

===Chinese===
这条新闻，用那套话昨儿讲过、前儿讲过，今儿要讲，明儿、后儿还要讲。它是谈话节目单上的头条，永远占着，直到另有通知为止。

===Original===
I had a pretty good idea where to find Mr. M’Swat, as he had lately purchased a pair of stud rams, and was in the habit of admiring them for a couple of hours every evening. I went to where they usually grazed, and there, as I expected, found Mr. M’Swat, pipe in mouth, with glistening eyes, surveying his darlings.

===Chinese===
去哪里寻麦斯沃特先生，我心里相当有数：他新近购进一对种公羊，每晚照例要欣赏它们一两个钟头。我走到它们惯常吃草的地方，果然不出所料，麦斯沃特先生正在那儿，嘴里衔着烟斗，眼睛亮晶晶地端详着他的心肝宝贝。

===Original===
“Mr. M’Swat, I have come to beg your pardon.”

===Chinese===
“麦斯沃特先生，我是来请你恕罪的。”

===Original===
“That’s all right, me gu-r-r-r-l. I didn’t take no notice to anything ye might spit out in a rage.”

===Chinese===
“没滴事儿，俺滴姑——娘——。你盛头上啐出来滴那些话，俺一点儿也没往心里去。”

===Original===
“But I was not in a rage. I meant every word I said, but I want to apologize for the rude way in which I said it, as I had no right to speak so to my elders. And I want to tell you that you need not fear me running away with Peter, even supposing he should honour me with his affections, as I am engaged to another man.”

===Chinese===
“可我并没有盛怒。我说出口的每一个字都是当真的，只是那话说得太粗鲁了，我要为这个赔不是——对长辈那样说话，是我没有规矩。我还要告诉你，你尽可不必担心我同彼得私奔，哪怕他当真赏脸对我动了情，因为我已经许配给另一个人了。”

===Original===
“By dad, I’ll be hanged!” he exclaimed, with nothing but curiosity on his wrinkled dried tobacco-leaf-looking face. He expressed no resentment on account of my behaviour to him.

===Chinese===
“老天爷，怪事一桩！”他叫了起来，那张皱巴巴、像晒干的烟叶似的脸上，明明白白只剩好奇。对于我待他的那番行径，他毫无记恨之意。

===Original===
“Are ye to be married soon? Has he got any prawperty? Who is he? I suppose he’s respectable. Ye’re very young.”

===Chinese===
“快结婚乐吗？他有没有家当？他四谁？俺琢磨着，他该四个体面人。你年纪还小着哩。”

===Original===
“Yes; he is renowned for respectability, but I am not going to marry him till I am twenty-one. He is poor, but has good prospects. You must promise me not to tell anyone, as I wish it kept a secret, and only mention it to you so that you need not be disturbed about Peter.”

===Chinese===
“是的；他的体面有口皆碑，不过我不满二十一岁不嫁他。他穷，却有光明的前程。你得答应我不告诉任何人，因为我要这事守成秘密；只说给你听，为的是你往后不必再为彼得悬心。”

===Original===
He assured me that he would keep the secret, and I knew I could rely on his word. He was greatly perturbed that my intended was poor.

===Chinese===
他向我担保一定守口如瓶，我也知道他的话靠得住。只是我的未婚夫是个穷人这件事，叫他大为不安。

===Original===
“Never ye marry a man widout a bit er prawperty, me gu-r-r-r-l. Take my advice﻿—the divil’s in a poor match, no matter how good the man may be. Don’t ye be in a hurry; ye’re personable enough in yer way, and there’s as good fish in the seas as ever come out of ’em. Yer very small; I admire a good lump of a woman meself﻿—but don’t ye lose heart. I’ve heerd some men say they like little girls, but, as I said, I like a good lump of a woman meself.”

===Chinese===
“你可千万别嫁一个一点儿家当都没有滴男人，俺滴姑——娘——。听俺一句劝——穷对亲里头藏着魔鬼，随那男人多好也白搭。你不用急；照你自己那个路数，你这模样也够瞧滴乐，海里滴好鱼，捞上多少还剩多少。你滴个头太小；俺自家嘛，喜欢滴四一大团壮实滴女人——可你也别灰心。俺听人说过，有些男人就喜欢小个头滴姑娘，不过俺说过了，俺自家喜欢滴四一大团壮实滴女人。”

===Original===
“And you’ve got a good lump of a squaw,” I thought to myself.

===Chinese===
“好一大团壮实的婆娘，你自己不就到手乐一个，”我心里暗想。

===Original===
Do not mistake me. I do not for an instant fancy myself above the M’Swats. Quite the reverse; they are much superior to me. Mr. M’Swat was upright and clean in his morals, and in his little sphere was as sensible and kind a man as one could wish for. Mrs. M’Swat was faithful to him, contented and good-natured, and bore uncomplainingly, year after year, that most cruelly agonising of human duties﻿—childbirth, and did more for her nation and her Maker than I will ever be noble enough to do.

===Chinese===
请不要误会我。我从不曾有一瞬间自以为高过麦斯沃特一家。恰恰相反；他们远胜于我。麦斯沃特先生品行端正洁净，在他那个小小的天地里，是个要多明白和善、就有多明白和善的男人。麦斯沃特太太忠于丈夫，知足而和气，一年又一年，毫无怨言地承受着人伦义务中最惨痛熬人的那一件——生育，并且为她的民族、为她的造物主所做的，多到我永远高贵不到能够做到的地步。

===Original===
But I could not help it that their life was warping my very soul. Nature fashions us all; we have no voice in the matter, and I could not change my organisation to one which would find sufficient sustenance in the mental atmosphere of Barney’s Gap.

===Chinese===
可是，他们的生活正在扭弯我灵魂的这个事实，我无从自主。造化塑造了我们每一个人；在这件事上我们全无发言权，我也变不成另一种构造——能在巴尼山口的精神空气里寻到足够养料的那种构造。

## XXXII Ta-Ta to Barney’s Gap / 第32章 别了，巴尼山口

===Original===
It chanced at last, as June gave place to July and July to August, that I could bear it no longer. I would go away even if I had to walk, and what I would do I did not know or care, my one idea being to leave Barney’s Gap far and far behind. One evening I got a lot of letters from my little brothers and sisters at home. I fretted over them a good deal, and put them under my pillow; and as I had not slept for nights, and was feeling weak and queer, I laid my head upon them to rest a little before going out to get the tea ready. The next thing I knew was that Mrs. M’Swat was shaking me vigorously with one hand, holding a flaring candle in the other, and saying:

===Chinese===
事情终于临头了。六月让位给七月，七月又让位给八月，我再也无法忍受下去。哪怕靠两条腿走，我也要离开；至于走了去做什么，我既不知道也不在乎，唯一的念头就是把巴尼山口远远地、远远地抛在身后。一天傍晚，我收到家里弟弟妹妹们寄来的一沓信。我为这些信烦闷了好一阵，把它们塞到枕头底下；由于接连好几夜不曾合眼，我只觉得虚弱又异样，便把头搁在信上，想在出去预备茶饭之前稍稍歇一歇。接下来我知道的事，便是麦斯沃特太太一手擎着一支火光直蹿的蜡烛，一手使劲摇我，嘴里说道：

===Original===
“Lizer, shut the winder quick. She’s been lyin’ here in the draught till she’s froze, and must have the nightmare, the way she’s been singin’ out that queer, an’ I can’t git her woke up. What ails ye, child? Are ye sick?”

===Chinese===
“莱泽，快把窗户关上。她躺在这穿堂风里，都快冻透了，准是做了噩梦，瞧她那样儿怪里怪气地喊叫，俺咋弄也弄不醒她。你这孩子是咋啦？是不是病了？”

===Original===
I did not know what ailed me, but learnt subsequently that I laughed and cried very much, and pleaded hard with grannie and some Harold to save me, and kept reiterating, “I cannot bear it, I cannot bear it,” and altogether behaved so strangely that Mr. M’Swat became so alarmed that he sent seventeen miles for the nearest doctor. He came next morning, felt my pulse, asked a few questions, and stated that I was suffering from nervous prostration.

===Chinese===
我不知道自己出了什么毛病，后来才听说，我当时又哭又笑闹得好不厉害，苦苦哀求外婆和某一位哈罗德来救我，嘴里反复念叨着“我受不了了，我受不了了”，总之举止那样古怪，把麦斯沃特先生吓慌了神，赶到十七英里外去请最近的医生。医生次日早晨到了，摸了摸我的脉，问了几句话，断定我害的是神经衰竭。

===Original===
“Why, the child is completely run down, and in a fair way to contract brain fever!” he exclaimed. “What has she been doing? It seems as though she had been under some great mental strain. She must have complete rest and change, plenty of diversion and nourishing food, or her mind will become impaired.”

===Chinese===
“哎呀，这孩子亏虚到了根子上，眼看就要转成脑炎症了！”他叫道。“她一直在做些什么来着？看她这样子，像是经受了什么了不得的精神重压。她必须彻底休养、改换环境，多多消遣散心、吃滋补助养的饭食，不然脑筋就要受损了。”

===Original===
He left me a bottle of tonic and Mr. and Mrs. M’Swat many fears. Poor kindhearted souls, they got in a great state, and understood about as much of the cause of my breakdown as I do of the inside of the moon. They ascribed it to the paltry amount of teaching and work I had done.

===Chinese===
他留给我一瓶补药，留给麦斯沃特先生和太太满怀的惶恐。可怜的热心肠人啊，他们慌得不成样子，而对我垮掉的缘由，他们明白的，同我对月亮内里的明白不相上下。他们把它归到我为数可怜的那点儿教书和活计上头。

===Original===
Mrs. M’Swat killed a fowl and stewed it for my delectation. There was part of the inside with many feathers to flavour the dish, and having no appetite, I did not enjoy it, but made a feint of so doing to please the good-natured cook.

===Chinese===
麦斯沃特太太宰了一只母鸡，炖了一锅供我享用。锅里带着半副下水，还搭着好些羽毛给这道菜提味；我毫无胃口，实在受用不起，但为了不叫那好心的厨娘扫兴，还是装出一副吃得津津有味的样子。

===Original===
They intended writing at once to give my parents notice when I would be put on the train. I was pronounced too ill to act as scribe; Lizer was suggested, and then Jimmy, but M’Swat settled the matter thus:

===Chinese===
他们打算立刻写信给我父母报信，说我哪一天会被送上火车。我被断为病得太重，执不得笔；先提议莱泽，又提议吉米，麦斯沃特却这样把事情一锤定音：

===Original===
“Sure, damn it! I’m the proper one to write on an important business matther like this here.”

===Chinese===
“可不，见鬼！这么要紧的一桩正经事儿，合该俺亲自来写。”

===Original===
So pens, ink, and paper were laid on the dining-room table, and the great proclamation went forth among the youngsters, “Pa is goin’ to write a whole letter all by hisself.”

===Chinese===
于是笔、墨、纸都摆上了饭厅的桌子，一道重大的公告在娃娃们中间传布开来：“爸要自个儿独力写一整封信喽。”

===Original===
My door opened with the dining-room, and from my bed I could see the proceeding. Mr. M’Swat hitched his trousers well through the saddle-strap which he always wore as a belt, took off his coat and folded it on the back of a chair, rolled his shirtsleeves up to his elbows, pulled his hat well over his eyes, and “shaped up” to the writing material, none of which met with his approval. The ink was “warter,” the pens had not enough “pint,” and the paper was “trash;” but on being assured it was the good stuff he had purchased especially for himself, he buckled to the fray, producing in three hours a half-sheet epistle, which in grammar, composition, and spelling quite eclipsed the entries in his diary. However, it served its purpose, and my parents wrote back that, did I reach Goulburn on a certain day, a neighbour who would be in town then would bring me home.

===Chinese===
我的房门开向饭厅，从床上就能看到全过程。麦斯沃特先生把他常年当腰带使的那条马鞍肚带好生勒上一道，把裤腰掖得服服帖帖，脱下外套叠在椅背上，衬衫袖子卷到胳膊肘，帽檐往下一拉，严严实实罩住眼睛，对着那套文具“摆开了架势”——而那几样东西没一样入得了他的法眼。墨水是“清汤寡水”，钢笔的“笔尖儿”不够利，纸又净是“破烂货”；可等到有人向他担保，这些正是他特意给自己置办的上好货色，他便毅然投入战斗，三个钟头拿下一封半张纸的书信——论文法、布局与拼写，把他日记里的各条记录远远盖了过去。不过信总算办成了事，我父母回信说，倘使我某一日抵达古尔本，那天恰巧进城的一位邻居会带我回家。

===Original===
Now that it was settled that I had no more to teach the dirty children, out of dirty books, lessons for which they had great disinclination, and no more to direct Lizer’s greasy fingers over the yellow keys of that demented piano in a vain endeavour to teach her “choones,” of which her mother expected her to learn on an average two daily, it seemed as though I had a mountain lifted off me, and I revived magically, got out of bed and packed my things.

===Chinese===
如今事情说定，我再也用不着教那群脏兮兮的孩子，用不着拿脏兮兮的书本教他们老大不情愿的功课，再也用不着引着莱泽油乎乎的手指在那架疯疯癫癫的钢琴的发黄琴键上摸索，枉费心力地教她“曲子”——她母亲指望她平均每天学会两支——我仿佛有一座大山从身上卸了下来，神奇地还了魂，下了床，收拾起自己的东西。

===Original===
I was delighted at the prospect of throwing off the leaden shackles of Barney’s Gap, but there was a little regret mingled with my relief. The little boys had not been always bold. Did I express a wish for a parrot-wing or water-worn stone, or suchlike, after a time I would be certain, on issuing from my bedroom, to find that it had been surreptitiously laid there, and the little soft-eyed fellows would squabble for the privilege of bringing me my post, simply to give me pleasure. Poor little Lizer, and Rose Jane too, copied me in style of dress and manners in a way that was somewhat ludicrous but more pathetic.

===Chinese===
一想到就要甩脱巴尼山口这副铅打的镣铐，我满心欢喜，可宽慰之中也掺着一丝怅惘。那些小男孩并非一向无礼。但凡我流露过想要一片鹦鹉翎羽或一块水磨的石子之类，过些时日，我一走出卧室，准会发现那东西已被人悄悄摆在了那里；几个眼睛温软的小家伙还会为争着替我送“邮件”的体面吵作一团——只为讨我高兴。可怜的小莱泽，还有罗丝·简，都在衣着做派上一味学我，学得几分滑稽，更多的却是叫人心酸。

===Original===
They clustered round to say goodbye. I would be sure to write. Oh yes, of course, and they would write in return and tell me if the bay mare got well, and where they would find the yellow turkey-hen’s nest. When I got well I must come back, and I wouldn’t have as much work to do, but go for more rides to keep well, and so on. Mrs. M’Swat very anxiously impressed it upon me that I was to explain to my mother that it was not her (Mrs. M’Swat’s) fault that I “ailed” from overwork, as I had never complained and always seemed well.

===Chinese===
他们围拢上来道别。我一定写信来的。噢，那自然；他们也要回信，告诉我那匹枣红母马好了没有，他们又在哪儿找到了那只黄毛火鸡的窝。等我病好了，一定得回来；到时候活计不会再叫我干那么多，还能多骑骑马出去走走，好保住身子，如此等等。麦斯沃特太太十万分焦灼地再三叮嘱，要我向我母亲解释明白：我因劳作过度而“病倒”，不是她（麦斯沃特太太）的过错，因为我从不曾叫过苦，看上去一向好好的。

===Original===
With a kindly light on his homely sunburnt face, M’Swat said, as he put me on the train:

===Chinese===
麦斯沃特送我上火车时，那张朴实、晒得黢黑的脸上泛着和善的光，他说：

===Original===
“Sure, tell yer father he needn’t worry over the money. I’ll never be hard on him, an’ if ever I could help ye, I’d be glad.”

===Chinese===
“可不，告诉你爹，钱的事犯不着愁。俺决计不会难为他，往后但凡有帮得上你的地方，俺都乐意。”

===Original===
“Thank you; you are very good, and have done too much already.”

===Chinese===
“谢谢您；您心眼儿真好，为我做的已经太多了。”

===Original===
“Too much! Sure, damn it, wot’s the good er bein’ alive if we can’t help each other sometimes. I don’t mind how much I help a person if they have a little gratitood, but, damn it, I can’t abear ingratitood.”

===Chinese===
“太多了！可不，见鬼，人活着要是不能时不时彼此帮衬一把，那活着还有啥子意思。只要人家存着一点儿感恩的心，俺帮多少都不嫌多；可是见鬼，忘恩负义这号事，俺断断受不了。”

===Original===
“Goodbye, Mr. M’Swat, and thank you.”

===Chinese===
“再见了，麦斯沃特先生，多谢您。”

===Original===
“Goodbye, me gu-r-r-r-l, and never marry that bloke of yours if he don’t git a bit er prawperty, for the divil’s in a poor match.”

===Chinese===
“再见喽，俺的姑——娘——，你那个后生要是置不下一点儿‘家业’，你可千万别嫁他——穷姻缘里头，住着老魔鬼哩。”

## XXXIII Back at Possum Gully / 第33章 重返袋熊谷

===Original===
They were expecting me on the frosty evening in September, and the children came bounding and shouting to meet me, when myself and luggage were deposited at Possum Gully by a neighbour, as he passed in a great hurry to reach his own home ere it got too dark. They bustled me to a glowing fire in no time.

===Chinese===
九月里一个霜冻的傍晚，家里人正等着我回来。一位邻人匆匆赶路，生怕天黑得太透赶不回自家，路过时便把我连人带行李卸在了袋熊谷，孩子们蹦着跳着、又嚷又叫地迎上来。一转眼，他们已把我簇拥到一炉烧得正旺的火跟前。

===Original===
My father sat reading, and, greeting me in a very quiet fashion, continued the perusal of his paper. My mother shut her lips tightly, saying exultingly, “It seems it was possible for you to find a worse place than home;” and that little speech was the thorn on the rose of my welcome home. But there was no sting in Gertie’s greeting, and how beautiful she was growing, and so tall! It touched me to see she had made an especial dainty for my tea, and had put things on the table which were only used for visitors. The boys and little Aurora chattered and danced around me all the while. One brought for my inspection some soup-plates which had been procured during my absence; another came with a picture-book; and nothing would do them but that I must, despite the darkness, straightaway go out and admire a new fowl-house which “Horace and Stanley built all by theirselves, and no one helped them one single bit.”

===Chinese===
父亲坐着读报，淡淡地招呼了我一声，便接着研读他的报纸。母亲紧紧抿住嘴唇，扬扬得意地说：“看来你到底还是找得到比家里更糟的地方。”这一句短短的话，便是我归家玫瑰上的那根刺。可格蒂的问候里没有半点芒刺——她长得多美啊，又蹿得这么高！见她特为我的茶点备了一样精致的吃食，还把平日只有待客才舍得用的东西摆上了桌，我心里不禁一热。几个男孩和小奥罗拉一直围着我叽叽喳喳、蹦蹦跳跳。一个捧来几只汤盘请我过目——那是我不在家期间新添置的；另一个抱来一本图画书；末了他们还不依不饶，非要我顶着黑立刻出去，赞赏一番那座新鸡舍不可——“全是霍勒斯跟斯坦利自个儿盖的，谁也没帮忙，一丁点儿也没帮。”

===Original===
After Mrs. M’Swat it was a rest, a relief, a treat, to hear my mother’s cultivated voice, and observe her ladylike and refined figure as she moved about; and, what a palace the place seemed in comparison to Barney’s Gap! simply because it was clean, orderly, and bore traces of refinement; for the stamp of indigent circumstances was legibly imprinted upon it, and many things which had been considered “done for” when thirteen months before I had left home, were still in use.

===Chinese===
挨过麦斯沃特太太那种人之后再听母亲那有教养的嗓音，看她那大家闺秀、斯文窈窕的身影在屋里走动，简直是一种安息、一种解脱、一种享受；比起巴尼山口来，这地方俨然一座宫殿！无非因为它干净、齐整，带着几分斯文的痕迹；其实穷困的印记分明烙在它身上，十三个月前我离家时早已算作“报废”的许多东西，如今也还在凑合着用。

===Original===
I carefully studied my brothers and sisters. They had grown during my absence, and were all big for their age, and though some of them not exactly handsome, yet all pleasant to look upon﻿—I was the only wanting in physical charms﻿—also they were often discontented, and wished, as children will, for things they could not have; but they were natural, understandable children, not like myself, cursed with a fevered ambition for the utterly unattainable.

===Chinese===
我细细端详我的弟弟妹妹们。我不在家的日子里他们都长了个子，个个超出自己的年纪，虽说其中有几个算不得标致，却个个耐看——唯独我一个人缺了体貌上的福气——他们也常常不知足，像天下孩子一样巴望着得不到的东西；可他们是自然的孩子、容易懂的孩子，不像我，被一场热病似的野心咒住了，一味渴求那全然够不着的东西。

===Original===
> “Oh, were I seated high as my ambition,
 

 I’d place this loot on naked necks of monarchs!”

===Chinese===
> “啊，倘若我坐得同我的野心一般高，
 

 我定要把这掠来的财宝，套上君王们赤裸的颈项！”

===Original===
At the time of my departure for Caddagat my father had been negotiating with beer regarding the sale of his manhood; on returning I found that he had completed the bargain, and held a stamped receipt in his miserable appearance and demeanour. In the broken-down man, regardless of manners, one would have failed to recognize Dick Melvyn, “Smart Dick Melvyn,” “Jolly-good-fellow Melvyn” “Thorough Gentleman” and “Manly Melvyn” of the handsome face and ingratiating manners, onetime holder of Bruggabrong, Bin Bin East, and Bin Bin West. He never corrected his family nowadays, and his example was most deleterious to them.

===Chinese===
我动身去卡达加特那阵子，父亲还在同啤酒就出卖男子气概一事讨价还价；回来一看，买卖已经拍板成交，他那副凄惨的形容举止，便是盖了印的收据。在这个垮掉了、连体统也不顾的男人身上，谁也认不出迪克·梅尔文来——“精明的迪克·梅尔文”“大好人梅尔文”“十足的绅士”“有丈夫气的梅尔文”，那个面庞英俊、举止讨喜、昔年坐拥布鲁加布朗、宾宾东与宾宾西的人。如今他再不管教儿女，而他这个榜样，对他们的戕害极深。

===Original===
Mother gave me a list of her worries in private after tea that night. She wished she had never married: not only was her husband a failure, but to all appearances her children would be the same. I wasn’t worth my salt or I would have remained at Barney’s Gap; and there was Horace﻿—heaven only knew where he would end. God would surely punish him for his disrespect to his father. It was impossible to keep things together much longer, etc., etc.

===Chinese===
当晚茶点过后，母亲私下里向我历数她的愁苦。她但愿自己从没嫁过人：丈夫一败涂地不说，看那光景，儿女们也要步他后尘。我要不是白吃饭不顶事，就该留在巴尼山口；还有霍勒斯——天晓得到头来落到什么下场。上帝必因他对父亲的不敬降罚于他。这个家再难撑持多久了，如此等等。

===Original===
When we went to bed that night Gertie poured all her troubles into my ear in a jumbled string. It was terrible to have such a father. She was ashamed of him. He was always going into town, and stayed there till mother had to go after him, or some of the neighbours were so good as to bring him home. It took all the money to pay the publican’s bills, and Gertie was ashamed to be seen abroad in the nice clothes which grannie sent, as the neighbours said the Melvyns ought to pay up the old man’s bills instead of dressing like swells; and she couldn’t help it, and she was sick and tired of trying to keep up respectability in the teeth of such odds.

===Chinese===
那晚上床的时候，格蒂把她满腹的苦恼东一句西一句地倒进我耳朵。摊上这么个父亲，真是可怕。她替他丢脸。他老是往城里跑，一去就泡在那里，直到母亲不得不亲自去寻，或是哪位邻人发善心把他送回家来。钱全拿去付酒馆老板的账了；格蒂穿着外婆寄来的好衣裳出门又怕人瞧见，因为邻居们说，梅尔文家该拿这钱去替老家伙清账，不该穿得像阔佬似的招摇；她没法子，顶着这样的逆流硬撑体面，撑得她又是厌倦又是恶心。

===Original===
I comforted her with the assurance that the only thing was to feel right within ourselves, and let people say whatsoever entertained their poor little minds. And I fell asleep thinking that parents have a duty to children greater than children to parents, and they who do not fulfil their responsibility in this respect are as bad in their morals as a debauchee, corrupt the community as much as a thief, and are among the ablest underminers of their nation.

===Chinese===
我安慰她说，唯一的办法是我们自己心里问心无愧，任凭人们爱讲什么，只当给那可怜的小头脑解闷。我入睡时想着：父母对儿女负的责任，重于儿女对父母负的；在这上头不肯尽责的人，论德行同荒淫之徒一样坏，腐蚀社会同窃贼一样深，都算得掘自家国家墙脚最得力的一批人。

===Original===
On the morrow, the first time we were alone, Horace seized the opportunity of holding forth on *his* woes. It was no use, he was choke full of Possum Gully: he would stick to it for another year, and then he would chuck it, even if he had to go on the wallaby. He wasn’t going to be slaving forever for the boss to swallow the proceeds, and there was nothing to be made out of dairying. When it wasn’t drought it was floods and caterpillars and grasshoppers.

===Chinese===
第二天，头一回只我们两个独处时，霍勒斯便抓住机会大倒*他*的苦水。横竖没用了，他对袋熊谷腻烦透顶：再熬上一年，然后撂挑子走人，哪怕落得背起行囊去流浪也认了。他才不打算一辈子当牛做马，流血流汗却叫老头子把出产吞下肚；再说养奶牛这营生根本榨不出名堂。不是旱就是涝，外加毛毛虫和蚂蚱。

---

===Original===
Among my brothers and sisters I quickly revived to a certain extent, and mother asserted her opinion that I had not been ill at all, but had made up my mind to torment her; had not taken sufficient exercise, and might have had a little derangement of the system but nothing more. It was proposed that I should return to Barney’s Gap. I demurred, and was anathematized as ungrateful and altogether corrupt, that I would not go back to M’Swat, who was so good as to lend my father money out of pure friendship; but for once in my life I could not be made submit by either coercion or persuasion. Grannie offered to take one of us to Caddagat; mother preferred that Gertie should go. So we sent the pretty girl to dwell among her kindred in a land of comfort and pleasure.

===Chinese===
夹在弟弟妹妹们中间，我很快便恢复了几分。母亲一口咬定，我压根儿没生过病，只是拿定了主意折磨她；说我运动不足，至多是身子有些小失调，再无其他。于是有人提议我回巴尼山口去。我表示不肯，便招来一顿咒骂，骂我忘恩负义、彻头彻尾地朽坏——竟不肯回麦斯沃特家去，人家纯出友谊、好心好意借钱给我父亲；可是平生头一遭，任凭威逼也好、哄劝也好，都休想叫我屈服。外婆提出接我们姊妹中一个去卡达加特，母亲属意让格蒂去。我们便打发这位漂亮姑娘，到那安逸快活的天地里投奔她的亲人去了。

===Original===
I remained at Possum Gully to tread the same old life in its tame narrow path, with its never-ending dawn-till-daylight round of tasks; with, as its entertainments, an occasional picnic or funeral or a day in town, when, should it happen to be Sunday, I never fail to patronize one of the cathedrals. I love the organ music, and the hush which pervades the building; and there is much entertainment in various ways if one goes early and watches the well-dressed congregation filing in. The costumes and the women are pretty, and, in his own particular line, the ability of the verger is something at which to marvel. Regular attendants, of course, pay for and have reserved their seats, but it is in classing the visitors that the verger displays his talent. He can cull the commoners from the parvenu aristocrats, and put them in their respective places as skilfully as an expert horse-dealer can draft his stock at a sale. Then, when the audience is complete, in the middle and front of the edifice are to be found they of the white hands and fine jewels; and in the topmost seat of the synagogue, praying audibly, is one who has made all his wealth by devouring widows’ houses; while pushed away to the corners and wings are they who earn their bread by the sweat of their brow; and those who cannot afford good linen are too proud to be seen here at all.

===Chinese===
我留在袋熊谷，在那条驯顺窄狭的老路上照旧过活——一轮又一轮从破晓到天黑没完没了的活计；至于消遣，无非偶尔一场野餐、一场丧事，或是进城过上一整天；若碰巧是星期天，我从不失约，总要光顾一座大教堂。我爱那管风琴的乐声，爱那漫溢全堂的肃静；去得早的话，看衣冠楚楚的会众鱼贯而入，种种趣味也不少。衣裳是好看的，女人也是好看的，而教堂司事在他那一门行当里的本事，更是令人叹绝。常来的会众自然出了钱、定了自家的座位；司事显露身手，却是在给生客分等的时候。他能从暴发的贵族里把平头百姓拣出来，各安其位打发得妥妥帖帖，手法之娴熟，恰似老到的马贩子在拍卖场上分拣自家的牲口。待到满堂坐定，大殿正中与前排坐的，是那些白净的手、精美珠宝的主儿；会堂最高的座位上出声祷告着的，是一位靠吞吃寡妇的房屋攒下全部家财的人物；而被挤到角落和两厢的，是那些凭额头的汗水挣面包的人；至于置不起好细麻布衣裳的，又自尊得厉害，索性全然不肯在这里露面。

===Original===
“The choir sings and the organ rings,” the uninteresting prayers are rattled off (“O come, let us worship, and fall down: and kneel before the Lord, our Maker”); a sermon, mostly of the debts of the concern, of the customs of the ancients, or of the rites and ceremonies of up-to-date churchism, is delivered, and the play is done, and as I leave the building a great hunger for a little Christianity fills my heart.

===Chinese===
“诗班唱起来了，风琴响起来了”，那些寡淡无味的祷词飞快地念完（“来啊，我们要敬拜、要俯伏，要跪在主、我们的造物主面前”）；接着讲一篇布道，多半讲这桩买卖的亏空、古人的风俗，或是摩登教会主义的仪文礼节；然后这出戏就此收场。走出教堂时，对哪怕一丁点真基督教的深深饥渴，涨满我的心头。

===Original===
Oh that a preacher might arise and expound from the Book of books a religion with a God, a religion with a heart in it﻿—a Christian religion, which would abolish the cold legend whose centre is respectability, and which rears great buildings in which the rich recline on silken hassocks while the poor perish in the shadow thereof.

===Chinese===
啊，但愿有一位传道人站起来，从那书中之书里阐发出一种有上帝的宗教、一种心里有热肠的宗教——一种基督的宗教，好废除那以体面为中心的冰冷传说——正是那传说，盖起巍巍大殿，叫富人倚着丝绸跪垫高卧，叫穷人在殿影之下凋零灭没。

---

===Original===
Through the hot dry summer, then the heartless winter and the scorching summer again which have spent themselves since Gertie’s departure, I have struggled hard to do my duty in that state of life unto which it had pleased God to call me, and sometimes I have partially succeeded. I have had no books or papers, nothing but peasant surroundings and peasant tasks, and have encouraged peasant ignorance﻿—ignorance being the mainspring of contentment, and contentment the bedrock of happiness; but it is all to no purpose. A note from the other world will strike upon the chord of my being, and the spirit which has been dozing within me awakens and fiercely beats at its bars, demanding some nobler thought, some higher aspiration, some wider action, a more saturnalian pleasure, something more than the peasant life can ever yield. Then I hold my spirit tight till wild passionate longing sinks down, down to sickening dumb despair, and had I the privilege extended to Job of old﻿—to curse God and die﻿—I would leap at it eagerly.

===Chinese===
自格蒂走后，炎旱的夏季过去了，无情严冬过去了，烤人的夏季又过去了；这一路我苦苦挣扎，在上帝乐意呼召我身处的那种生活境遇里勉尽本分，有时也略略成了几分。我没有书，没有报，只有庄户的环境和庄户的活计，还有我悉心养成的庄户式愚昧——愚昧是知足的发条，知足是幸福的基石；可这全都白费。另一个世界飘来一记音符，敲在我生命的琴弦上，那在我体内打盹的精神便醒了，狂暴地撞击着樊笼的栏杆，索要更高贵的思想、更高远的志气、更宽广的作为、一场更恣肆的狂欢——索要庄户生活永远给不出的一切。这时我便把我的精神死死摁住，直到那狂野炽烈的渴望沉下去，沉下去，沉成一种叫人作呕的哑然绝望；倘若古时约伯享有的那份特权也赐给我——咒诅上帝，然后死掉——我会迫不及待地扑上去。

## XXXIV But Absent Friends Are Soon Forgot / 第34章 然而故人易忘

===Original===
We received a great many letters from Gertie for a little while after she went up the country, but they grew shorter and farther between as time went on.

===Chinese===
格蒂去了内地以后，起初我们还常收到她的许多来信，可随着时光推移，信越来越短，间隔也越来越长。

===Original===
In one of grannie’s letters there was concerning my sister: “I find Gertie is a much younger girl for her age than Sybylla was, and not nearly so wild and hard to manage. She is a great comfort to me. Everyone remarks upon her good looks.”

===Chinese===
外婆有一封信里提到我妹妹：“我看格蒂就她的年纪而言，比西比拉当年稚嫩得多，也远没有那么野、那么难管教。她真是我莫大的安慰。人人都夸她模样标致。”

===Original===
From one of Gertie’s letters:

===Chinese===
从格蒂的一封信里：

===Original===
> Uncle Julius came home from Hong Kong and America last week, and brought such a lot of funny presents for everyone. He had a lot for you, but he has given them to me instead as you are not here. He calls me his pretty little sunbeam, and says I must always live with him.

===Chinese===
> 朱利叶斯舅舅上礼拜从香港和美国回来了，给每个人都带来好多有趣的礼物。他也给你备下好多，可你不在这儿，便都给了我。他管我叫他的漂亮小阳光，说我必须永远跟他一起过。

===Original===
I sighed to myself as I read this. Uncle Jay-Jay had said much the same to me, and where was I now? My thoughts were ever turning to the people and old place I love so well, but Gertie’s letters showed me that I was utterly forgotten and unmissed.

===Chinese===
读到这里，我暗暗叹了口气。杰杰舅舅当年对我说过几乎一模一样的话，可我如今在哪里呢？我的心思总是一再飞向我深爱的那些人、那个旧地方，可格蒂的信让我明白：我已被人忘了个干净，没有一个人挂念我。

===Original===
Gertie left us in October 1897, and it was somewhere about January 1898 that all the letters from Caddagat were full to overflowing with the wonderful news of Harold Beecham’s reinstatement at Five-Bob Downs, under the same conditions as he had held sway there in my day.

===Chinese===
格蒂是 1897 年 10 月离开我们的；大约到了 1898 年 1 月，卡达加特来的信便封封满溢，盛载着哈罗德·比彻姆重掌五博丘的妙讯——条件同我当年他在那里当家称雄时一模一样。

===Original===
From grannie’s letters I learnt that some old sweetheart of Harold’s father had bequeathed untold wealth to this her lost love’s son. The wealth was in bonds and stocks principally, and though it would be some time ere Harold was actually in possession of it, yet he had no difficulty in getting advancements to any amount, and had immediately repurchased Five-Bob.

===Chinese===
从外婆的信里我得知，哈罗德父亲的一位旧日情人，把一笔数说不尽的巨财遗赠给了她那位旧情人的儿子。这笔财产主要是债券与股票，虽则还要过些时日哈罗德才能实际到手，但他要预支多大数目都不费事，当即把五博丘重新购置了回来。

===Original===
I had never dreamed of such a possibility. True, I had often said were Harold a character in fiction instead of real life, some relative would die opportunely and set him up in his former position, but, here, this utterly unanticipated contingency had arisen in a manner which would affect my own life, and what were my feelings regarding the matter?

===Chinese===
这样的可能，我做梦也不曾想到。不错，我常说，哈罗德若是小说里的人物而非现实中的活人，自会有个亲戚死得正是时候，把他重新扶回原先的地位；可如今，这桩全然料想不到的意外竟当真冒了出来，而且牵动我自己的生活——对这桩事，我心里究竟是何滋味？

===Original===
I think I was not fully aware of the extent of my lack of wifely love for Harold Beecham, until experiencing the sense of relief which stole over me on holding in my hand the announcement of his return to the smile of fortune.

===Chinese===
我想，直到手捧他重又博得时运微笑的消息、一阵如释重负之感悄悄漫过全身的那一刻，我才算认清：自己对哈罗德·比彻姆的为妻之爱，原来欠缺到了这个地步。

===Original===
He was rich; he would not need me now; my obligation to him ceased to exist; I was free. He would no longer wish to be hampered with me. He could take his choice of beauty and worth; he might even purchase a princess did his ambition point that way.

===Chinese===
他有钱了；如今他用不着我了；我对他负的义务就此消散；我自由了。他再不会愿意让我拖累着了。美貌与贤德，任凭他挑拣；他的雄心若是往那边去，便买一位公主也买得起。

===Original===
One of Gertie’s letters ran:

===Chinese===
格蒂有一封信里写道：

===Original===
> That Mr. Beecham you used to tell me so much about has come back to live at Five-Bob. He has brought his aunts back. Everyone went to welcome them, and there was a great fuss. Aunt Helen says he (Mr. B.) is very conservative; he has everything just as it used to be. I believe he is richer than ever. Everyone is laughing about his luck. He was here twice last week, and has just left this evening. He is very quiet. I don’t know how you thought him so wonderful. I think he is too slow, I have great work to talk to him, but he is very kind, and I like him. He seems to remember you well, and often says you were a game youngster, and could ride like old Nick himself.

===Chinese===
> 你从前老跟我念叨个没完的那位比彻姆先生，回五博丘来住了。他把两位姑奶奶也接了回来。大家都赶去欢迎，热闹得不亦乐乎。海伦姨妈说他（比彻姆先生）保守得很，样样东西都照老样子摆。我看他比从前更有钱了。人人都拿他的好运打趣。上礼拜他来了两趟，今晚刚刚走。他话少得很。我不明白你怎么会觉得他那么了不起。我觉得他太闷，得费我好大的劲才跟他聊得起来；不过他心眼儿很好，我喜欢他。他好像把你记得很清楚，常说你是员有胆量的小将，骑起马来赛得过老尼克本人。

===Original===
I wrote to the owner of Five-Bob desiring to know if what I heard concerning his good fortune was correct, and he replied by return post:

===Chinese===
我写信给五博丘的主人，想弄明白我听说的他那桩好运是否属实，他随即由回程邮班回了信：

===Original===
> Yes, thank goodness it is all true. The old lady left me nearly a million. It seems like a fairy yarn, and I will know how to value it more now. I would have written sooner, only you remember our bargain, and I was just waiting to get things fixed up a little, when I’m off at great tracks to claim you in the flesh, as there is no need for us to wait above a month or two now if you are agreeable. I am just run to death. It takes a bit of jigging to get things straight again, but it’s simply too good to believe to be back in the same old beat. I’ve seen Gertie a good many times, and find your descriptions of her were not at all overdrawn. I won’t send any love in this, or there would be a “bust up” in the post-office, because I’d be sure to overdo the thing, and I’d have all the officials on to me for damages. Gather up your goods and chattels, because I’ll be along in a week or two to take possession of you.
> —Yr devoted
> Hal.

===Chinese===
> 是真的，谢天谢地，全是真的。老太太留了将近一百万给我。这简直像个童话，往后我可知道该怎样更看重它了。本该早些写信，只是你记得咱们的约定，我便一直等着把事情稍稍安顿妥帖——到时我就大老远火速赶去，把你本人娶到手；只要你肯点头，如今咱们顶多再等一两个月，无须更久了。我这阵子忙得要死。要把种种事情重新理顺，得费好一番折腾；可是能重回这片老地界，简直好得叫人不敢相信。格蒂我见过好多回了，你从前对她的描摹一点儿也不曾过甚其词。这封信里我就不附情意了，要不然邮局里准要“炸锅”——我保准会把分量使过了头，闹得那班当差的统统来找我索赔。收拾好你的家什动产，过一两个礼拜我就来“接收”你了。
> ——你忠实的
> 哈尔。

===Original===
I screwed the letter in two and dropped it into the kitchen-fire.

===Chinese===
我把那封信拧作两团，丢进了厨房的火里。

===Original===
I knew Harold meant what he had said. He was a strong-natured man of firm determinations, and having made up his mind to marry me would never for an instant think of anything else; but I could see what he could not see himself﻿—that he had probably tired of me, and was becoming enamoured of Gertie’s beauty.

===Chinese===
我知道哈罗德句句都是当真的。他是个天性刚强、主意坚定的人，既然拿定主意要娶我，便一刻也不会再想别的；可有一层我能看见、他自己却看不见——他多半已经对我腻烦，正爱上格蒂的美貌了。

===Original===
The discordance of life smote hard upon me, and the letter I wrote was not pleasant. It ran:

===Chinese===
生活的失谐重重击在我身上，我写的那封回信便并不动听。信是这样：

===Original===
> Your favour duly to hand. I heartily rejoice at your good fortune, and trust you may live long and have health to enjoy it. Do not for an instant consider yourself under any obligations to me, for you are perfectly free. Choose someone who will reflect more credit on your taste and sense.
> With all good wishes,
> Faithfully yrs,
> S. Penelope Melvyn.

===Chinese===
> 惠书奉悉。阁下鸿运，我由衷庆幸，并祝您福寿绵长、康健长享此福。请切莫以为自己于我负有任何义务，您已完全自由。请另择一位更能为您眼光与见识增光的人吧。
> 谨致美好的祝愿，
> 您忠实的，
> S.佩内洛普·梅尔文。

===Original===
As I closed and directed this how far away Harold Beecham seemed! Less than two years ago I had been familiar with every curve and expression of his face, every outline of his great figure, every intonation of his strong cultivated voice; but now he seemed as the shadow of a former age.

===Chinese===
封好这封信、写上收信人地址的时候，哈罗德·比彻姆显得多么遥远啊！不到两年以前，他脸上的每一道曲线、每一种神情，他那伟岸身形的每一处轮廓，他那洪亮而有教养的嗓音里的每一种语调，我无一不熟；可如今，他已像一个逝去时代的影子。

===Original===
He wrote in reply: What did I mean? Was it a joke﻿—just a little of my old tormenting spirit? Would I explain immediately? He couldn’t get down to see me for a fortnight at the least.

===Chinese===
他回信问：这是什么意思？是玩笑么——不过又耍一点儿我那折磨人的老性子？要不要立刻解释清楚？他至少要等两个礼拜，才抽得开身下来见我。

===Original===
I explained, and very tersely, that I had meant what I said, and in return received a letter as short as my own:

===Chinese===
我解释了，而且解释得干干脆脆：我说的话句句当真。作为回报，我收到一封同我那封一样短的信：

===Original===
> I regret your decision, but trust I have sufficient manhood to prevent me from thrusting myself upon any lady, much less you.
> Your sincere friend,
> Harold Augustus Beecham.

===Chinese===
> 你的决定令我遗憾，但我自信尚有足够的男子气概，不至于死乞白赖缠上任何一位女士——更不消说是你。
> 你真诚的朋友，
> 哈罗德·奥古斯都·比彻姆。

===Original===
He did not demand a reason for my decision, but accepted it unquestionably. As I read his words he grew near to me, as in the days gone by.

===Chinese===
他没有要求我说明决定的缘由，而是毫不置疑地接受了。读着他这些话，他向我走近了，一如往昔的日子。

===Original===
I closed my eyes, and before my mental vision there arose an overgrown old orchard, skirting one of the great stock-routes from Riverina to Monaro. A glorious day was languidly smiling good night on abundance of ripe and ripening fruit and flowers. The scent of stock and the merry cry of the tennis-players filled the air. I could feel Harold’s wild jolting heartbeats, his burning breath on my brow, and his voice husky with rage in my ear. As he wrote that letter I could fancy the well-cut mouth settling into a sullen line, as it had done on my birthday when, by caressing, I had won it back to its habitual pleasant expression; but on this occasion I would not be there. He would be angry just a little while﻿—a man of his strength and importance could not long hold ill-will towards a woman, a girl, a child! as weak and insignificant as I. Then when I should meet him in the years to come, when he would be the faithful and loving husband of another woman, he would be a little embarrassed perhaps; but I would set him at his ease, and we would laugh together re what he would term our foolish young days, and he would like me in a brotherly way. Yes, that was how it would be. The tiny note blackened in the flames.

===Chinese===
我阖上眼睛，心灵的眼前便浮起一座草木疯长的老果园，傍着一条从里弗赖纳通往莫纳罗的赶畜大道（stock-route）。一个壮丽的白日正懒洋洋地笑着道晚安，照拂满园熟透与将熟的果实和花丛。空气里满是牲口的气味，还有打网球的人们快活的喊声。我还能觉出哈罗德那狂野颠撞的心跳、他灼热的呼吸扑在我额上、他那因愤怒而发哑的声音响在我耳畔。写那封信的时候，我可以想见他那张轮廓分明的嘴抿紧成一条愠怒的直线——我生日那天它就是这样，经我一番温存才哄回惯常的和悦神色；可这一回，我不会在场了。他会恼怒那么一小会儿——像他这样有力量、有身份的男人，不会长久记恨一个女人，一个女孩，一个孩子！一个同我一样软弱、一样无足轻重的人。往后岁月里我再遇见他时，他已是另一个女人忠实体贴的丈夫，他或许会有一点儿窘迫；但我会让他自在起来，我们会一同笑着谈起那些他会称作我们年少痴傻的日子，他会拿兄长般的情分喜欢我。是的，将来准是这样。那封小小的便条在火焰里黑了下去。

===Original===
So much for my romance of love! It had ended in a bottle of smoke, as all my other dreams of life bid fair to do.

===Chinese===
我的爱情罗曼史，不过如此罢了！它到头来化作了一瓶子烟，正如我人生所有别的梦，眼看都要这样收场。

===Original===
I think I was not fully aware how near I had been to loving Harold Beecham until experiencing the sense of loss which stole over me on holding in my hand the acceptance of his dismissal. It was a something gone out of my life, which contained so few somethings, that I crushingly felt the loss of anyone.

===Chinese===
我想，直到把那封他认下遭拒的短信拿在手里、一阵失落之感悄悄漫上心头，我才算明白，我曾离爱上哈罗德·比彻姆有多近。那是我生命里又一样东西没了——我的生命里本来就没有几样东西——所以无论失去哪一个，我都痛切地感到缺失。

===Original===
Our greatest heart-treasure is a knowledge that there is in creation an individual to whom our existence is necessary﻿—someone who is part of our life as we are part of theirs, someone in whose life we feel assured our death would leave a gap for a day or two. And who can be this but a husband or wife? Our parents have other children and themselves, our brothers and sisters marry and have lives apart, so with our friends; but one’s husband would be different. And I had thrown behind me this chance; but in the days that followed I knew that I had acted wisely.

===Chinese===
我们心底最大的珍藏，是确知天地之间有一个人，我们的存在于他是必需的——那个人是我们生命的一部分，一如我们也是他们生命的一部分；我们确信，在那个人的生命里，我们的死会留下一两天的空缺。而这样的人，除了丈夫或妻子，还能是谁呢？父母还有别的儿女，还有他们自己；兄弟姊妹要婚嫁，各有过活，朋友也是如此；唯独一个人的丈夫不一样。而我竟把这个机会抛在了身后；可在其后的日子里，我知道自己做得明智。

===Original===
Gertie’s letters would contain: “Harold Beecham, he makes me call him Harry, took me to Five-Bob last week, and it was lovely fun.”

===Chinese===
格蒂的信里会有这样的话：“哈罗德·比彻姆——他非要我管他叫哈里——上礼拜带我去了五博丘，好玩极了。”

===Original===
Again it would be: “Harry says I am the prettiest little girl ever was, Caddagat or anywhere else, and he gave me such a lovely bracelet. I wish you could see it.”

===Chinese===
有时又是：“哈里说，卡达加特也好，别处也好，我是从来不曾有过的好看小姑娘；他还送了我一只顶可爱的手镯。你要是能瞧瞧就好了。”

===Original===
Or this:

===Chinese===
再不然便是：

===Original===
> We all went to church yesterday. Harry rode with me. There is to be a very swell ball at Wyambeet next month, and Harry says I am to keep nearly all my dances for him. Frank Hawden sailed for England last week. We have a new jackeroo. He is better-looking than Frank, but I don’t like him as well.

===Chinese===
> 昨天我们大伙儿都上教堂去了。哈里同我一路骑马。下个月怀安比特要办一场盛大的舞会，哈里说我得把差不多所有的舞都留给他。弗兰克·霍登上礼拜坐船去英国了。我们新来了一个牧场学徒。人比弗兰克俊，可我不像喜欢弗兰克那样喜欢他。

===Original===
Grannie’s and aunt Helen’s letters to my mother corroborated these admissions. Grannie wrote:

===Chinese===
外婆和海伦姨妈写给我母亲的信，印证了格蒂信里的这些自供。外婆写道：

===Original===
> Harry Beecham seems to be very much struck with Gertie. I think it would be a good thing, as he is immensely rich, and a very steady young fellow into the bargain. They say no woman could live with him on account of his temper; but he has always been a favourite of mine, and we cannot expect a man without some faults.

===Chinese===
> 哈里·比彻姆像是被格蒂迷得不轻。我看这是桩好事，他极其富有，外加又是个十分稳重的年轻人。人家说他的脾气，没有哪个女人能同他过活；可他一向是我中意的人，再说男人总不能指望一点毛病都没有。

===Original===
Aunt Helen remarked:

===Chinese===
海伦姨妈则说：

===Original===
> Don’t be surprised if you have young Beecham down there presently on an “asking papa” excursion. He spends a great deal of time here, and has been inquiring the best route to Possum Gully. Do you remember him? I don’t think he was here in your day. He is an estimable and likeable young fellow, and I think will make a good husband apart from his wealth. He and Gertie present a marked contrast.

===Chinese===
> 说不定过些日子，小比彻姆就要到你们那边去，来一趟“问爸爸”式的提亲远行，到时候你可别奇怪。他在这儿消磨的时光可不少，还打听过去袋熊谷最好走的路线呢。你还记得他么？我想你在的那阵子他不曾来过这里。他是个可敬又讨人喜欢的年轻人，且不说家财，我想他也会做个好丈夫。他同格蒂恰好是一对鲜明的对照。

===Original===
Sometimes on reading this kind of thing I would wax rather bitter. Love, I said, was not a lasting thing; but knowledge told me that it was for those of beauty and winsome ways, and not for me. I was ever to be a lonely-hearted waif from end to end of the world of love﻿—an alien among my own kin.

===Chinese===
有时读到这类文字，我会变得相当刻薄。我说，爱情原不是长久的东西；但见识告诉我，爱情是给那些有美貌、有娇态的人的，不是给我的。我注定要从爱的世界的这一头到那一头，做个心怀孤寂的流浪儿——在自己的亲人中间做一个异乡人。

===Original===
But there were other things to worry me. Horace had left the family roof. He averred he was “full up of life under the old man’s rule. It was too slow and messed up.” His uncle, George Melvyn, his father’s eldest brother, who had so often and so kindly set us up with cows, had offered to take him, and his father had consented to let him go. George Melvyn had a large station outback, a large sheepshearing machine, and other improvements. Thence, strong in the hope of sixteen years, Horace set out on horseback one springless spring morning ere the sun had risen, with all his earthly possessions strapped before him. Bravely the horse stepped out for its week’s journey, and bravely its rider sat, leaving me and the shadeless, wooden sunbaked house on the side of the hill, with the regretlessness of teens﻿—especially masculine teens. I watched him depart until the clacking of his horse’s hoofs grew faint on the stony hillside and his form disappeared amid the she-oak scrub which crowned the ridge to the westward. He was gone. Such is life. I sat down and buried my face in my apron, too miserable even for tears. Here was another article I ill could spare wrenched from my poorly and sparsely furnished existence.

===Chinese===
不过还有别的事叫我烦心。霍勒斯已经离开了这个家。他宣称自己“在老头子手底下过日子我受够了。又沉闷，又乱糟糟”。他的伯父乔治·梅尔文（George Melvyn）——父亲的长兄，就是屡屡好心送我们奶牛的那位——提出接纳他，父亲也应允放他走了。乔治·梅尔文在内地有一座大牧场，有一台大型剪羊毛机，还有别的种种新设施。于是，怀着十六岁少年的一腔热望，霍勒斯在一个不见半点春意的春日清晨、太阳还没升起的时候骑马出发了，全部家当都捆在身前鞍上。那马雄赳赳地踏上要走一星期的路程，马背上的人也坐得雄赳赳的，把我、把山坡上那座无荫无蔽、木板搭就、让日头烤透的房子抛在身后，满没有一丝悔意——少年人的无悔，尤其是少年男子的无悔。我望着他走远，直到马蹄的嗒嗒声在碎石山坡上渐渐低下去、他的身影没入西面岭脊上的木麻黄（she-oak）树丛。他走了。人生就是如此。我坐下来，把脸埋进围裙里，苦得连眼泪都流不出来。这又是一件我本就缺少不得的东西，从我那陈设贫乏而稀落的生涯里，被生生拧了去。

===Original===
True, our intercourse had not always been carpeted with rose-leaves. His pitiless scorn of my want of size and beauty had often given me a sleepless night; but I felt no bitterness against him for this, but merely cursed the Potter who had fashioned the clay that was thus described.

===Chinese===
诚然，我们姊弟之间的相处，并非总铺着玫瑰花瓣。他对我个头小、容貌差的那种毫无怜惜的讥诮，常常害我彻夜无眠；可我对此并不记恨他，只咒骂那抟泥的陶匠——竟把泥抟成这副任人如此指摘的模样。

===Original===
On the other hand, he was the only one who had ever stood up and said a word of extenuation for me in the teeth of a family squall. Father did not count; my mother thought me bad from end to end; Gertie, in addition to the gifts of beauty and lovableness, possessed that of holding with the hare and running with the hound; but Horace once had put in a word for me that I would never forget. I missed his presence in the house, his pounding of the old piano with four dumb notes in the middle, as he bawled thereto rollicking sea and comic songs; I missed his energetic dissertations on spurs, whips, and blood-horses, and his spirited rendering of snatches of Paterson and Gordon, as he came in and out, banging doors and gates, teasing the cats and dogs and tormenting the children.

===Chinese===
从另一方面说，他又是唯一一个曾在家庭风波的风口上站起来、替我说过一句开脱话的人。父亲算不得数；母亲认定我从头坏到尾；格蒂呢，美貌与讨人喜欢之外，还天生怀着一手“跟着兔子跑、又随猎犬追”的本事；唯独霍勒斯，有一回替我说过一句叫我永世不忘的话。我想念他在屋里的身影，想念他捶打那架当中哑了四个键的老钢琴、冲着琴吼唱喧闹的水手歌谣和谐谑小曲的模样；我想念他进进出出、摔门撞栅、逗猫撩狗、捉弄弟弟妹妹的当儿，那一篇篇关于马刺、鞭子和纯种马的热烈宏论，和他兴致勃勃高声吟诵的帕特森与戈登的诗句断章。

## XXXV The 3rd of December 1898 / 第35章 1898 年 12 月 3 日

===Original===
It was a very hot day. So extreme was the heat that to save the lives of some young swallows my father had to put wet bags over the iron roof above their nest. A galvanized-iron awning connected our kitchen and house: in this some swallows had built, placing their nest so near the iron that the young ones were baking with the heat until rescued by the wet bagging. I had a heavy day’s work before me, and, from my exertions of the day before, was tired at the beginning. Bushfires had been raging in the vicinity during the week, and yesterday had come so close that I had been called out to carry buckets of water all the afternoon in the blazing sun. The fire had been allayed, after making a gap in one of our boundary fences. Father and the boys had been forced to leave the harvesting of the miserable pinched wheat while they went to mend it, as the small allowance of grass the drought gave us was precious, and had to be carefully preserved from neighbours’ stock.

===Chinese===
那天真热得出奇。热到这般地步，为救几只雏燕的性命，父亲只好往燕巢上方的铁皮屋顶上加盖湿麻袋。有一道镀锌铁皮搭的凉棚，把我们的厨房和正屋连在一起：几只燕子就在棚里筑了巢，巢贴着铁皮贴得那么近，雏鸟们眼看要烤个半熟，全靠湿麻袋搭救。这天我面前压着一大堆繁重的活计，加上头一天的劳顿，一开头人就是乏的。这一礼拜，左近的丛林野火烧得正凶，昨天更是逼到近旁，害得我被叫出去，顶着毒日头提了一整个下午的水桶。火总算扑灭了，却在我家一道边界围栏上烧出一个豁口。父亲和男孩们只得撂下那可怜巴巴、遭旱歉瘦的麦子，先去修补围栏——旱年赏给我们的那一点点草料金贵得很，须得仔仔细细护住，别叫邻居家的牲口糟蹋了。

===Original===
I had baked and cooked, scrubbed floors and whitewashed hearths, scoured tinware and cutlery, cleaned windows, swept yards, and discharged numerous miscellaneous jobs, and half-past two in the afternoon found me very dirty and very tired, and with very much more yet to do.

===Chinese===
我烤过饼、做过饭，刷洗过地板、粉刷过炉台，擦过锡器和刀叉餐具，抹过窗户，扫过院子，办完一大堆五花八门的杂务；到下午两点半，我已是又脏又乏，而手头还有许多许多没干。

===Original===
One of my half-starved poddy calves was very ill, and I went out to doctor it previous to bathing and tidying myself for my finishing household duties.

===Chinese===
我那头饿得半死的手喂牛犊病得不轻，我先出去给它瞧病，再洗漱拾掇自己，回头好干家里剩下的活。

===Original===
My mother was busy upon piles and piles of wearying mending, which was one of the most hopeless of the many slaveries of her life. This was hard work, and my father was slaving away in the sun, and mine was arduous labour, and it was a very hot day, and a drought-smitten and a long day, and poddy calves ever have a tendency to make me moralize and snarl. This was life, my life and my parents’ life, and the life of those around us, and if I was a good girl and honoured my parents I would be rewarded with a long stretch of it. Yah!

===Chinese===
母亲正忙着对付一堆又一堆磨人的缝补——那是她一生诸多苦役里最没盼头的一桩。这活计辛苦，父亲在毒日头底下卖命，我的活计也是苦工，天又这么热，又是旱魃肆虐、漫长难熬的一天；而手喂牛犊这类东西，天生就有一股本事，引得我又发感慨又咬牙。这就是生活，我的生活、我父母的生活、我们四周围人的共同生活；只要我做个乖女孩、孝敬父母，往后自会得着长长的一大段这样的生活，算是犒赏。呸！

===Original===
These pagan meditations were interrupted by a footfall slowly approaching. I did not turn to ascertain who it might be, but trusted it was no one of importance, as the poddy and I presented rather a grotesque appearance. It was one of the most miserable and sickly of its miserable kind, and I was in the working uniform of the Australian peasantry. My tattered skirt and my odd and bursted boots, laced with twine, were spattered with whitewash, for coolness my soiled cotton blouse hung loose, an exceedingly dilapidated sunbonnet surmounted my head, and a bottle of castor-oil was in my hand.

===Chinese===
这些异教徒式的冥想，被一阵渐渐走近的脚步声打断了。我没有回头去看是谁，只指望来的不是什么要紧人，因为那牛犊同我凑在一处，模样实在有几分滑稽。它是它那可怜一族里最可怜、最病歪歪的一头，我则穿着澳大利亚庄稼人的工作制服：我那条破裙子和那双不成对儿、胀裂了口的靴子——鞋带拿麻绳充数——溅满了白灰浆；为了凉快，我那件脏了的棉布罩衫敞着怀；头上扣一顶破旧不堪的遮阳帽，手里还握着一瓶蓖麻油。

===Original===
I supposed it was one of the neighbours or a tea-agent, and I would send them to mother.

===Chinese===
我猜是哪位邻居，再不就是个上门兜售茶叶的，叫他们找母亲去就是。

===Original===
The footsteps had come to a halt beside me.

===Chinese===
脚步声在我身旁停了下来。

===Original===
“Could you tell me if﻿—”

===Chinese===
“劳驾，请问您是不是——”

===Original===
I glanced upwards. Horrors! There stood Harold Beecham, as tall and broad as of yore, even more sunburnt than ever, and looking very stylish in a suit of grey and a soft fashionable dinted-in hat; and it was the first time I had ever seen him in a white shirt and high collar.

===Chinese===
我抬眼一望。天哪！站在那儿的正是哈罗德·比彻姆，还是从前那样又高又壮，晒得比以往更黑，一身灰色套服，头戴一顶时兴的软呢帽，帽顶塌出凹痕，瞧着非常入时；而且这是我有生以来头一回见他穿白衬衫、竖硬领。

===Original===
I wished he would explode, or I might sink into the ground, or the calf would disappear, or that something might happen.

===Chinese===
我但愿他当场炸个粉碎，或者我自己一头扎进地里去，或者那牛犊凭空消失，或者随便出点什么岔子才好。

===Original===
On recognizing me his silence grew profound, but an unmistakable expression of pity filled his eyes and stung me to the quick.

===Chinese===
他认出是我，愈发一言不发，可一双眼里明明白白盛满了怜悯，把我刺了个透心彻骨。

===Original===
I have a faculty of self-pity, but my pride promptly refuses the slightest offer of sympathy from another.

===Chinese===
自怜的本事我倒是有，可我的自尊心向来一口回绝旁人施舍的哪怕一丝怜悯。

===Original===
I could feel my heart grow as bitterly cold as my demeanour was icily stiff, when I stood up and said curtly:

===Chinese===
我站起身，简慢地说道——只觉得心同我那副冷冰冰的僵硬做派一样，寒得透骨：

===Original===
“This is a great surprise, Mr. Beecham.”

===Chinese===
“这真是天大的意外，比彻姆先生。”

===Original===
“Not an unpleasant one, I hope,” he said pleasantly.

===Chinese===
“但愿不是个不愉快的意外。”他和颜悦色地说。

===Original===
“We will not discuss the matter. Come inside out of the heat.”

===Chinese===
“这件事咱们不谈。快进屋吧，外头热。”

===Original===
“I’m in no hurry, Syb, and couldn’t I help you with that poor little devil?”

===Chinese===
“我着什么急，西比。让我搭把手，帮你照看照看这可怜的小鬼头，不行吗？”

===Original===
“I’m only trying to give it another chance of life.”

===Chinese===
“我不过是想再给它一条活路。”

===Original===
“What will you do with it if it lives?”

===Chinese===
“它要是活下来，你打算拿它怎么办？”

===Original===
“Sell it for half a crown when it’s a yearling.”

===Chinese===
“等它长成周岁牛犊，卖它半克朗（half a crown）。”

===Original===
“It would pay better to shoot the poor little beggar now.”

===Chinese===
“依我看，趁早崩了这可怜的小东西，倒还上算些。”

===Original===
“No doubt it would the owner of Five-Bob, but we have to be more careful,” I said tartly.

===Chinese===
“对五博丘的东家来说自然上算，可我们不得不多加小心。”我尖刻地说。

===Original===
“I didn’t mean to offend you.”

===Chinese===
“我不是存心冒犯你。”

===Original===
“I’m not offended,” I returned, leading the way to the house, imagining with a keen pain that Harold Beecham must be wondering how for an instant he could have been foolish enough to fancy such an object two years ago.

===Chinese===
“我才没有恼。”我答道，一边领路往屋里走，心里却针扎似的痛，想着哈罗德·比彻姆这会儿准在纳闷：两年前自己怎么会有那么一时糊涂，竟看上了这么一件货色。

===Original===
Thank goodness I have never felt any humiliation on account of my mother, and felt none then, as she rose to greet Harold upon my introduction. She was a lady, and looked it, in spite of the piles of coarse mending, and the pair of trousers, almost bulletproof with patches, out of which she drew her hand, roughened and reddened with hard labour, in spite of her patched and faded cotton gown, and the commonest and most poverty-stricken of peasant surroundings, which failed to hide that she had not been always thus.

===Chinese===
谢天谢地，我从没因为母亲感到过半分羞辱，那一刻也没有。经我引见，她起身招呼哈罗德。任凭眼前堆着一堆堆粗布缝补，任凭她从那条补丁摞补丁、几乎刀枪不入的裤子里抽出来的那只手，已被苦役磨得又粗又红，任凭她身上是打了补丁、褪了色的棉布衣裙，处在最寻常、最赤贫的庄户环境当中——她终归是位淑女，而且一望便知；这环境遮掩不住：她并非生来如此。

===Original===
