Leaving them together, I expeditiously proceeded to relieve the livery-stable horse, on which Harold had come, of the valise, saddle, and bridle with which it was encumbered, and then let it loose in one of the grassless paddocks near at hand.

===Chinese===
把他们俩留在客厅，我手脚麻利地跑去卸哈罗德骑来的那匹租马行的马，把它驮的提包、马鞍和笼头一一卸下，随后撒开放进近旁一块寸草不生的围场里。

===Original===
Then I threw myself on a stool in the kitchen, and felt, to the bone, the sting of having ideas above one’s position.

===Chinese===
然后我一屁股跌坐在厨房的凳子上，彻头彻尾、入骨地尝到了那份“心气儿高过命”的刺痛。

===Original===
In a few minutes mother came hurrying out.

===Chinese===
不多会儿，母亲急匆匆出来了。

===Original===
“Good gracious, what’s the matter? I suppose you didn’t like being caught in such a pickle, but don’t get in the dumps about it. I’ll get him some tea while you clean yourself, and then you’ll be able to help me by and by.”

===Chinese===
“老天爷，这是怎么了？我猜你是嫌让人撞见那副狼狈相，可别为这个蔫头耷脑的。趁你拾掇自己，我去给他备茶，待会儿你也好帮我搭把手。”

===Original===
I found my little sister Aurora, and we climbed through the window into my bedroom to get tidy. I put a pair of white socks and shoes and a clean pinafore on the little girl, and combed her golden curls. She was all mine﻿—slept with me, obeyed me, championed me; while I﻿—well, I worshipped her.

===Chinese===
我寻到小妹妹奥罗拉，娘儿俩从窗口爬进我的卧室去梳洗打扮。我给小姑娘穿上白袜子和鞋，罩上一件干净的围涎衫，又给她梳好一头金色鬈发。她整个儿是我的——同我睡，听我的话，处处护着我；而我呢——嗐，我把她当神明似的拜着。

===Original===
There was a hole in the wall, and through it I could see without being seen.

===Chinese===
墙上有一个洞，隔着洞我看得见人，人却看不见我。

===Original===
Mother was dispensing afternoon tea and talking to Harold. It was pleasant to see that manly figure once again. My spirits rose considerably. After all, if the place was poor, it was very clean, as I had scrubbed it all that morning, and when I came to consider the matter, I remembered that men weren’t such terrible creatures, and never made one feel the sting of one’s poverty half as much as women do.

===Chinese===
母亲正张罗午后茶，陪着哈罗德说话。又见到那副男子汉的身影，真叫人愉快。我的心绪高涨了不少。毕竟，这地方虽说寒素，却非常干净——一早上全是我刷洗出来的；细想起来，我又记起男人原不是那么可怕的生灵，他们叫人尝到的贫穷刺痛，从来抵不上女人给的一半。

===Original===
“Aurora,” I said, I want you to go out and tell Mr. Beecham something.

===Chinese===
“奥罗拉，”我说，我要你出去，告诉比彻姆先生一句话。

===Original===
The little girl assented. I carefully instructed her in what she was to say, and dispatched her. She placed herself in front of Harold﻿—a wide-eyed mite of four, that scarcely reached above his knee﻿—and clasping her chubby hands behind her, gazed at him fearlessly and unwinkingly.

===Chinese===
小姑娘应承下来。我把她该说的话仔仔细细嘱咐了一遍，便打发她出去。她在哈罗德跟前站定——一个睁圆了眼睛的四岁小不点儿，个头儿刚够着他膝盖——两只胖乎乎的小手扣在背后，毫不怯场、一眨不眨地盯着他瞧。

===Original===
“Aurora, you mustn’t stand staring like that,” said mother.

===Chinese===
“奥罗拉，不许那样直勾勾盯着人家。”母亲说。

===Original===
“Yes, I must,” she replied confidently.

===Chinese===
“对，我就得这样盯着。”她信心十足地答道。

===Original===
“Well, and what’s your name?” said Harold laughingly.

===Chinese===
“那么，你叫什么名字呀？”哈罗德笑着说。

===Original===
“Aurora and Roy. I belong to Sybyller, and got to tell you somesing.”

===Chinese===
“奥罗拉和罗伊。俺是西比拉尔的，有件事要告许你。”

===Original===
“Have you? Let’s hear it.”

===Chinese===
“是吗？说来听听。”

===Original===
“Sybyller says you’s Mr. Beecher; when you’re done tea, you’d like me if I would to ’scort you to farver and the boys, and ’duce you.”

===Chinese===
“西比拉尔说你是比彻先生；等你喝完茶，要是我肯领你去见俺爹和哥哥们，再给你引见引见，你就会喜欢俺的。”

===Original===
Mother laughed. “That’s some of Sybylla’s nonsense. She considers Rory her especial property, and delights to make the child attempt long words. Perhaps you would care to take a stroll to where they are at work, by and by.”

===Chinese===
母亲笑了。“这是西比拉的胡闹名堂之一。她把罗瑞（Rory）当成自个儿的专属私产，就爱支使这孩子去啃长字眼。待会儿您若愿意，可以溜达着到他们干活的地方去一趟。”

===Original===
Harold said he would go at once, and accepting Rory’s escort, and with a few directions from mother, they presently set out﻿—she importantly trudging beneath a big white sunbonnet, and he looking down at her in amusement. Presently he tossed her high above his head, and depositing her upon his shoulder, held one sturdy brown leg in his browner hand, while she held on by his hair.

===Chinese===
哈罗德说这就去，于是应下了罗瑞带路，又听母亲指点了几句，两人随即出发——她顶着一顶大白遮阳帽，郑重其事地迈着小短腿往前赶；他则饶有兴味地低头看她。不多时，他把她高高抛过头顶，又把她安放上自己的肩头，用一只更棕黑的手攥住她一条结实的棕色小腿，她呢，则揪住他的头发不放。

===Original===
“My first impressions are very much in his favour,” said mother, when they had got out of hearing. “But fancy Gertie the wife of that great man!”

===Chinese===
“我对他的头一印象好得很。”等他们走远听不见了，母亲说道。“可你想想，格蒂给这么一位大人物做太太！”

===Original===
“She is four inches taller than I am,” I snapped. “And if he was as big as a gum-tree, he would be a man all the same, and just as soft on a pretty face as all the rest of them.”

===Chinese===
“她比我高出四英寸呢。”我没好气地说。“就算他长得同桉树一般高大，他也终归是个男人，见了漂亮脸蛋儿照样心软，跟其余那帮人一路货。”

===Original===
I bathed, dressed, arranged my hair, got something ready for tea, and prepared a room for our visitor. For this I collected from all parts of the house﻿—a mat from one room, a toilet-set from another, and so on﻿—till I had quite an elaborately furnished chamber ready for my onetime lover.

===Chinese===
我洗了澡，穿好衣裳，理了头发，备下茶点的吃食，又给我们的客人收拾出一个房间。为这个，我从屋子各处搜集——这屋拿一块地席，那屋凑一套梳妆用具，如此等等——直到为我昔日的情人备好了一间陈设得颇为讲究的寝室。

===Original===
They returned at dusk, Rory again seated on Harold’s shoulder, and two of the little boys clinging around him.

===Chinese===
黄昏时分他们回来了，罗瑞又骑在哈罗德肩上，两个小男孩一左一右攀着他。

===Original===
As I conducted him to his room I was in a different humour from that of the sweep-like object who had met him during the afternoon. I laughed to myself, for, as on a former occasion during our acquaintance, I felt I was master of the situation.

===Chinese===
领他进房时，我的心情已同下午那个烟囱刷子似的人物判若两人。我暗自发笑，因为正如我们相识以来的前一回那样，我觉得这场面的主宰又是我。

===Original===
“I say, Syb, don’t treat a fellow as though he was altogether a stranger,” he said diffidently, leaning against the doorpost.

===Chinese===
“我说，西比，别把人家当成十足十的外人嘛。”他倚着门柱，讷讷地说。

===Original===
Our hands met in a cordial grasp as I said, “I’m awfully glad to see you, Hal; but, but﻿—”

===Chinese===
我们热络而真诚地握了手，我说：“见到你我高兴极了，哈尔；可是，可是——”

===Original===
“But what?”

===Chinese===
“可是什么？”

===Original===
“I didn’t feel over delighted to be caught in such a stew this afternoon.”

===Chinese===
“下午让你撞见我那副烂糟糟的狼狈相，我心里可算不上舒坦。”

===Original===
“Nonsense! It only reminded me of the first time we met,” he said with a twinkle in his eye. “That’s always the way with you girls. You can’t be civil to a man unless you’re dressed up fit to stun him, as though you couldn’t make fool enough of him without the aid of clothes at all.”

===Chinese===
“胡说！那倒叫我想起咱们头一回见面来。”他眼里闪着促狭的光，说道。“你们这些姑娘从来都是这路数：不打扮得能把男人震晕过去，就没法对人家客气，仿佛不借衣裳的力，你们就没本事把男人耍得够傻似的。”

===Original===
“You’d better shut up,” I said over my shoulder as I departed, “or you will be saying something better left unsaid, like at our first meeting. Do you remember?”

===Chinese===
“你还是闭嘴的好，”我临走时回头甩下一句，“不然你又要说出些不说为妙的话来，就像咱们初次见面那回一样。你还记得不？”

===Original===
“Do I not? Great Scot, it’s just like old times to have you giving me impudence over your shoulder like that!” he replied merrily.

===Chinese===
“怎么不记得！我的老天，你这样扭过头来给我上脸，可真跟从前一个样！”他乐呵呵地答道。

===Original===
“Like, yet unlike,” I retorted with a sigh.

===Chinese===
“像，又不像。”我叹了口气，回敬道。

## XXXVI Once Upon a Time, When the Days Were Long and Hot / 第36章 从前，日子漫长而炎热

===Original===
Next day was Sunday﻿—a blazing one it was too. I proposed that in the afternoon some of us should go to church. Father sat upon the idea as a mad one. Walk two miles in such heat for nothing! as walk we would be compelled to do, horseflesh being too precious in such a drought to fritter it away in idle jaunts. Surprising to say, however, Harold, who never walked anywhere when he could get any sort of a horse, uttered a wish to go. Accordingly, when the midday dinner was over, he, Stanley, and I set out. Going to church was quite the event of the week to the residents around Possum Gully. It was a small Dissenting chapel, where a layman ungrammatically held forth at 3 p.m. every Sunday; but the congregation was composed of all denominations, who attended more for the sitting about on logs outside, and yarning about the price of butter, the continuance of the drought, and the latest gossip, before and after the service, than for the service itself.

===Chinese===
第二天是星期天——而且是个火烧火燎的大热天。我提议下午我们几个人去做礼拜。父亲一听便把这主意断为疯念：这样的暑天，白白走上两英里！何况我们非靠两条腿走不可——大旱年头马力金贵，犯不上为闲逛糟蹋牲口。然而说来出奇，哈罗德——那个但凡弄得到一匹马、再蹩脚也决不肯走路的人——竟说他想去。于是正午的饭一吃完，他便同斯坦利和我一道上了路。在袋熊谷左近的居民看来，上教堂实在是当周的一件大事。那是一座小小的非国教礼拜堂，每逢星期日下午三点，由一位平信徒文法不通地宣讲一番；可会众各宗各派的人都有，他们上这儿来，与其说是为了礼拜本身，毋宁说是为了礼拜前后能在外头的圆木上坐一坐，讲讲黄油的价钱、旱情还要拖多久，再交换些最新的闲话。

===Original===
I knew the appearance of Harold Beecham would make quite a miniature sensation, and form food for no end of conjecture and chatter. In any company he was a distinguished-looking man, and particularly so among these hard-worked farmer-selectors, on whose careworn features the cruel effects of the drought were leaving additional lines of worry. I felt proud of my quondam sweetheart. There was an unconscious air of physical lordliness about him, and he looked such a swell﻿—not the black-clothed, clean-shaved, great display of white collar-and-cuffs swell appertaining to the office and city street, but of the easy sunburnt squatter type of swelldom, redolent of the sun, the saddle, the wide open country﻿—a man who is a man, utterly free from the least suspicion of effeminacy, and capable of earning his bread by the sweat of his brow﻿—with an arm ready and willing to save in an accident.

===Chinese===
我知道哈罗德·比彻姆这一露面，准会引起一场小小的轰动，招来没完没了的揣测与嚼舌。无论在什么人堆里，他都是个仪表堂堂的人物，在这群操劳过度的选地农中间尤其如此——旱灾的酷烈，正在他们愁苦的面容上添刻一道道忧虑的纹路。我为昔日的情郎感到骄傲。他周身有一种不自觉的体魄上的王者气派，看上去好一派时髦人物的丰采——不是那种黑呢衣装、脸刮得精光、白领白袖口大张旗鼓、属于写字楼与通衢大街一路的时髦，而是舒展从容、晒得黢黑的大牧场主型时髦，浑身散发着阳光、马鞍和辽阔旷野的气息——一个顶天立地的男子汉，全然不带一丝娘娘腔的嫌疑，凭额头的汗水挣得自己的面包——还有一条臂膀，遇险时随时乐意出手相救。

===Original===
All eyes were turned on us as we approached, and I knew that the attentions he paid me out of simple courtesy﻿—tying my shoe, carrying my book, holding my parasol﻿—would be put down as those of a lover.

===Chinese===
我们走近时，所有的眼睛都转向我们；我知道，他出于寻常礼数对我献的那些殷勤——替我系鞋带、替我拿书、替我撑阳伞——都会被记到情郎账上。

===Original===
I introduced him to a group of men who were sitting on a log, under the shade of a stringybark, and leaving him to converse with them, made my way to where the women sat beneath a gum-tree. The children made a third group at some distance. We always divided ourselves thus. A young fellow had to be very far gone ere he was willing to run the gauntlet of all the chaff levelled at him had he the courage to single out a girl and talk to her.

===Chinese===
我把他引见给坐在一棵条皮桉树荫下一根圆木上的一群男人，把他留给他们说话，自己径直走到女人们围坐的那棵桉树底下。孩子们自成第三堆，离得稍远。我们向来这样三分。一个小伙子必得神魂颠倒到了不轻的地步，才肯豁出去闯那一片众口齐发的打趣的火力网——倘若他真有胆量单单挑出一位姑娘来攀谈的话。

===Original===
I greeted all the girls and women, beginning at the great-grandmother of the community, who illustrated to perfection the grim sarcasm of the fifth commandment. She had worked hard from morning till night, until too old to do so longer, and now hung around with aching weariness waiting for the grave. She generally poured into my ears a wail about her “rheumatisms,” and “How long it do be waiting for the Lord;” but today she was too curious about Harold to think of herself.

===Chinese===
我挨个儿问候姑娘们和女人们，头一位是全社区的老曾祖母——她把第五诫的冷酷讽刺演绎得淋漓尽致。她从早到晚苦干了一辈子，直到老得再也做不动，如今拖着酸痛的困乏在人前打转，只等坟墓来收。她往常总往我耳朵里灌一通“风湿病”的哀叹，念叨“俺等主啊，还得等多久哟”；可今天她对哈罗德的好奇心太重，顾不得想自己了。

===Original===
“Sure, Sybyller, who’s that? Is he yer sweetheart? Sure he’s as fine a man as iver I clapped me eyes on.”

===Chinese===
“可不，西比拉尔，那是谁呀？是你的情郎吗？说真的，打俺生眼珠子瞧人起，还没见过这么齐整的一条汉子哩。”

===Original===
I proceeded to give his pedigree, but was interrupted by the arrival of the preacher, and we all went into the weatherboard iron-roofed house of prayer.

===Chinese===
我正要细数他的家世来历，讲道人到了，话头就此打断，大家便一齐走进那座木板铁皮顶的祈祷之所。

===Original===
After service, one of the girls came up to me and whispered, “that is your sweetheart, isn’t it, Sybyller? He was looking at you all the time in church.”

===Chinese===
礼拜完毕，一个姑娘凑上来咬耳朵：“那是你的情郎吧，西比拉尔？刚才在教堂里，他一双眼睛就没离开过你。”

===Original===
“Oh dear, no! I’ll introduce him to you.”

===Chinese===
“哎呀，才不是呢！我引见给你。”

===Original===
I did so, and watched him as they made remarks about the heat and drought. There was nothing of the cad or snob about him, and his short season of adversity had rubbed all the little crudities off his character, leaving him a man that the majority of both sexes would admire: women for his bigness, his gentleness, his fine brown moustache﻿—and for his wealth; men, because he was a manly fellow.

===Chinese===
我果真引见了，一边看他同她们寒暄，谈这暑热与旱情。他身上没有半点粗坯或势利鬼的味道，而短短一段逆境，已把他性格里所有细小的毛糙磨得干干净净，留下的这个男人，两性中的大多数都会倾慕：女人们倾慕他的魁伟、他的温存、他那两撇漂亮的棕色髭须——还有他的家财；男人们倾慕，因为他是个有丈夫气的汉子。

===Original===
I know he had walked to church on purpose to get a chance of speaking to me about Gertie, before approaching her parents on the matter; but Stanley accompanied us, and, boy-like, never relaxed in vigilance for an instant, so there was no opportunity for anything but matter-of-fact remarks. The heat was intense. We wiped the perspiration and flies from our face frequently, and disturbed millions of grasshoppers as we walked. They had devoured all the fruit in the orchards about, and had even destroyed many of the trees by eating the bark, and now they were stripping the briers of foliage. In one orchard we passed, the apricot, plum, and peach-stones hung naked on their leafless trees as evidence of their ravages. It was too hot to indulge in any but the most desultory conversation. We dawdled along. A tiger-snake crossed our path. Harold procured a stick and killed it, and Stanley hung it on the top wire of a fence which was near at hand. After this we discussed snakes for a few yards.

===Chinese===
我知道，他特意走路来做礼拜，就为寻个机会，在为此事去向我父母开口之前，先同我谈谈格蒂；可斯坦利一路跟着我们，一副孩子脾性，警惕片刻不曾松懈，于是除了就事论事的闲话，什么机会也没有。暑热逼人。我们频频抹去脸上的汗水和苍蝇，一路惊起千千万万只蚂蚱。它们把四周果园里的果子啃了个精光，甚至啃掉树皮毁了许多树，如今又在剥荆棘丛的叶子。路过一处果园，只见杏核、李核、桃核光溜溜地挂在精光的树上，正是它们肆虐的罪证。天热得只容得下最散漫的交谈。我们磨磨蹭蹭地走。一条虎蛇（tiger-snake）横过我们的路。哈罗德寻了根棍子把它打死，斯坦利把它挂上近旁栅栏最上头的一道铁丝。此后我们就蛇的话题，又谈了几步路的工夫。

===Original===
A blue sea-breeze, redolent of the bushfires which were raging at Tocumwal and Bombala, came rushing and roaring over the ranges from the cast, and enshrouded the scene in its heavy fog-like folds. The sun was obscured, and the temperature suddenly took such a great drop that I felt chilled in my flimsy clothing, and I noticed Harold draw his coat together.

===Chinese===
一股蓝色的海风，带着托库姆瓦尔（Tocumwal）和邦巴拉（Bombala）两地熊熊野火的气味，从东面翻过山岭奔涌呼啸而来，用它浓雾似的重重褶层把四野罩住。太阳隐没了，气温骤然一跌，我穿着单薄的衣裳只觉寒意袭身，又瞥见哈罗德把外衣拢紧了。

===Original===
Stanley had to go after the cows, which were little better than walking hides, yet were yarded morning and evening to yield a dribble of milk. He left us among some sallie-trees, in a secluded nook, walled in by briers, and went across the paddock to roundup the cows. Harold and I came to a halt by tacit consent.

===Chinese===
斯坦利得去追牛了——那些牛瘦得同会走路的皮囊差不了多少，却还早晚两趟赶进栏里，挤出一线细流似的奶。他把我们留在金合欢树间一个僻静的角落，四围荆棘环抱如墙，自己穿过围场拢牛去了。哈罗德和我心照不宣地停住了脚。

===Original===
“Syb, I want to speak to you,” he said earnestly, and then came to a dead stop.

===Chinese===
“西比，我有话要同你说。”他恳切地说，随即戛然而止。

===Original===
“Very well; ‘tear into it,’ as Horace would say; but if it is anything frightful, break it gently,” I said flippantly.

===Chinese===
“好嘛；用霍勒斯的话说，‘放马过来吧’；不过要是桩骇人的事，可要委婉着慢慢说。”我油嘴滑舌地说。

===Original===
“Surely, Syb, you can guess what it is I have to say.”

===Chinese===
“西比，你总猜得着我要说的是什么呀。”

===Original===
Yes, I could guess, I knew what he was going to say, and the knowledge left a dull bitterness at my heart. I knew he was going to tell me that I had been right and he wrong﻿—that he had found someone he loved better than me, and that someone being my sister, he felt I needed some explanation before he could go in and win; and though I had refused him for want of love, yet it gave me pain when the moment arrived that the only man who had ever pretended to love me was going to say he had been mistaken, and preferred my sister.

===Chinese===
是啊，我猜得到，我知道他要说什么，而这知情在我心头留下一片钝钝的苦涩。我知道他要对我说，一直是我对、他错——他找到了一个他爱之胜于我的人，而那人既是我的妹妹，他觉得总该先向我交代一番，才好进场去赢她的心；尽管我当初因缺了爱而拒绝过他，可当这一刻当真到来——世上唯一假装爱过我的男人就要亲口说，他从前认错了人，宁要我妹妹——我心里还是一阵刺痛。

===Original===
There was silence save for the whirr of the countless grasshoppers in the brier bushes. I knew he was expecting me to help him out, but I felt doggedly savage and wouldn’t. I looked up at him. He was a tall grand man, and honest and true and rich. He loved my sister; she would marry him, and they would be happy. I thought bitterly that God was good to one and cruel to another﻿—not that I wanted this man, but why was I so different from other girls?

===Chinese===
四下里一片沉静，只有数不清的蚂蚱在荆棘丛里嗡嗡振翅。我知道他指望我替他解围，我却憋着一股狠劲，偏不。我抬眼望他。他是个高大堂堂的男人，诚实、可靠，又有钱。他爱我的妹妹；她会嫁给他，他们会幸福。我辛酸地想，上帝待一个人慈，待另一个人就狠——倒不是我想要这个男人，可为什么我同别的姑娘这样不同？

===Original===
But then I thought of Gertie, so pretty, so girlish, so understandable, so full of innocent winning coquetry. I softened. Could anyone help preferring her to me, who was strange, weird, and perverse﻿—too outspoken to be engaging, devoid of beauty and endearing little ways? It was my own misfortune and nobody’s fault that my singular individuality excluded me from the ordinary run of youthful joyous-heartednesses, and why should I be nasty to these young people?

===Chinese===
可接着我就想到了格蒂——那样漂亮，那样娇憨，那样容易看懂，那样满是天真的、讨人欢喜的小娇态。我心软了。比起我来——这么古怪、这么乖僻、这么执拗，说话太直叫人亲近不来，又没有姿色，没有一点招人疼的小情态——谁又能怪人家偏爱她呢？我这份独一无二的个性把我摈在了寻常少年轻快心怀的门外，这是我自己的不幸，怪不得任何人，我又何苦刁难这两个年轻人？

===Original===
I was no heroine, only a common little bush-girl, so had to make the best of the situation without any fooling. I raised my eyes from the scanty baked wisps of grass at my feet, placed my hand on Hal’s arm, and tiptoeing so as to bring my five-foot stature more on a level with his, said:

===Chinese===
我不是什么女主角，不过是个平平常常的丛林小丫头，只好不闹虚头、就着眼前的局面尽力应付。我把目光从脚边稀稀拉拉、烤得焦干的几绺草上抬起来，把手搭上哈尔的臂膀，踮起脚尖，好让我五英尺的个子同他近乎齐平，说道：

===Original===
“Yes, Hal, I know what you want to say. Say it all. I won’t be nasty.”

===Chinese===
“好吧，哈尔，我知道你要说什么。全都说了吧。我不会使性子。”

===Original===
“Well, you see you are so jolly touchy, and have snubbed me so often, that I don’t know how to begin; and if you know what I’m going to say, won’t you give me an answer without hearing it?”

===Chinese===
“唉，你是知道的，你那脾气火爆得厉害，抢白我又抢白惯了，教我不知打哪儿开口；既然你已经知道我要说什么，能不能不听就把答复发给我？”

===Original===
“Yes, Hal; but you’d better say it, as I don’t know what conditions﻿—”

===Chinese===
“可以，哈尔；不过你还是说出来的好，因为我不知道有些什么条件——”

===Original===
“Conditions!”﻿—catching me up eagerly at the word. “If it is only conditions that are stopping you, you can make your own conditions if you will marry me.”

===Chinese===
“条件！”——他一听这字眼便急急地一把抓住。“倘若拦住你的只是条件，那么只要你肯嫁我，条件尽由你开。”

===Original===
“Marry you, Harold! What do you mean? Do you know what you are saying?” I exclaimed.

===Chinese===
“嫁给你，哈罗德！你这是什么意思？你可知道自己在说什么？”我叫了起来。

===Original===
“There!” he replied: “I knew you would take it as an insult. I believe you are the proudest girl in the world. I know you are too clever for me; but I love you, and could give you everything you fancied.”

===Chinese===
“瞧！”他答道，“我就知道你会把这当成一桩侮辱。我看你是天底下顶骄傲的姑娘。我知道你聪明得叫我追不上；可我爱你，凡你中意的东西，我都给得起。”

===Original===
“Hal, dear, let me explain. I’m not insulted, only surprised. I thought you were going to tell me that you loved Gertie, and would ask me not to make things unpleasant by telling her of the foolish little bit of flirtation there had been between us.”

===Chinese===
“哈尔，亲爱的，听我解释。我并没有受辱之感，只是吃惊。我只当你是要告诉我，你爱的是格蒂，还要我别把我们之间有过的那段傻乎乎的小调情讲给她听，免得把事情弄僵。”

===Original===
“Marry Gertie! Why, she’s only a child! A mere baby, in fact. Marry Gertie! I never thought of her in that light; and did you think I was that sort of a fellow, Syb?” he asked reproachfully.

===Chinese===
“娶格蒂！嗐，她还是个孩子！实实在在是个小娃娃。娶格蒂！我从没往那上头想过她；西比，你莫非当我是那一路的人？”他带着责问的口气说。

===Original===
“No, Hal,” I promptly made answer. “I did not think you were that sort of fellow; but I thought that was the only sort of fellow there was.”

===Chinese===
“不是，哈尔，”我立刻答道。“我并没把你当成那一路的人；我不过以为，天底下统共就只有那一路的人。”

===Original===
“Good heavens, Syb! Did you really mean those queer little letters you wrote me last February? I never for an instant looked upon them as anything but a little bit of playful contrariness. And have you forgotten me? Did you not mean your promise of two years ago, that you speak of what passed between us as a paltry bit of flirtation? Is that all you thought it?”

===Chinese===
“老天爷，西比！今年二月你写给我的那些古怪的小信，竟是当真的？我从前一刻也不曾把它们看作别的，只当是一点儿闹着玩儿的别扭。这么说，你把我忘了？两年前你许下的诺言也不算了，倒把我们之间的事说成一段无足轻重的调情？你当初想着的，就只有这么一点点？”

===Original===
“No, I did not consider it flirtation; but that is what I thought you would term it when announcing your affection for Gertie.”

===Chinese===
“不，我没把它当调情看；我不过料定，等你宣布对格蒂的情意时，多半会把它叫成那么一回事。”

===Original===
“Gertie! Pretty little Gertie! I never looked upon the child as anything but your sister, consequently mine also. She’s a child.”

===Chinese===
“格蒂！漂亮的小格蒂！我从没拿这孩子当过别的，只当是你的妹妹，自然也是我的妹妹。她还是个孩子。”

===Original===
“Child! She is eighteen. More than a year older than I was when you first introduced the subject of matrimony to me, and she is very beautiful, and twenty times as good and lovable as I could ever be even in my best moments.”

===Chinese===
“孩子！她都十八了。比你头一回同我提起婚姻大事那阵子，我还大着一岁多哩；何况她生得很美，心肠又好、又惹人疼，哪怕我在自己最好的时刻，也及不上她的二十分之一。”

===Original===
“Yes, I know you are young in years, but there is nothing of the child in you. As for beauty, it is nothing. If beauty was all a man required, he could, if rich, have a harem full of it any day. I want someone to be true.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，我知道论年纪你还轻，可你身上没有一点儿孩子气。至于美貌，那不算什么。男人要的若光是美貌，那他只要有钱，哪一天都能置满一屋子后宫。我要的是一个真心实意的人。”

===Original===
> “The world is filled with folly and sin,
 

 And love must cling where it can, I say;
 

 For beauty is easy enough to win,
 

 But one isn’t loved every day,”

===Chinese===
> “这世界满是愚妄与罪愆，
  
 
 我说，爱情只得攀住它攀得住的地方；
  
 
 因为美貌原就不难赢得，
  
 
 可被人爱着，并非每天都有的事。”

===Original===
I quoted from Owen Meredith.

===Chinese===
我引的是欧文·梅雷迪思（Owen Meredith）的诗。

===Original===
“Yes,” he said, “that is why I want you. Just think a moment; don’t say no. You are not vexed with me﻿—are you, Syb?”

===Chinese===
“正是，”他说，“所以我要的正是你。想想吧，先别说‘不’。你没有恼我吧——是不是，西比？”

===Original===
“Vexed, Hal! I am scarcely inhuman enough to be angry on account of being loved.”

===Chinese===
“恼你，哈尔！我还不至于狠毒到那步田地，竟会因为被人爱着而动怒。”

===Original===
Ah, why did I not love him as I have it in me to love! Why did he look so exasperatingly humble? I was weak, oh, so pitifully weak! I wanted a man who would be masterful and strong, who would help me over the rough spots of life﻿—one who had done hard grinding in the mill of fate﻿—one who had suffered, who had understood. No; I could never marry Harold Beecham.

===Chinese===
啊，我为什么不能爱他——用我心底蕴着的那份爱去爱！为什么他瞧上去谦卑得这样叫人着急！我软弱，唉，软弱得可怜！我要的是一个有丈夫气概、强有力的男人，能扶我越过人生崎岖之处的男人——一个在命运的磨盘里狠狠研磨过的——一个受过苦、懂得了的人。不；我永远嫁不得哈罗德·比彻姆。

===Original===
“Well, Syb, little chum, what do you say?”

===Chinese===
“那么，西比，小伙伴，你怎么说？”

===Original===
“Say!”﻿—and the words fell from me bitterly﻿—“I say, leave me; go and marry the sort of woman you ought to marry. The sort that all men like. A good conventional woman, who will do the things she should at the proper time. Leave me alone.”

===Chinese===
“怎么说！”——这话从我嘴里落下来，字字带着苦味——“我说，离开我；去娶你该娶的那类女人吧。全体男人都喜欢的那一类。一个规规矩矩的好女人，该做什么事的时节，就把什么事按时做好。别再管我了。”

===Original===
He was painfully agitated. A look of pain crossed his face.

===Chinese===
他激动得难受。一丝痛楚掠过他的脸。

===Original===
“Don’t say that, Syb, because I was a beastly cad once: I’ve had all that knocked out of me.”

===Chinese===
“别那么说，西比。我从前确实是个十足的下流坯：可那一切，如今都从我这身上敲打出去了。”

===Original===
“I am the cad,” I replied. “What I said was nasty and unwomanly, and I wish I had left it unsaid. I am not good enough to be your wife, Hal, or that of any man. Oh, Hal, I have never deceived you! There are scores of good noble women in the world who would wed you for the asking﻿—marry one of them.”

===Chinese===
“下流坯是我。”我答道。“我方才那话又恶毒又不像个女人，但愿我没说出口才好。我做你的妻子不够格，哈尔，做任何男人的妻子都不够格。啊，哈尔，我从没有骗过你！这世上有的是善良高贵的女人，只消你开口就肯嫁你——娶她们中的一个吧。”

===Original===
“But, Syb, I want you. You are the best and truest girl in the world.”

===Chinese===
“可我要的就是你，西比。你是世上最好、最真心的姑娘。”

===Original===
“Och! Sure, the blarney-stone is getting a good rub now,” I said playfully.

===Chinese===
“哎呀！可不，这会儿巧言石（blarney-stone）可给你磨得光溜溜喽。”我打趣道。

===Original===
Annoyance and amusement struggled for mastery in his expression as he replied:

===Chinese===
他答话时，神情里恼意和好笑相持不下：

===Original===
“You’re the queerest girl in the world. One minute you snub a person, the next you are the jolliest girl going, and then you get as grave and earnest as a fellow’s mother would be.”

===Chinese===
“你是天底下顶顶古怪的姑娘。这一分钟抢白人，下一分钟又活得比谁都乐呵，转眼又板起脸、一本正经，赛过人家小伙子的老娘。”

===Original===
“Yes, I am queer. If you had any sense, you’d have nothing to do with me. I’m more queer, too. I am given to something which a man never pardons in a woman. You will draw away as though I were a snake when you hear.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，我是古怪。你要是有半分见识，就断不该同我沾边。我还有更古怪的哩。我染上一样毛病，是男人在女人身上永远不肯饶恕的。等会儿你一听，就会像躲蛇似的躲开我。”

===Original===
“What is it?”

===Chinese===
“什么毛病？”

===Original===
“I am given to writing stories, and literary people predict I will yet be an authoress.”

===Chinese===
“我染上了写小说的毛病，而且懂文学的人预言，我早晚要当个女作家。”

===Original===
He laughed﻿—his soft, rich laugh.

===Chinese===
他笑了——那柔和而饱满的笑。

===Original===
“That’s just into my hand. I’d rather work all day than write the shortest letter; so if you will give me a hand occasionally, you can write as many yarns as you like. I’ll give you a study, and send for a truck-load of writing-gear at once, if you like. Is that the only horror you had to tell me?”

===Chinese===
“那可正中我的下怀。叫我干一整天活也强过写一封顶短的信；往后你只要时不时帮我搭一把手，爱写多少故事尽管写去。你若愿意，我给你一间书房，即刻派人去订上一卡车的文具。这就是你非告诉我不可的唯一一桩可怕事了？”

===Original===
I bowed my head.

===Chinese===
我低下头去。

===Original===
“Well, I can have you now,” he said gently, folding me softly in his arms with such tender reverence that I cried out in pain, “Oh, Hal, don’t, don’t!” and struggled free. I was ashamed, knowing I was not worthy of this.

===Chinese===
“那么，这会儿我可得到你了。”他温柔地说，带着那样柔情的敬意把我轻轻搂进怀里，疼得我叫出声来：“啊，哈尔，别，别这样！”挣脱开了。我羞愧难当，因为我知道自己不配受这个。

===Original===
He flushed a dusky red.

===Chinese===
他脸上泛起一层暗红。

===Original===
“Am I so hateful to you that you cannot bear my touch?” he asked half wistfully, half angrily.

===Chinese===
“我竟这样惹你厌，连碰一碰你都受不住？”他问道，半是怅惘，半是恼怒。

===Original===
“Oh no; it isn’t that. I’m really very fond of you, if you’d only understand,” I said half to myself.

===Chinese===
“啊，不；不是那么回事。我是真心喜欢你，只要你肯明白。”我半是自言自语地说。

===Original===
“Understand! If you care for me, that is all I want to understand. I love you, and have plenty of money. There is nothing to keep us apart. Now that I know you care for me, I *will* have you, in spite of the devil.”

===Chinese===
“明白！只要你心里有我，我要弄明白的就全有了。我爱你，又有的是钱。没有什么能隔开我们。如今我知道你心里也有我，任凭魔鬼作对，我*一定*要得到你。”

===Original===
“There will be a great tussle between you,” I said mischievously, laughing at him. “Old Nick has a great hold on me, and I’m sure he will dispute your right.”

===Chinese===
“那你俩可有一场大架好打啦。”我淘气地说，一面笑话他。“老尼克在我身上的势力可大着呢，我敢保他定要出来争一争你的所有权。”

===Original===
At any time Harold’s sense of humour was not at all in accordance with his size, and he failed to see how my remark applied now.

===Chinese===
哈罗德的幽默感无论何时都同他的块头全不相称，此刻也没听出我这话用意何在。

===Original===
He gripped my hands in a passion of pleading, as two years previously he had seized me in jealous rage. He drew me to him. His eyes were dark and full of entreaty; his voice was husky.

===Chinese===
他攥住我的双手，满腔恳求的狂热，恰如两年前他满腔妒火攥住我那样。他把我拉向自己。他的眼睛黑沉沉的，盛满哀恳；他的嗓音发哑。

===Original===
“Syb, poor little Syb, I will be good to you! You can have what you like. You don’t know what you mean when you say no.”

===Chinese===
“西比，可怜的小西比，我会待你好的！你要什么就有什么。你说‘不’的时候，自己都不知道这个‘不’字是什么分量。”

===Original===
No; I would not yield. He offered me everything﻿—but control. He was a man who meant all he said. His were no idle promises on the spur of the moment. But no, no, no, no, he was not for me. My love must know, must have suffered, must understand.

===Chinese===
不；我不肯屈服。他什么都肯给我——唯独“驾驭”除外。他是个句句话都作数的男人。他许的绝不是一时兴起的空口诺言。可是不，不，不，不，他不是给我的那个人。我的爱人必须懂得，必须受过苦，必须理解。

===Original===
“Syb, you do not answer. May I call you mine? You must, you must, you must!”

===Chinese===
“西比，你不作声。我能把你叫作我的人吗？你必须，你必须，你必须！”

===Original===
His hot breath was upon my cheek. The pleasant, open, manly countenance was very near-perilously near. The intoxication of his love was overpowering me. I had no hesitation about trusting him. He was not distasteful to me in any way. What was the good of waiting for that other﻿—the man who had suffered, who knew, who understood? I might never find him; and, if I did, ninety-nine chances to one he would not care for me.

===Chinese===
他火热的呼吸扑在我脸上。那张讨人喜欢、开朗、有丈夫气的脸离得那样近——近得危险。他的爱的酒力正在把我灌醉。要信任他，我没有半点迟疑。论哪一头，他都谈不上叫我嫌恶。等那另一位——那受过苦、懂得了、理解了的人——又有什么益处？我兴许永远寻不着他；即便寻着了，九十九比一，他也未必把我放在心上。

===Original===
“Syb, Syb, can’t you love me just a little?”

===Chinese===
“西比，西比，你就不能稍稍爱我一点点？”

===Original===
There was a winning charm in his manner. Nature had endowed him liberally with virile fascination. My hard uncongenial life had rendered me weak. He was drawing me to him; he was irresistible. Yes; I would be his wife. I grew dizzy, and turned my head sharply backwards and took a long gasping breath, another and another, of that fresh cool air suggestive of the grand old sea and creak of cordage and bustle and strife of life. My old spirit revived, and my momentary weakness fled. There was another to think of than myself, and that was Harold. Under a master-hand I would be harmless; but to this man I would be as a two-edged sword in the hand of a novice﻿—gashing his fingers at every turn, and eventually stabbing his honest heart.

===Chinese===
他的举止里有一股摄人的魅力。造物主慷慨地赋了他一身男子气的魔力。我那艰苦悖逆天性的日子已把我熬得软弱。他在把我往他身边引；他叫人无力抗拒。是的；我愿意做他的妻子。我一阵眩晕，猛地把头向后一仰，深深吸了一口那清凉的新鲜空气，一口，又一口——那空气里透着苍茫古老的海洋，透着缆绳的吱嘎、人生的奔忙与争斗。我旧日的精神还了魂，片刻的软弱逃走了。除了我自己，还有另一个人要顾念，那就是哈罗德。落在一双妙手里，我兴许无害；可到了这个人手里，我就如生手握着的双刃剑——回回割破他的手指，到头来还要刺穿他诚实的心。

===Original===
It was impossible to make him see my refusal was for his good. He was as a favourite child pleading for a dangerous toy. I desired to gratify him, but the awful responsibility of the aftereffects loomed up and deterred me.

===Chinese===
要叫他明白我的拒绝正是为他好，那是办不到的。他就像一个得宠的孩子，哭求一件危险的玩物。我很想依了他，可那后果的可怕责任隐隐迫来，叫我止住了手。

===Original===
“Hal, it can never be.”

===Chinese===
“哈尔，这事永远成不了。”

===Original===
He dropped my hands and drew himself up.

===Chinese===
他松开我的手，挺直了身子。

===Original===
“I will not take your No till the morning. Why do you refuse me? Is it my temper? You need not be afraid of that. I don’t think I’d hurt you; and I don’t drink, or smoke, or swear very much; and I’ve never destroyed a woman’s name. I would not stoop to press you against your will if you were like the ordinary run of women; but you are such a queer little party, that I’m afraid you might be boggling at some funny little point that could easily be wiped out.”

===Chinese===
“到明天早晨以前，我不接受你这一声‘不’。你为什么拒绝我？是为我的脾气？那个你不用怕。我想我不会伤害你；我不喝酒，不抽烟，粗话也很少骂；我还从没毁坏过哪个女人的名声。你若同寻常女子是一路，我决不至于低三下四去强你所难；可你偏偏是这么个古怪的小人儿，我怕你正是卡在某个古里古怪的小节点上——其实那一点，轻轻一抹就没了。”

===Original===
“Yes; it is only a little point. But if you wipe it out you will knock the end out of the whole thing﻿—for the point is myself. I would not suit you. It would not be wise for you to marry me.”

===Chinese===
“正是；那确只是一点。可你若把它抹掉了，整件事也就见了底——因为那一点就是我自己。我配不上你。你娶我，是不明智的。”

===Original===
“But I’m the only person concerned. If you are not afraid for yourself, I am quite satisfied.”

===Chinese===
“可事关的只有我一个人。只要你自己不怕，我就十分满足了。”

===Original===
We faced about and walked homewards in unbroken silence﻿—too perturbed to fall into our usual custom of chewing bush-leaves as we went.

===Chinese===
我们掉转身，一路往家走，沉默没断过——心绪太乱，连往常边走边嚼灌木叶子的习惯也顾不上了。

===Original===
I thought much that night when all the house was abed. It was tempting. Harold would be good to me, and would lift me from this life of poverty which I hated, to one of ease. Should I elect to remain where I was, till the grave there was nothing before me but the life I was leading now: my only chance of getting above it was by marriage, and Harold Beecham’s offer was the one chance of a lifetime. Perhaps he could manage me well enough. Yes; I had better marry him.

===Chinese===
当夜全家睡下之后，我想了许多。这诱惑着实撩人。哈罗德会待我好，会把我从我所憎恨的这种穷苦日子里提起来，放进安逸的日子里。倘若我选择留在原地，那么直到进坟，摆在我面前的就只有眼下过的这种日子：要翻身，我唯一的路子是嫁人，而哈罗德·比彻姆的求婚，是一辈子只有一回的机会。或许，他还能把我管教得服服帖帖。是的；我还是嫁他为好。

===Original===
And I believe in marriage﻿—that is, I think it the most sensible and respectable arrangement for the replenishing of a nation which has yet been suggested. But marriage is a solemn issue of life. I was as suited for matrimony as any of the sex, but only with an exceptional helpmeet﻿—and Harold was not he. My latent womanliness arose and pointed this out so plainly that I seized my pen and wrote:

===Chinese===
而且我是信婚姻的——就是说，迄今为止为着给国家添丁补员想出的种种安排里，我认为婚姻是最明白懂事、最体面的一种。但婚姻是人生的严肃大事。论适婚，我不输这性别里的任何一个，却只配一个非凡的伴侣——而哈罗德不是那个人。我潜藏的女人天性站了起来，把这一点明明白白指了出来，于是我抓起笔，写道：

===Original===
> I will not get a chance of speaking to you in the morning, so write. Never mention marriage to me again. I have firmly made up my mind﻿—it must be No. It will always be a comfort to me in the years to come to know that I was loved once, if only for a few hours. It is not that I do not care for you, as I like you better than any man I have ever seen; but I do not mean ever to marry. When you lost your fortune I was willing to accede to your request, as I thought you wanted me; but now that you are rich again you will not need me. I am not good enough to be your wife, for you are a good man; and better, because you do not know you are good. You may feel uncomfortable or lonely for a little while, because, when you make up your mind, you are not easily thwarted; but you will find that your fancy for me will soon pass. It is only a fancy, Hal. Take a look in the glass, and you will see reflected there the figure of a stalwart man who is purely virile, possessing not the slightest attribute of the weaker sex, therefore your love is merely a passing flame. I do not impute fickleness to you, but merely point out a masculine characteristic, and that you are a man, and only a man, pure and unadulterated. Look around, and from the numbers of good women to be found on every side choose one who will make you a fitter helpmeet, a more conventional comrade, than I could ever do. I thank you for the inestimable honour you have conferred upon me; but keep it till you find someone worthy of it, and by and by you will be glad that I have set you free.
> Goodbye, Hal!
> Your sincere and affec. friend,
> Sybylla Penelope Melvyn.

===Chinese===
> 明天早晨我不会有机会同你说话，所以写了这几行。永远不要再向我提起婚姻。我已拿定主意——非“不”不可。知道自己也曾被人爱过，哪怕只爱了几个钟头，在往后的岁月里，这于我将永远是一点安慰。并不是我不把你放在心上，我喜欢你还胜过我所见过的任何男人；只是我不打算嫁人，永远不。你破了产的那阵子，我甘愿应承你的请求，因为我想你是需要我的；如今你重新阔了起来，就用不着我了。我做你的妻子不够格，因为你是个好人；而且好上加好——因为你并不知道自己好。你也许会有一小阵子不自在、孤单，因为你这个人一旦拿定主意，是不肯轻易受人阻挠的；可你会发现，你对我的这番情意很快就会过去。那不过是一时迷恋，哈尔。照照镜子吧，你会看见里面映出一条魁伟汉子的身影，纯然是个男子，找不出半点弱性别的属性，因此你的爱不过是一簇过眼的火苗。我不是说你薄幸，只是指出一条男子汉的特性，指出你是一个男人，而且仅仅是一个男人，纯而又纯、不掺一丝假。往四下里看看吧，从身边成群的好女人里挑一位，她会成为比我合适得多的贤内助、比我合常规得多的伴侣。我谢谢你赐予我这无价的荣宠；且把它收好，等你寻到配得上它的人；到那时，你就会庆幸是我放你自由了。
> 再见，哈尔！
> 你诚挚而深情的朋友，
> 西比拉·佩内洛普·梅尔文。

===Original===
Then I crept into bed beside my little sister, and though the air inside had not cooled, and the room was warm, I shivered so that I clasped the chubby, golden-haired little sleeper in my arms that I might feel something living and real and warm.

===Chinese===
随后我爬上床，睡到小妹妹身边。屋里的空气不曾凉下来，房间还暖烘烘的，我却抖得厉害，便把那胖乎乎、金头发的小睡人搂进怀里，好教自己触着一点活的、真实的、温暖的东西。

===Original===
“Oh, Rory, Rory!” I whispered, raining upon her lonely-hearted tears. “In all the world is there never a comrade strong and true to teach me the meaning of this hollow, grim little tragedy﻿—life? Will it always be this ghastly aloneness? Why am I not good and pretty and simple like other girls? Oh, Rory, Rory, why was I ever born? I am of no use or pleasure to anyone in all the world!”

===Chinese===
“啊，罗瑞，罗瑞！”我悄声唤着，把泪珠纷纷洒在她孤寂的心上。“这茫茫世上，就没有一个又强又真的同伴，来教给我这空洞冷酷的小悲剧——人生——的意义吗？这种可怕的孤单，要一直这样下去吗？为什么我不能像别的姑娘那样善良、漂亮、简单？啊，罗瑞，罗瑞，我为什么要生下来？普天之下，没有一个人用得着我，也没有一个人能从我这里得到快乐！”

## XXXVII He That Despiseth Little Things, Shall Fall Little by Little / 第37章 轻视小事者必渐倾覆

### I / 一

===Original===
The morning came, breakfast, next Harold’s departure. I shook my head and slipped the note into his hand as we parted. He rode slowly down the road. I sat on the step of the garden gate, buried my face in my hands, and reviewed the situation. I could see my life, stretching out ahead of me, barren and monotonous as the thirsty track along which Harold was disappearing. Today it was washing, ironing tomorrow, next day baking, after that scrubbing﻿—thus on and on. We would occasionally see a neighbour or a tea-agent, a tramp or an Assyrian hawker. By hard slogging against flood, fire, drought, pests, stock diseases, and the sweating occasioned by importation, we could manage to keep bread in our mouths. By training and education I was fitted for nought but what I was, or a general slavey, which was many degrees worse. I could take my choice. Life was too much for me. What was the end of it, what its meaning, aim, hope, or use?

===Chinese===
早晨来了，早饭过了，接下来便是哈罗德的启程。分手时我摇了摇头，把那封信塞进他手里。他缓缓策马沿大路而去。我坐在花园门口的台阶上，双手掩面，把眼下的处境掂量了一番。我看得见自己的一生在前头铺展开去，荒芜而单调，恰似哈罗德身影正渐渐消失的那条干渴的路。今天是洗衣，明天是熨衣，后天是烤面包，再往后是擦洗——如此绵绵无尽。我们偶尔会见到一个邻居或一个茶叶推销员，一个流浪汉或一个亚述货郎。凭着同洪水、野火、旱灾、虫害、牲畜疫病以及进口货压逼出来的血汗苦干，我们好歹能把面包糊在嘴上。论所受的调教与教育，我只配做眼下这样的人，再不然就去做个打杂女仆（slavey），那还要糟上好几倍。我尽可以在这两者里任选其一。生活对我来说太沉重了。它到头来是个什么结局，它的意义、目标、指望、用途又何在？

===Original===
In comparison to millions I knew that I had received more than a fair share of the goods of life; but knowing another has leprosy makes our cancer none the easier to bear.

===Chinese===
比起千千万万的人来，我知道自己从人生的种种福分里所得的，已经多过应得的一份；可是明知别人害着麻风，并不能叫我们自己的癌症好受半分。

===Original===
My mother’s voice, sharp and cross, roused me. “Sybylla, you lazy unprincipled girl, to sit scheming there while your poor old mother is at the washtub. You sit idling there, and then by and by you’ll be groaning about this terrible life in which there’s time for nothing but work.”

===Chinese===
母亲的声音又尖又恼，把我惊醒。“西比拉，你这个偷懒没规矩的丫头，你可怜的老娘在洗衣盆跟前泡着，你倒坐在那儿想心事。你这会儿闲坐着，往后准又要哀叹这可怕的日子——这种日子里除了干活，别的一点工夫也没有。”

===Original===
How she fussed and bothered over the clothes was a marvel to me. My frame of mind was such that it seemed it would not signify if all our clothes went to the dogs, and the clothes of our neighbours, and the clothes of the whole world, and the world itself for the matter of that.

===Chinese===
她对那些衣裳那样操心受累，在我看真是一桩奇事。我的心境又是那样：哪怕我们全家的衣裳都遭了殃，连带左邻右舍的衣裳、普天之下的衣裳，说句实话连整个世界一齐遭了殃，似乎都算不得什么。

===Original===
“Sybylla, you are a dirty careless washer. You’ve put Stanley’s trousers in the boil and the colour is coming out of them, and your father’s best white handkerchief should have been with the first lot, and here it is now.”

===Chinese===
“西比拉，你洗起衣裳来又脏又不上心。你把斯坦利的裤子搁进了煮锅里，颜色都要煮掉了；你爹顶好的白手绢本该跟头一批一起下水的，瞧，这会儿还搁在这里。”

===Original===
Poor mother got crosser as she grew weary with the fierce heat and arduous toil, and as I in my abstraction continued to make mistakes, but the last straw was the breaking of an old cup which I accidentally pushed off the table.

===Chinese===
可怜的母亲叫烈日与繁重的活计熬得越累，火气也越大；我又心不在焉，错处一犯再犯；可压垮她的最后一根稻草，是我失手从桌上碰落摔碎的一只旧杯子。

===Original===
I got it hot. Had I committed an act of premeditated villainy I could not have received more lecturing. I deserved it﻿—I was careless, cups were scarce with us, and we could not afford more; but what I rail against is the grindingly uneventful narrowness of the life in which the unintentional breaking of a common cup is good for a long scolding.

===Chinese===
我挨了一顿好骂。哪怕我蓄谋干下一桩恶行，受的训斥也不过是这个分量。这我认——我确实粗心，家里的杯子本就稀少，我们也添不起新的；可我愤愤然不能平的，是这日子磨人心性的狭窄乏味——在这样的日子里，无意间打碎一只寻常的杯子，就够换来一顿长长的数落。

===Original===
Ah, my mother! In my life of nineteen years I can look back and see a time when she was all gentleness and refinement, but the polish has been worn off it by years and years of scrubbing and scratching, and washing and patching, and poverty and husbandly neglect, and the bearing of burdens too heavy for delicate shoulders. Would that we were more companionable, it would make many an oasis in the desert of our lives. Oh that I could take an all-absorbing interest in patterns and recipes, bargains and orthodoxy! Oh that you could understand my desire to feel the rolling billows of the ocean beneath, to hear the pealing of a great organ through dimly lit arches, or the sob and wail of a violin in a brilliant crowded hall, to be swept on by the human stream.

===Chinese===
啊，我的母亲！在我十九年的生命里回头望去，我看得见曾几何时她通身是温柔与文雅；可年年岁岁的擦擦刮刮、洗洗补补，贫穷，做丈夫的疏于体恤，还有那副娇嫩肩膀挑不动的重担，把那层光华都磨蚀尽了。但愿我们母女能更相知些，那便会在我们生命的荒漠里造出一片片的绿洲。啊，但愿我也能对纸样与食谱、便宜货与正统教门起一份全神贯注的兴趣！啊，但愿你能懂得我的渴望——去感受脚下翻滚的海涛，去听大风琴的轰鸣穿过幽暗的拱廊，或听小提琴的呜咽哀泣响彻灯火辉煌、宾客满堂的大厅，去教人潮席卷着向前！

===Original===
Ah, thou cruel fiend﻿—Ambition! Desire!

===Chinese===
啊，你这残忍的恶魔——雄心！欲望！

===Original===
> “Soul of the leaping flame,
 

 Heart of the scarlet fire,
 

 Spirit that hath for name
 

 Only the name﻿—Desire!”

===Chinese===
> “跳跃的火焰之魂，
 

 猩红的烈火之心，
 

 那以之为名的精灵，
 

 其名唯有——欲望！”

===Original===
To hot young hearts beating passionately in strong breasts, the sweetest thing is motion.

===Chinese===
对一颗火热年轻的心——在结实的胸膛里激越搏动的心——来说，最甜美的事莫过于奔动。

===Original===
No, that part of me went beyond my mother’s understanding. On the other hand, there was a part of my mother﻿—her brave cheerfulness, her trust in God, her heroic struggle to keep the home together﻿—which went soaring on beyond my understanding, leaving me a coward weakling, grovelling in the dust.

===Chinese===
不，我的这一部分，超出了母亲的理解。反过来，母亲身上也有一部分——她那勇敢的快活、她对上帝的信靠、她那苦苦撑持这个家不散的英雄奋战——又高飞在我的理解之外，把我撂在底下，做个匍匐于尘埃的懦弱的废物。

===Original===
Would that hot dreary day never close? What advantage when it did? The next and the next and many weeks of others just the same were following hard after.

===Chinese===
这闷热难熬的一天难道永远不肯收场？收了场又有什么好处？紧接着的明天、后天，还有多少个一模一样的礼拜，正紧追在脚跟后头。

===Original===
If the souls of lives were voiced in music, there are some that none but a great organ could express, others the clash of a full orchestra, a few to which nought but the refined and exquisite sadness of a violin could do justice. Many might be likened unto common pianos, jangling and out of tune, and some to the feeble piping of a penny whistle, and mine could be told with a couple of nails in a rusty tinpot.

===Chinese===
倘若众生的灵魂都能化作音乐倾吐，有的灵魂非大风琴不足以表达，有的须得整支乐队的铿锵齐鸣，有几许则唯有小提琴那精致入骨的哀愁才担当得起。许许多多的灵魂尽可比作寻常的钢琴，叮叮当当，五音不全；还有些可比便士哨笛细弱无力的咝咝声；至于我的这一副，拿两枚铁钉敲一只生锈的铁皮罐，也就说尽了。

===Original===
Why do I write? For what does anyone write? Shall I get a hearing? If so﻿—what then?

===Chinese===
我为什么要写作？任何人又为什么写作？会有人肯听我吗？纵然有——往后又怎样？

===Original===
I have voiced the things around me, the small-minded thoughts, the sodden round of grinding tasks﻿—a monotonous, purposeless, needless existence. But patience, O heart, surely I can make a purpose! For the present, of my family I am the most suited to wait about common public-houses to look after my father when he is inebriated. It breaks my mother’s heart to do it; it is dangerous for my brothers; imagine Gertie in such a position! But me it does not injure, I have the faculty for doing that sort of thing without coming to harm, and if it makes me more bitter and godless, well, what matter?

===Chinese===
我已把周遭的事物、那些琐屑狭小的念头、那泡得发胀的一轮又一轮苦活都倾诉了出来——一种单调、无目的、也无必要的生存。可是耐心些，我的心啊，我总归能造出一个目的来！眼下这一程，一家人里数我最合适在父亲喝醉时到寻常的酒馆去守着照看他。这差事伤了母亲的心；对弟弟们又有危险；设想格蒂处在那样的境地吧！可它伤不到我，我有这套本事，干这种事不受损伤；倘若这叫我更刻毒、更不信神，嘿，那又有什么要紧？

### II / 二

===Original===
The next letter I received from Gertie contained:

===Chinese===
接下来我收到的格蒂来信里写道：

===Original===
> I suppose you were glad to see Harry. He did not tell me he was going, or I would have sent some things by him. I thought he would be able to tell me lots about you that I was dying to hear, but he never said a word, only that you were all well. He went travelling some weeks ago. I missed him at first because he used to be so kind to me; but now I don’t, because Mr. Creyton, whom Harry left to manage Five-Bob, comes just as often as Harry used to, and is lots funnier. He brings me something nice every time. Uncle Jay-Jay teases me about him.

===Chinese===
> 我猜你见到哈里一定挺高兴的。他没有告诉我要出门，不然我会托他给你捎些东西去。我原想着他能同我讲好多你的事，都是我急死了想听的，可他一个字也没说，只说你们都好。他是几个星期前出门游历的。起先我还想他，因为他从前待我那么好；如今倒不想了，因为哈里留下来照管五博丘的克赖顿先生（Mr. Creyton）来得同哈里往常一样勤，人还风趣得多。他每回来都给我带点好东西。杰杰舅舅拿他来打趣我。

===Original===
Happy butterfly-natured Gertie! I envied her. With Gertie’s letter came also one from grannie, with further mention of Harold Beecham.

===Chinese===
快快活活蝴蝶般性情的格蒂！我真羡慕她。同格蒂的信一道，还来了外婆的一封，信里又提到了哈罗德·比彻姆。

===Original===
> We don’t know what to make of Harold Beecham. He was always such a steady fellow, and hated to go away from home even for a short time, but now he has taken an idea to rush away to America, and is not coming home till he has gone over the world. He is not going to see anything, because by cablegrams his aunts got he is one place today and hundreds of miles away tomorrow. It is some craze he has suddenly taken. I was asking Augusta if there was ever any lunacy in the family, and she says not that she knows of. It was a very unwise act to leave full management to Creyton and Benson in the face of such a drought. One warning and marvellous escape such as he has had ought to be enough for a man with any sense. I told him he’d be poor again if he didn’t take care, but he said he didn’t mind if all his property was blown into atoms, as it had done him more harm than good, whatever he means by talking that way. Insanity is the only reason I can see for his conduct. I thought he had his eye on Gertie, but I questioned her, and it appears he has never said anything to her. I wonder what was his motive for going to Possum Gully that time?

===Chinese===
> 我们真摸不透哈罗德·比彻姆是怎么回事。他向来是个沉稳的人，连短期离家都不乐意，如今却突然起意一头奔美洲去了，不把世界走遍不肯回家。他也不是去看什么名堂的，因为据他两位姑母接到的电报，他今天在一处，明天又到了几百英里外。他是忽然着了什么魔症。我问过奥古斯塔，他家从前可曾有过疯病，她说就她所知没有。当着这样的大旱，把全部产业交由克赖顿和本森照管，实在是极不明智的举动。像他这样经过一场警醒、死里逃生逃过一回的人，但凡有点脑筋，一回也该够了。我警告过他，再不当心就要重新受穷的，可他说哪怕全部家当炸成齑粉他也不在乎，说这份家当害他多、益他少——随他那样说话是什么意思吧。疯癫，是我能替他的行径想出的唯一缘故。我原以为他相中的是格蒂，可我盘问过她，看来他对她从来没有说过一句那样的话。我倒纳闷，他那一回去袋熊谷，究竟是什么来由？

===Original===
Travel was indeed an unexpected development on the part of Harold Beecham. He had such a marked aversion to anything of that sort, and never went even to Sydney or Melbourne for more than a few days at a stretch, and that on business or at a time of stock shows.

===Chinese===
出门游历，在哈罗德·比彻姆身上确乎是一桩出乎意料的发展。他对这一类事向来避之惟恐不及，连悉尼或墨尔本也从不去一连待上几天，去也无非是为了买卖，或是赶牲畜展览会的时节。

===Original===
There were many conjectures re the motive of his visit to Possum Gully, but I held my peace.

===Chinese===
关于他那次袋熊谷之行的动机，众说纷纭；我却守口如瓶。

## XXXVIII A Tale That Is Told and a Day That Is Done / 第38章 一个讲完的故事，一个了结的日子

===Original===
> “There are others toiling and straining
 

 ’Neath burdens graver than mine;
 

 They are weary, yet uncomplaining
 

 —I know it, yet I repine:
 

 I know it, how time will ravage,
 

 How time will level, and yet
 

 I long with a longing savage,
 

 I regret with a fierce regret.”

===Chinese===
> “另有一些人劳碌挣扎，
 

 在比我更沉的担子底下；
 

 他们疲惫，却不诉苦
 

 ——我明知，却仍旧怨嗟：
 

 我明知，时间会怎样摧残，
 

 会把一切夷为平地，然而
 

 我怀着蛮野的渴望渴念，
 

 我怀着凶烈的悔恨追悔。”

===Original===
Possum Gully, 25th March, 1899.

===Chinese===
袋熊谷，1899 年 3 月 25 日。

===Original===
Christmas, only distinguished from the fifty-two slow Sundays of the year by plum-pudding, roast turkey, and a few bottles of homemade beer, has been once more; New Year, ushered in with sweet-scented midsummer wattle and bloom of gum- and box-tree has gone; February has followed, March is doing likewise, and my life is still the same.

===Chinese===
圣诞节又来过一回了——它同一年里五十二个慢吞吞的星期日相比，分别只在李子布丁、烤火鸡和几瓶自酿啤酒；伴着香馥馥的仲夏金合欢、伴着桉树与黄杨桉的花开迎进来的新年，也已去了；二月接踵而过，三月正照样走着，而我的生活依旧原封未动。

===Original===
What the future holds I know not, and am tonight so weary that I do not care.

===Chinese===
前途握着什么，我不知道；今夜我又倦到这般地步，连管也懒得管了。

===Original===
> “Time rules us all. And life, indeed, is not
 

 The thing we planned it out, ere hope was dead;
 

 And then, we women cannot choose our lot.”

===Chinese===
> “时间主宰着我们众人。而人生，实在
 

 已不是希望未死时我们筹划的模样；
 

 再说，我们女人拣选不得自己的命运。”

===Original===
Time is thorough in his work, and as that arch-cheat, Hope, gradually becomes a phantom of the past, the neck will grow inured to its yoke.

===Chinese===
时间做起事来向来彻底；随着那位头号大骗子——希望——渐渐化作往昔的幽灵，脖颈也就渐渐熬惯了它的轭。

===Original===
Tonight is one of the times when the littleness﻿—the abject littleness﻿—of all things in life comes home to me.

===Chinese===
今夜又是那样一种时刻：人生万事的渺小——卑贱得可怜的渺小——一齐涌上我的心头。

===Original===
After all, what is there in vain ambition? King or slave, we all must die, and when death knocks at our door, will it matter whether our life has been great or small, fast or slow, so long as it has been true﻿—true with the truth that will bring rest to the soul?

===Chinese===
说到底，虚妄的抱负里又有什么呢？帝王也罢，奴隶也罢，我们都免不了一死；当死亡来敲我们的门，只要这一生是真切的——真切得能给灵魂带来安息的那种真切——它是伟大还是渺小、是迅疾还是迟缓，又有什么要紧？

===Original===
> “But the toughest lives are brittle,
 

 And the bravest and the best
 

 Lightly fall﻿—it matters little;
 

 Now I only long for rest.”

===Chinese===
> “可最坚韧的性命也脆薄易折，
 

 而最勇敢、最优秀的人
 

 也轻轻倒下——这无甚要紧；
 

 如今我只渴慕安息。”

===Original===
To weary hearts throbbing slowly in hopeless breasts the sweetest thing is rest.

===Chinese===
对于在无望的胸膛里缓缓悸动的疲惫的心，最甜美的东西就是安息。

===Original===
And my heart is weary. Oh, how it aches tonight﻿—not with the ache of a young heart passionately crying out for battle, but with the slow dead ache of an old heart returning vanquished and defeated!

===Chinese===
我的心就是疲惫的。啊，今夜它痛得多么厉害——不是年轻的心意气飞扬、高呼求战的那种痛，而是一颗老去的心铩羽而归、一败涂地时那种迟钝的死痛！

---

===Original===
Enough of pessimistic snarling and grumbling! Enough! Enough! Now for a lilt of another theme:

===Chinese===
悲观的咆哮与牢骚，够啦！够了！够了！现在换个题目，来一支轻快的调子：

===Original===
I am proud that I am an Australian, a daughter of the Southern Cross, a child of the mighty bush. I am thankful I am a peasant, a part of the bone and muscle of my nation, and earn my bread by the sweat of my brow, as man was meant to do. I rejoice I was not born a parasite, one of the bloodsuckers who loll on velvet and satin, crushed from the proceeds of human sweat and blood and souls.

===Chinese===
我自豪，因为我是个澳大利亚人，是南十字星的女儿，是雄伟丛林的孩子。我感恩，因为我是个庄稼人，是我民族骨与肉的一部分，凭额头的汗水挣得自己的面包——人生在世，本该如此。我庆幸，自己没有生作寄生虫，生作那班慵然高卧在天鹅绒和缎子上的吸血鬼——那绫罗绸缎的进项，是从人的汗、血和灵魂里榨取的。

---

===Original===
Ah, my sunburnt brothers!﻿—sons of toil and of Australia! I love and respect you well, for you are brave and good and true. I have seen not only those of you with youth and hope strong in your veins, but those with pathetic streaks of grey in your hair, large families to support, and with half a century sitting upon your work-laden shoulders. I have seen you struggle uncomplainingly against flood, fire, disease in stock, pests, drought, trade depression, and sickness, and yet have time to extend your hands and hearts in true sympathy to a brother in misfortune, and spirits to laugh and joke and be cheerful.

===Chinese===
啊，我晒得黝黑的兄弟们！——劳作之子，澳大利亚之子！我深深地爱你们、敬重你们，因为你们勇敢、善良而真诚。我见过的，不只是青春与希望还在血管里强劲奔涌的你们，也有鬓发间夹着令人心酸的缕缕灰白、要拉扯一大群儿女、半个世纪压在劳作满载的双肩上的你们。我见你们毫无怨言地同洪水、野火、畜疫、虫害、旱灾、商市萧条和疾病搏斗，却还有工夫向身遭厄运的兄弟伸出双手、捧出真心，还有精神去笑去打趣、快快活活。

===Original===
And for my sisters a great love and pity fills my heart. Daughters of toil, who scrub and wash and mend and cook, who are dressmakers, paperhangers, milkmaids, gardeners, and candle-makers all in one, and yet have time to be cheerful and tasty in your homes, and make the best of the few oases to be found along the narrow dusty track of your existence. Would that I were more worthy to be one of you﻿—more a typical Australian peasant﻿—cheerful, honest, brave!

===Chinese===
而对我的姐妹们，一股深厚的爱与怜悯在我心里涨满。劳作的女儿们啊，你们又擦又洗、又缝又补、又煮又烧，一身兼任裁缝、裱糊匠、挤奶女工、园丁和制烛匠，却还有工夫在自己家里快快活活、有滋有味，把你们那条狭窄多尘的人生辙道上所能寻见的几片绿洲，享用到十足。但愿我更配得上做你们中的一员——更像一个地道的澳大利亚庄稼人——快活、诚实、勇敢！

===Original===
I love you, I love you. Bravely you jog along with the rope of class distinction drawing closer, closer, tighter, tighter around you: a few more generations and you will be as enslaved as were ever the muzhiks of Russia. I see it and know it, but I cannot help you. My ineffective life will be trod out in the same round of toil﻿—I am only one of yourselves, I am only an unnecessary, little, bush commoner, I am only a﻿—woman!

===Chinese===
我爱你们，我爱你们。你们勇敢地一路趱行，阶级分野的绳索却在你们周身越收越近、越收越紧：再过几代人，你们就会沦落到同俄国的农奴（muzhiks）一般无二的受奴役地步。我看得见，也知道，可我帮不了你们。我这条不中用的性命，也将在这同一轮劳作里被踩磨殆尽——我不过是你们中的一个，不过是一个多余的、小小的丛林平民，我不过是个——女人！

---

===Original===
The great sun is sinking in the west, grinning and winking knowingly as he goes, upon the starving stock and drought-smitten wastes of land. Nearer he draws to the gum-tree scrubby horizon, turns the clouds to orange, scarlet, silver flame, gold! Down, down he goes. The gorgeous, garish splendour of sunset pageantry flames out; the long shadows eagerly cover all; the kookaburras laugh their merry mocking good night; the clouds fade to turquoise, green, and grey; the stars peep shyly out; the soft call of the mopoke arises in the gullies! With much love and good wishes to all﻿—Good night! Goodbye!

===Chinese===
大太阳正在西天沉落，一面去，一面会心地咧着嘴、眨着眼，望着那饿羸的牲口和遭旱抽打的荒地。他离那桉树丛莽杂乱的地平线越来越近，把云霞烧成橙色、猩红、银焰、金黄！落啊，落啊，他沉下去了。落日仪仗那绚烂刺目的华彩轰然烧尽；长长的影子急切地把一切掩住；笑翠鸟笑吟吟道出它们欢快而嘲弄的晚安；云霞褪成青碧、苍绿与灰白；星星羞怯地探出头来；袋鸮柔声的啼唤在沟谷间升起！谨向诸位奉上满心的爱与祝福——晚安！再见！

===Original===
**Amen.**

===Chinese===
**阿门。**

## Endnotes / 尾注

===Original===
1. Shouting﻿—i.e., treating ↩︎
2. Poddied﻿—i.e., hand-fed. ↩︎

===Chinese===
1. 请客（Shouting）——即请客款待 ↩︎
2. 手喂（Poddied）——即人工喂养。 ↩︎
